- Logging Level points are awarded only by DB triggers into `athlete_xp_events` with unique per-record/day/week `source_key`s — why: idempotent, no client-side farming, history backfillable.
- Achievement "seen" state lives in `athlete_achievement_views` (server-side), never localStorage — why: reveals must not replay across devices.
- Member-made chats (`chat_groups.kind` = `'direct'` 1:1s and `'crew'` groups) are private to their members: staff RLS, group lists and "View as client" must never include them, and request/invite state (accept, join, decline, block, remove, report) changes only through `chat_direct_respond` / `community_message_author` / `chat_direct_start` / `chat_block_person` / `crew_*`; a new 1:1 is a request (capped per day by `chat_direct_open`) unless the person written to follows the writer and Block is per person (`chat_user_blocks`), and a person's own Pin / Hide Alerts / Delete / Mark as Unread live in `chat_thread_prefs` (own rows only) and never move a read marker — why: declines, blocks and removals must stay silent (nobody can tell a decline from unread), nobody can cold-message the whole community, and reports are the coach's only window in.
- PT session sync and reminder bookkeeping (`gcal_dirty`, `time_set_at`, `reminder_24h_sent_at` reset on move, auto-closing `pt_session_change_requests`) is done by DB triggers on `pt_sessions`, never by callers — why: sessions are edited from several screens, and every path must move Google and the evening-before text with it.
- A client changes a booked session only by asking: tapping a session anywhere in their app (Home's calendar card, the Calendar page, Workouts, the Booked list) opens `ClientSessionSheet` (when, length, where, video, the coach's note; Change time = an exact `proposed_date` / `proposed_start` in the session's zone, same length, plus other times and why; or Can't make it with why), saved by `requestSessionChange` (one pending per session, never a past or unchanged time; the session stays booked) with a Scheduling chat message that carries the request as a card (`kind: "session_change"`, `change_request_id`; the words are only the inbox / push preview). The coach answers right in the chat (`SessionChangeCard`): Approve moves the session to the exact time asked for in one tap (`answerSessionChange`: same length, never into the past, clashes shown before the tap, "Approved: …" to the client, a last-minute move texted), Decline keeps it booked with an optional reason, Other time opens the session's move form, and a "can't make it" opens the session's options (bank or count the credit); the same request also sits in `SessionActionsSheet` with the same choices, and moving the session closes it through `tg_pt_session_close_change_requests` — why: clients couldn't see a session's details or ask for a specific time without a back-and-forth in chat, and Marc's "Can I move my Tuesday to Thursday?" landed in the coach's chat as plain text with nothing to tap.
- A client's session request (`session_requests`, the chat's `SessionRequestCard`) is answered in one tap by `answerSessionRequest`: when it's instead of a booked session (`replaces_session_id`, picked by the client under "Is this instead of a booked session?" in Request a session, else `suggestReplaced` from the note, e.g. "Changing Tuesday session") Approve moves that session to the asked-for time, keeping its length, so its credit, Google event and reminders move with it; otherwise it books an extra one as staff from the client's own latest credit session (a call from the in-app video type), never twice for the same time. The card shows clashes before the tap and where a new session's credit comes from (`creditOutlook`, session-credit.ts): past the last credit it goes on the client's next package (packageRenewal), never a block, and only a client with no package at all needs "Approve anyway"; the booking dialog is More options and follows the same rule (`mustConfirmOverbook`) — why: approving Marc's swap opened the full booking form blocked at "0 sessions available", because the session it replaced was holding the credit.
- A session booked for a client tells them, whichever screen booked it: one message in their coach chat with the sessions as a card (`kind: "session_booked"`, `session_ids`, `SessionBookedCard`: read live, so a later move or cancel shows as it is now; a client's tap opens `ClientSessionSheet`, a video session has Join right on it) plus a `session_booked` push, from their coach (`is_automated`, `message_type` Scheduling, so it never closes "Needs reply" or earns an unread text). The database wakes the sender on every insert into `pt_sessions` (`pt_sessions_booked_kick`, a statement trigger, only for the first of a spree) and pg_cron `session-bookings-tick` every minute while one has waited 20 s (20270227090000_session_booked_message.sql); `announceBookedSessions` (session-booked.server.ts) waits until a client's bookings have been quiet for `BOOKED_QUIET_MS`, so Book ahead's sessions, written one at a time, and a repeating booking are one "Booked N sessions" message, then claims each session through `confirmation_sent_at` before sending (never twice; a message that fails gives the claim back). Who's told is `shouldAnnounceBooking` (session-booked.ts) and the same SQL in the trigger and the job: Scheduled, visible, `send_confirmation_email` on (it's every booking type's default), still ahead, booked in the last 2 days, not booked by the client themselves or brought in from the coach's Google (`SELF_OR_IMPORTED_VIA`: `client_app`, `booking_page`, `google_calendar`), and not already announced. An approved session request's own "✅ Booked" message is its confirmation (it inserts with `confirmation_sent_at`), and a client with no login is stamped and skipped (no chat to read). The booking form's toast says "… gets a message in their chat" only when they will (`bookedToast`) — why: on Nov 10 2026 the coach booked Devin for 4 PM the next day from his chat and Devin got nothing from the app, so the coach typed "Booked you in for 4pm tomorrow ✅" himself; every booking stored send_confirmation_email = true and nothing read it, and a session arranged after 6 PM the evening before is never reminded by text.
- The database converts a client's wall-clock time to an instant (and back) only through `public.local_to_instant` / `public.instant_to_local` (20270111090000_manitoba_time_zone.sql): Manitoba (America/Winnipeg and its links) is a fixed UTC-5 from Nov 1, 2026, every other zone uses its own rules; PT sessions' `starts_at` / `ends_at` (`tg_pt_session_compute_ts`) and `pt_local_today` use them, and new SQL that turns a local time into an instant uses them too, never a bare `AT TIME ZONE 'America/Winnipeg'` — why: Manitoba kept UTC-5 when the clocks would have fallen back on Nov 1, 2026 (Google, iPhones and the app's server all know it), but this database's rules still drop Winnipeg to UTC-6, so every session from that night was stored an hour late: 11 AM in Google and in clients' calendars for a 10 AM session, and an evening-before text saying 11.
- Google events from several calendars are merged per occurrence (`mergeGoogleCalendarLists`: iCalUID + original start), never by iCalUID alone, keeping the organizer's copy over an invitation copy (`isOrganizerCopy`: the organizer's address against the calendar the copy is on, since Google's `organizer.self` is true on every calendar the coach owns), and listing follows `nextPageToken`; the session sync writes to the event itself, never an invitation copy (`eventHome`, gcal-own-guests.server.ts: when the linked calendar only holds an invitation and the organizer is one of the coach's writable calendars, it updates, or on a cancel deletes, that one), and every agenda that mixes Google and app times orders them by instant (`compareInstants`), never as text — why: every repeat of a recurring event shares one iCalUID, so UID-only dedupe shows a weekly event once and the double-booking check misses the rest; Nicolas's sessions were linked to the invitation copies on the coach's main calendar of events the JF Effect calendar organizes, so the app's edits changed only those copies and Google showed two events an hour apart; and Google's "…-05:00" against the app's "…Z" sorted a 10 AM event before an 8:30 AM session.
- One session is one event in the coach's Google: the app never adds a guest to a PT session's event (it's made with none, every write is `sendUpdates=none`), and an event on the coach's calendar that lists one of the coach's own calendars as a guest (`coachOwnCalendarIds`: their main calendar and the one the app writes to, owned; never a calendar someone else shares) has that guest taken off (`ownGuestEmails` / `attendeesWithout`, gcal-own-guests.ts) in the same write when the sync updates it or the import tags it, clients kept invited and nobody emailed; a guest copy is only ever taken away when it says what the event says and no session points at it (`ownGuestDrop`, `copyMatchesEvent`), and a repeating event comes off as a whole series only once every copy of it is checked (`dropOwnGuestsFromSeries`, `seriesGuestCopiesSafe`). The `gcal-session-import` hook's `inspect` lists each copy's `ownGuests`, and `ownInvites` (report, or `apply`) fixes a client's existing ones — why: Nicolas's weekend series was made in Google on the Fitness Coaching calendar with the coach's own jaredjamesfit@gmail.com as a guest, so Google put an invitation copy (Yes / No / Maybe, "Organized by 49b37…@group.calendar.google.com") of every session on his main calendar, and each one showed twice; the app's October writes had gone to those copies, so some of them hold the only record of what happened and must never be removed blind.
- The coach's calendars read like Google Calendar's (Nov 15 2026): Day and Week are timelines (`TimeGrid`, time-grid.tsx; placement in calendar-timeline.ts, pure, tested): hours down the side, a column per day, each booking a block at its start and as tall as it lasts (`spanOnDay`: minutes off the device's clock, so a clock-change day lines up with its hour labels; past midnight clipped to each day), overlapping bookings side by side (`layoutDay`: earliest on the left, the longer of two that start together first, a freed column reused, spreading into free columns; back-to-back don't overlap), all-day things in a row under the day names, a Now line in today's column, ended blocks quieter, cancelled and no-shows dashed and crossed out. A block's words fit the block: its width by container query (a phone's week column is the first name, then the start time, then who · time range · what and where) and its lines by its height (`Lines`, a word clipped, never broken mid-word). It opens on the busy part when that fits, else near now, else before the first booking (`openingMinute`), and keeps that place as bookings load until the coach scrolls. A tap on empty time books there (`slotTime`, half hours, into the booking form's `initialTime`): a mouse click at once, a finger shows "+ 3 PM" first so a stray tap never opens the form. Nothing is dragged, so no booking's time changes from the grid; moves stay in the session sheet. The Calendar page (`CalendarBoard timeline`: the coach's, and a client's since Nov 16 2026, the next rule) is Day · Week · Month · List, and a day's name or a month day opens that day; the Home card (`DashboardScheduleCard`) is Day (the day's week strip picks a day in place, its timeline, then Coming up: what's still ahead after the day on screen, from today when looking back, `comingUpFrom`, never the day the timeline already shows), Week (the week's timeline; a day's name opens it for the visit, `dayFocus`, only the tabs are remembered), Month (who's booked each day, sessions before Google, `monthChipOrder`; a day opens its timeline) and List (Nov 16 2026: the week's strip with its dots and a Week spot after Sunday, `onSelectWeek`; a day (today to start) is its bookings one under another on a time column, "Marc Asugui" over "10:30–11:30 AM · 1:1 Training" (a call named, never the gym's address, `call`), a Now line through today, then Coming up; Week is the week's bookings one line each by day, "Mon 16 · 10:30 AM Marc Asugui · 1:1 Training"; cancelled and no-shows crossed out, ended ones quieter, a row opens it; the rule below); the period ("Tomorrow", "Nov 16 – 22") is never cut short and takes its own line under Day · Week · Month · List when they don't fit together, and a Day view's data is the same for every day of a week (`homeRange`), so stepping a day loads nothing. The timeline opens a few pixels above its opening hour (`LABEL_ROOM_PX`) so that hour's label shows whole. Week and month blocks are neutral, one family each (`BLOCK` = `FAMILY_BLOCK`, calendar-palette.ts, the next rule) and today stays a neutral filled circle — why: the coach found the month grid of dots with a separate list under it hard to read, and wanted to see at a glance when each booking starts and ends and where his free time is, the way Google Calendar shows it, without opening Google; then that he also liked the old compact week list (who's in when, without a full timeline) as a view of its own, and on a phone the day's label read "To…" and Tomorrow's Coming up repeated Monday's sessions right under Monday's timeline.
- Calendars have no purple (Nov 21 2026, calendar-palette.ts, calendar-neutral.test.ts): every calendar (the coach's and a client's Day / Week / Month / List, the Home card, the week strips, today's panel, the booking clash list, availability) reads its colours from one file, `FAMILY_BLOCK` / `FAMILY_MONTH_CHIP` / `FAMILY_ACCENT` / `FAMILY_CHIP`: a booking with a person in the text colour on a light tint, training lighter, the coach's own outlined, Google muted grey, in light and dark from the theme tokens; red only for what the design system already makes red (an error, an overdue, the screen's one main action), never a booking. A booking's state never rests on colour: done gets a check before its name, a cancel, no-show or move is crossed out and named ("Cancelled", "No-show", "Moved", `statusMark` / `statusWord`), ended ones quieter; Google, booking and status logic are unchanged — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach asked for the dashboard calendar without purple (white / off-white events, muted grey Google, red only for deliberate emphasis) with completed, missed and pending told apart without relying on colour.
- Every account's calendar works the coach's way (Nov 16 2026): the Home calendar card (a client's or member's `UpcomingScheduleCard`, a staff home's My calendar, the coach's `DashboardScheduleCard`) is Day · Week · Month · List from the same pieces (home-calendar-views.tsx: `HomeViewBar`, `HomeMonth`, `HomeWeekList`, `HomeEntry`), and the client's Calendar page is the coach's board (`CalendarBoard timeline`, Day · Week · Month · List); each person's choice is theirs, remembered on their own account (`useCalendarPrefs`: `homeView`, `boardView`), and a coach previewing a client picks views for that visit only (`useCalendarPrefs({ local: true })`), never saved to his account or theirs. A client's calendar is appointments and dates (sessions, appointments, check-ins, coach events, key dates, their own Google): workouts stay the Workouts schedule's, so only List and Day show a workout, the day on screen's (`TodayRow`: today's says its week and keeps its red Start / Continue, another day's opens its preview, Done on any day). A client's List is the card they've always had (Nov 16 2026): the week's strip (a day shows in place, staying on List), the day in full on a time column (the workout first, a Now line through today, `nowIndex`, "Nothing scheduled today. A good day to recover." when it's empty), then Coming up. Every List (the coach's and a client's) is one day at a time or the whole week, never a day picked over the week's rows: the strip's last spot, Week (after Sunday, a list icon, ringed while it's on, `AgendaWeek onSelectWeek` / `weekSelected`), shows every day of the week one line each (`HomeWeekList`; a client's is each day's own rows, `homeAgendaDays`, a workout by its name with no time and "Done" once it is, `weekEntryOf`), and tapping any day goes back to just that day; the period, ‹ › and Today follow what's shown (`listPeriod`: "Today", a day a step; "Nov 16 – 22", a week a step), Day or Week is remembered on the account (`calendar_view_prefs` `listScope`), and List loads like Day (`homeRange`), so switching never loads anything. A client's Day puts the workout as rows above the timeline (`dayRows`), and a day with nothing at a set time shows no empty hours (the timeline only when it has something timed, `dayTimed`). Coming up starts after the day on screen for everyone (`buildHomeAgenda` `day`, `comingUpFrom`). On a client's calendar a phone's narrow week column and month cell say when it starts ("10:30a", `blockLabels` `tiny`: every session is with their coach, and "1:1 Training" never fits), a List line names a call, never the gym's address (`callLabel`), and a coaching client's tap on empty time on a day ahead asks for a session then (`requestAt` / `canCreateOn`, `RequestSessionSheet initialTime`; "Request a session at 3 PM", `slotVerb`; never today, never a member, the page's header has no second Book); a session opens `ClientSessionSheet` as before. The client's page opens on List (`CLIENT_DEFAULT_VIEW`: a client's calendar is a few sessions a week at most, so what's coming says more than an empty day) and a `?date=` link on Day; a client's Home card opens on List, the coach's on Day — why: on Nov 16 2026 the coach found the List confusing (tapping a day still showed the whole week) and wanted one day at a time with the whole week a tap away; before that, the coach asked for the calendar he'd just been given (timelines, a month of names, the compact week list, a remembered view) for every app user; of 17 client logins only 4 had sessions booked in the next four weeks, so a client's day needed its workout's Start kept and no screen of empty hours; and that day he wanted the client card's List to be exactly the card clients had before (the strip, today in full, Coming up, Request a session).
- Online booking (`/book/<slug>`, booking.server.ts): a public, signed-out booking is always an `appointments` row and never touches session credits, even when the email matches a client; credit-using `pt_sessions` are only created for a trusted identity (signed-in client, or a staff-minted personal link, `/b/<token>`, resolved by `booking-invite.server.ts`: the personal-link rule below). Every online booking holds its time in `booking_slot_claims` (overlap-excluded) around the final re-check, and Google must be readable on every calendar (`requireAll`) or no times are offered — why: anyone can type a client's email, two people can tap the same slot, and a time we couldn't check could double-book.
- Where calls can be booked is one engine, smart-availability.ts (pure, tested; `planAvailability`), for every booking type that follows the coach's availability (`booking_cards.follows_smart`: calls and meetings, never a training session, which keeps its own weekly hours exactly as before). Two layers, always in this order: hard rules never broken in any mode (his boundaries: `days`, `dayStart`–`dayEnd`, or in Manual a type's own hours; protected time `protected` (training, personal, focus, every week), away dates `away`, blocked times; clear of everything busy by `max(gapMin, buffer)`; the notice (24 h when `lastMinute` is off) and the booking window; the daily and weekly call limits counting every appointment and every non-training session, `loadBookedCalls`), then the smart picks (Automatic, an Assisted day he accepted, `kind: 'smart'`): a gap shorter than `focusMin` is offered whole, a longer stretch gives up one `callBlockMin` block at the edge next to something already on (else the preferred time, else its end) and the rest stays clear (`placeBlock`: where the block goes and what leftover is too short to work in are judged before rounding to the start-time grid, so rounding never costs focus time), a busy day (4+ things or 60% full) offers one window, pinned windows are always offered, and a week short of `weeklyFloor` opens the next best time (`stretch`: twice the floor once that many calls are booked). An explicit `open` for a date wins over the boundaries and protected time, never over anything busy. Each day carries one short reason. Settings are one row, `booking_availability.settings` (`sanitizeAvailability`), date changes `booking_availability_overrides` (block / open / off / smart), both staff-only RLS, read by the server with the service role (20270330090000_smart_availability.sql). Nothing changes for anyone booking until he saves his boundaries (`confirmedAt`; `loadBookingType` marks a type `smart` only then, and a smart type is shown in his zone); the booking page's slots for a smart type are `smartPlan`, and the final re-check is `isHardOpen` (free, on the grid, inside the hard rules, never the smart picks, which shift as a day fills), held in `booking_slot_claims` with the larger of the buffer and the gap. Google is read live on every calendar (`requireAll`) or nothing is offered, so a new or cancelled event moves the times at once with no polling; `extraCalendars` only add busy time to the two that always block. Learning is suggestions only (`learnFromOverrides`: the same block / open / day off in 3 of the last 8 weeks; `recurringBlocks`: a calendar habit from start and end times alone, never a title, never client sessions), applied when he accepts, dismissed for good, reset with `learningSince`. The coach works it in Calendar → Booking → Availability (`AvailabilityCard`, availability-panel.tsx: the mode, this week in one line, the week as bars per day with its reason, a day's sheet for block / open / day off / use the smart pick, one-off or every week (`protected` / `pinned`), boundaries in one sheet with the rest under More settings, Pause a week = Manual until then) through `getAvailabilityPreview`, which plans with the same code as the booking page and shows Google time as "Busy", never its title — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for booking availability that follows his real schedule instead of a rigid weekly table: calls grouped where they cost him least, training and focus time protected, busy days lighter, and nothing offered that could clash; the only online type (the discovery call) had its 9–5 saved in New York time (an hour off Manitoba from March to November), set to America/Winnipeg by the migration.
- A coach chat's + menu sends a form (Send Form: `FormPickerDialog`, which grants the form and posts its live status card) and schedules (Schedule: `ScheduleSheet`, messages/booking-invite.tsx, the one tile for both ways to get a time on the calendar: a booking type taking online bookings, General Call picked, its length, an optional note, then Send link in chat (primary), Copy / Text / Email the same personal link, or Pick the time yourself, which opens the booking dialog (`BookSessionFromChat`) on that type and length; the client profile's Send booking link is the same sheet, `ClientScheduleSheet`, posting the card to their chat through `sendMessage`). A booking invite is a `kind: 'booking_invite'` attachment carrying the type, the client, `sent_at`, its link (`url` = `/b/<token>`) and `invite_id`; the client's card opens that link (signed in or not; in View as client with `?pv=1`, where nothing books), every side's card reads the booking it led to live (`useInviteBooking`: their first session or appointment of that type created after the invite), so it shows Booked with the time (the client's taps open `ClientSessionSheet`, a video call has Join) once they've picked one, and the coach's card reads the link's own row (staff RLS: Opened, Link cancelled, Link expired) with Copy link and Cancel link (`revokeBookingInvite`) — why: the coach wanted to send a booking invitation or a form from inside the conversation, and on Nov 20 2026 that Book Session and Booking Link stop being two look-alike tiles.
- Calls with people the coach already coaches are the General Call booking type (Nov 20 2026, 20270421090000_general_calls.sql: the old 1:1 Meeting row, `slug` general-call), beside the Coaching Discovery Call, which stays the public link for people who aren't clients yet. A booking type can offer lengths (`booking_cards.duration_options`, General Call 15 · 30 · 45 · 60, `duration_minutes` the default; `durationChoices` / `pickDuration` / `withDuration`, booking-types.ts: slots and the booking are worked out at the length picked, never one the type doesn't offer) and say who can book it online (`booking_cards.audience`: `anyone`, or `clients` = a coaching client we know is them, signed in or through a personal link; `audienceAllows`, checked in `bookOnline`, `bookOnlineAsMe` and again in `createOnlineBooking`, and `listMyBookingTypes` never lists one to a JF member). The booking page asks "How long do you need?" unless the coach's personal link fixed it (the link's `duration_minutes`, enforced by the server; old links: `?d=`), and shows anyone who isn't a coaching client who it's for (Sign in / Apply for coaching) instead of times it would refuse. The chat's Schedule lists calls for clients first (`inviteTypeGroups`: General Call picked to start, a length chosen there; the discovery call under For new people), the coach's booking dialog has a Length row for any call, and Home's calendar card keeps Request a session for training and adds Book a call beside it (`isCall` from `listMyBookingTypes`; a call never takes the training action's place). A call a client books themselves is a session like any other (Google with a Meet link, the evening-before text, Change time through `ClientSessionSheet`) and shows in their coach chat as a message from them with the call as a card (`postSelfBookedCall`, `selfBookedMessageBody`, `booked_by: "client"` on the `session_booked` card: "They booked"), unless the coach's invite card already shows it (`inviteCoversBooking`); the coach's push opens that chat. Availability is the coach's own (one host: the coach whose Google is connected; Calendar → Booking → Availability) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found the only call anyone could book or be sent was the Coaching Discovery Call, which makes no sense for people he already coaches, and wanted general calls (check-ins, consultations, program talks, anything) at 15, 30, 45 or 60 minutes on the same booking system, with discovery calls kept for potential clients.
- A personal booking link is `jfeffect.com/b/<token>` (Nov 20 2026, 20270422090000_booking_invites.sql, booking-invite.ts pure and tested, booking-invite.server.ts): the token is 24 random bytes that name nobody, only its SHA-256 is stored (`booking_invites`: the client, the booking type, the coach's length, `expires_at` = `INVITE_DAYS` (30), `revoked_at`, opened, `booked_session_id`; written only by the server after a staff check through the caller's own login, `createBookingInvite` / `revokeBookingInvite`; staff read their own clients' rows, admins all; clients and the public nothing), and the URL carries no client id or name. Who a booking is for is the server's call, never the page's (`bookingContext` → `pickBookingIdentity`): a link that works books as its client with no sign-in or form (a session: their credits, their app, Google with Meet, the evening-before text, the type's length fixed by the link); someone signed in as a client who isn't the link's client books as themselves and sees nothing about the link's client (a forwarded link never books on someone else's account while they're signed in as themselves); staff (a coach testing, a dual account) never turn a client's link into their own booking; the link's own client signed in always books as themselves, so a link that ran out never blocks them. A link holds one booking at a time (`booking_invite_claim`: held before the booking, `finishInvite` keeps the session on it, a second tap is "busy"): while that session is booked, done or counted as missed the link shows it ("You're booked", the day and time, change it in the app; their own signed-in client can Book another time), and once it's cancelled or moved away the link books again. Run out, cancelled, an archived or deactivated client, or a token that isn't one each say so with Sign in, never the public form or Apply for coaching; the page shows only the client's first name, sends no referrer, notes who opened it (never staff) for the coach's card, and labels every time with its zone (the viewer's, "Your coach is on …" when it differs); booking pages and minted links are never written to the phone's saved query cache (`booking-` and `schedule-sheet-` in `DO_NOT_PERSIST_PREFIXES`), so a reload always asks the server whether the link still books. A booking from a personal link goes in their coach chat as "They booked" (`postSelfBookedCall`, training too, since a text or email reaches no chat) unless the coach's invite card already shows it; the announce job skips it (`booking_page`), so nothing is told twice. Links minted before Nov 20 (`<client uuid>.<expiry>.<hmac>`, `?i=` on `/book/<slug>`) still book until they run out (`verifyLegacyInvite`) and are never minted again — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found a booking link he sent from a client's chat still asked the client to sign in or apply for coaching (the chat card carried no token at all, so anyone not signed in as the client, a forwarded text, Safari or his own View as client, got the public page), and the old personal link put the client's id in the URL and could never be cancelled; and in that day's production test a reload after booking showed the open times again, restored from the phone's saved cache.
- Booking types live in `booking_cards` (+ `booking_card_hours` for online hours); `booking_links` / `booking_link_availability` are retired history — why: one list for in-app booking and shareable links.
- A staff login linked to the person's own client account (`community_profiles.same_person_as`, written only by the admin-only `linkTeamMemberClient`) gets read-only "Linked login reads own …" RLS on the client-calendar tables via `public.linked_client_id()`; any new table the client calendar reads needs the same policy — why: client-read rules check `clients.user_id = auth.uid()`, so without it the staff home's "My calendar" is empty for the person themselves (it only looked right in the owner's preview).
- Client "Connect Google Calendar" (`client-gcal.server.ts`) uses the client's own Google sign-in with only `calendar.app.created`, writes only to the "JF Effect" calendar it created, keys events by `googleEventId(feed uid)` and only deletes events it tagged (`extendedProperties.private.jf`); its content comes from `buildClientFeedEvents`, the same as the feed — why: one source for what's in a client's calendar, no access to anything else in their Google account, and re-syncs never duplicate or touch their own events. The Google button is offered only while `clientGoogleReady()` passes (keys checked with Google, cached in `app_settings.client_google_calendar_check`; a broken setup raises one `google_calendar_setup` support alert) — why: a wrong or leftover client id sends clients to Google's "OAuth client was not found" page.
- Clients' own Google events in the app are opt-in and read-only: an extra consent for `calendar.calendarlist.readonly` + `calendar.events.readonly` (recorded in `client_google_calendars.granted_scopes`), read live by `listClientGoogleEvents` for the calendars in `show_calendar_ids` (null = their main one) and never stored or written; `getMyGoogleEvents` answers only for the signed-in person's own client account, and `pickImportedEvents` drops anything the app already shows (its own calendar, `jf`-tagged events, `@jfeffect.com` feed uids, invite copies of the client's appointments/sessions by `google_event_id`) — why: private to the client (a coach previewing them sees none), no duplicates, and nothing the app writes can come back in as an import.
- One client, one coordinated check-in experience (src/lib/checkin-coordination/, 20270402091000_checkin_coordination.sql): the weekly check-in is the only recurring form, and anything else a week needs from the client rides on it as a step, never a second form. Its plan (`planCheckin`, plan.ts, pure and tested; worked out once from the records on first open by `getCheckinPlan` → `ensureCheckinPlan` and kept on `messenger_checkins.plan`, first writer wins) adds only what's due and not on file: bodyweight is never a question (read from their log; a "log this morning's weight" step only with nothing in 7 days), one weight-making question with a class meet within 16 weeks (or a dated make-weight goal), photos and measurements when the coach's Progress-tab schedule says so (`progress_check_in_schedules`: switched on is his schedule, "No reminder" is never), else their goal's default (`progressDefault`: photos every 4 weeks and the waist every 2 on fat loss or recomp, both every 4 on muscle gain, neither for maintenance, no goal or a weight-class meet, which is made on the scale), always optional, skipped when already on file, with why; and a Nutrition Update waiting: answered right inside the check-in for a client who has sent one before (`nutritionSteps` / nutrition-fold.ts below), else one line pointing at the form; `addPlanSteps` (steps.ts) places them (weight-making after the nutrition rating; weigh-in, photos, measurements as optional steps that open the Progress page's own entries, so nothing is uploaded twice) and the coach's check-in card shows what was added and skipped and why (`CheckinPlanNote`). Delivery: the automated card goes out from 7am in the client's own time (never at midnight) to coaching clients who aren't archived or deactivated; any weekly check-in in the last 4 days (a coach's by hand included) stops the automated one, and submitting one sent by hand completes the week's open occurrence; the Nutrition Update goes out the week its plan is due a review (`nutritionReviewDue` = `public.nutrition_review_due`: the plan's own "Next update due", `nutrition_targets.end_date`, else `reviewWeeks` by phase, fat loss 4, recomp and gain 6, maintenance and performance 8, from the later of its start and their last update; `update_cadence` belongs to a retired flow and is "manual" on every plan, so it's never read), never within 21 days of their last update or 25 of the last request, never for a plan marked not needed, and through that week's check-in (`nutritionUpdateDecision` mirrors `enqueue_monthly_nutrition_updates`, 20270403090000_nutrition_update_in_checkin.sql): a client who has sent one before answers it inside the open check-in ("fold": `messenger_checkins.nutrition_requested_at`, no second card), the meals (keep / swap which foods / start fresh) and what changed since last time (goal, allergies or diet, health, supplements, training or activity, the free meal), each with one follow-up only when ticked; on submit `foldNutritionUpdate` (service role, only while one is genuinely waiting) writes the submission the form would have made (`foldNutritionAnswers`: last time's answers carried, sex, height, this week's weigh-in and training days from their records, never last month's weight, a change to allergies or health added under what they said before, `missed` from the check-in's own help answer), links it (`nutrition_submission_id`) and queues the AI plan exactly as the form does; a first one, or a client with no check-in coming, gets the form itself (on the due day from 9am), which opens on their last answers for the 20 questions that don't change month to month (`validation.remember`; never the meals, the swap, "anything I missed" or the bodyweight), and a fold whose check-in went without it (an old app) falls back to the next card or the form; both senders take an advisory lock. One reminder per outstanding thing: the 8am digest groups everything waiting (`digestLines`: the check-in, the Nutrition Update, a meet-week weigh-in, `weighInDue`) and stops once answered; the unread nudge leaves automated request cards to it (`isNudgeable`); a resend stays what it was (`is_automated` kept) and nutrition pushes dedupe per client per day; meet reminders read each client's own day. A coach's send by hand is warned, never blocked (`getRequestStatus` → `requestWarnings`: one still open, the automated one it replaces, a Nutrition Update waiting). A form question can fill itself from records instead of asking (form-prefill.ts; the Nutrition Update's bodyweight from a weigh-in today or yesterday, in the unit it asks) — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for the information coaching needs with the fewest interruptions: the monthly Nutrition Update landed the same week as the weekly check-in (both asking about nutrition, and for bodyweight already logged), a check-in sent by hand was followed by the automated one, two reminders fired for one check-in while the card itself arrived at midnight with no push, progress-photo schedules did nothing, a deactivated client could still get Friday's card, a resend could be texted when the original couldn't, and nutrition pushes never deduped; and in that day's audit 6 of the 13 clients on a plan had never answered a 24-question Nutrition Update (asked monthly since June), three clients two weeks into new 4-week plans would have been asked again on Nov 27, and powerlifters cutting to a class were asked for photos they don't need.
- Weight management is one deterministic read, `buildAssessment` (src/lib/weight-management/, pure and tested; no model decides a number), built from what's already on file by `loadWeightManagement` (load.server.ts, with whatever session it's given: the coach's own login on the card, so RLS decides, the service role in the check-in hook; every read but the client row best-effort): weigh-ins (`progress_bodyweight` + legacy `progress_metrics`, merged), the goal (a meet's weight class from `pl_preps` within 26 weeks first, planned to the limit less a buffer of half a percent or their own scatter, at most 1% (`plannedClassWeight`) in a normal, fed and hydrated state, weigh-in timing from the federation (`weighInTiming`: IPF affiliates 2 h, the 24 h federations, else "check"); else `clients.bodyweight_goal_*` with `bodyweight_goal_date`), the current nutrition plan, the weekly check-ins, the cardio plan (`cardio_targets`) and what was done (`cardio_completions`), water (`progress_water_targets` / `_entries`), the waist tape, steps, the health profile (walking-limiting injuries, a fluid limit), the squat and deadlift from comparable sets (strength-read.ts) and their earlier cuts (`pastCutPhases`). The trajectory (trajectory.ts) is a least-squares line over the last 4 weeks (6 when thin; 4+ weigh-ins over 10+ days, never a trend from two numbers) with its scatter and standard error, the level with the noise taken out, the pace the goal needs, a projection to the date with a band that widens with distance and noise, a planned path with an on-track band, and a weigh-in well off the line read against the line through the others (water, glycogen, salt, gut content). Realism (target.ts) judges the pace by sport and goal (loss %/week comfortable / limit: weight class 0.5 / 1.0, bodybuilding and fat loss 0.75 / 1.0, recomposition 0.35 / 0.75; gain by training age), tightened by their own record (`cutHistory`: a faster cut that cost them strength lowers the limit), and a too-fast class cut names the next IPF class up and what it takes. Treadmill is the default cardio (treadmill.ts): a prescription by the ACSM walking equation (speed and incline in mph or km/h, `fmtSpeed` always labelled, set to a treadmill's 0.1 steps, `treadmillSpeedMph`), from their own recent sessions first, adjusted for height, bodyweight, conditioning and walking-limiting injuries (incline ≤ 3%), the smallest weekly dose for the goal (less with 10k+ steps), most of it on days off, a short easy walk after lifting (never before; a powerlifter's ≤ 15 min and ≤ 4%), days off picked away from the day before a heavy squat / deadlift day (`heavyLowerWeekdays`: a main lift in a workout's first 3), easy and flat within 4 weeks of a meet, walks only in its last 2; `stepTreadmill` changes one variable at a time (up: days-off minutes, then another walk, then the walk after lifting, then incline, then speed, none near a meet; down: incline first), and a coach's own non-treadmill type is kept (`plannedModality`): only its time and frequency ever move. Water (hydration.ts, `FORMULA_VERSION` 2) is 30 ml/kg (15 past 100 kg), floor 2 L, a training day adding back ~80% of the session's estimated sweat, sodium only for long or sweaty sessions, ≤ 1 L an hour and 5.5 L a day, a ramp when they drink far less, and nothing at all when a fluid limit is on file (their doctor's number); the training-day difference is `progress_water_targets.training_extra_ml`. Digestion (digestion.ts) is the weekly check-in's own follow-ups, asked only when the flag is ticked (`digestion_symptoms` / `_timing` / `_severity`, and `hunger_level`, `cardio_issue`, `water_issue`, messenger-checkin-card.tsx `FOLLOW_UPS`), read over 6 weeks as patterns worth checking, never causes, never a food group cut, persistent or severe ones to a doctor. One recommendation (decision.ts `decide`) that answers the one question first (`limiting`: progressing, so leave it, or the most likely thing holding them back), in this order: a digestion red flag; not enough or stale weigh-ins; losing too fast (food back, or the cardio eased when it went up in the last 3 weeks and the lower lifts dropped; only past the limit by more than the trend's own standard error, and never in a new plan's first 14 days, `WATER_DAYS`, when fast loss is water); the plan and the goal pointing different ways (`goalPlanConflict`: a Maintenance plan with a "lose" goal, say; the coach confirms which is current, nothing moves); a new plan settling (`SETTLE_DAYS` 28: judged on its own weigh-ins after its first week, so calories normally change at the next Nutrition Update, which reads this); a bloated or backed-up week before calories; consistency under 4/5 before numbers; cardio costing the lifts; a target that can't be made safely (reconsider, never a harder cut); waist down with strength holding (hold); under-recovered with the lower lifts down (recovery first, no bigger deficit; never cardio on top while sleep or energy is flagged), then the smallest change that closes the gap (food first, never under the 1,200 / 1,500 kcal floor, 250 kcal at most, a powerlifter's from fats first with the carbs around training kept, hunger high or the floor near → one cardio step for the rest); holding is the most common answer, and a hold never hides a gut problem flagged in 2+ of the last 3 check-ins (`persistentGut`: "Calories can stay: look at the digestion"). A stale weigh-in is never "reached", the goal's pace is said only with a usable trend, and a weight-class meet within 4 weeks with no usable weigh-ins says so ("Meet in 3 weeks: not enough weigh-ins since Oct 19") and pushes the coach (`needsCoach`). `scripts/replay/weight-replay.ts` (with `memory-sb.ts`) replays the real loader on a read-only snapshot, as of any date, to check a change against every client's records. Every change is the coach's: after a weekly check-in `analyzeMessengerCheckin` records the read once (`recordCheckinReview`, `weight_management_reviews`, unique by its hash, staff only, the record itself never rewritten, `weight_management_reviews_keep_record`), supersedes the open one before, pushes the coach (`weight_review_ready`) only when it needs them (`needsCoach`), and hands the read to the recap; the coach's card (`WeightManagementCard`, the client's Nutrition tab, `#weight`) approves a cardio step or water as written (`saveCardioPlanFn` / `saveWaterTargetFn`, before and after kept on the review), sends a nutrition change to Change it with AI (`prefillPlanAiChange`, the plan's own reviewed, undoable path; nothing here writes `nutrition_targets`) or dismisses it, and the nutrition targets AI reads the same read as text (`loadWeightText` → `assessmentText`, with its rules: follow it, treadmill for new cardio, never dehydration, water loading, sodium manipulation or a crash cut). Photos are compared in the progress library itself (`PhotoCompareDialog`: 1–12 weeks back, `pickComparison` / `availableWeeks`, side by side, slider, every check-in in order, tap to zoom, each with the 7-day average and the waist around its date); no model reads a photo — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for weight management that gets an athlete to their class or goal with the least cost to strength, muscle and recovery, treadmill cardio by default and individualized, hydration and digestion read alongside, and recommendations a coach approves, built on the systems already here (one bodyweight log, one cardio plan, one check-in, one water target, one photo library) rather than beside them; a weight-class gap must never be closed with dehydration, and a photo can't tell body fat, lean mass or hydration; and the same day's replay of every coaching client's records week by week found a +150 kcal call on a maintenance drift a hair past the limit (flat a week later), a −250 kcal call on day 14 of a new gain plan (its first-week water read as gain), three weeks of digestion flags hidden behind "hold", a 57 kg meet 3 weeks out read as "reached" from one weigh-in in October, and a Maintenance plan beside a "lose to 65 kg" goal.
- Training roadmap words (block purpose, weekly focus) live in `pl_roadmap_notes`: Cleo (`training-roadmap.server.ts`, on the router's `roadmap` task: GPT-5-mini then GPT-5.5 on the OpenAI key) writes only the `ai_*` columns and never the program itself, a coach's `coach_*` wording always wins and is never overwritten, and `hidden` notes never reach the athlete (RLS). Rewrites are driven by DB triggers queueing the block in `pl_roadmap_queue` on any block/week/day/exercise-row or meet-date change, and happen only when `source_hash` changes — why: coaches' programming and wording stay theirs, the athlete's roadmap can't drift from the program, and an unchanged program never spends an AI call.
- A client's default weight unit is `clients.preferred_weight_unit` (NOT NULL, lb default); `weight_unit_confirmed_at` marks that they actually chose it, and every client-side unit save goes through `saveWeightUnitChoice` (weight-unit-pref.ts), which stamps it — why: lb can't tell "chose pounds" from "never asked", and the "Pounds or kilograms?" prompt keeps asking each app open until it's stamped.
- RLS policies check the signed-in user once per query, never per row: wrap user-only helpers as `(SELECT has_role((SELECT auth.uid()), 'admin'))` / `(SELECT auth.uid())`, and use set lookups (`client_id IN (SELECT coached_client_ids())`, `block_id IN (SELECT client_visible_pl_block_ids())`) instead of per-row `is_assigned_coach(...)` or multi-table `EXISTS`. A migration that adds policies can end with `SELECT public.rls_evaluate_once('<table>');` (idempotent, same access) — why: bare per-row checks made a client's workout block take ~540ms instead of ~20ms, across every table.
- Double-tapping a message likes it, Instagram style, in the coach chat and the group / 1:1 chats alike (`onDoubleTapLike` → `doubleTapAdds`, message-like.ts): it leaves your reaction as the default (`app_settings.chat_default_reaction`, ❤️ unless the coach picks another in Chat Settings; `DEFAULT_REACTION` when unset), adding it or switching yours to it, and never takes it back (tapping the heart under the message removes it); each double-tap springs the heart in (`animate-like-pop`). ❤️ leads every reaction bar — why: a double-tap that toggled meant a quick second double-tap silently unliked, and a ✅ read like a task tick, not a like.
- Coach posts in the community (daily series, holiday, birthday, "+ Post") show their caption in the regular 15px post font (`NoteCaption`), with or without a picture, never a bigger or bolder caption style. A coach lesson (`series = 'lesson'`) is the same: its slides (its deck, the deck rule below; else its card: title + diagram, the struck-out belief, the week's three things) as the picture, a photo on it after them, and his words, with no series label ("Methods & cues"), sources list or disclaimer in the feed or its story; the words cite research the way he'd say it, and `refs` show only to him in the lesson planner. Holiday captions (`community_holidays.body`, ≤160 chars) are two short lines in the coach's voice: a happy-holiday (+ thanks), then one thing to do today; written the way he'd type them (casual, capitals as he'd type them, apostrophes kept), one emoji at most, no forced slang ("yall", "v", "rn") or "no guilt", nothing on his My Voice never-say list (e.g. "fr") — why: the coach wants posts to read like his own normal posts and texts (labels, source lists and fine print make them look like they weren't posted by him), and the whole caption must show in the feed without tapping "more".
- One coach post a day: Monday lesson, Tuesday tip (and the crew's numbers), Wednesday Wins, Thursday lesson, then an Athlete Insight on the coach's picked weekdays (default Friday, Saturday and Sunday), holidays and birthdays on top. Wins is the one weekly crew post: the Sunday Recap reported the same week, so it posts on its own only while the coach switches it on (`series_on`, `community_series_set_on` takes `sunday_recap`; off by default, Post now still sends one), and with no Recap the week before, Wins carries the week's numbers card (20270205090000_one_weekly_crew_post.sql). The daily picture (Athlete Insights) is planned only on a picked weekday (`community_insight_days()`) that nothing else takes (`community_coach_post_day`, 20270204090000_one_coach_post_a_day.sql: a lesson planned, approved or out that day, or a lesson day not planned yet; Wins; a library series that's on; a holiday), never on a lesson the coach skipped, and Sunday's always runs; the coach's card says which days (`pictureDaysLabel`). The five library series (Monday Motivation, Tuesday Tips, Try it Thursday, Finish Strong Friday, Saturday Spirit) post only while the coach has them on (`community_series_settings.series_on` via `community_series_set_on`, off by default), at their own time; only Tuesday Tips is on (Monday's and Thursday's share the lessons' days, and the picture is the better Friday and Saturday post), and one switched on takes its day from the picture. Wednesday Wins always runs, and Pause stops everything. Every automated post goes out on its own at its time, with no approval first (Nov 8 2026, 20270213090000_posts_on_their_own.sql): lessons are `lesson_mode = 'auto'` ("Post on their own", the default; "Review each one" is still a switch away), an Athlete Insight is never held and is made a week ahead (`AHEAD_DAYS` 7, planned `PLAN_DAYS` 14 out, daily-visuals.server.ts; the cron's wake-up, `community_daily_visuals_kick`, looks as far ahead, 20270219090000_insights_wake_a_week_ahead.sql, or a day sent back for a remake waits until it's 3 days out) so he can read and edit it any time before, and a birthday draft goes out at 8am on the day as the account coach posts go out as (`community_birthdays_publish_due`, unless he skipped it or posts are paused; the evening-before push says so, no "still waiting" nudge, and Crew's Needs you never lists one); he can edit, move or skip any of them any time before. The publisher (`community_publish_series`) and the coach's "Next: …" (`community_next_post`: a lesson, Wins, a series that's on, or the picture on its days) read Winnipeg's clock through `instant_to_local` / `local_to_instant`; a bare `AT TIME ZONE` had every series an hour late. Lessons go out twice a week, Monday and Thursday, the tracks taking turns week by week (`community_lesson_track` = `lessonTrackFor`, lessons.ts, weeks counted from Monday 2025-12-29: Monday strength science → hypertrophy → technique fix, Thursday methods & cues → mind & recovery → uncomfortable truth; 20270130090000_lessons_twice_a_week.sql), so an uncomfortable truth (track `truth`, its card the struck-out belief over the truth) comes once every three weeks and the Sunday reset (track `reset`, the week's three things) isn't planned any more (its posts and library items stay); those two cards are type (`TruthCard` / `ResetCard`, theme colours), not drawings or image-model art (20261225090000_lesson_truth_reset.sql). A technique fix's level takes turns (`community_lesson_pick` counts the fix days left in the month), and a lesson's default time goes through `local_to_instant` (`community_lesson_default_at`). There's no Tuesday lesson, so the crew's-numbers post (`community_compose_observation`) is Tuesday Tips' while it's on; a lesson the coach moves to a Tuesday still yields to the `tuesday_tips:<week>` slot (`community_lesson_post` and `community_lessons_overview` check `series_on` and the slot). The admin Posts tab shows every automated post: lessons, the seven series and the holidays coming up (`community_series_overview.holidays`) — why: about 6 people open Crew a day (12 a week) and a visit reads one or two posts, yet there were 16 automated posts a week (three on Mondays and Thursdays, the Friday picture and Finish Strong Friday an hour apart): 55 in the 30 days to Nov 6 against 28 by members, and not one automated post drew a comment while members' posts did (the week's 10 extra coach posts nobody engaged with were archived, leaving the "new to you" week at 10 coach posts to 3 by members); Nov 1's Recap ("50 PRs between 9 of you") and Nov 4's Wins ("50 PRs last week") said the same thing, and people stayed on Wins (36% left Crew on it against 60% on the Recap); the lessons replaced these five outright and the coach lost posts he wanted, a Tuesday tip and a Tuesday lesson both claiming the week's data slot would collide, automated posts the coach can't see read as missing, and a lesson every day was half of all automated posts (20 of 42 in the three weeks to Nov 5, against 15 posts by members), drew the fewest likes per view of any coach post and buried the crew's own posts, while a weekly uncomfortable truth made the feed read as confrontational.
- A person a caption tags reads as their @username everywhere a caption shows (`withHandles` in `MentionText` and `NoteCaption`, which measures the feed's cut-off on the text as shown), whatever the caption typed ("Marc", "@Marc"); every person card carries `username` (`community_author`), the @ picker finds people by name or username and puts in `@username`, and `community_caption_mentions` tags a whole `@username` wherever it is (a tie with someone's name goes to the username) as well as `@Name` and the recaps' plain names — why: the coach wants tags to read like Instagram's, and old captions name people by first name.
- In the feed a coach's own note shows its words whole up to 12 lines; a longer one stops at the end of a line, sentence or emoji (`captionBreaks`), never mid-sentence, with "… more" that opens the rest in place (`NoteCaption`, measured at the card's real width as it reads: a stop's period, colon, comma or own "…" gives way to the "…", a ? or ! keeps a space before it, `cutPreview` / `previewLine`, caption-preview.ts). Under an automated post's picture it's Instagram's short preview: whole up to 4 lines, else the first clean stop within 3 (`PICTURE_CAPTION_LIMITS`), the post's own page shows it all. Auto posts (daily series, Wins, holidays, birthdays) lead with the hook: the first line is the reason to keep reading, and the numbers or names people want to see come before anything that can get cut — why: a caption chopped at a fixed line count mid-sentence with no visible "more" reads broken, and when one is long the part people see first has to earn the tap; "sneaky.… more" read like a typo.
- Logged training is never deleted by editing a program: `pl_guard_history_delete` triggers refuse a direct delete of any `pl_exercise_rows` / `pl_days` / `pl_weeks` / `pl_blocks` holding a logged set (`pl_row_results`) or a finished workout (`pl_day_completions.completed_at`); editors and copy/clear tools check `loggedIds()` (`pl_logged_ids`) first and keep those parts, and `deleteBlock` archives a program with logged workouts instead. Deleting a whole client still cascades (deliberate purge); `SET LOCAL jf.allow_history_delete = 'on'` is the admin-only escape — why: every one of those deletes cascades to the client's logs, so a removed exercise, "Copy week" onto a trained week or "Delete program" silently wiped history.
- Member access (`isMemberAccessActive`, and in SQL `member_can_consume` / `jf_member_has_full_access`) follows billing's own statuses: "Active", "Trialing", "Complimentary" and "Free" (a free account) get in, "Active (Cancels at period end)" gets in until `cancel_at` / `current_period_end`, "Hold Plan" and "Paused" stay locked (data kept, features off); change all three together — why: they drifted, and members who cancelled mid-month were locked out of the month they'd paid for.
- One athlete record: every JF member has a `clients` row with `service_tier = 'self_serve'` and `status = 'Member'` (no coach), made by the `app_members_ensure_client` trigger / `ensure_my_member_client()`; only an admin (or the server) changes the tier (`guard_clients_service_tier`), and "Move to coaching" flips it on the same row. Members use the portal (`/m/*` only redirects there); the coach's layer (Messages, check-ins, agreements, Goals & Setup, purchases, PT sessions, coach notes) is gated by `useEntitlement` / `COACHING_ONLY` in `service-tier.ts`, and any new job, roster, count or automation that means "coaching clients" must filter `service_tier = 'coaching'` (status 'Active' alone isn't enough: several jobs only check `archived`) — why: members share the engine (workouts, PRs, history, Crew) but must never get a coach's check-ins, texts or agreement, or inflate coaching rosters.
- A JF member starts a library plan only through `startLibraryPlan` → `pl_assign_template_to_client` (let through by `member_can_start_plan`), which copies the plan's template into `pl_blocks` the member owns (`created_by` = their user id); a replaced plan is archived, never deleted. The old member-only workout tables (`member_plan_enrollments`, `member_plan_day_schedule`, `member_set_logs`, `member_workout_completions`, `member_workout_reviews`, `member_exercise_swaps` / `_notes` / `_unit_prefs`) are read-only history (members have SELECT only) and no new code reads or writes them; `/m` only redirects to the portal (keep its index + `$` catch-all, or old links 404) — why: one program system, so the logger, PRs, analytics and history are the coaching ones, and "who owns the block" (not the tier) is what decides who may edit it.
- The client Home (`/portal`) is two sections: Today (schedule, then needs-you cards that hide once done) and Progress, where Bodyweight, Water and Progress are three cards of their own, one after another (no tabs or swipe carousel), each in its own `DeferRender`, with matching headers (icon + 16px title). Rankings (League, Hall of Strength) aren't on Home; they live in Crew → Rankings — why: the coach found Home's rankings block went unused once Crew had a Rankings tab, and Water / Progress hidden behind tabs were easy to miss.
- The Home calendar card (`schedule/agenda.tsx`, every account's Home) is neutral: the same header as Home's other cards (a plain `CalendarDays` icon, "Calendar ›" at 16px, the month on the right; no red date tile, no "Today · …" line), weekday names in sentence case, today a plain filled circle (`bg-foreground text-background`), training rows' hairline neutral (`FAMILY_ACCENT`, every family neutral since Nov 21 2026, the calendar colour rule), a neutral Now line and a neutral "Request a session"; the only red is today's workout's Start / Continue / Review (`ActionPill`). Today's workout's line is "Block N · Week N of M" (`workoutContext`), and nothing coming up gets no empty row of its own — why: the coach found the red tile, red weekday, red circle, cyan workout line and red action fighting each other, and a bare "Week 1 of 5" didn't say of what.
- Program Intelligence (`/admin/client-programs/$clientId/intelligence`, `src/lib/program-intel/`) is coach / admin only: every number comes from the deterministic engine (`buildIntelReport`: core.ts lines up each prescribed exercise with its logged sets, analyze.ts measures, brains.ts writes findings), never from a model, and each finding is a `fact` (measured, its records named as evidence), a `hypothesis` (an interpretation) or `missing`. A logged set counts toward a prescription when it has reps (or a time) and isn't flagged a warm-up, capped per exercise at what was prescribed, whatever its RPE (`SetLine.done`); effort only decides working volume (`working`) — the definitions (prescribed, completed, records, skipped, incomplete, extra, modified, not counted) are spelled out on the page and in `quality.ts`. Records are read whole, page by page (`fetchAll` / `fetchByIds`, never one capped request) with the coach's own session in the app and the service role only in the worker after `program_intel_can_coach`; a source that can't be read is listed in `unreadable`, never dropped. Archived plans and at-home backups count only trained days. The program a client is on is the one running today that they actually train (`currentProgramBlocks` with `lastTrainedByBlock`: latest finished workout, then the later start), never whichever was created first when an old program was never archived (Alyssa's untrained Sep 7 program read as her "previous program" over the meet prep she trained), and a lift's trend across blocks says every mix as it is (`liftTrend`: +2% progressed, +1–2% a small gain, under +1% none). Exports (`export.ts`: Markdown, JSON with every raw record, CSV with every logged set plus each exercise with nothing logged, the AI prompt, `pdf.ts`) keep prescribed apart from actual and facts apart from hypotheses, carry the data hash and an export id logged in `program_intel_exports`, and the privacy-safe copy (`privacySafeRecords`) drops the name everywhere and everything the athlete typed. AI runs (`program_intel_jobs`: interpret / strategy / generate) are written only in the background (`program-intel-tick`, jobs.server.ts). Generate Next Program (`/admin/client-programs/$clientId/next-program`, `generator/workstation.tsx`) is staged: stages 1–3 are deterministic and run on the phone and again in the worker from one full-history read (`buildGeneratorIntel`: athlete context, `quality.ts`, `review.ts`, `patterns.ts` (established = coach-confirmed, recurring = 2+ blocks, an association), `equipment.ts` (coach-confirmed `program_intel_profiles.equipment_profile`, else what the last program proved, else Goals & Setup; conflicts named), `recommend.ts` (setup from what they actually trained; days per week and training days are their training schedule whenever one is set (workouts can only land on it; adherence is a note, and fewer days means changing the schedule first); frequency targets are minimums: at least 2×/week for the squat, bench and major muscles, 1× for the deadlift, never more than the days; `redistribution` when the days change; session length is as long as their sessions take within Goals & Setup and, for a client who books PT sessions (`bookedSessionLength`: 4+ booked from 8 weeks back to 4 ahead, cancelled / moved ones left out), within that hour, warm-up, lifting and the cardio after it all inside (`maxSessionMinutes`, a training-day walk over a quarter of the hour is the `pt-cardio` conflict); `findConflicts`, blockers refuse to run)); a client with nothing logged gets a first program (`firstProgram`, first-program.ts) from what they told you at setup and nothing else it doesn't need: the framework from the goal and styles they picked (`goalBrain`, deciding only when no training is on file, so "Build muscle" reads as bodybuilding), their experience as a level (`experienceLevel`: under a year beginner, 1–5 years intermediate, more than 5 advanced; missing = a beginner start), an evidence-based starting dose for that framework and level (`startingDose`: hard sets per muscle in the audit's own groups, lift sets, each major muscle and the squat / bench twice a week and the deadlift once, rep ranges, an easier week 1, progression, scaled to fit their days and lifting minutes and saying so), 12 weeks like any program, Goals & Setup's last answer when it says something (`setupNotes`), maxes on file as its only load references, warnings (never blockers) for a missing Goals & Setup, experience or equipment, and the prompts' `FIRST_PROGRAM_RULES` (no previous program, simple stable exercises kept all block, no starting weight ever written, library exercises with a demo video first); the coach's notes & ideas (`coachNotes`) are their own card above the advanced controls (`ProgramNotesCard`, never folded away) and direction the AI follows and accounts for in `coach_requirements`, and a client with no program starts one from the Training tab's Write it with AI; then `pipeline.server.ts` runs one stage per request (`stage` / `progress` on the job; a retry resumes the failed stage): context (the brief, `brief.ts`: the previous program set by set always, earlier programs set by set unless over `BRIEF_BUDGET_CHARS`, then summarized and said so; the snapshot in `context`) → strategy (`strategySchema`, read tolerantly like every model answer: text over its cap is cut, never wiped, and a list keeps every item that reads (`clip` / `clipReq` / `list`, generator-schemas.ts), since a 900-character cap once emptied the most thorough strategies' intensity and progression; skipped when the coach approved one; empty intensity, progression model, fatigue plan, frequency targets or a day with no main lifts go back with its one retry, `strategyGaps`, and what's still empty is kept on the run, never stopping it) → one block per request → `auditProgram` (15 criteria: passed / needs review / failed / not checked with reasons, never a score; volume is read like with like: over the strategy's target counts only sets aimed at the muscle (the exercise's own target, `direct`: a bench set is chest work, never a full triceps set), under it counts everything that reaches it (squats do train glutes), and frequency "well over" counts only days with work aimed at it; a heavy squat day and a heavy deadlift day on back-to-back days (Sunday into Monday counts) are never a finding or moved by code: they're a fact (`heavyClashes` → `audit.facts`) the strategy decides on purpose and the coaching review judges in context; a first program's missing history and unknown equipment are "not checked" (never a review item), and a draft with nothing to review reads "Partly checked"; training a target less often than planned or keeping an exercise the strategy replaced fails and goes back for up to 2 targeted repairs, training it more often never fails; a trained exercise proves its equipment even when its note doesn't say which machine; a walk or bike is written as a timed row, `exerciseRow` / `isTimedCardio`; a superset ("A1" / "A2", stations that don't clash, never the day's main lift) is timed from each member's own rest, `supersetSeconds`, and its saved notes name the partner, `supersetNote`) → coaching review (`coaching-review.ts`: the strongest model reads the built program like a head coach, fatigue, recovery in context, selection, specificity, progression, this athlete; only genuine problems, a `must_fix` naming its block goes to one targeted repair of that block within the same 2 rounds, a `consider` shows on the scorecard; `withReview`) → save. How long: 12 weeks by default for every program type, a custom length, or the goal timeline (`horizon.ts`, pure, tested: the planned start is the Monday after today or after the current program ends; a meet or dated goal (`program_intel_profiles.long_term_goal`, coach-set once, never asked again) ≤ 12 weeks out is written to that date, 13–16 weeks out is everything but the last 4 (final prep written later), further out is the first 12 weeks of a roadmap to it, `planHorizon` / `lengthForDeadline`, conflicts with options when a chosen length runs past the meet or leaves a short final prep); a run writes every week of that length in full (blocks, phases and deloads are the strategy's call, never 3×4 or a deload every 4th week by default), keeps the rest as the strategy's `roadmap`, and the next run reads the last assigned run's roadmap and how they actually did. Assign refuses a start inside another live program unless the coach ticks Start anyway (`allowOverlap`) and suggests the start that lands the meet in the last week (`assignChecks`). Which model answers what is `src/lib/ai/routing.ts` (docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md): strategy, review and interpret go only to the top tier (`strongOnly`: GPT-6 Astra, then GPT-5.5; if none answers the run stops and says so, never a quiet downgrade), construct and repair to GPT-5.5 then Astra (the Nov 9 2026 blind benchmark on real athletes found Flash and GPT-5.5 build to the same audit result, but Flash only exists through the Lovable gateway; docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md; Claude through the gateway can't finish inside one request, so it isn't routed), every default on the OpenAI key and none on Lovable credits (an admin override can still name a `gateway:` model), fallbacks named in the call log; OpenAI calls run in background mode across requests through the shared `stagedCall` (staged-call.server.ts: `WaitingForModel`, `result.pending`, answers kept per stage in `result.calls` so a resume never re-asks); `app_settings.ai_routes` overrides it; benchmark runs (`/api/public/hooks/ai-benchmark`, `options.bench`) never write a draft or show in the coach's list; admins see routes, reachability, call stats and cost at `/admin/settings/ai`. Every model call is logged in `program_intel_job_calls` (coach-readable, worker-written; the inspector shows it). Outcomes of assigned generated programs (`outcomes.ts`) are confirmed or dismissed as `outcome` observations. A generated program is hidden, undated `Draft` blocks (`client_visible = false`, no dates, so re-alignment never places it) with exercises from the library only (catalog ids, else `resolve_exercise_id`, else a name-only row for the coach to pick; never a new exercise), and it reaches the client only through the coach's Assign (`assignIntelDraftFn`: dates, visible, `realignClientToCommittedDays`). AI statements and decisions are stored as proposed hypotheses (`program_intel_observations`, a check constraint) until the coach approves, corrects or rejects them; rejected ones never feed a later report Assigning a draft that failed validation needs `acknowledgeAudit` — why: Goals & Setup goes stale (the home-gym client's said 3 days at a commercial gym while he trained 4 days at home), effort can't be read from load, a one-shot prompt both analyzed and wrote the program unchecked, and the coach plans the next cycle from what actually happened, a model must never invent or estimate a logged result, a long history must never be silently cut short, and nothing generated may reach a client unreviewed; Colten and Marc train in booked 60-min PT sessions, yet their programs were built to Goals & Setup's 90 / 75 min with a 25 / 15-min walk after, so sessions ran 75–100 min, and the pairing the generator wrote never reached the saved program; the coach wants to write a new client's first program from their setup alone (with his own notes), and a client with no training on file read "Build muscle" as general fitness and got no dose, effort or progression, 6 weeks, and a notes box folded away in Advanced; and Devin Buth's first program, which hit its plan set for set, showed five "Needs review" items ("Triceps: 16.5 sets vs 7" counting all 8 bench sets as triceps, "Back 5×/week" from squats' upper back, no history and no equipment list on a first program) around the one real problem worth a coach's look, a heavy deadlift Tuesday into a heavy squat Wednesday, which is a judgment call for a coach reading the loads, not a rule (the coach: "Do not automatically classify adjacent squat and deadlift days as mistakes"); four-week programs meant planning a new one every month, a program that ran past a meet or a timeline nobody wrote down, and the slow reasoning models timed out inside a request and silently fell back to Flash.
- A Generate Next Program run never sits stuck and always says how far along it is (Nov 23 2026, run-eta.ts pure and tested, staged-call.server.ts, jobs.server.ts, 20270516090000_program_runs_never_stranded.sql; program-run-progress.test.ts). Its routes are the OpenAI key only (`resolveRoute(…, { openaiOnly: !bench })`, the interpret job too): an `ai_routes` override naming a `gateway:` model is dropped, so no run spends Lovable credits; only a benchmark compares gateway models. A background call still running at `HEDGE_AFTER_MS` (the strategy 15 min, past the longest Astra strategy on file; build and repair 6, review 5) gets a backup: the route's next OpenAI model is started beside it and whichever answers first is used, the other cancelled (`hedgeAfterMs`, `pending[key].hedge`); `backgroundLimitMs` (25 min) only hands over to another model, and the route's last model with nothing left is waited on to `FINAL_LIMIT_MS` (45 min), never cut off into a certain failure; a call cancelled from outside (Discard) is never replaced by the next model, and every worker write (`save`, the hand-back to pending, the error) applies only while the run is still `running`, so a discarded run stays discarded. OpenAI's own status is kept on the call (`phase`: queued / in_progress, `startedAt`), so a run says when it's waiting in OpenAI's queue rather than thinking. A request the host cuts off (Cloudflare 1102 / 502, which cluster at the quarter hours when a dozen scheduled jobs hit the worker together) costs about two minutes, not 10–15: a run untouched for 2 min on a model stage (5 on a code stage; interpret and benchmarks 10) is picked up again by pg_cron `program-intel-tick`, now every minute while one waits; no request is claimed in the quarter-hour rush (`inQuarterRush`) or polls through it (`pollWindowMs`); a run cut off on all `MAX_ATTEMPTS` stops with Try again (`stopStuckRuns`) instead of spinning forever; nothing finished is redone (answers kept per call, `result.calls`). The library catalog a build reads is cached for 5 min per worker, so a poll is a poll. Progress is real milestones only: each stage stamps `startedAt` / `doneAt` on `progress.stages`, the context stage stores each step's first model and its measured median from `program_intel_job_calls` (`progress.typical`, `measuredTypical`, else `TYPICAL_MS`) with the strategy scaled by the brief (`briefScale`), and `runEta` gives the bar's % (the share of the run's usual total behind it, never 100 until done), the time left, or "taking longer than usual" / "waiting on OpenAI" with how long the model has been at it, never a made-up number (`RunProgressBar`, `stepLine`, the workstation and Program Intelligence's link; Edit with AI's turn the same from its stages, `editEta`). A run that finishes or stops pushes the coach (`program_run_ready` / `program_run_stopped`, opening the workstation), and one that finishes on screen says so with Open it (the draft's first block) — why: on Nov 23 2026 Dwayne Gordon's strategy took over an hour: GPT-6 Astra thought for 34 min (its strategies on file take a median 8.5 min, the longest 14.5) and was thrown away at the 25-min limit for GPT-5.5 to start from scratch, which the same limit would then have cut off with no model left; three of its worker requests were cut off at :45, :00 and :15, each leaving it idle 10–15 min before the 5-minute cron and 10-minute stale check noticed; and the card showed a spinner with no idea how long was left.
- Edit with AI (`src/lib/program-ai/`, `components/program-ai/edit-with-ai.tsx`: the client's Training tab, the block builder and the template editor; admins and the client's own coach, templates admins only) is the one way a model changes an existing program: the coach types, a background turn (`program_ai_edits`, the `program-ai-tick` hook, `runProgramAiEdit`, calls logged in `program_ai_edit_calls`) reads the program (`loadClientSnapshot`: current and upcoming primary blocks, every workout locked when trained, started or dated before today; `loadTemplateSnapshot` from the v2 payload), the athlete (Program Intelligence's full-history brief, minus its setup sections), the catalog (exercises the coach's programs use plus this program's, codes "X12"; names looked up in the library, never made up) and the rules (`program_rules`: hard constraint / coach / client preference / temporary restriction, an athlete's or every athlete's; the AI only suggests one, the coach saves it), and replies with schema-checked operations on refs ("B1W3D2E4", `ops.ts`: update, swap, add, remove, move, rename_day, add_day, remove_day, schedule) labelled requested / data / speculative, never ids. `engine.ts` (pure, tested) applies them to a copy and is the only judge: locked workouts and cardio-plan rows are left out, values checked (RPE or RIR, a % or a load, caps, tempo), warnings for duplicates, a session over the limit, the exercise standard, a % with no max; each change shows exactly what it does workout by workout. Refining carries the current proposal forward (`mergeProposal`: only new / replaced ids and drops, unticked changes dropped), re-coded when the catalog moves (`recodeChanges`). Apply rebuilds the plan from the program as it is now (`buildPlan`), refuses if a ref now names another record or a touched workout changed since the proposal (`refs`, `bases` on the result), then `program_ai_apply` does it in one transaction (access, ownership, locks, library, the stored bases, a `pl_program_versions` restore point of every touched workout with its calendar entries, then the rows; only whitelisted columns); training days and re-dating go through `writeCommittedDays` + `realignClientToCommittedDays` after it and are put back on Undo (`pl_schedule_audit` batch); templates save through `program_ai_apply_template` (payload_revision checked; "Save as a new template" makes a Draft copy; assigned programs are copies and never change). Undo is `program_ai_restore` (trained-since workouts stay, and the undo itself can be undone); the sheet's History lists Cleo's changes too and undoes either kind. A new program or full rebuild is not an edit: it hands the coach's words to Generate Next Program (`?notes=&weeks=&days=`) — why: the coach wants to change any program in plain words and see exactly what changes before anything reaches a client, the model must never write to the database or touch trained work, a concurrent edit must never be overwritten by a stale proposal, and small follow-ups must not quietly rewrite the rest of the proposal.
