- Every program the app writes (Generate Next Program, Cleo's build / add / swap) meets one exercise standard (`exercise-standard.ts`, program-intel): stable, loadable exercises that progress in small steps for every level. `lowValueReason` names what fails it: isolation (raises, flyes, curls, triceps / overhead extensions, kickbacks) on a landmine, plate or kettlebell, a barbell lateral raise, combo moves (lunge to press, squat to press, thrusters, "X to Y"), twists and side bends, wall sits / wall squats, supermans, loaded work on a ball, Bosu or balance board, raises with no load; landmine compounds (row, press, belt squat, RDL), shrugs, calf raises and kettlebell compounds are fine, and stretches / mobility are never judged (121 of 2,043 library exercises fail it, each one read). The generator's catalogs are filtered by `meetsStandard`, the prompts carry `EXERCISE_STANDARD`, the strategy gets one retry for them (`strategyStandardIssues`) and then `withStandardStrategy` takes any left out (`result.standardRemoved`) instead of failing the run (only `strategyIssues`, a mismatch with the coach's setup, stops it), the audit's selection check fails any that slip into a block (repairable) and `withStandardSubstitutions` drops them from "Swap if needed"; Cleo's library lookup and approval cards flag them. A coach's required exercise is always kept — why: Colten's generated program had a Landmine Lateral Raise, which starts at the bar's ~45 lb and is useless for almost everyone, plates and kettlebells jump 10-25 lb / 4 kg at a time, and a model that kept such a pick twice used to fail the whole run blaming the coach's setup.
- Every exercise the app writes is chosen for its job first and fitted to the session second (Nov 18 2026, `EXERCISE_SELECTION`, exercise-standard.ts, which `EXERCISE_STANDARD` now opens with, so the strategy, build, repair, Edit with AI and Cleo's library lookup all carry it): each exercise has a job (a lagging muscle, the lift it supports, a weak range) and is the one that does it best for this athlete: a full range with the stretched position, stable enough that the target muscle ends the set, loading in small steps, a fatigue cost that fits the session, kind to a reported injury, and what they've already progressed on beats a new pick of the same quality; no variety for its own sake, no popular pick without a purpose, no redundancy. Convenience only decides between options of equal quality: the flow rules (`ACCESSORY_FLOW_RULES`, `ACCESSORY_FLOW_HINT`, Edit with AI's session flow, Cleo's programming line) never trade down (a floor version, a shorter range, a less stable or harder-to-load exercise) to save a station, keep a superset together or avoid a bench in use, the station cap gives way to a clearly better exercise (`accessoryStationsLine`: "quality beats the count"), and the coaching review makes a convenience downgrade a must_fix naming the swap. `lowValueReason` adds the two families that are dominated on any equipment: skull crushers, triceps extensions, flyes and pullovers on the floor (the floor stops the stretch; an overhead extension needs no bench), and bench, chair and floor dips; a floor press stays (a lockout variation, or the press with no bench), and parallel-bar, ring, weighted and machine dips are fine. The same day's hand review of every live client program and template changed 4 workouts by migration (20270413090000_accessory_selection_quality.sql, untouched workouts only, rows as read, old values in `accessory_flow_backup` batch `accessory_selection_2026_11_18`): Colten's floor skull crushers (Nov 20, 27) → Cable Triceps Overhead Extension (Ez Bar), which he'd progressed on, with a rope for his left wrist; Nicolas's bent-over rear delt fly right after bent-over rows (Nov 21, 28) → Lying Rear Delt Fly - Dumbbell; the Apex templates' paired lying leg curls (an intensification method), Marc's two-dumbbell calf raise (progressing, 90 lb × 15) and the JF home programs' floor presses and bent-over flies (no bench there) were kept — why: the generator gave Colten floor skull crushers so the superset could keep the bench for leg curls, because the flow rules asked for fewer stations without saying quality comes first.
- A session's accessories flow like a coach who runs real sessions built them (Nov 18 2026, accessory-flow.ts, program-intel, pure, tested): the main lifts (the session's first exercise, anything labelled Primary / Secondary / top set / back-off, a competition lift) keep the order performance needs and never move for convenience; after the last of them, accessories are grouped by station (`flowStation`: barbell, dumbbells, cable, machine, a home gym's leg attachment; bodyweight and bands anywhere) so a session finishes one station before the next and never walks back to one it left. Generate Next Program's prompts carry one rule for choosing and ordering them (`ACCESSORY_FLOW_RULES` in strategy and build: of two options that train the target about as well, the one at a station already in use or with less setup; fewer stations, not less work; training order wins over grouping; fewer stations at home or in a short session, `accessoryStationsLine`), the repair keeps it, and the coaching review weighs station hopping as a consider. After every build and repair the audit stage regroups what the model left scattered (`withAccessoryFlow` → `orderAccessories`), conservatively: an exercise moves ahead of another only when it trains no muscle the other works (no pushdowns before close-grip bench, no curls before rows), an isolation never ahead of a compound, nothing past a priority muscle's work (setup priorities and the strategy's), nothing past a name the classifier can't read, a superset as one, only a compound on its own may follow straight on from the main lift's setup (a hip thrust after squats, never an EZ-bar curl after deadlifts), and the new order is kept only when it has fewer station changes; what moved is said under Session practicality (`flowNotes`, `result.flowAdjusted`). Edit with AI reads each row's station (`@cable` in the program text), is told the same rule (pick and place an addition with its station, never reorder what the coach didn't ask about; "make it faster" may propose grouping as its own change) and warns when a change sends a workout back to a station it already left (`stationReturns`); it never reorders anything itself; Cleo's library lookup carries the rule in one line (`ACCESSORY_FLOW_HINT`). The code never rewrites an assigned or running program by itself; the one pass over live programs was the coach's ask (the next rule) — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach found generated accessory work full of equipment changes and trips back to the same stations, and asked for accessories that work together and flow, with the main lifts kept performance-driven; replayed on the 94 distinct sessions the generator had written, 21 went back to a station they'd left, most for a sound training reason (curls after the pulldown, pushdowns after the press, lateral raises after a pulldown), and 2 were regrouped (a hip thrust brought next to the squat, 3 → 1 station changes); the bigger lever is choosing accessories that share stations, which is the prompts' job.
- The Nov 18 2026 accessory pass over live programs and the library was reviewed by hand, workout by workout, and changed order only (plus three cues that named the wrong station), never an exercise, set, rep, load or main lift: 20270411090000_accessory_flow_live_programs.sql moved 70 rows in 34 untouched workouts (`pl_day_touched` checked again at apply time, a workout applied only while every moved row sat where it was read, every old value in `accessory_flow_backup`, staff-free table); the home clients (Marc, Colten, Nicolas) from that week were already laid out by station with supersets, so only their stale cues changed (Marc's pull-ups said "High cable", Colten's dumbbell lateral raises said "cable"); everyone else from Nov 23: Jared's cable hip abduction joins the other cable work before the calf raise, Nicole's upper day pulls on the cable then the dumbbell work then the pushdown, Devin's Block 2 Mondays do the incline press after the bench then the cable row and pushdown together, and Reece's, Ashley's and Dwayne's posterior-chain days do the split squats (or back extension) before both lying leg curls, back to back on one machine; peaks, tapers, the Intensification days' deliberate push / pull alternation and the members' own programs were left as they were. The library: 6 JF Effect sessions reordered in programs.ts (`changes` says so), the installed and members' copies brought in line by 20270411091000_jf_session_order.sql (generated by scripts/jf-programs/write-session-order-migration.ts from session-order-sql.ts, only a copy still byte for byte as installed, checked against the definitions or rolled back, members' versions moved with the template's so none reads "older version"), and the Apex / Girl Pop templates the clients came from got the same fixes (20270411092000_coach_templates_session_order.sql, md5-guarded, `template_flow_backup`) — why: the coach asked for every live program and template to get the new accessory flow, with completed work, history and progression untouched and no change for the sake of change.
- How an athlete is developing is one deterministic read, `buildAthleteDevelopment` (athlete-development.ts, program-intel), computed inside `buildGeneratorIntel` and given to every AI that plans or changes training: the brief's "Athlete development" section (`developmentText`, so Generate Next Program's every stage and Edit with AI), the interpret job, and `DEVELOPMENT_RULES` in each prompt. It reads only what's on file and lists what isn't (`missingInfo`: date of birth, sex, height, a recent bodyweight, training experience, a weight class for a meet; shown in the Edit with AI sheet and Generate Next Program's "How they're developing" card with a link to fix each), never estimates a measurement. Bodyweight phases are a least-squares trend (3+ weigh-ins over 2+ weeks, ±0.25%/week = gaining / cutting), lifts are compared like with like (each competition lift's RPE-adjusted e1RM from the sessions that can show a max, `strengthPoints`: a top set at RPE 7+ (`INFORMATIVE_RPE`), every session as floors and said so when there are under 3, so a technique single @5 or an unrated set is never strength lost; the best of the first / last few, per month, relative to bodyweight, each bodyweight phase's best at that phase's bodyweight, against the rate expected for their training age), IPF GL points use the published coefficients (`glPoints`, checked against OpenPowerlifting) and a gym total is always labelled an estimate, never a meet result. Per exercise, `progressionSignals` decides progress / hold / back off / too little data from the last 8 weeks' exposures (sets done, reps hit, RPE against prescribed, the e1RM trend, a related pain report, a cut, a meet within 3 weeks) with the method for that lift and level: one hard session is a fluctuation, never a back-off, no lift is pushed every week, and PROGRESS asks a little more than last time (one load step from a logged load, or a rep; writing the last load again is a hold). Age, sex and height inform but never decide (same data, same decision; height never sets a stance or style), and Edit with AI warns when a change asks more of an exercise the records say to back off (`asksMore`), or backs off one they say to hold or progress with no pain report behind it (`asksLess`: fewer sets, or a load under both what was written and their last session's top set) — why: the coach wants the AI to individualize from the athlete's real data, a model must never invent a measurement or treat an estimate as a result, a factor that only sits in the prompt's context doesn't change a decision, and Jared's light bench singles once read as "153 → 84 kg (−45%)" while his last competition single was 150 kg @8.
- On Workouts (`WorkoutsExperience`) each thing is said once, where it belongs: the header is just "Workouts" and ⋯ (no block tag); one neutral Week · Month · Block control (`ScheduleViewSwitch`, `aria-label="Schedule view"`, no second Schedule / Block View tab row): compact in the athlete's header beside ⋯, so the calendar and today's workout are the first thing on the page, and a full-width row only on a coach's view of a client (whose header holds the client's name); the week strip is a calendar and says dates only (`weekRangeLabel`: "Nov 2 – 8"); the day's card is the workout, then "Today" (or the date) and how long, with its status only when it's news, said in that line in words (`dayStatusTone`: green ✓ Done, red Missed, amber Review to finish, "In progress · 8 of 18 sets" with one thin neutral bar; `QUIET_DAY_STATUSES` get none, never a badge or a ring); and where you are in the program (block, block week, program week) is only the Program card's. A workout from another block than the current one (`currentBlockId`) names its own block on its card. The training-days chip shows only while the days still need setting (`trainingDaysState(...).needed`) or read-only for a coach; once set, the athlete's days are ⋯ → Training days (`TrainingDaysSheet`, the same editor and save). No brand red on the strip or month: the day picked is a plain filled circle (`bg-foreground text-background`), today a thin ring, an in-progress ring is neutral; red is the day card's one main button — why: the coach found the screen repetitive (the block named in the header and the Program card, three different week numbers on one screen, "Today" as a pill under a day the strip had already picked), the dates and blocks in the wrong places, and the red selected day, red Today pill and red bars reading like alerts.
- Workouts reads the day first (Nov 20 2026, workouts-daily-first.test.ts): the week strip and month sit on the page with no card of their own (`WeekStrip flat`, `data-month-grid`), then the day picked as the page's card, then the program, then one quiet card of readiness and the month (`data-workouts-progress`: `RecoveryPreviewCard bare` and `TrainingAnalyticsPreviewCard bare` as rows, neutral bars, gone while empty). The day's cardio is read once per week (`useDayCardio`, ClientCardioSection.tsx: the selected day's query seeds the rest of that week's `client-cardio-resolved` keys, so tapping a day never waits): a training day's is the workout's last exercise (its `data-day-cardio` row, a pulse line), a day off's is the day's card (`CardioCompletionCard emphasis="primary"`: its red Done as planned, Log details for every number, Skip and Edit / Undo top right, the next workout under it; a skeleton while it loads, never "Rest day" first), a training day whose workout doesn't hold the plan's cardio gets it under the workout as secondary, and a day with neither is one compact Rest day card with the next workout. The cardio card is plain lines (the machine's settings in one line, the effort, "Done at any one" only with more than the time, the coach's note), never caps labels, a nested box or a tinted card. The program card says the block and "This week: <focus>" only; what the week is for and the roadmap fold under Plan details — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach asked for Workouts to feel like a purpose-built training app: a cardio-only day showed "Rest day" with the real cardio card buried under the program card behind a "Today's plan: Full Cardio Rest" header, a workout in progress said it twice (an amber "In Progress" pill and an amber 44% ring), the program card opened with a three-line week paragraph, and readiness and the month were two more cards with three big tiles and blue bars.
- A fresh launch of the app (a cold start always loads `/`, the manifest's `start_url`) opens Crew: `/portal/community` for clients, members and a dual account's client view (and it's where every "View as client" / member preview a coach enters lands, `CLIENT_VIEW_HOME`, unless the button means a specific screen), `/admin/community` for coaches and admins, `/finance` for the finance login (`crewHome` / `launchTarget`, launch-home.ts; signing in lands there too unless they were following a link (`/auth` `next`), and so do the old member app's `/m`, a client on a staff page and a push with nowhere more specific (`notificationDeepLink` generic, the monthly recap and birthday pushes), all through `CLIENT_VIEW_HOME` in client-home.ts; `cachedLaunchTarget()` goes there before auth resolves from the cached role and steps aside for "View as client" and a dual account's chosen view; `/app` goes to the same place). The one exception is mid-workout: if the person's last screen (`saveLastRoute`, route-persistence.ts, pathname + `?instance`, cleared on sign-out) is a workout day that's still unfinished in the Workout Session clock and was on screen in the last 2 hours (`inProgressWorkoutDay`, workout-resume.ts), the launch reopens that workout. Switching apps and coming back is not a launch: nothing redirects or reloads it (the iPhone app's own web-view reload stays on its page; a new build only prompts on non-safe screens) — why: the coach wants every fresh open to start on Crew, but going to Spotify mid-workout (even if iOS closes the app meanwhile) must land back on the sets.
- A story is only ever something someone chose to put there (Nov 18 2026): nothing posts one by itself (20270409090000_no_automatic_stories.sql dropped `trg_community_pulse`, and Privacy has no automatic-sharing switch; `auto_share_workouts` and `community_set_auto_share` stay only so an older app's switch still saves), and the story rail holds only stories (`storyWorthy`: a story-only post, `story` = `story_only`: the recap's Share to Story, the camera's STORY, Add to your story; a coach lesson, made as a story with its Questions / Poll stickers; and a session that posted itself before, a Pulse, `auto_shared` / `isPulsePost`, until its 24 hours run out), never a copy of a feed post: a workout, a photo, the daily series and every other feed post stay the feed's (`crewStories`, `myStory`, `storyEnd`, which points at what's new in the feed). The 197 Pulses already posted were archived on Nov 20 2026 (the next rule's cleanup), off their athletes' profiles; a Pulse is never in the feed or the Crew "N new" count (`community_activity`), and there is no crew-wide Activity bubble / story / list. The recap's footer (`RecapPostFooter`, recap-post.tsx) keeps the two shares apart: Story is the quick one (`useQuickStory` / `QuickStoryThumb` / `QuickStorySheet`, quick-story.tsx; quick-story.ts pure and tested), painted for them already from the workout with `paintShareCard` (preview, thumbnail and file are one painter, so what's shown is what posts), opening on the look that fits (`defaultStoryLook`: a PR leads with the record, else the look they last shared in (`keepStoryLook`, this phone only), else a strength day's Summary, else the Clean card), one tap on Share to Story posts it as a story-only post (`createCommunityPost(..., story: true)`, 24h, never the feed, no League points), and a tap on the preview opens it big: the looks as one stable row of chips (`storyLookOrder`), one Add photo (the phone's own Take photo / Choose from library, a photo kept on the phone until Share, dragged and pinched with `PhotoFramer`), a line of words (shown along the story's bottom), Crew or Close friends; a video is a Crew post ("Post a video instead" opens the studio); closing never posts, Undo takes it back, one share at a time. Crew is the considered post: Create Crew Post opens the full studio (`WorkoutShareStudio`, every tool kept), carrying any words; its "+15 league points" shows only when the post would earn them, and scoring is unchanged (`community_post_xp_sync`). An automatic story says so with Hide it, and a story they make replaces it; Done always closes — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach found the automatic PR stories unnecessary and the row cluttered: it replayed every crew post of the last day (each workout twice, once in the feed), and in the 30 days to Nov 18 144 automatic stories from 16 people (110 tagged PR) were seen by 0.95 people each and 16 drew a single reaction, while 20 workouts people chose to put in the feed were seen by 8.2 each; nobody had made a story by hand in 60 days (the studio's full composer was the only way); and the recap said "On your story for 24 hours" beside Share to Crew, so nobody could tell what was already public. The coach wants a story to go up only on the athlete's tap, the quick story to be one tap, and the feed kept for posts people mean to make; Activity repeated Stories, Wins, Rankings and the recap, cost the Crew five extra feed reads per visit, and auto sessions in the badge sent people to a feed with nothing new.
- Crew, Following and profiles are what people post, never an activity log (Nov 20 2026, 20270428090000_crew_feed_no_workout_cards.sql, crew-feed-cleanup.test.ts): a workout post that is only its generated card (a Pulse, `auto_shared`, or one shared with no photo, clip, words, GIF or voice memo of its own; never a lock in or a story) is never shown (`isWorkoutCard` in `inFeed` and the profile grid's filter, the server's `community_is_workout_card`), and nothing makes one again: `community_save_post` refuses a finished workout's post to anyone but the athlete without a photo or clip ("Add a photo or clip to post this workout"), the studio says so (`StudioPost.needsMedia`, set by `useWorkoutStudio`: Post stays off with "Add a photo or clip to post this to Crew. The card on its own can go on your story." and a Your story button), and so does the video composer. The 197 Pulses and 16 card-only posts already up were archived, never deleted (`archived_reason = 'workout_card'`, every row in `community_feed_cleanup_backup` first, rollback in docs/rollback/2026-11-20-crew-feed-cleanup-rollback.sql): their likes, comments and views stay, the workouts, sets, PRs and analytics are the training tables' and untouched, the League points the 16 earned when shared stay earned (`community_post_xp_sync` counts a `workout_card` archive as still shared; an author's own archive still takes them back), they don't fill the author's Archived (`community_my_archived`) and can't be put back as a card (`community_archive_post` refuses; sharing that workout again with a photo brings the post back with its likes, `community_save_post` clears the archive). An older post that can't show well on the feed's canvas is archived the same way with `archived_reason = 'feed_fit'` and stays in its author's Archived to put back (the Oct 10 Saturday picture, a 540x450 cartoon); anything that brings a post back clears its reason (`community_posts_clear_archived_reason`). On a post's screen a photo framed for the screen (9:16, `screenFit` cover) fills it edge to edge like a clip, the words over its foot (`data-photo-fit="cover"`), and a photo a little taller than the room between Crew's bar and the words is zoomed to that room's width, giving up at most `PHOTO_ZOOM_MAX_TRIM` (10%) of its height by its focus, never stretched and never cut at the sides where the studio's numbers and logo sit (`photoZoomBox`, `data-photo-fit="zoom"`); a clip shown whole there the same, up to `CLIP_ZOOM_MAX_TRIM` (15%, half off its top and half off its bottom; `InlineVideo` `focusY`, `data-clip-fit="zoom"`, never a clip framed in the studio; Nov 21 2026: Jared's 3:4 block press set sat as a narrow box with black down both sides); a 9:16 photo whose own words sit where the post's words go (an older story card saved as a photo) is marked `"fit": "whole"` in its `media_focus` and shown whole the same way instead of filling the screen (`MediaFocus.whole`, 20270428091000_crew_feed_whole_photo.sql: Jared's Oct 8 "Locked in" card); an automated post keeps its 4:5 picture (it matches its painted card), and a photo wider than the room shows across its width, over a soft, dark copy of itself rather than black above and below (`AmbientFill`, Nov 23 2026) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach wanted the feeds to feel like a polished fitness social platform rather than an activity log: 16 "Workout completed" cards (Oct 9 to Nov 5) sat among the crew's posts and 197 automatic ones on profiles, while the members' own photos, films and the coach's posts were the content worth scrolling; a 9:16 photo (Dwayne's lock in, Jared's) showed as a narrow box with black down both sides because photos ignored `screenFit` on their screen, and a 2:3 picture left black strips at its sides on a home-screen iPhone.
- A workout leads with a PR only when it's one worth leading with (Nov 18 2026, `headlinePr` / `headlineFromRecords` / `isBarbellBigThree`, community.ts, pure, tested): an all-time PR at `HEADLINE_PR_MAX_REPS` (10) reps or fewer on the session's main lift (`top_lift`: the lift the day is named for, else its heaviest), else on a barbell squat, bench or deadlift (the competition lift or a barbell variation; never a dumbbell, machine, cable, belt or split-squat lift of those families). That one is the featured lift everywhere a workout is shown (`featuredLift`: the feed's card, the grid tile, Home's line, the share cards) and the only PR moment (`isPrMoment`: the PR look, the quick story's PR default, a card's gold); the feed's workout chip is amber with the trophy only for it (`data-page-prs="headline"`, else the count said quietly); and the recap gives only it the trophy, its name as the headline ("Competition Squat PR", sentence case, never "NEW ALL-TIME PR!"), the amber New records box and the full sting (`lead`, `bigMoment`; it waits for the session's main lift as it does for the records). Every other record (an accessory's 12- or 15-rep best, a block or program best the plan was always going to bring) is still recorded, still listed under New records, still a PR chip under its set and still scored by the League exactly as before; it just never headlines — why: in the 30 days to Nov 18 2026, 120 of 165 workouts had a PR of some kind (139 of the 249 records were at 10+ reps), so the story row, the feed's amber chips and the recap's trophy and "3 NEW ALL-TIME PRs!" called nearly every session a PR and none of them meant anything; 57 had a headline PR, and those are the ones people talk about (Competition Squat 1–8, Competition Deadlift 2–4, Barbell Bench 5–10, Hip Thrust, a Primary Bench on a squat-named day).
- A workout's share cards (`paintShareCard`, workout-share-card.ts: the studio, the quick story, Share to Instagram) say a load in the unit it was typed in with the other unit beside or under it, smaller (`ShareCardData.lift.alt`, `ShareCardExercise.alt`, filled by `buildShareCardFields` from `topSetOther` / `exerciseBestLines`: "455 lb × 1" over "206.4 kg", the post-weight rule; a set with no load has none; nothing stored changes), and the Summary look gives a long exercise name a second line instead of cutting it (`wrapLines(ctx, r.name, …, 2)`). The recap's score has "How it's scored" behind it (`explainWorkoutScore`, workout-summary.ts: the share of planned sets logged, 10 off for pain, 3 on for a note, the stars; the formula unchanged) and a new milestone is one line each — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach asked for dual units on share graphics, no truncated exercise names, the score explained and the milestone compact.
- A program's upcoming workouts always sit on the client's training days (`clients.committed_training_days`), however the program or the days changed: starting, moving or assigning a block (`setBlockScheduleFn` re-places after every date change it moves workouts for, `start: true` also shows it and makes it Active; End early + start next starts the next one; `assignIntelDraftFn`, library assign, Cleo) and every training-days save run `realignClientToCommittedDays` at once, and the database queues the client in `pl_schedule_sync_queue` on any change of their days, a dated block's dates / visibility / week length, or a dated program's weeks / workouts (never on a date move alone), which the `schedule-sync` hook (pg_cron `pl-schedule-sync` every minute while something has waited 20 s, 20270220090000_program_follows_training_days.sql) runs through the same realign as `role: "system"`, plus `program_cardio_sync` (`runScheduleSyncQueue`). Done, started and past workouts and ones placed by hand (`manual` / `schedule_locked`) never move. On the Training tab a dated block the client can't see is its own alert ("… is hidden from Devin" · Start, the roadmap row says "Hidden from client" with Start program in its ⋯), opening the Start dialog (hidden dated blocks are candidates; it opens on the coach's own date while it's ahead, `defaultStartFor`, else the best start; a hidden block never counts as running for `bestNextStart`), and "N workouts need a date" · Place them places them in one tap (`rescheduleFromCommittedDays`, then cardio; no training days set: Athlete setup). A gap between blocks is only training days with nothing planned between what one block covers and the next (`blockGap` over `blockCoverEnd` / `blockCoverStart`: a block covers its own weeks or its workouts, whichever reaches further), never a rest day, and its Fix dates opens the next block on the day after this one ends — why: Jared's Volume 4s/6s (Sun Nov 1 – Sat Dec 5) and Strength Check-In (from Sun Dec 6) showed "1-day gap" for a Sunday he never trains on, which saving the dates could never clear; and Devin's 12-week program was dated Nov 9 by a dialog that only set dates, so it sat hidden with all 72 workouts undated and "Place them" only scrolled to 72 Pick date buttons, and the builder, Cleo or a member's app could leave a program off the days with nothing to fix it.
- A client can always move their own upcoming workouts (calendar drag, Move, Swap, bulk move, "re-plan to my days"); there is no account-level or coach schedule lock. `pl_days.schedule_locked` only means "placed by hand: automatic re-plans (new program, Cleo, `includePinned: false`) keep its date" and is never a permission check; only completed workouts stay fixed (`isCompletedItem`). A drag never stacks two workouts on a day by itself: a drop on a day that already has one (`workoutsAlreadyOn`, workout-move.ts; the Workouts calendar and the Move workouts page alike) opens the move sheet on that day (Swap days by default, Do both a tap away) and moves nothing until it's confirmed, and a drop on a free day moves at once with "Moved to Sat, Nov 21 · Undo" (`useMoveWithUndo`, use-move-workout.ts), so a workout never changes day without the person seeing it — why: a coach-placed program stamped every day locked, and clients were told "locked by your coach" when they just needed to train on another day; and on Nov 17 2026 Devin's press-and-hold on the week strip dropped Wednesday's pull day onto Saturday's bench day with no question, no message and no Undo, and he only found it later ("Am I seeing that I'm doing 2 workouts on Saturday?").
- Switching pages never shows the wrong screen or the whole-app splash: a screen that differs by service tier waits on `useServiceTier().loading` (true until the tier is actually known; seeded on the first frame from the per-device hint in `service-tier-hint.ts`) behind a skeleton, never a guessed branch or `null`; the query cache saved on the device belongs to one person (`claimPersistedCacheFor`, query-persister.ts: anyone else's is dropped, never painted), so new device-saved keys that aren't per-user are still safe; a slow page waits inside the shell (router `defaultPendingComponent: RoutePending`), and the `/_authenticated` guard lets an in-app navigation through without awaiting Supabase while the stored session is good — why: a member's Nutrition (or another account's screens) flashed as the coaching one before correcting, and a stalled session read swapped the app for the launch splash between two tabs.
- Follow is Instagram-style: `community_follows` holds approved follows only; a private account (`community_profiles.is_private`, members only, never staff; switched in Edit profile → Privacy, `PrivateAccountRow`, where a coach sees "Public account" instead of a switch) gets `community_follow_requests` it confirms or deletes (silently). A private account's crew posts are stored as `visibility = 'followers'` by the `community_posts_private_audience` trigger (every save path, and `community_set_private` re-stamps old posts both ways); automatic workout stories (`auto_shared`) always stay `'community'`. `'close_friends'` posts reach only the author's `community_close_friends` list (green story ring), and on a private account only those of them who also follow. Blocking someone (`chat_block_person`) ends what following or Close Friends let them see (unblocking gives it back), and a coach, or a coach's linked athlete login, can't go private, so coach posts are always `'community'`. Coaches/admins see every audience except `'private'`/`'coach'` rules as before. The feed and the "N new" badge read followers / Close Friends access as sets (`community_followed_authors()` / `community_close_friend_authors()`), never a per-row `community_post_visible` call. The app sends `'community'` for the crew choice and reads `'followers'` as it (`pickerAudience` / `isCrewAudience`); anything that means "shared with the crew" (League points, Stories) must accept both — why: one rule in `community_post_visible` decides access, nothing is copied, and flipping private/public or unfollowing changes who sees what at once.
- A story sticker's spot is `y` on the sticker itself (its middle as a share of the story's height, missing = just above the reply bar), kept to 0.20–0.66 by both `stickerY` (lessons.ts) and `community_sticker_clean`, placed by `stickerSpot` in the real story and the coach's preview alike; every sticker editor keeps the spot when the words change. A story frame's words (lesson, words post, workout win) go through `storyFrameLayout`: they start at `STORY_SAFE_TOP` (the phone's top inset + 72px, under the progress + name row) and end above the reply bar, from `--story-safe-top/bottom` that the viewer sets from the phone (`STORY_DEVICE_INSETS`) and the coach's preview sets to an iPhone's (`STORY_PREVIEW_INSETS`); never a fixed `pt-24` — why: a fixed top padding put a lesson's title under "Your story" on Dynamic Island iPhones while the notch-less preview looked fine, and the coach arranges a scheduled lesson's story in its full-screen preview (`LessonStoryPlayer`), and what he sees there must be exactly where the crew sees it.
- jfeffect.com's HTTPS is checked from a clean network every 3 hours (`.github/workflows/site-health.yml` → `scripts/site-health/check.py`, standard library + dig / openssl only): every address the public resolvers and the domain's own nameservers give jfeffect.com and www.jfeffect.com must serve a certificate a phone trusts for that name (an IPv6 address the host doesn't serve fails too), the certificate needs `CERT_MIN_DAYS` (14) left, the registration `DOMAIN_MIN_DAYS` (14, a warning from 45: a registrar's auto-renew can run on the expiry date itself) and the home page must load. A failed scheduled run opens a `site-health` issue (one comment per change in what's wrong, never every run) and the next passing run closes it; a test sandbox's own TLS interception can't stand in for this check, since it re-signs every site — why: on Nov 17 2026 a client (Ashley) got Safari's "This Connection Is Not Private" on jfeffect.com while the site served a valid Google Trust Services certificate to every resolver minutes later, so the coach needs to know at once whether it's the site (a certificate that didn't renew, a stray DNS record, a lapsed domain) or the person's network or phone, and to hear about the site's side before a client does.
- The service worker (Workbox `sw.js` + `public/push-sw.js`) is for push notifications and icons only: it never answers a page load (`navigateFallback: null`: left out, the plugin uses "index.html", which isn't precached, and Workbox threw at every worker start), never caches HTML/JS/CSS, and nothing reloads or navigates the app when a new worker activates (no `client.navigate` on activate, no `controllerchange` reload, `onNeedReload` given to the plugin's autoUpdate so it doesn't reload either); picking up a new build is `app-freshness.ts`'s job (on reopen, safe screens only). Bump the `push-sw.js?v=` in vite.config.ts whenever push-sw.js changes — why: every publish ships a new worker, and those reloads plus a worker answering navigations froze the published app on a blank white screen after a run of publishes (with the worker blocked the same page loaded fine).
- The Crew feed leads with what's new to you (Instagram's): `community_feed_ranked` (20270131090000_feed_new_first.sql, `useRankedFeed`) pages first the posts from the last 7 days you hadn't seen before the visit started (`community_post_views`, the first time a post was on your screen, against `seen_before` = `feedAnchor()`, feed-anchor.ts) plus your own from the last 12 hours, ranked (a point a day newer, someone's own post +1 over an automated one and +1 more when you follow each other either way, Wins and the Recap +0.5, the daily picture +0.25, the crew's likes and comments from before the moment on a log, your just-made post on top), then `CaughtUpLine` ("You're all caught up" · Earlier posts, placed by `caughtUpAt`) and everything else newest first. The moment holds for the whole visit and rides in every cursor (`feedCursorOf`), so every page and refetch agrees and nothing jumps out from under you; a new visit is a launch, 30 minutes away (`RESUME_NEW_VISIT_MS`) or asking for the newest (a tap on Crew or the New posts pill: `rollFeedAnchor`). It never holds a story or a Pulse, and visibility, blocks and reposts are `community_feed`'s exactly; the story rail, Home's deck, the coach's Needs you and profiles keep reading `community_feed` in time order (`useCommunityFeed`), and older builds are unchanged — why: a visit reads about one post, so anything that slid under a post you'd already seen was never seen (over the 14 days to Nov 5 there were 41 feed posts, 8 of them by members, and most people missed most of those 8 under the coach's automated posts).
- Crew posts are never rebuilt from scratch on a read (20270317090000_crew_feed_speed.sql): a post's workout stats come from `community_workout_stats_cached` (`community_post_json`), the stats stored per finished workout in `community_workout_stats_cache` and used only while their athlete's version (`community_workout_stats_versions`) hasn't moved since they were made, and for a day at most, else worked out as before; pg_cron `community-workout-stats` (`community_workout_stats_refresh`, every minute, 40 a run, newest first) stores what's missing or stale, reading the version before the stats so a change that lands meanwhile leaves a copy stale, never wrong. A workout's stats depend only on its own sets and the athlete's earlier training, its day and block names and their time zone, so the version moves (`community_stats_bump`) only when one of those changes at or before a finished workout: a logged set changing (never one in a workout still going, nothing finished comes after it), a workout finished or changed, a finished day or its exercises renamed or reordered, the athlete's blocks or weeks, their time zone, or an exercise renamed. Anything new a post shows that reads the athlete's training must move the version the same way, or be worked out on the read. People lookups (`community_author`, `community_avatar`, `community_is_coach`) run the role check only on the person's own accounts (`accounts AS MATERIALIZED`; unmaterialized, the planner ran `has_role` on every profile), and `community_is_coach` never builds the whole card. The clients' paged feed asks for its next page four screens before the end (`FEED_READ_AHEAD`) — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach asked for posts to load very fast for a smooth watch and scroll; every workout post cost the database 140–320 ms (half of it the stats, which walk the athlete's whole history for PRs, most of the rest people lookups at ~7 ms each), so a page of ten took ~1.75 s and the story rail's 2.1 s, and the next page was asked for only ~1.7 screens before the end; after: ~0.19 s and ~0.18 s, every stored copy identical to a fresh calculation.
- Finding athletes ("Find athletes", `discover.tsx`: Activity's search icon, the people-to-follow row's See all and the caught-up card) has one source: `community_people_search` (the shown name, i.e. a client's first name, or @username; never a last name) and `community_suggestions` (server-ranked with the reason it shows: your coach, follows you, followed by people you follow, same gym, shared training, new). Both read `community_findable_people`, which leaves out you, linked logins and blocks either way, and shows someone who turned off "Suggest my account" (`community_profiles.discoverable`; coaches can't) only to people already connected to them. Usernames live on the person's main account's `community_profiles` row, made by the `community_username_on_*` triggers, which never fail the write that added the person. A block (`chat_user_blocks`) also hides the two people's posts, Crew list rows and profiles from each other through the `community_blocked_authors()` set (staff still see everything). Every person card carries `mutuals` (people you follow who follow them, via `community_mutuals`, photos only in the face stack); people who follow you that you don't follow back live only in Follow back (`community_follow_back`), never twice; the feed's suggestion row sits right after the first post (Instagram's spot; in Crew's own view data 63% of visits see one new post and under 3 in 10 reach the third) and its ••• → Hide is `community_profiles.feed_suggestions_hidden_until` (30 days, the account, not the device); the caught-up card at the end of the feed points to Find athletes — why: one ranked list instead of a client-side guess, nobody can be found by a name the Crew doesn't show, and "Suggest my account" plus blocks have to hold everywhere someone can be found.
- Captions read on a phone the way they were written (Nov 21 2026, caption-preview.ts, caption-text.tsx, caption-shape.ts, crew-caption-readability.test.ts): a caption's paragraphs are never collapsed into one block. Shown whole (the post's sheet, the post page, the card feed, an automated post's words) it's `CaptionText`: each paragraph its own block with a measured gap (0.7em, never a full empty line), a single line break inside one kept, 15px at 1.5 leading. A feed screen's two-line preview (`PageCaption`) puts each paragraph on its own line (`previewText`) and stops where a thought does (`choosePreviewStop`: the opener whole when it fits, else a sentence end that keeps `SENTENCE_STOP_SHARE` of the room, else the last word). In the post's sheet the caption sits apart from the comments: the author's row, then the words full width in the post font, "Liked by", a hairline, and the comments under it indented beside their photos at 14px / 1.45. The sheet opens at half (`POST_SHEET_HEIGHT`) and a drag up grows it to nearly the whole screen (`POST_SHEET_FULL_HEIGHT`, `sheetDragMode` "grow", `sheetDragGrows`, before anything inside scrolls, Instagram's), a tap on the grip does the same and back (never while typing), and from full a pull down goes back to half or, far enough, closes it (`sheetFullRelease`); every other bottom sheet keeps swipe down to close as it was. Automated captions are written for that: the deck writer asks for one idea said once, an opening line that stands on its own and earns the "more", short paragraphs of one to three sentences with a blank line between, a varied shape post to post, opinions owned, a real question only now and then; and code catches a model that hands back one block anyway (`shapeCaption`: the opener on its own, the rest one paragraph, split once more only when long and never before a sentence that leans on the one before it (`LEANS_BACK`), never a word changed; deck captions only, since an Athlete Insight's one quick paragraph is his shape). 20270430100000_crew_caption_readability.sql rewrote by hand the 31 automated posts that had gone out as one block (lessons, Tuesday tips, Monday Motivation, Finish Strong Friday, Saturday Spirit) and the 65 library deck captions, in short paragraphs with filler and repeated kickers cut, every fact, number and opinion kept and nothing added, md5 guarded, captions only (ids, dates, likes, comments, views and slides kept, nothing moved in the feed, never edited_at), every old one in `community_caption_readability_backup` (rollback in docs/rollback/2026-11-21-crew-caption-readability-rollback.sql); members' posts and comments are never touched — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach found Crew's captions too dense and exhausting to read: every lesson, tip and older series caption had gone out as one block of three or four sentences, the feed's preview ran paragraphs together into one run of words, and the sheet showed the caption as one more comment in the list with nowhere to read a long thread but half the screen.
- Every automated post (lessons, the daily series, Wins, the Recap, Tuesday's numbers, holidays, birthdays; the daily pictures have their own rule, below) reads like the coach typed it himself (Nov 8 2026, which replaces Nov 7's sentence case): a powerlifting coach who knows his stuff, direct, confident, conversational, occasionally dry, about execution, accountability and measurable progress, never a fitness motivation page, and never professionally edited: casual grammar, contractions with their apostrophes (don't, that's, you're), fragments and the odd run-on, sentence starts lowercase sometimes (some posts all lowercase, some with normal capitals, most in between, varied post to post), no fixed shape (no forced hook / explanation / conclusion: one paragraph or a few short thoughts, a detailed explanation or a quick observation), his habits ("i think", "honestly", "gonna", "lol") occasional and never a format (at most one a post, most posts none, "lol" never two posts running), never a misspelling or broken grammar put in to sound human, one emoji at most where he'd use one, his opinions as his ("i think", "my take") and never a story he didn't live, no slogans, hype ("that's insane", "craaazy!!", 🔥🔥🔥), kickers, formal transitions ("however", "additionally", "moreover", "in conclusion", "ultimately", "that said") or texting shorthand beyond his habits ("til", "vid", "yall", "lowkey", "rn"), never his banned lines ("every little bit counts", "give yourself grace", "remember to listen to your body", "you got this", "movement is medicine", "there are worse places to spend your time"), nothing on his never-say list, no dashes or semicolons. 20270214090000_casual_voice_library.sql (casual-voice-library.test.ts) rewrote them all again in that voice on Nov 8: the 81 lessons not yet out, all 22 Tuesday Tips, the 10 holidays and the 42 posts already out under his name, in place (captions only, so dates, feed order, likes, comments and existing tags stay; a lesson or tip that went out gets its library text to match), md5 guarded, every fact, number, study, name, quote and safety line kept, varied post to post (about a third lowercase, few question hooks, far fewer bullets, "lol" in under 1 in 10), his own words untouched. Before that, 20270209090000_coach_voice_posts.sql rewrote every post already out under his name in place (ids, dates, likes and comments kept, md5 guarded, his own words and members' birthday posts left alone) and 20270210090000_coach_voice_libraries.sql what goes out next (Tuesday Tips, the 87 lessons still in texting lowercase, holidays, the Wins / numbers / feature / birthday templates; coach-voice-libraries.test.ts); the switched-off series (Monday Motivation, Try it Thursday, Finish Strong Friday, Saturday Spirit) and the Recap template are still in the old voice and need the same pass before one is switched back on. Lessons are held to `plainCheck` (plain-words.ts: Flesch-Kincaid grade ≤ `PLAIN_GRADE_MAX` (5), no sentence over `LONGEST_SENTENCE_MAX` words, at most `NUMBERS_MAX` numbers and `STUDIES_MAX` studies, no `JARGON`, `voiceProblems`); the five library series, the holidays and the Wins / Recap / numbers / birthday templates (20270202090000_automated_posts_new_voice.sql, automated-voice.test.ts) to the daily pictures' bar: grade ≤ `DAILY_GRADE_MAX`, no sentence over `DAILY_SENTENCE_MAX` words, none of `GENERIC_PHRASES` / `AI_TELLS` / `SCRIPTED_PHRASES`, no dropped apostrophe. A coaching-only feature (the weekly check-in, a video to the coach) says it's "for my coaching clients", since free members get these posts too. A lesson's card (title, belief / truth, a reset's three things) stays sentence case, as plain as the caption. Lessons: a hook that earns the tap (a real athlete, coach or team when one makes the point), studies by year in plain words ("a 2013 study found", "a 2022 look at 69 studies"), never "(Author & co)"; any athlete or coach fact or quote is checked against a source before it goes in and that source sits in the lesson's `refs` (only the coach sees them), and a rewrite never adds a fact (quotes stay word for word). A library rewrite only touches text still exactly as it was (md5 guard) and a post already out only while its caption is still the library text it went out with, so the coach's own edits stay his. Every lesson, new or rewritten, also meets the thinking standard in docs/EDUCATIONAL_POSTS.md: research → question the study (subjects, size, what was measured vs inferred, whether it holds for competitive lifters) → mechanics → competing explanations → coaching experience → its own conclusion → how an athlete tells the causes apart and what to investigate, by skill level. The post says which part is evidence and which is the coach's own take ("my take:"), never forces certainty or stretches a study, fits 1200 characters, follows no fixed template, and its diagram shows what the words claim (the sticking region runs from the bar's fastest point to its slowest, `sticking_point` `variant: "window"` labels both) — why: the coach wants every automated post to read "dummy proof, like a 5th grade reading level" and sound like him (the sentence-case lessons and Monday / Friday posts read like a textbook and an influencer, not his texts), and on Nov 5 that it read like something he'd naturally post ("one thing i think people overlook is…"), with clear punctuation and no forced slang or typos (the dropped apostrophes read as careless next to his own posts), his standing rule is verified quotes and facts only, and he wants an app that teaches athletes to think about powerlifting at a higher level, not one that repeats what researchers or other coaches already said.
- A story's question box is Instagram's Questions: anyone posting a story can put Questions, Poll or Slider on it from the studio's sticker tray (`StickerLayer interactive`, placed live by `LiveStickerSpot` and put on with `setPostSticker` once the story is up, never drawn into the picture). The poster sees the same box as everyone and can fill it in and send too (it lands in their own Responses, "In your responses"; `community_sticker_answer` has no own-question refusal since 20261211120000_own_question_answers.sql). A poll is the same for everyone, the poster too: its options with no results until you've voted, then the bars and percentages (`PollSticker`, `shown` = your own vote); no sticker on a story ever shows a count, and the poster's "See results" / "See responses" opens their own sheet, where a poll's tally is each answer's share and votes with who picked it (`PollTally`). The poster's Responses (`StickerAnswersSheet`, `questionResponses`: not answered first) answer one with a story: `openStoryReply` → `StoryReplyHost` (mounted on the Crew screen, never in View as client) opens `ShareStudio reply` on a backdrop (`paintReplyBackdrop`) with the response card (`responseCardItem`: the question and what was sent, never who sent it) and the text tool open, and posting marks it answered (`community_sticker_mark_answered`: the asker only, with their own story; deleting that story un-answers it, `answered_post_id` ON DELETE SET NULL). Who answered is only ever known to the poster and the person who answered, the poster's own answers included: the shared card and its story carry no name, tag or caption, `answers` is only in the poster's own `community_sticker_json`, and that function takes the viewer as an argument, so it runs only inside the SECURITY DEFINER functions that pass the signed-in person (no EXECUTE for `authenticated` / `anon`, 20261211130000_sticker_answers_private.sql; `community_sticker_answers` has RLS with no policies and no table grants). "View as client" loads the crew as the coach, so every post goes through `seenAs` (`usePovViewer` in `useCommunityFeed` / `usePostDetail`): ownership is the previewed client's (and never the coach's own posts, loaded as `is_mine`, even when the client previewed is the coach's own linked client account, which authors their stories), poster-only sticker answers are dropped, the coach's extras (`viewerIsStaff`) are off, and owner actions toast `PREVIEW_ONLY_MESSAGE` — why: the coach's own story showed as "Your story" with its replies count in View as client instead of the box a client types in, answering a question publicly must never name the person who asked, any signed-in client could call `community_sticker_json` with the poster's id and read every name, and a poll that showed its results and "0 votes" before anyone had voted gave the answer away and read empty.
- App sounds (`app-sounds.ts`: sets, PRs, rest timer, chat and notification blips, Cleo's music) share one AudioContext that is kept awake: it wakes on every `touchend` / `pointerup` / `click` / `keydown` and on coming back to the app (those listeners are never removed; `pointerdown` doesn't count as a tap that may start audio on a touch screen), and a sound asked for while it's suspended plays once it resumes (`whenRunning`, at most 400ms late) instead of being dropped. They follow the silent switch ("ambient"); a sound someone taps to hear through a detached `Audio` element (`sound-player.ts` chat sound effects, voice memos) holds "playback" itself (`holdPlaybackSession`), because the page's media watcher only hears elements in the document — why: iPhones suspend the context whenever the app goes to the background, so after switching to Spotify every sound stayed silent until a reload, and with the switch on silent tapped sound effects played without a sound.
- Feedback is one call, `feedback(event)` (src/lib/feedback.ts, `FEEDBACK`): each event has one haptic and one sound, and a screen never calls `navigator.vibrate`, the haptics plugin or `playAppSound` itself (feedback-haptics.test.ts checks). Restraint is the design: everyday acts get a light tap and no sound (like, follow, save, repost, comment, a pick), a set is a medium tap with the set sound, a finished exercise the exercise sound, a PR / finished workout / sent post a success pattern with its sting, an achievement the heavy one, an error the error pattern, a timer's end the alarm; scrolling, typing, sliders, page changes and repeats get nothing, a burst keeps only its strongest (`admitHaptic`: 45ms, each style its own gap) and reduced motion folds every pattern to one pulse (the timer's alarm excepted). Haptics go the best way a device has (`hapticRoute`): the app's own Capacitor Haptics plugin when the build links it (the app's bridge puts it on `Capacitor.Plugins`, `registerPlugin` the fallback; the Android app links it, android-build.yml, pinned and added in the build only so the web app's dependencies never change; the iPhone app links no Capacitor plugins yet, so there the web's own routes below apply), else `navigator.vibrate` (Android; the Android app declares VIBRATE, android-build.yml), else on an iPhone or iPad (Safari, the home-screen app, the app's web view, iOS 17.4+) WebKit's switch control: `HapticTap` (haptic-tap.tsx), a see-through label tied to an undrawn `<input type="checkbox" switch>`, sits last inside the buttons that matter (like, follow, save, repost, a set's done button, Suggested's fill chip, the chat and comment sends, the review's save, every `Checkbox` and `Switch`) so the person's own tap flips it and the Taptic Engine taps; a scripted tick (`iosTick`, an undrawn switch clicked from code) covers what no finger touches (a double-tap like, a PR, a timer, a set logged by typing), which iOS allows only 17.4–26.4, and stays quiet for `TRUSTED_TICK_QUIET_MS` after a real tap ticked so nothing taps twice; both switches are never drawn, so neither can take focus from a box the button opens. `HapticTap` only ever goes in a `relative` `<button type="button">`, never a link or a submit (the label would take their activation). Sounds and haptics are each a switch on the phone (Account → Notifications, sounds & haptics: `jf-app-sounds`, `jf-haptics`, both on by default, the Haptics row only where the device can); sounds keep following the silent switch — why: on Nov 19 2026 the coach wanted JF Effect to feel like a premium native app rather than a website with random sounds: taps buzzed from a dozen places with their own patterns (a like, a repost, a sticker and the rest timer each called `navigator.vibrate` its own way), most acts that matter (a set, a PR, a follow, a sent message) gave no haptic at all, nothing could turn haptics off, and an iPhone, where most of the crew trains, has no vibrate API, so none of it ever reached them.
- Crew has one top bar, its own (`CrewBar`, crew-header.tsx): + (the studio) on the left, three tabs in the middle, Crew · Following · Rankings (`CrewFeedTabs`, `CREW_TABS`, the rule below; Nov 19 2026), the Activity heart and your photo (your profile) on the right; there is no trophy button. The middle is a grid cell centred whenever it fits and giving way only as much as the buttons need (`minmax(max-content,1fr)` sides around `minmax(0,auto)`, never an absolute strip under the buttons), the tabs a size smaller under 390px and 360px, and `compact` (smaller still) for the coach's two left buttons, so all three fit a 320px phone. Rankings is a tab, not a page: the same bar on the feed and on Rankings (the one you're on underlined, no ‹ back), `#rankings` in the address (replaced, so Back never steps through tabs), its body easing in from the side (`data-crew-tab-body`), a tap on Crew or Following from it back to the feed where you left it, a tap on the tab you're on to its top. There is no Feed / Rankings / You toggle, and Rankings is never a bubble among the clients' stories (they fold away as you scroll, and a bubble reads as a story); Find athletes is Activity's search, the people-to-follow row (See all) and the caught-up card; the coach's Crew page is this same bar (the coach-page rule below), with his + and Manage on the left and the same Rankings tab (the crew's board, no "you"), and only the finance login's look at the crew (no bar of its own) keeps the League as a trophy bubble at the start of its stories (`StoryRail rankings`, `data-story-rankings`, `/admin/league`). Profiles and Activity open as pages with ‹ back that lands on the same spot in the feed (`goBack`). The shell's phone bar steps aside on Crew (`AppShell ownTopBarOn`, the DualAccountSwitcher moves under Crew's bar) and More is your profile's ☰ (`OPEN_MORE_EVENT`); on phones the bar is fixed and slides away reading down (`useBarHidden`), except on the paged feed, where it stays put, see-through over the posts (`overlay`). A coach viewing as a client gets the same one bar (the shell's steps aside there too, there is no inline Crew bar): the preview banner holds the notch and publishes its height (`--pov-banner-h`, `data-pov-banner-on`), so every phone top bar starts at `--app-chrome-top` with `--app-safe-top` of notch padding (styles.css), never a second notch under the banner — why: two stacked bars plus a segmented toggle took a fifth of the screen before the first post, the toggle's Rankings repeated the trophy, and in View as client the shell's bar sat over Crew's with a notch-sized gap above it; and on Nov 13 2026 the coach found Rankings as a bubble between Your story and the crew's stories neither well placed nor a good way in (it looked like a story and was gone once the stories folded away); and on Nov 19 2026 he asked for the standalone trophy in the top-left to go and Rankings to sit in the top navigation as Crew | Following | Rankings, as a refinement, keeping the + and everything else.
- Crew → Rankings leads with what's yours, and the League has a week (Nov 19 2026, 20270417090000_league_weekly_spotlight.sql, league-week.ts pure and tested, league-week-card.tsx). The page is the board switch first (`BOARD_TABS`: Monthly League · Hall of Strength, named for what they are), then in recap week the monthly recap, then the board; the other boards' podiums (`RankingsSpotlight`) flip under it, a tap from opening theirs. Monthly: you first (rank, how far you've moved since Monday, `data-you-move`, and the points you've added this week, `data-you-week-points`, beside your points and the gap line), then the Top 10, each with its move since Monday in its own column (`data-row-move`: green up, quiet down, nothing when unchanged, never a made-up ▲ for someone new this week, `boardMoves`), then Last week (the card below), last month's final standings, Milestones and How points work, folded. Hall of Strength reads the same way: what it ranks in plain words under its title (`strengthExplainer`: "Total ÷ bodyweight · training and meets · 12 athletes"), you on the right, sentence case, its numbers in the text colour (never the brand red). A coach on his own Crew sees the crew's Monthly board with no "you" (`AthleteLevelCard clientId={null}`). Movement and the card come from `league_week_snapshots`: where everyone stood each Monday 00:00 in the league's clock (`local_to_instant`), taken by `league_month_scores(_month, _now)` itself, which already counts points only up to `_now`, so scoring never changes and a snapshot taken late is still exact (`league_week_snapshot_take`, one per Monday under an advisory lock; pg_cron `league-week-snapshots` hourly at :07 takes this week's and last week's if missing; the first reader on Monday takes them too). The table is server only; `get_league_week_movement` (this week's Monday, only while it's in the month shown) and `get_league_week_spotlight` read it through `portal_viewer_uid`, showing nothing the board doesn't (its top 50 and you). The Monday card (`LeagueWeekCard`): last week's dates and the League month's week, your rank as a big number with the move as a chip that pops and the number ticking from last week's (none under reduced motion), +points last week · total, one reason to come back read off the live board when it's opened (`chaseLine`: the gap to the next spot in fully logged workouts, 15 points each, when it's one or two of them, else just the gap; holding #1; a tie broken by your next points; the Final Week Boost first while it's chased; how to join; never "more workouts", since the League counts only planned ones), your week in facts that happened (`weekFacts`: workouts, fully logged, bodyweight days, Crew posts) and your all-time PRs (finished workouts, 10 reps or fewer, `prLine`), then Top week and Biggest climbs (three each) and See the rankings. It's a screen of the feed right after the first post (Instagram's spot; people to follow take it the visits it doesn't), from 6 AM Monday on the person's own clock (`spotlightReady`), decided once a visit while they're still on the first screen so nothing slides in under someone mid-feed (`weekSlot`), never on the Following tab, the coach's own page or the finance feed; seen once a week server-side (`feature_announcement_views`, `league_week:<Monday>`, after a beat on screen or ✕, which also moves on to the next post), never recorded while a coach views as the client; no push, no popup; one tap away all week from Rankings' Last week row (`LeagueWeekSheet`). There's no card in a month's first week (the monthly recap is that week's moment) and no movement the week a month starts — why: on Nov 19 2026 the coach asked for precision UX on Crew and Rankings without a redesign: Rankings in the top navigation, the Rankings page prioritising your rank, points, movement and the gap to the next spot with Monthly and Strength clearly distinct (a Hall of Strength podium flipped above the Monthly board, the Strength numbers were the brand red under caps labels), and a polished, dismissible Monday card from real data (movement, the gap to overtake, climbers, top performers, PRs, consistency, one reason to compete) shown once a week and revisitable, with no notification spam, meaningless achievements, fabricated competition or nudges to train beyond the plan; the League scored live by a ~1.6 s function, so movement is kept once a week instead of computed on every board load.
- The Hall of Strength takes every total apart and puts two lifters side by side (Nov 22 2026, strength-split.ts pure and tested on the Hall's real totals, strength-split-view.tsx; 20270503090000_hall_of_strength_total_split.sql). Both boards send each total's parts (`squat_kg`, `bench_kg`, `deadlift_kg`, with `*_reps` for a training total, and a meet total's official `gl_points` on the all-time board too): a training total is its three best training lifts as the board ranks them, a meet total that day's lifts, so the split always adds up to the total on the board (`partsOf` → `splitOf`). Typical is the average raw meet split, men 35 / 24 / 41 and women 35.5 / 20.5 / 44 (`TYPICAL_SPLIT`: Stronger By Science's analysis of OpenPowerlifting, which OpenPowerlifting's own medians agree with; heavier and elite lifters lean on the squat, said as a direction only), a woman always read against women's numbers and nobody without a sex against anyone's; "leans" is 2+ percentage points (`LEAN_POINTS`, the app's reading, said as such, never a published standard), and a profile names what carries the total (`liftProfile`: Squatter, Bencher, Puller; with nothing clearly over and one lift under, the two that carry it: Leg-dominant, Bench & pull, Squat & bench; else All-rounder). A total built on rep sets counts as lifted like the board, says so wherever its split shows (`repsNote`), and never sets the Hall's average or wins a standout (`hallSplits` / `standoutShares` read totals of singles only); GL is a meet's official points, else the IPF formula on a gym total labelled an estimate (`glOf`, gl-points.ts, the formula's own module so Crew never loads Program Intelligence). On the Total board each lifter carries the split as a small bar with the key said once (`SplitBar`, `SplitLegend`; `--split-squat` / `--split-bench` / `--split-deadlift`, the money charts' validated slots in light and dark, always with labels or a table beside them); a tap on any lifter opens their breakdown (`LifterSplitSheet`: the split against typical with ticks and a table, what it says, the classic ratios, a lagging lift at a typical share holding the other two (`typicalAround`, singles only), standouts and the Hall's average, then Compare and their meet career). Compare two lifters (`CompareSheet`, the board's own rows, you on the left when you have a total) is deterministic, never a model: who wins each lift and by how much and for their size (× bodyweight), the two splits, the gap between their totals lift by lift (`compareLifters`: the board's totals, so the numbers match the duels), pound for pound by GL (the fair comparison between bodyweights and between a man and a woman, said once in `notes`), what sets each apart in short lines (`profileShort`, never the same percentage twice), "you" spoken to as you — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for a powerlifting-nerd percentage breakdown of each Hall of Strength total per lift, and a mode to compare athletes with analysis of what makes each lifter unique and where each has the advantage; the board's rows had no way to say what a total was made of (a meet total's best squat and its total could be different meets), and on the Hall's real totals the men average 35.3 / 23.6 / 41.1 and the women 35.5 / 20.8 / 43.7, right on the published splits.
- Crew → Rankings has theme music and sound effects (Nov 22 2026, rankings-music.ts, rankings-music.tsx, music-hold.ts): two original pieces synthesized live on the app's one sound engine (`startAppMusic`, app-sounds.ts: no files, the sounds switch and the silent switch, "ambient" so it mixes with the athlete's own music), Monthly League "Points on the Board" (124 BPM, A minor, four on the floor, pumping saw bass, a hook on the second half) and Hall of Strength "Iron Legacy" (76 BPM, D minor to an A major, war drums, low-string ostinato, choir, brass over struck iron plates, `plateStrike`); never a copyrighted song (nothing licensed can ship in the app). A music button beside the board switch (`RankingsMusicButton`) is off until the person turns it on, remembered on that phone (`jf-rankings-music`), and the module loads only then; the theme follows the board (a crossfade), stops in the background, with sounds off and while the Monthly Recap plays its own music (`holdBackgroundMusic`, taken by recap-music.ts), and fades out when Rankings closes. Both mixes are high-passed at 45 Hz and sit at about −23 dBFS RMS, peaks under −4 dBFS (`THEME_VOLUME`, measured by rendering the real code offline in Chromium; change the music only with those numbers). The effects go through `feedback()` like every sound: switching boards `board` (selection tick + a soft swish), two lifters squaring up `versus` (medium tap + a short iron hit, once per pairing), a lifter's breakdown opening `reveal` (light tap + one plate tap) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for theme music for the Monthly League and the Hall of Strength, a song picked for each, and sound effects; music that played by itself would cut across the athlete's own music in the gym.
- The Crew feed has two looks, TikTok's Following / For You: Crew (everyone, every post) and Following (people's own posts from the ones you follow: by them, a collab with them, or reposted by them or you, and yours; never an automated post, a lesson, the daily series, Wins, the Recap, a holiday or a birthday, which stay Crew's), two of the tabs in the middle of Crew's bar (`CrewFeedTabs`, crew-header.tsx, Rankings the third: all bold so nothing shifts, the one you're off at 55%, a short neutral line under the one you're on, the icons' shadow over the paged feed; never the brand red), there whether or not you follow anyone. Following is read on the server, the same ranked feed narrowed by `community_feed_ranked(…, _following => true)` (20270402092000_crew_following_feed.sql: same moment, ranking, visibility, blocks and paging; the crew feed sends no `_following`, so installed apps read it as before), its own pages under the crew feed's key with who you follow in it (`useRankedFeed(enabled, { follows })`), and the app's mirror of the rule is `inFollowingFeed` (community.ts), which the Following tab's story rail reads (`railPosts`: the people you follow and yours). It always opens on Crew: the choice is screen state only (`feedTab`, never saved on the phone or the account), and every new visit puts it back (`onNewVisit`, feed-anchor.ts: back after `RESUME_NEW_VISIT_MS`, a tap on the Crew tab while on it, the New posts pill), while a profile or Rankings opened from Following comes back to Following. A tap on the tab you're on goes to its top. Following nobody shows "Follow people to fill this feed" with Find people to follow and asks the server nothing; loading is a spinner, a failed load Try again, and nothing yet "Nothing yet from people you follow" with Back to Crew; the people-to-follow and share screens are Crew's only. In View as client Following toasts instead of showing the coach's own follows — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach wanted the Following system to do more, TikTok's way (a switch at the top to see just their friends) with the default always going back to the crew feed; it was a ⌄ on the JF Effect name that only appeared once you followed someone, so nobody found it; and 50 of the 84 feed posts were automated while 13 of the 14 people following anyone followed the coach, so with them Following was the Crew feed less a few posts (72–81 of 81 for the members checked, 19–28 from 3–5 people without), and filtering it on the phone read through every crew page to find a few people's posts.
- The Activity heart's bubble (`ActivityHeart`, client Crew and the coach's Crew header) rises once per batch of activity you haven't been shown (♥ likes · 💬 comments/replies · 👤 follows), holds ~4s and folds into the red dot, which stays until Activity is read. "Shown" is server-side, one `notification_state` row per login (kind `crew_preview`, source `activity`, written with `notif_mark_read`), and `community_my_notifications` gives each line `fresh` (its notifications after both that and the line's own read time), so it costs no extra request. It fires only from a fetch made on this visit (`isFetchedAfterMount`; returning to the app refetches), waits while a dialog or sheet is open or the heart is off screen, folds on any scroll, tap, key or app switch, and fades in place under reduced motion — why: it must never replay on another phone or on every launch, never compete with another popup, and never sit over something you're about to tap.
- Phones stay upright, Instagram's way, and turning one never interrupts or breaks anything (Nov 19 2026, hardened Nov 20 2026; portrait-only.ts, rotation-settle.ts): where the platform can hold a phone in portrait it does: the installed iPhone app (`UISupportedInterfaceOrientations` portrait only, ios-build.yml's "Keep the iPhone app upright", every build since `cap add ios` regenerates Info.plist, and the built app's Info.plist checked portrait-only before TestFlight; Capacitor's `CAPBridgeViewController` reads that list; iPads keep every orientation), the installed Android app (MainActivity's `android:screenOrientation="portrait"`, android-build.yml's "Keep the Android app upright", every build; an Android WebView refuses `screen.orientation.lock`, and Android 16+ lets large screens turn anyway) and Android's installed web app (the manifest's `"orientation": "portrait-primary"`, plus `lockPortrait` on launch and on coming back, `PortraitLock` in the root, which draws nothing; the app's own camera and a video played full screen let it go while they're up). Where it can't (iPhone Safari and the iPhone home-screen web app support neither the manifest's orientation nor the lock, nor anything else a page can use; an Android browser tab locks only in fullscreen), the page turns with the phone and a phone keeps the phone layout: `data-phone` on <html> (`PHONE_BOOT_SCRIPT` in <head> before the first paint, `isPhone`: a finger for a pointer and the screen's short side under `PHONE_SHORT_SIDE_MAX`, read from the screen, never the window, so a keyboard never changes it; `syncPhoneAttr` on every turn, resize and settle for a foldable) keeps Tailwind's `sm` and `md` (and `max-sm` / `max-md`) off a phone (`@custom-variant sm` / `max-sm` / `md` / `max-md` in styles.css; phone-only CSS is `@variant max-md` or `max-sm` inside its selector, never a top-level `@variant` block, whose phone branch compiles to `:scope:where(…) html …` and matches nothing, and never a raw 640 / 768px media query), and every screen that chooses in JS reads `phoneLayoutNow()` / `onPhoneLayoutChange` (useIsMobile, useIsMobileNow, the shell's tablet check), never a bare width, so turning the phone mid-workout, mid-form or mid-chat swaps no tree, keeps what's typed, open and focused, and never shows a blocking screen. On its side a phone is one centered column, the tab bar's and Crew's bar's 560px row (`.phone-column`: the page's <main>, the full-height chats, bottom sheets and drawers, and every bar a phone screen pins outside <main>: Focus's top and bottom bars, both program builders' header and week bar, Crew's comment sheet, the chats' action bars; the shell bar's padding; toasts), so nothing stretches to 852pt or sits under the rounded corners or the camera, and nothing that pops up is taller than the 393pt screen (styles.css, `(orientation: landscape)` only: a `.phone-column` dialog scrolls inside the screen's height, and a popup in a `fixed inset-0` box aligns `safe`, so a tall card starts at its ✕ instead of losing it off the top); an upright phone is narrower, so none of it changes anything there, and tablets and computers never get it. Every size kept across a turn is read again as the turn settles (`onRotationSettle`: `orientationchange`, the screen's own `change`, a width-changing resize and coming back turned, at once and at `ROTATION_SETTLE_MS` up to 2.5s), and a reading bigger than the screen is never kept (`plausibleViewport`: mid-turn WebKit gives the new width with the old height, WebKit 322083): `--app-vh` (`IOS_WEB_APP_BOOT_SCRIPT`, also never read with the keyboard up), Crew's `--feed-vh` / `--feed-gap` (`watchFeedViewport`, which also keeps the post that was on screen on screen, `postOnScreen` / `keepOnScreen`), the keyboard detector's and `useVisualViewportBox`'s no-keyboard heights (`tallestPerWidth`: one per width, plausible readings only), the bottom-gap probe, the pinned bars and `data-phone`; and the keyboard is only ever "open" while a text box is focused (`typingNow`), so no reading can hide the bars. Verified with Chrome's device emulation (screen size, `screen.orientation`, safe areas and the events, with WebKit's stale mid-turn heights reproduced), never assumed from the manifest; a real device's lock can't be emulated — why: the coach found the full-screen "Turn your phone upright" message (Nov 13 2026) interrupted people mid-workout, and wanted the app to stay portrait like Instagram wherever the platform allows and stay usable, with nothing lost, where it doesn't (iOS web can't be locked at all; and the Android app had no lock); then on Nov 20 2026 that turning the phone still broke the app everywhere, not just Crew: the first resize of a turn read the old height, so turned back upright the tab bar was gone (a stale 393pt read as a 400pt keyboard) and Crew's posts were half a screen until the app was closed, a sideways phone (852pt) got Tailwind's `sm` layouts (a tall popup centred off both ends of the screen with its ✕ and Next out of reach), and the member program builder's tab-bar rule never matched sideways.
- The phone's tab bar (`MobileBottomBar`, app-shell.tsx; every account) is TikTok's / Instagram's: edge to edge and flush with the bottom, opaque (`bg-background`, black over Crew's paged feed, `useFeedPaging`) with a hairline on top, no rounding, shadow, blur or fade under it; one row of equal tabs `--mobile-bar-h` (50px) tall over the home indicator's room (`env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, the full 34pt on Face ID iPhones), the row centred at most 560px wide. Each tab is a 24px icon, a little heavier on the tab you're on (`barIcon`), over a 10px label on one line (`barLabel`), the whole column the tap with the icon dipping on press; the tab you're on is the brand red (the design system's accent), with no underline, and counts are a red badge on the icon read out in its label. There is no raised centre button or satellite: Crew is a tab like the others (`featured` / `shortcut` are gone), and Rankings is Crew's third top tab (the Crew bar rule).
