The order (Nov 21 2026) is Crew first, far left, where every launch opens (launch-home.ts), then: clients Home · Workouts · Messages · Nutrition (`clientBottomNav`), members Home · Workouts · Support · Nutrition (`selfServeBottomNav`, Support in Messages' place), coaches and admins Dashboard · Clients · Messages · Tasks (`STAFF_BAR`; a staff bar saved before it starts over, `jf-floating-bar-v5`); the finance login keeps its home first, since that's where it opens (`FINANCE_BAR`). A tab says it's the current page only when it's lit (the bar's Link is `activeOptions` exact, since the router's own fuzzy match called every portal page Home) Its top edge from the bottom of the screen is `--mobile-bar-top` (`--mobile-bar-h` + the safe area): `--bottom-nav-clearance` is exactly it, so pages, the chats and Crew's posts end at it, and floating buttons stack from it — why: the coach found the floating, rounded bar with its raised Crew disc and trophy satellite didn't feel like TikTok or Instagram, sat in mid-air with the page showing under it, and the trophy standing 29px over the bar kept Crew's captions high. On Nov 21 2026 the coach asked for a social-first order, Instagram's and TikTok's: Crew, where the app opens, as the first tab on the far left instead of the middle, the same for clients and staff.
- The bottom bar's tabs behave like Instagram's: tapping the tab you're on goes back to the top (the page and any list under the screen's middle that scrolls on its own, `scrollPageToTop`), fires `nav-retap` for the page's own reset (Crew: out of a profile to the feed) and refreshes it; pulling down from the very top of a tab's own page refreshes too (`TabRefresh`, first in `<main>`; never inside a dialog, on a field, sideways, or inside something scrolled on its own). Switching tabs keeps your place, Instagram's way (tab-places.ts, `useTabPlaces` in AppShell): a tab's own page (the bar's `to`, no #view; on Crew only the feed on screen, `html.feed-paging`, never a profile opened over it) keeps where it was read to, and the bar bringing you back puts it there once the page is tall enough, within `TAB_PLACE_RESTORE_MS` of arriving, never under a moving finger, read from the page on screen (`resolvedLocation`), in memory for the visit only (`TAB_PLACE_TTL_MS` = RESUME_NEW_VISIT_MS), so a launch always opens at the top of Crew; Back is the router's own scroll restoration and a page under a tab starts at its top. A refresh reloads only the page's active queries, from a tap only those at least 10s old (`needsRefresh`, `RECENT_MS`; a pull: 2s), joining fetches already running (`cancelRefetch: false`), never while a mutation is saving (`isMutating`) or for a gesture something else claimed (`defaultPrevented`, e.g. a calendar drag), capped at 8s, with Instagram's tick spinner (`ActivitySpinner`) in room the page makes under its top bar — why: a second tap must never load the page twice, a refresh must never restart the page's own in-flight load or spin forever offline, and the spinner must never sit on top of the stories or posts; and on Nov 21 2026 the coach moved Crew to the far left as the app's first tab and asked that switching tabs keep each page's place, as Instagram and TikTok do (every tab tap started its page over at the top).
- Crew clips (posts and stories): the studio films with a held shutter (`useClipRecorder`, clip-recorder.ts; the mic is asked on the first hold, a tap is still a photo; up to a story's 60s, `RECORD_MAX_SECONDS`) or takes clips from the library. A clip starts uploading the moment it's picked and trimmed (the studio's tray, `useEarlyUpload` in the composer) while you edit and write, so Post only saves; swapping or re-trimming one aborts its upload. The poster comes from `captureVideoPoster` (at the trim's start), never a detached `<video>` + `onseeked` (iPhones never fire it, and every clip was refused). In the card feed (the finance login's look at the crew) a clip (`InlineVideo`) is never cropped: a clip cover gives its frame the clip's own shape (`videoAspect`: 9:16 to 16:9, unknown 4:5), as tall as the screen allows (`FEED_VIDEO_MAX_HEIGHT`), and every clip, a carousel's too, fits whole inside its frame (`fit="contain"`, black either side); on the clients' paged feed the screen is the clip's canvas instead (the paged-feed rule, `screenFit`); it plays with its sound and looping while ≥60% of it is on screen and pauses off it (and while the full-screen clips cover the feed, `holdFeedVideos`), with one speaker for the visit (`useFeedSoundMode`: "auto" by default, every open starts with sound; "off" from the speaker keeps every clip quiet; "on" once the speaker turns it back on); reduced motion or Save Data means tap to play. Sound a clip plays by itself ("auto") is marked `data-ambient-audio` and sits on the page's "ambient" audio (`preferAmbientAudio`; the media watcher never holds "playback" for it), so it follows the silent switch and plays over the person's music instead of stopping it (Instagram's feed); sound turned on with the speaker plays through the switch like any video. Feed clips borrow one of `FEED_POOL` reused video elements while within two screens and give it back further away (`acquireFeedVideo` / `releaseFeedVideo`, video-pool.ts), because an iPhone outside the installed app allows sound only on an element first played from a tap: every real tap anywhere (a click, a touch that didn't move, a key) lets every pooled element make sound from then on (`unlockVideoSound`), and a clip that had to start muted plays muted with its speaker saying "Tap for sound" (`soundBlocked`, `askTap`, `data-sound-held`) until that tap gives it its sound (never while the speaker is off, and a tap on its own speaker decides for itself); the tap that gives a held clip its sound does nothing else to it (`justGaveSound`: no pause on a post's screen, no mute on a post page; opening the full-screen clips still goes ahead), and a swipe's end tries too, in case the phone counts it, until the first time it says no (`swipeGivesSound`, asked once a visit, the held clip kept playing muted). The installed app (Capacitor) never asks, so there sound starts with the first clip. Its link is signed with the feed page (`usePostMediaUrls` takes every clip and a carousel's other thumbnails, `postMediaExtras`, one round trip), its header loads two screens away and the whole clip within a screen (`preload="auto"`), so it plays the moment it's on screen. In a carousel (a workout film) the slides either side of the one showing keep or borrow an element while their post is on screen (`InlineVideo warm`, from `PostCarousel`: a slide off to the side is clipped by its carousel, so its own box never reads as near; the carousel's track is observed instead), the next one loading ahead, so a swipe plays at once and a swipe back carries on where it was, nothing downloaded again; `FEED_POOL` is 8 for them. A borrowed element always sits in the box its clip is shown in now (`keepInHost`, video-pool.ts, which InlineVideo runs in a layout effect after every render, the decoded canvas moved with it): a clip has a box per fit (filling the screen, shown whole, framed) and its fit is decided again whenever the screen is measured (a layout before the box has a size, a post's sheet opening), so an element put in a box only when it's borrowed must never be left in one that's gone; the poster goes only once the clip really plays (a `playing` that lands after a tap's play-and-pause of every pooled element, `unlockVideoSound`, finds it paused and is ignored), and a clip that failed is tried again with a fresh link or when it comes back near the screen. A clip that should be playing keeps playing (`keepPlaying`, video-keep-playing.ts, pure and tested, in `InlineVideo` and `ReelsViewer`): a pause nobody here asked for (the app away and back, a call, an audio-session change, iOS pausing a clip unmuted without a tap) plays it again after a beat (`playFeedVideo`'s sound rules, at most `RESUME_MAX` in `RESUME_WINDOW_MS`), coming back to the app does the same, waiting on data shows a quiet spinner after `WAIT_SHOW_MS` (`data-video-buffering`), no new frame for `STALL_MS` reloads it where it was, and an error is reloaded once before it's failed; who wants it playing stays the caller's (`playingWanted`), so a clip held, scrubbed or off screen is never played by it. A clip the phone won't start by itself even muted (an iPhone in Low Power Mode refuses every video that isn't started from a tap) is marked refused (`playRefused`, `PLAY_REFUSED_EVENT`, video-pool.ts) and keeps playing anyway, decoded on the phone and drawn on a canvas in the element's place (`startDecodedClip`, decoded-clip.ts: mediabunny's `VideoSampleSink` on the phone's own WebCodecs decoder from the clip's signed link, `drawWithFit`, frames on time by `paceFrame`, a stall moving the clock rather than racing, its long side at most `DECODED_LONG_SIDE`; the canvas takes the element's class and object-fit so it shows exactly what the element would, `data-decoded-clip`, `data-inline-video="decoded"`), muted with "Tap for sound" and no scrub bar, paused by a hold and off screen like the element, looping; a decoder isn't a media element, so no autoplay rule stops it. The next tap anywhere starts the element inside that tap with its sound at the decoded clip's time (`setRefusedClock`, read by `startRefused` from `unlockVideoSound`, the tap doing nothing else to it, `justStartedPlay`; the decoded copy holds its frame from the element's `play` and goes at its `playing`), and the same tap's play-and-pause of every other pooled element lets the clips after it start by themselves; a clip the caller stops wanting (`pauseFeedVideo`) is never started or given its sound by a tap. Only where it can't play decoded (no decoder, a codec or link it can't read, no frame in `DECODED_FIRST_FRAME_MS`) does it show a play button over its frame (`data-video-refused`, also after `DECODED_SLOW_MS` with no frame yet, the full-screen clips always), never a frame that just sits there, and only then does one toast say why (`noteAutoplayRefused`, autoplay-notice.ts: "Videos need a tap right now", on an iPhone "Low Power Mode is usually why" and where to turn it off; from a feed clip or story clip that can't decode and the full-screen clips; only while the app is on screen, once a launch and not again on that phone for `NOTICE_GAP_MS`, a week, `jf-autoplay-notice`; sonner loaded only then), never a popup over the feed, since a page can't read Low Power Mode, only what it does. A story clip refused the same way plays decoded too, its frames running the story's bar (`until` its first minute, its end moving the story on), and its "Tap for sound" (z-40, above the reply bar's shade, which used to take that tap) hands it to its own element at the same time. Every Crew link is signed for a day and kept on the phone across launches (`crewUrlCache`, community-media.ts, `CREW_SIGN_SECONDS`, refreshed an hour early, the chat's `createSignedUrlCache` with `localSignedUrlStorage`), and so is every photo's (`avatarUrlCache`, user-avatar.tsx: the faces asked for in the same moment signed in one request, never one each); both are cleared on sign-out (`clearAllAppCaches`). Each feed read starts from the links already there (`cachedCommunityUrls` as its `initialData`), so a launch paints the feed with no signing round trip and the phone's cache serves what it downloaded last time. On the clients' paged feed (rule below) a clip is already full size and is its own speaker, Instagram's Reels: one tap turns the sound off or on after the double-tap window (`toggleFeedSound`) and says which with a speaker in the middle for a moment (`SoundFlash`, `jf-sound-flash`), two taps ❤️ and leave the sound as it was, and a finger held on it (`HOLD_TO_PAUSE_MS`, a finger that moves is a swipe) pauses it with the words, buttons and Crew's bar out of the way until it lets go (`useHoldToPause`, clip-gestures.tsx: one hook for the feed's screens, the coach's cards, a post's page and the full-screen clips; `CLIP_HOLD_EVENT`, `holding`, `html[data-clip-held]`; letting go is never a tap); there is no speaker button there, only "Tap for sound" while the phone holds a clip's sound back, and a clip the phone stops when a tap turns its sound back on plays again muted with it (`playingWanted`); on the card feed a tap on a clip opens the feed's clips full screen (`ReelsViewer`, reels-viewer.tsx, reels.ts: one per swipe up / down with snap, every clip in the feed in order, a workout film's sets one after another (`reelItems`), whole on black with sound, Reels' gestures (a tap is its sound, said in the middle, `SoundFlash`; a finger held down pauses it with everything else out of the way; two taps ❤️ where the finger was), ❤️ 💬 🔊 on the right, the author, the set's caption and the post's words at the bottom; only the clip on screen and the one either side hold a video, three pooled elements moved page to page (`reelVideo`, `reelSlot`, video-pool.ts), which the tap that opens them unlocks for sound (since a swipe isn't a tap); a refetch that moves nothing never touches its scroll). Posts below the first screenful have finished easing in a screen and a half early (`FeedItem`, the card feed only: the paged feed's screens never ease in, the rule below), painted cards start two screens ahead (`useNearScreen`), and a feed photo is never held back by lazy loading. In a story (`StoryClip`) it plays with sound (muted with "Tap for sound" when the phone won't), at most its first 60s (`STORY_CLIP_MAX_SECONDS`), and its length drives the progress bar; a clip that won't start falls back to the story clock on its poster — why: stories were a still on a 5s timer, iPhone clips were rejected before upload, and waiting until Post to upload made every clip feel slow; and on Nov 12 2026 the coach found a workout film's 9:16 Hanging Leg Raises clips cut to 4:5 in the feed and wanted clips to work like TikTok / Reels, whole on every swipe, with no waiting on any post as you scroll, and their sound on by default rather than muted; and on Nov 13 2026 he found clips muted while swiping (an iPhone's web view refuses a clip's sound until a tap, and a swipe isn't one) with only a small crossed-out speaker to say why, and a tap on the clip to get its sound paused it instead; and that day that a tap on a clip should be its sound, as on Instagram (it paused it), with the double tap still a like; and every launch (Crew is the screen it opens on) signed every Crew link and face afresh for an hour, in memory only and each face on its own request, so the phone downloaded the whole feed again and the first post's picture waited on a round trip before it could start; and on Nov 22 2026 the coach found the clips freezing: in a film post the next clip only started loading when it was swiped to and a swipe back loaded the last one from scratch (each sat on a still frame meanwhile), and a clip the phone paused by itself was never played again; then, the same night, that videos weren't playing at all: his iPhone was in Low Power Mode, which refused every clip, and the feed showed each one's first frame with nothing to say a tap would start it, and he asked for a heads-up that the phone's setting is what's affecting the app; then that, if possible, videos should just keep playing even in Low Power Mode (no web page can make an iPhone's video element start there without a tap, but decoding the clip itself isn't a video element); and on Nov 23 2026 that Jarrett's 9:16 deadlift sometimes showed black on its screen: its element had been put in the box of the fit decided before the screen was measured, the fit flipped to fill the screen, and the element kept playing in the box React had taken off the page, its poster already faded (reproduced in Chromium on the real feed, as was a 16:9 clip going black when its comments opened).
- Every Crew clip has a progress bar along its bottom that a finger scrubs, Instagram Reels' (Nov 20 2026, `VideoScrubber`, video-scrubber.tsx; video-scrub.ts pure and tested): the feed's screens (right on top of the tab bar), the coach's cards, a post's page and the full-screen clips (`ReelsViewer`, which replaces its old read-only line, its strip over the home indicator's safe area). It drives the clip element it's given (the feed's pooled one, `InlineVideo` `el`; the reels' slot), never a player of its own. What's drawn is one thin line, nearly the clip's width (inset 12px each side, `data-scrub-track`), 2px at rest (white at 80% over a 25% track), painted straight onto the bar a frame at a time while the clip plays (no React state per frame); there is no handle, no dot and no bubble. It's bigger for a finger than it looks (Nov 20 2026, the second pass): a sideways drag that starts anywhere in the clip's bottom `SCRUB_BAND_PX` (44px, `inScrubBand`) scrubs, even over the post's words, which keep their taps (fingers are read as capture-phase touch events on the band's root, `[data-scrub-band]`: a post's screen, a full-screen clip), and a tap on the strip right at the bar (`SCRUB_TAP_PX`, 24px, the element itself) jumps there; a clip post's words and buttons sit a lane above that strip (`--feed-scrub-lane` 12px on `[data-feed-page-post][data-has-clip]`, counted in `--feed-words-room`), so nothing covers it. A finger resting on the strip wakes it within `WAKE_MS` (4px, no time yet); scrubbing it is 6px and full white, and above the finger rides a small preview of the frame (`data-scrub-preview`, at the clip's own shape, its long side 128px, less on a short card) with the time under it in plain words ("0:12 / 0:45", `clipClock`, `data-scrub-time`), both kept clear of the clip's sides (`data-scrub-float`), Reels'; the preview is the clip's own frame copied onto a canvas as each seek lands (`drawImage`, a frame at most per animation frame), never a second video, so it costs no decoding and can't lag the clip; back to a line `LINGER_MS` after release; a mouse hovering thickens it too. Sideways first scrubs (`scrubIntent`: 4px sideways before 8px up or down), up or down first is the feed's swipe (`touch-action: pan-y`, so the browser keeps it), a still tap on the strip jumps (`isBarTap`), a long still press that lets go does nothing; once it's in hand nothing under the finger scrolls or swipes (`touchmove` prevented and stopped in capture: no feed page, no carousel), and a finger in the band is never the clip's press-and-hold pause (`inClipScrubBand` in `useHoldToPause`'s start). The drag maps onto the line as drawn (`ratioAt` over the track's own rect), absolute, never relative to where it went down. While a finger drags it the clip holds still (`onHold` → `scrubbing` in `shouldPlay`) and follows it, a fast seek at a time where the browser has one (`makeScrubSeeker`: one in flight, the newest next), then the exact time on release (`seekTarget`: inside the clip, a hair before its end since clips loop, inside `seekable`, held on the line until the clip is there, so it never flicks back), and it plays on only if it was playing; a mouse drags or clicks it at once, and ←/→ move 5s. A carousel slide that isn't on screen takes no fingers (`enabled`). Its events never reach the post (no ❤️, no sound, no hold-to-pause, no carousel, no full-screen clips), and while it's in hand and `LINGER_MS` after, the post's words, buttons and Crew's bar step aside like a held clip (`CLIP_SCRUB_EVENT` → PostCard's `holding`; the reels' `scrubShown`); waking alone moves nothing. A clip whose length can't be known yet (a recording with no duration in its header reads Infinity until it's all read) has no bar until it is (`clipLength`: its duration, else the end of its seekable range). A paused clip, held or scrubbed, shows the frame it's on, never its cover again (`shown`, set on `playing` or a `seeked` with a frame). A tap on the clip itself stays its sound and a hold its pause (the coach's call, Nov 20 2026) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach asked for Reels-style scrubbing in Crew: a subtle bar that's easy to grab, drag anywhere without leaving the feed, the time while dragging, play or pause kept, and never a like, swipe or post change by accident; the full-screen clips had a line you couldn't touch, and a clip held to pause flashed back to its cover; then, the same day, that the first version was hard to grab (only the bottom 12px of its zone took a finger on a post's screen, the words covered the rest) and its big white handle and time bubble looked cluttered, where Reels is a thin line you can grab from anywhere near it, with a small frame preview and the time riding above the finger while it's dragged (the coach's Reels screenshot).
- The clients' Crew feed is one post per screen, swiped one at a time (TikTok's; the coach's Crew page too since Nov 14 2026, and only the finance login's look at the crew, `hideTabs`, keeps a feed of cards): `CommunityScreen` pages it while `ownBar && scope.kind === "feed"` (`paging`, `FeedPager`), with `html.feed-paging` making the window snap (`scroll-snap-type: y mandatory`, each `.feed-page` `scroll-snap-align: start` + `scroll-snap-stop: always`, so a hard fling still stops at the next post) and every box between the window and the posts (body, `[data-shell-root]`, `<main>`) clipping sideways instead of hiding (a hidden overflow makes a box a scroll container that keeps the snap points from the window). A screen is the whole screen under the status bar (`--feed-page-top` = `--app-chrome-top` + `--app-safe-top` − `--app-edge-room`: on the iPhone home-screen app a post runs up through the band the bars leave for iOS's blur, where a picture can blur and nothing needs to be sharp, while Crew's icons stay below it, `--feed-bar-room` = 3rem + that band; 0 on a computer, where the bar sits in the page and scrolls away), sized from the window's measured height (`--feed-vh`, `watchFeedViewport` in feed-paging.ts, read again only when the width changes, never from a toolbar or the keyboard; never `100svh`, which came out ~75pt short on the iPhone home-screen app), with a post's bottom, words and buttons moved down by however far the screen runs past that (`--feed-gap`, `feedGap`: a fixed box from top to bottom against the window, since on the iPhone home-screen app the window reads the notch's height short while the tab bar sits at the screen's real bottom; read again as the launch settles, `FEED_GAP_SETTLE_MS`, on coming back to the app, on any resize and on the first touch, never while typing, since iOS can report the screen at the window's height for a moment and one reading used to stick for the whole visit; the pages keep the window's height so every swipe lands on the next post), and every post fills it down to the tab bar, TikTok's way: Crew's bar floats over its top (`CrewBar overlay`: see-through on a phone with a soft shade under its icons and the notch solid black, `.crew-bar[data-overlay]`, never a backdrop blur) and the post ends at the tab bar (`--feed-nav-room`, black over the feed), so on today's iPhones a post's screen is about 9:16; what covers a screen is `--feed-bar-room` / `--feed-nav-room`, and anything that sits at a post's top (the set's caption, "2/4", the speaker) moves down under the bar. The paged feed is always dark, whatever the theme (TikTok's, Reels'): the pager is `dark bg-black`, Crew's bar and the tab bar (`useFeedPaging` in `MobileBottomBar`) are dark over it, and the page, the shell and the status bar strip are black (`html.feed-paging`, phones only). The stories (`.feed-first`) sit on top of the first post, TikTok's Friends: the first post is a full screen like every other, running off the bottom with its top corners rounded (`pageFirst`) and its words and buttons hidden until it's up whole (`under`, `belowSnap`: against where it snaps, its `.feed-page` scroll margin), its picture sitting right under the stories rather than a bar's height lower so a picture shown whole shows whole above the tab bar, easing to its place as it comes up (`--first-under` / `data-first-under`, never on a clip that fills its screen), and they fold away as it comes up and back in on the way down, tied to the scroll (`useStoriesMotion`: the row sinks at 40% of the page's speed so the post rises over it, each bubble shrinks up toward the bar and fades, right to left so they come back left to right, `bubbleFold`, all gone two thirds of the way up, and the first post's rounded top squares off; measured from where it's laid out, never a post still easing in, `firstPostUp`; reduced motion keeps the fade), the very top is the stories' own snap point (a pull there still refreshes), one swipe up from them (`useStoriesSwipe`: on the stories and the first post only, while the stories show; a pull down, a sideways swipe across the stories and a tap stay theirs) or a tap on the post brings it up whole, never past it onto the second (the stories are shorter than a swipe, so the window's own snapping overshot), and swiping back down from the first post brings the stories back; only an empty feed fills the screen with the stories (its height set from where it starts). Every post is `PostCard layout="page"`: a picture, a clip or an automated post's card on black whatever the theme (`dark bg-black`), and the screen is the canvas, TikTok's / Reels' way (`PostMedia page`: each slide decides for itself, `screenFit` in photo-frame.ts, against the screen's shape measured as it changes, `useBoxRatio`): a clip close to the screen's shape fills it, up under Crew's bar, centred, trimmed evenly off whichever sides stick out (a phone's 9:16 clip loses ~18% of its sides on a tall phone, as on TikTok), never more than `SCREEN_FILL_MAX_TRIM` (a quarter) of it, else it shows whole; anything wider (a 16:9, square or 4:5 clip, every photo but one framed 9:16, which fills it, and a clip framed in the studio the same, since what was framed is never cut by more than a sliver, a clip of unknown shape) shows whole across the full width with black above and below (a photo over a soft, dark copy of itself instead, `AmbientFill`, Nov 23 2026), centred between Crew's bar and the top of the post's words (`BETWEEN_BARS`: `--feed-words-room` from `--page-words-h`, the words' own height measured by PostCard, never where they sit, which `--feed-gap` settling moves with nothing resizing; 8px clear of them), so the name and caption never cover it, TikTok's; an automated post's 4:5 card the same (`FitBox ratio={0.8} page`), a little smaller when the words leave less room; a carousel says where you are with "2/4" up top (no dots on a screen), and a post whose sheet is open shrinks and shows everything whole; the name, the words (a short preview that opens in place) and a workout's line low on the screen, just above the tab bar (`--feed-words-bottom` = `--mobile-bar-top` + 12px), over a shade only where a clip fills the screen under them (`:has([data-fit="cover"])`, styles.css; under a picture shown whole they sit on black), ❤️ 💬 🔁 ✈️ 🔖 ⋯ down the right right down to the bar (`--feed-actions-bottom`, `ReactionBar column`, counts under the icons); words, a workout or a lock in are centred clear of both bars and scroll inside their screen when long. A clip's "Tap for sound" sits at the top on a screen (`controls="top"`); a film's set is said in the post's words, never over the clip (`data-page-set`, the View workout rule). A post's words on its screen are Reels' preview (`PageCaption`, page-caption.tsx: two lines and "… more", each paragraph on its own line and stopping where a thought does, the caption-readability rule below; never the name again, it's right above; never our own film caption under the workout's line, `isOwnFilmCaption`, nor the film's own summary of its sets, `isFilmSummary`; the views and the coach's ❤️ / reply share one line, `data-page-meta`, since every line under a post is room its picture loses), and a tap on them opens the post's sheet (`PostSheet`, post-sheet.tsx) at the caption, 💬 at the comments, laid out like Instagram's and TikTok's comments (Nov 14 2026): the grip, "Comments" and ✕; the post's own words as the first line, written like a comment (photo, name, when, the whole caption; an automated post's with its poll and the Recap's "work on this week", `AutoPostCaption named={false}`; none when it has no words; no Follow, the post's screen has it), then the comments in the same scroll (`CommentThread` `header` / `startAtComments`, `data-sheet-scroll`): a 36px photo (28 for a reply), the name and when, the words, Reply under them, the heart with its count under it on the right, replies indented under the words behind "View N more replies", "No comments yet / Start the conversation." when there are none; and pinned under them Instagram's quick emoji (`QUICK_COMMENT_EMOJI`, a tap puts it in at the cursor and never sends it, `insertAtCursor`, comment-composer.ts) over your photo and one rounded box (`data-comment-box`: the words, then a photo, a GIF and the mic inside it, `MicButton quiet`, the send arrow in the mic's place once there's something to send), filling what's above the keyboard while typing (`useVisualViewportBox`); the coach's card feed's `CommentsSheet` has the same header and thread. It takes the bottom `POST_SHEET_HEIGHT` (62svh) and the post shrinks into the space above it, up to Crew's bar (`belowBar`; over the story rail on the first screen, `data-crew-bar`), nothing floating over it (`[data-sheet-open]` zeroes the bar and tab bar room), still playing, its words and buttons faded out (PostCard `sheetOpen`), and there it's Instagram's: a rounded card at the post's own shape (`postMediaRatio`, an automated post's 4:5) with room around it, centred above the sheet, the speaker in its bottom-right corner (`controls="corner"`), "2/4" in its top corner and no set caption over it (`FitBox screen shrunk`: only the boxes change, never what's in them, so a playing clip carries on through opening and closing). One swipe down closes it (`sheetDragCloses`, lib/post-sheet.ts: a quarter of its height, or a flick), from the grip, the caption, or the comments once they're at the top, never from the comment box; a tap on the post above it or Escape does too, and it never opens the keyboard by itself. The coach's card feed keeps `CommentsSheet`. The feed's own moments (all caught up, people to follow after the first post, an invite to share, more loading, the end) are screens of their own only while they have something to show (`.feed-page:empty` takes none) — why: the coach wanted Crew to work like TikTok / Reels, a full-size post on every swipe, one at a time; and on Nov 13 2026 a 9:16 clip sat in a narrow box with black down both sides (fitted whole into the screen less Crew's bar and the tab bar), where TikTok fills the screen with it and puts black above and below only what's wider; and that day the words weren't set up for reading on a screen (an Athlete Insight's whole caption covered its picture, and a film post repeated the name before its words), and he wanted Reels' way: a short preview, the full words with the comments under them as you scroll, and one swipe to close; and the first post was squeezed into the room under the stories (the tightest fit on the feed), where TikTok's Friends puts the stories over a full-size first post that a swipe or a tap brings up; and then that the posts still didn't fill the screen like TikTok's (an opaque bar on top and, from `100svh` running short, every post stopping ~170pt above the bottom with black under it), the caption and buttons needed to sit a lot lower, as TikTok's do, and the stories needed an animation as they disappear and reappear; and then that every post stopped 59pt above the tab bar with black under it (his iPhone's home-screen app read the window as 793pt on an 852pt screen), and on Nov 14 2026 that it was back (61pt short: the one reading at the launch had caught the screen at the window's height); and then that the caption covered the bottom of a picture or card that didn't fill the screen (the Wins card's numbers, a workout's stats), where TikTok puts it above the words and lets only a full-screen clip run under them, and then that a 4:5 card or picture still sat small and high with black under it (the room above the words was worked out from where the words sat at launch, 60pt higher than once the screen reading settled) and, under the stories, a bar's height lower than them with its bottom under the tab bar; and that Fionna's film post (a 4:5 card and 3:4 clips) still showed narrow, under five lines of words (her name, the workout, "Glutes + Legs · 3 sets on film" saying the workout again, the coach's ❤️ and the views); and on Nov 13 2026 that the post above an open sheet had no shape (the clip filled the room edge to edge with the set's caption, "1/4" and the speaker strung across its top), where Instagram shows a rounded card at the post's own shape; and on Nov 14 2026 that the comments read like the opposite of Instagram's and TikTok's (no title, the poster's name with Follow and the caption first, "N likes" beside Reply, a big red mic beside a bare box with the photo and GIF buttons outside it).
- A post's screen is never shown half painted (Nov 15 2026): the paged feed's screens are plain `.feed-page` boxes that are simply there when swiped to (`FeedPager`, community-screen.tsx), never `FeedItem`'s fade and rise or any other opacity / transform / `will-change` on a whole screen (the coach's card feed keeps `FeedItem`), and the first post's moving layer (`[data-first-under]`'s 3D transform) comes off once it's within `FIRST_UNDER_MIN` of up (feed-paging.ts), since a snap lands a fraction of a pixel short. An automated post appears all at once (Nov 20 2026): its card is read back from the phone's store as soon as its post is on the page (`usePostArt`, a read, nothing drawn; the font and logo a paint needs fetched as soon as one is missing), decoded before it's put on the page (`decodePostArt` / `useDecodedArt`: one load in the document makes the `<img>` complete at once, `decoding="sync"`, no fade) and shows nothing until then (black on a post's screen, `bg-muted` elsewhere, never blank paper whose words fill in), and on a post's screen its words and buttons wait for the card it opens on (`AutoPostHero page` → `PostArtImage page hold`, `data-pending`, `.feed-page:has([data-post-art][data-pending])` hides `[data-page-words]` / `[data-page-actions]` by visibility, never opacity, at most `ART_HOLD_MS` on screen), so the whole post lands in one frame (measured with the real feed: 226 ms from mount for a card painted fresh, 163 ms for one kept on the phone, no frame of paper or of words without their card); the coach's 'blank white before the font appears' was the paper placeholder, then the card read back from the store only once on screen, then a 280 ms fade. Work that holds the main thread waits for a still screen: the feed's painted cards (`usePostArt` → `loadPostArt(…, { whenStill: true, rank })`, post-art.ts) start only with no finger down and `STILL_MS` (250ms) since the last scroll or touch (`stillScreen`, `whenStill`), the card nearest the screen first (`nextPaint`, `rank` = its distance from the screen), and pause again between drawing and encoding (`paintCardFile`'s `pause`); a tap's ask (Save image, Share to Instagram) never waits and takes over a paint the feed queued. New per-screen effects, or anything heavy that runs while the feed is up, follow the same rule — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found a lesson's card showing its top half over black, cut off mid-line with no name, words or buttons under it: each screen eased in, which made it a layer of its own that an iPhone paints a 512px strip at a time (the cut was exactly two strips, 1024px, below the screen's top), and the phone was busy drawing and JPEG-encoding the next cards while the swipe landed, so the rest was never painted until it was free.
- Videos everywhere (Crew posts / stories / comments, chats, progress and check-in videos, broadcasts) go through one engine (`src/lib/video-edit/`): any recording up to 20 minutes can be picked (`SOURCE_MAX_SECONDS`), and only the part published leaves the phone. Published limits per place live in `limits.ts` (story 60s, comment 60s, workout post 90s, post 180s, member 1:1 / crew chat 300s, coach chats / coach-run groups / progress / check-in / broadcast 600s, demo 180s) and must match `20261210090000_video_limits.sql`. `pickVideo` reads the header and opens the trimmer only when a clip is over its limit (or what a phone without an encoder can send), or when the place offers a quick look (`TrimOffer`: a set video); `useVideoTrimmer().element` is rendered inside the caller's own dialog (a nested Radix dialog), never a second React root (the studio would close and lose focus). `planVideo` (pure, tested; a clip posted with no sound never goes as the original, the rule below) picks original / copy (a remux: MP4 / MOV starts on the exact frame through an edit list; WebM can't, so a no-encoder WebM cut snaps its start back to a keyframe in the trimmer) / transcode (720p H.264 sized to the place's byte budget, a copy fallback only when the encoder is clearly slower than the upload it saves, `encodeTooSlow`; that fallback is offered for anything the server takes, a whole clip up to the place's hard limit and a cut estimated within `AS_IS_MAX_SHARE` of it, never only up to the target: a 143.6 MB Belt Squat, 3.6 MB over the coach chat's 140 MB target, once sat 6.5 minutes encoding on the phone, from the start each time the app was left); there is no original-vs-copy upload race (it sent footage that was then thrown away). Uploads are resumable (`resume: true`: TUS over 6 MB, fingerprinted by path, so a retry or a dropped connection carries on), each send (`createVideoSend`) keeps its finished steps for retries, and the server re-reads every upload (`verifyVideoUploadFn` → `media_video_checks`, owner-only, header only); the `*_video_guard` triggers refuse a video that wasn't checked, was replaced after its check, or is too long / big for its table (stories by `story_only`, workout posts by `completion_id`, chats by `chat_kind`). Files uploaded before `media_video_checks_since()` are grandfathered. The trimmer never crops (the preview is `object-contain`; framing is the feed's `media_focus`, not the file) — why: athletes film whole sessions and want one lift, phones shouldn't send gigabytes they'll discard, real iPhones re-encode at ~1-4x real time, and a phone's own numbers can't be trusted.
- Finding the lift (`lift.ts` / `lift-window.ts`, video-edit; rebuilt Nov 21 2026): a picked clip's lift is found on the phone, never by a server or a model: tiny greyscale frames (64px on the long side), about one a second, read the cheapest way the clip allows (`liftSamplePlan`): keyframes about a second apart (a phone's own camera) one decode each (`keys`); keyframes up to `KEYS_USABLE` (4s) apart (a clip filmed in the app: a browser's recorder puts one every few seconds) the keyframes first, then the gaps filled in while there's time (`keys+fill`, a gap over `FILL_OVER` 1.6s gets frames in between); hardly any keyframes, a frame a second decoded straight through on a clip of `GRID_MAX_SECONDS` (120s) or less (`grid`); 240 frames at most. It has `LIFT_BUDGET_MS` (12s): out of time in the first pass is no suggestion (`slow`), in the fill-in what's read is read. The in-app cameras ask for a keyframe a second (`recorderKeyframes`, film-settings.ts, in `FilmCamera` and the Crew studio's recorder; a browser that doesn't take the setting ignores it). The frames are read by `readLift` (lift-window.ts, pure, tested; `liftWindow` is its window): movement between frames measured against the picture's own shift for its dark and bright halves apart (the phone re-exposing is not movement); the walks read from the picture itself (`findWalks`: the clip opening or closing on a picture that isn't its usual one, `awayFromUsual`, run on while a lot keeps moving; only at the clip's ends, so someone crossing mid-set is no walk to the phone); the lifter's own pixels (`liftSpot`: movement that keeps coming back and moves together with where the walk ended, so someone waiting beside the bar or lifting at the next station isn't part of it); each moment lift / still / off the bar / phone (`classifyMoments`: a lot moving away while they lift, the platform shaking as the bar lands or someone crossing close, stays lifting but is never what the set is measured by, `spill`); the set is the bout with the most lifting joined to bouts as strong a pause away (`LIFT_GAP_MAX` 3s splits, `LIFT_PAUSE_MAX` 10s joins, never across a walk), held to `LIFT_LEAD_MAX` / `LIFT_TAIL_MAX` (3.5s) around its first and last hard movements so a wait at the bar isn't kept, padded `LIFT_PAD_BEFORE` 0.5s and `LIFT_PAD_AFTER` 0.8s. Every doubt is settled toward keeping more: the worst outcome is a cut into the reps. No suggestion, with why (`LiftRead.why`): a clip held in a hand (`handheld`), frames over `FRAME_GAP_MAX` (2.2s) apart across a quarter of the clip (`sparse`), nothing clear (`no-movement`, `no-lift`, `unclear`), under `LIFT_MIN` 2.5s (`short`), or a cut too small to matter (`nothing-to-cut`). What it did is kept by the picked file (`findLiftRead` → `noteLift`, lift-note.ts, which never loads the video engine): the trimmer says "Couldn't pick out the lift in this one. Drag the ends to trim it yourself." when it looked and couldn't (`liftNotFound`; never for nothing-to-cut), and a set video's upload carries one line (`pl_set_videos.transfer.lift`, `liftTransferNote`: "found keys+fill key2 n52 3140ms 3.1-14.2 kept" / "slow grid key- n20 12004ms": why, how it read the clip, keyframe spacing, frames, time, the window, and whether that's what went), so a report that it missed is read back from the record. It is tuned and tested on simulated sets (src/test/helpers/lift-scenes.ts, seeded: lifts, cameras, people crossing, standing around and lifting nearby, dark clothes, shake, handheld; video-lift-scenes.test.ts holds the rates), since the app keeps only the trimmed clip, never the source: change a constant only with those numbers. It is only ever a suggestion, shown before anything goes: a set video opens a quick look (`TrimOffer` on `pickVideo`: the trimmer titled "Exercise · Set N", Done reads Send) on the whole clip with "Finding the lift…" and glides there once it's found (`LIFT_GLIDE_MS`, instant under reduced motion) unless a handle was already moved, "Trimmed to the lift" with Whole clip one tap back; a clip opened again (`retrimVideo`) only offers it (Trim to the lift); a Crew post's clip is searched as it's added (`findLiftFor`, slide-tray.tsx) and offered as one chip on its slide (`data-studio-trim-lift` "Trim to the lift · 0:25", then "Trimmed to the lift" · Whole clip, `trimToLift` / `undoLift`; a hand trim afterwards clears `liftBefore`). A part chosen without the trimmer goes through `fixedTrim` (pick.ts: the place's limit, what this phone can send, the keyframe snap), so what goes is what the trimmer would send. Nothing waits on it — why: athletes prop the phone, press record and walk to the bar, so a set clip is a walk, a wait and a walk back around 20 seconds of lifting; on Nov 13 2026 the coach asked for a quick, smooth trim that suggests cutting to the start and end of the lift, and a wrong cut would send the coach the wrong footage, so it's shown before it goes and one tap undoes it; and on Nov 21 2026 he found it doesn't always work: his 35s Competition Deadlift filmed in the app got no suggestion at all and he trimmed it by hand (that day's three in-app clips, 33 to 35s, were all cut by hand to 10 to 12s). The first reader decoded a clip whose keyframes were over 1.6s apart frame by frame inside 6s and gave up silently, and on the simulated sets its read was right for 20%, cut into the reps for 16% (it took the walk to and from the lens for lifting) and found nothing for 27%; the rebuilt one: 70% right, 2% cut, 1% nothing at a frame a second, with a clip held in a hand refused. It has not been measured on real footage (none is kept), which is what the upload's record is for.
- A clip can be posted with no sound, Instagram's way: a speaker beside Trim on the clip in the Crew studio (posts and stories, `data-studio-sound`) and in the workout composer (`data-composer-sound`), shown only on a clip with a sound track and never on one already on the post; one tap says it in the middle (`useSoundFlash`) and marks the clip in the strip (`data-tray-muted`). The sound comes out of the file itself, never a flag on the post, so it's silent everywhere it plays or goes (the feed, a story, saved, Share to Instagram): `PickedVideo.muted` → `planVideo(…, { muted })`, where a clip with sound never goes as the untouched original (a copy without the audio track, a remux, ~instant; a re-encode drops it and spends its bits on the picture) and `renderVideo` discards the track (`audio: { discard: true }`, the "never lose the sound" check skipped, a slow re-encode's fallback never the original). Toggling swaps only the upload: `withSound` makes the same clip as a new object (its own upload) sharing the preview, poster, trim and framing, which the tray (`setSound`) and the composer drop without releasing, so nothing on screen reloads; turning sound back on plays the studio's stage with sound from that tap (`TrimmedVideo sound`, muted again if the phone refuses). In the feed a clip with no sound track (`clipIsSilent`, video-silent.ts: Safari's `audioTracks`, Firefox's `mozHasAudio`, Chrome only from sound it decoded while unmuted, never a guess) shows no speaker and no "Tap for sound" (`data-silent`), and a tap on its screen says no sound without changing the feed's sound — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach wanted a quick, smooth way to post a clip with no audio (gym music, people talking), and a muted flag on the post would still let the sound out wherever the file goes.
- The workout logger has two views of the same cards: the list (default) and Focus (stored and coded as `story`: one exercise per screen, the card at its own height right under the top bar like the list, no gap inside it; the room under it is swipe space; `workout-story.tsx`). Moving between exercises is a swipe up or left (next) / down or right (back) on a touch screen, ‹ / Next, the bars or arrow keys; a tap never moves (in a logger, a tap that misses a set box by a hair must not throw you to another exercise). A swipe (touch events) is a long enough drag (`SWIPE_COMMIT_PX`) or a quick flick, up / down when it's steep enough (`SWIPE_VERTICAL_RATIO`, a thumb's arc included) and sideways otherwise, one rule for Focus and the card under the finger (`focusAxis`, `pagerGesture`: every swipe is exactly one of them), started anywhere on the card's screen or on Focus's own bars (the thumb rests on the bottom one); the card follows the finger and the next one comes in from that side (`onJump(i, { instant: true })`, no glide along the row). On a card that can be deleted a swipe left is the card's own: on release it asks Focus (`SWIPE_PAGE_EVENT`, in the list Focus marks `data-swipe-pager`), a quick one moves on (`focusSideDirection`) and only a slow drag reveals Delete, which stays hidden while the finger moves fast enough to move on (`revealHidden`); everywhere else (the space under the card, the bars) any long enough sideways swipe moves, and the list view's swipe to delete is unchanged. A card with more to show that way scrolls to its end first and what the finger travels past the end is the swipe (`pastEdge`, `pastEdgeDirection`): a card that nearly fits (`NEARLY_FITS_PX`) moves on with one swipe or flick, a long one only on a deliberate pull past its end, so reading it never skips it; never with the keyboard up, inside a box that scrolls on its own or over an open menu, and the click its release would land on the new card is swallowed. The row of screens is `overflow: hidden` and only moves when told to. Quick swipes queue (each moves on from where the last was heading); at either end the card holds, a chevron nudges (`focus-end-nudge`) and the bottom button pulses. Which exercise is on screen is tracked by the bars themselves (`StoryChrome`; the logger keeps only a ref), so moving never re-renders the logger. A finished exercise's bar is green (never the brand red, which reads as "not done"), and the one you're on is the only bar at full strength and taller (the other finished ones are a softer green); the moment one finishes while Focus is open its bar fills green once and "✓ Done" pops by "Exercise N of M" (`jf-bar-fill`, `jf-done-pop`, played once, still under reduced motion). Focus starts under the phone's notch (`--focus-top`); while it's open the View as client banner steps aside (`data-focus-open` on `<html>` hides `[data-pov-banner]`; the red `PovFrame` stays), so a coach previewing gets a client's room. Its top bar is the bars and one row under them: the way out where the list has it, top left: ← (`focus-leave`, back to Workouts) leaves the workout exactly like the list page's back button (`usePageBack`: in-app history, else `backTo`), after committing a number being typed, and Focus stays the saved view; "Exercise N of M" is whole on any phone with Done (green, when the exercise is done), the clock and the workout under it, then ? and List (right), which is only the switch to the list. "How Focus works" (`FocusTips`, a finger swiping up) opens by itself once per account the first time in Focus (`workout_view_prefs.focus_tips_at`, `workout_view_tips_seen` records the latest time; a stamp before `FOCUS_TIPS_SINCE`, an older version, counts as unseen) and from the ? in its top bar. Story view is the list's own container with its classes swapped (`.workout-story`), never a second render of the cards; switching animates in a view transition (`runStoryTransition`, the card glides) or, without one, a solid stage. The choice is saved per login in `workout_view_prefs` (own table, not `user_preferences`; `useWorkoutView` paints from a per-phone copy and pushes an unsaved switch on the next open), and opens story view once per workout before the first paint, never on a finished workout or a `focus` / `review` / `recap` / `edit` link. The last exercise's button is Finish Workout (or Review to Finish) while there's something to finish, else "Exit to Workouts" (`story-leave`: the same way out as the ←, `back.onBack`; the list is only the top bar's List), and every button in the top row is a 40px tap. Inside a workout (`topFirstPage`) the Workouts tab's first tap goes back to the top and the next (or a quick double tap) to Workouts — why: a second copy remounted every card (typed values, undo) and stacked two review sheets, a long workout should be one tap from its top and two from the list, with the bottom bar hidden the only way out of Focus was switching to the list, taps on empty space threw people to another exercise when they missed a set box, swipes that started on the bottom bar, arced, or met a card a few pixels too tall needed a second try, a full-height card left a confusing gap between its top and its sets, the coach's View as client banner covered Focus's top bar (and then the room kept for it left a blank band over Focus), a third row in the top bar cost the card room, the last screen of a finished workout offered only "Back to the list" (the top bar's List again) and no way out of the workout, red bars for finished exercises didn't read as done and, once they were green, a slightly thicker bar didn't show which one you're on, the coach wanted left / right as well as up / down (Delete is rare, moving on is every exercise, so a quick swipe always moves and only a deliberate slow drag deletes).
- Save and Repost are Instagram's (20261207090000_crew_saves_reposts.sql, `community-saves.ts`, `repost-save.tsx`, `saved-view.tsx`): the row is ♡ 💬 🔁 ✈️ … 🔖 with who reacted on its own line under it. Save is private to the person (their main account): Saved is "All posts" plus their own albums on their profile's Saved tab, an album only ever holds saved posts (`community_save_album_posts` → `community_saves` FK cascade, so unsaving takes a post out of every album, and `community_save(false)` returns `removed_from` for Undo), and putting a post in an album saves it. Repost (`community_repost`) works on someone else's crew-wide (`'community'`) post and on your own posts and collabs you're on that you shared with the crew (`'community'`, or a private account's `'followers'`; 20270311090000_repost_your_own_posts.sql), never a story, an automatic workout story or a Close Friends / coach-only / private post (`community_repost_refusal`, which sets `can_repost`); your own repost brings it back up in the feed like anyone's, tells nobody, and never counts in its Activity's 🔁; its note is up to 6 lines and 60 characters, a line break counting as one (Return starts a line, Instagram's; `cleanRepostNote` = `community_repost_note_clean`, 20261211100000_repost_note_lines.sql, the same test cases run on both; `fitRepostNoteDraft` while typing), sits in the picture's bottom-left corner as Instagram's stamp (`RepostStamp`: the reposter's photo with a green 🔁 badge, the note's lines in a thought bubble above it; on a post that's words it sits above the words, bubble beside the photo, never over them). Yours shows on every post you reposted, else the first other reposter's who wrote one (`floatingNote`, tap: their profile); "Add a note…" floats for 8s right after reposting. Tapping yours opens `RepostNoteEditor` (repost-note-editor.tsx), never a bottom sheet: full screen, the post dimmed behind your photo, the bubble is the text box (its hidden copy sizes it, so the box's font is set inline: the phone-wide 16px text-box rule would wrap it differently), focused in the tap that opened it, quick emoji go in at the caret, emoji + Save sit on the keyboard, the whole screen is pinned to the visible part above it (`useVisualViewportBox`), "Remove" takes the repost back with Undo, and leaving with changes (back, Escape) asks "Discard note?" (Discard / Keep editing). It is never on the lockscreen. In the feed a post moves up to its latest repost the viewer may see (`feed_at`, which the feed pages by in `nextFeedCursor`; one card per post, "Vicky reposted" over it): a private account's reposts only count for its followers, a block hides them both ways, and a profile's own grid stays in posting order. Following includes what the people you follow reposted (`inFollowingFeed`); the author hears about it like a like (`community_notifications` kind `repost`, grouped per post, 🔁 in the Activity bubble), and reposting never reloads the feed under you. Every table is closed to clients; the RPCs are the only way in, and `community_post_json` gains its fields as a second `jsonb_build_object` (the first sits at Postgres's 100-argument limit) — why: saves must never leak or be seen by anyone else, a repost must not copy a post or reach people the original wasn't for, and Undo must put things back exactly; and on Nov 13 2026 the coach found no 🔁 under his own posts ("people who post should be able to repost their own content too").
- Add to your story and Activity are Instagram's (20261212090000_story_shares_activity.sql): a post's ✈️ opens Share (`PostShareSheet`, the share-sheet rule below), and a story moment that's a crew-wide post has ✈️ beside the heart; Add to story opens the story camera (`openStoryShare` → `StoryReplyHost`, mounted on the Crew screen only, never in View as client, so it's offered only while `useStoryShareReady()`) with the post drawn as a card (`postShareCardItem`: author, picture at the feed's shape or its words, caption; never a program name) on a backdrop, and the story keeps `shared_post_id` only while that card is on it. Only your own post or someone's `'community'` post can be shared, never a story or an automatic workout story (`canShareToStory` = `community_share_refusal`, checked again in `community_create_post`, which is the only version: the 4-argument one is dropped); View post on the story opens the shared post through `storyTarget` (`community_shared_post_json`: null when the viewer can't see it or a block hides it). Your story's viewers are Instagram's (`StoryViewersPanel`): Seen by, the hearts or a swipe up shrink the story to the top (`renderPreview`, the real `StoryFrame` scaled) with your other moments beside it (tap one to switch, tap the one you're on to go back), the 👁 and ❤️ counts, then everyone who saw it by name, whoever liked, shared or reposted first, a ❤️ / reaction / ✈️ / 🔁 badge on their photo and no Follow button; a story made in the camera (`story`, not `auto`) can be deleted from its bin. Your own post's views row opens `PostActivitySheet`: icon tabs with counts (👁 views, ❤️ likes, ✈️ shares to stories, 🔁 reposts) over the same rows, Follow only for people you don't follow. Both read `community_post_activity` (the poster only). There is no viewing privately: everyone who views is named (`community_profiles_views_named` keeps `private_views` off, `community_set_private_views` is gone); only views made while it was still a switch stay nameless (`community_post_views.anonymous`, 20261224090000_views_always_named.sql), and a share is named only when its story is one the poster may see — why: people wanted to see who liked and shared, and to put crew posts on their story, without a share reaching anyone the post wasn't for; the coach wanted views to work like Instagram's (no private viewing, no column of "Following" under a story), and people who viewed privately were promised those views stay nameless.
- A post's ✈️ is Instagram's share sheet (`PostShareSheet`, post-share-sheet.tsx; 20270312090000_share_sheet.sql; post-share.ts / post-share.queries.ts), on every post, mounted on its first tap: Search, then a grid of the people, group chats and (a coaching client's) coach chat it can go to, best guess first (`community_share_targets`: the 1:1s you've talked in lately, who you've sent posts to (`community_post_sends`, server only), your likes and comments on theirs and theirs on yours, follows; a new account gets the crew's best-followed; with a search, everyone findable by name or @username, `community_findable_people` minus `community_staff_people()` and `community_blocked_authors()`; a coach gets clients' coach chats (theirs, everyone's for an admin) and their group chats); tap one or several (up to `SHARE_SEND_MAX`, a blue check), add a message, Send / Send separately (`community_share_post`: each on its own, a 1:1 through `chat_direct_open` so a new one is a request unless they follow you, a double tap within a minute is one send, one that fails never stops the rest, 200 a day), and each goes as a `community_post` card with `shared: true` that leads its message (`postReplyCard`, `data-post-shared-card`: who posted, the picture or its words, the caption) and pushes like any message ("sent you a post"). Only someone who can see the post gets it (`community_share_refusal`): a crew-wide post anyone, a followers-only post only its author's followers and never a group, Close Friends / coach-only only your coach chat, a private one nobody, never across a block with the author. With nobody picked, the row underneath is every other way out, Instagram's order: Add to story, Copy link, Save, Messages (a phone's sms:), Share to… (the phone's own sheet), WhatsApp, Snapchat, Messenger, Facebook, X, Threads, Email, Instagram (a coach's), each app by its documented share link (`shareAppHref`); Messenger has none a web page can open on an iPhone, so there it opens the phone's share sheet. Every link is the post's own, `https://jfeffect.com/p/<id>` (`postLink`): signed in it opens the post on your Crew page (`postLinkTarget`, p.$postId.tsx), signed out it asks you to join or sign in and never shows the post. In View as client nothing sends (it would go as the coach) — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach wanted the share sheet set up like Instagram's, with the people someone is most likely to send it to on top ("a smart system for the app to know who they most likely will share to") and every share option (Messenger, copy link, save…); before, ✈️ only offered Add to story and messaging the poster.
- A bottom sheet with a grip closes with one swipe down, Instagram's (Nov 17 2026): one gesture, `useSwipeDownToClose` (use-swipe-down-to-close.ts; its rules `sheetDragIntent` / `sheetDragCloses`, lib/post-sheet.ts), used by the post's sheet (`PostSheet`) and by any `SheetContent side="bottom" swipeToClose` (closed through a Radix Close it holds, so `onOpenChange` runs as for ✕). Only a mostly downward finger drags it (up scrolls its list, sideways is tabs or a carousel), never from a list scrolled down (that scrolls back up first), a text box, a slider or `[data-no-sheet-drag]`; the rest of the touch is followed on the element it started on, so a list that loads mid-swipe never freezes it; far enough or a flick closes it, sliding out from where the finger left it, else it springs back. Off by default, since a press-and-hold reorder inside a sheet would fight it; every Crew sheet that draws a grip turns it on (sheet-swipe-to-close.test.ts checks) — why: the coach opened his post's Activity (views, likes) over the feed and couldn't swipe it down to close it, though it had a grip: only the comments sheet knew the gesture.
- Who liked a post is Instagram's, for anyone who can see the post (Nov 19 2026): the number by the heart is its own tap (`ReactionButton onCount`, `data-like-count`: under the heart on a post's screen, Reels', beside it in the card row; the heart still likes, a hold still opens the other reactions), and so is "Liked by Marc and 12 others" with their faces (`LikedBy`, reactors-sheet.tsx, `likedByLine`: under the card row's buttons, under a post's words in its sheet (`PostSheet`, where a post with no words shows only that line), and on a post's screen in the feed in its one meta line under the words, never a line of its own (Nov 20 2026, `LikedBy variant="page"`: three 20px faces, "Liked by Jared, Marc and 11 others" where the names give way before "and 11 others", no reaction emoji (they rise out of the heart there), the views shrink to 👁 and the number beside it, and the coach's chip says only what the line doesn't: "Coach replied", or the coach's ❤️ when the coach isn't among its three faces; the post keeps one Likes for the line and the heart's number, `ReactionBar onOpenLikes`); each opens the Likes (`ReactorsSheet`: a grip that swipes down, "Likes" and how many, each person's photo, name and @username with Follow, a ❤️ never badged and any other reaction on the photo, a tap opens their profile; over the post's sheet when opened from it). Who shows is one rule, `community_post_likers` (20270415090000_likers_one_rule.sql: plain SQL with no SET clause, so it's inlined, index lookups per liker, never a whole set per post; never callable by a client), read by the list (`community_post_reactors`, whoever can see the post, `community_post_visible`) and by the post's own JSON for the line's three faces and the counts by the heart (`community_post_json` `reactors`, `reactions`, `reaction_count`), so "Liked by Marc and 12 others" names the list's first people and the 12 are people the list shows: you always, then people you follow, then everyone else, newest first, never an account blocked either way (a coach is never hidden), deactivated or deleted (the same people `community_blocked_authors()` hides; Instagram hides a deactivated account's likes too). League points, post scores and the feed's ranking read the table and are unchanged. The Likes start loading as the finger lands on the number or the line (`usePrefetchPostReactors`, `onCountPress`). In View as client "you" is the client being previewed (`reactorsSeenAs`), never the coach's own like. The poster's own Activity (views, likes, shares, reposts) stays the poster's — why: the coach wanted people to see who liked a crew post the way Instagram shows it; the list existed, but on the paged feed (where nearly everyone reads Crew) nothing opened it, since the number under the heart was part of the like button; and the line, built per post, still named any three likers (coaches first, a deactivated account's included) while the list left that account out and counted 3 where it showed 2.
- Likes and reposts animate the way Instagram's do: a double-tap's ❤️ springs in where the finger was, tilted (`burstTilt`), then floats up and goes (`useBurst` → `ReactionBurst` x / y, `community-burst`; every place a post can be double-tapped: its screen, the coach's cards, its page, the full-screen clips); the heart button springs (`community-pop`) and reposting turns the arrows once as they go green (`repost-spin`); a post people are reacting to (`isEngaged`) has several of its reactions rise out of the heart with a little sway once per app session (`useEngagementPop`, `ReactionsRise` with `riseCount`; on a post's own screen high out of the heart down the right, `size="lg"`), and only once what it rises out of is showing (`hidden`: the feed's first post under the stories, its sheet open, a clip held) — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach found the like and repost animations had stopped: the rise played on the feed's first post while it was still under the stories with its buttons hidden, so nobody saw it, and it never played again that session.
- Every post grid (a profile's Posts, Reposts and Saved, and Add from Saved) is one component, `PostGrid` (post-grid.tsx), Instagram's: three across, edge to edge on a phone, 3:4 tiles with a 2px gap, photos filling from the middle and fading in; an automated post's tile is its painted card (`AutoPostTile`, loaded only when one shows); any other tile with no picture (a coach's note, a workout, a lock in) is a `GridCard` (post-tile.tsx): its words centred both ways with equal room above and below, a lesson by its title, and nothing in its corner but what it is (PR, Poll, Live), never a "Note" / "Workout" label. Grids load in whole rows (`GRID_FIRST_PAGE` 12, then `GRID_PAGE_SIZE` 15; `useCommunityFeed` with an author, `useSavedPosts`, `useUserReposts`) by themselves once the end of the grid is within `GRID_PREFETCH_PX` (one screen) of the bottom. That is checked against where the end really is, because the observer still says "near" for a moment after a page lands. Instagram's spinner shows under the grid only while a page loads, a failed page gets "Tap to try again", and once there's no more the grid just ends: no Show more button, no spinner, no extra room — why: a Show more button and small square tiles inset from the edges looked nothing like Instagram, and each grid post costs ~70ms on the server (`community_workout_stats` walks the person's whole history), so loading more than a page ahead wastes seconds of database time per profile visit; and the coach's automated posts sat bottom-left beside centred workout tiles (a grid tile is a button, which centres its text by default), so the grid looked misaligned.
- Crew's + is Instagram's two ways to share (Nov 22 2026, share-workout-picker.tsx, post-composer.tsx, story-filters.ts; crew-posting-instagram.test.ts, story-filters.test.ts). Post is the New post screen (`PostComposer`, lazy, for members and the coach's Crew +): the tap on + opens the phone's photo library in that same tap on a finger (`useComposerPicker`: its file inputs live in the page, never inside the lazy composer, since a browser opens a picker only from a tap; `opensLibraryOnTap`), and what's picked lands in three steps that slide one into the next (the step on screen rests at `transform: none`, `pane()`, so the text editor and sticker tray, fixed overlays, cover the screen; never `scrollIntoView` inside it, which scrolled the steps' box): New post (the picks numbered in post order, hold and drag to reorder, `tray.move`; the one in focus big at the post's shape, drag and pinch to frame it, the corner button for the shape; Camera and Library tiles; Post · Story along the bottom), Edit (the slides full width; on the first photo Filter, Text, Stickers, on a clip Trim, Sound, Cover, the lift; Tag; Ratio) and the details (caption with the @ picker, the coach's Poll, GIF / voice, Tag people, Audience, the Workout row, Hide my weights, what it earns), then Share with its upload in the button. Every pick starts uploading as it's added (`useSlideTray`, coverCount 0); a filter or stickers on the first photo re-draw it at the post's shape (`needsRender`: that one posts as drawn, `tray.ready(shape, coverId)` leaves its plain copy out and `tray.remove` deletes it once the post is up), everything else goes as the tray framed it; a design (a card, a filter's words) is drawn at 9:16 (the `full` card) or 4:5 only, a grade at any shape. Which post it makes: today's lock in card starts the session (`lockPost`), a workout card or the Workout row switched on posts that workout (`workout.post`, League points; on by default for a workout finished in the last day not shared yet), a meal look `mealPost`, anything else or Write (words only) `plainPost`; the coach's is `community_create_note` (1-1200 characters, a caption always). Leaving with something picked asks "Discard post?"; the caption is kept on the phone until it's posted. Story is the camera (`ShareStudio`, `startAs="story"`; its Post tab hands over to the New post screen, `onPostFlow`): every filter is a circle in the shutter's ring (`LookCarousel` with `filters` and `filterGroups`), one row of Normal then groups ordered for right now (`buildFilterRow` / `groupOrder`: a workout just finished puts Today first, a session to start Lock in, a post-workout meal Fuel; chips Today · Lock in · Train · Block · Fuel · Time · Mindset · Vibe jump the row, the one the ring's in lit as it glides), the cards a lock in, a workout and a meal have leading their groups, then the story filters (story-filters.ts, ~50: a design `over` drawn once a minute and kept, a grade `under` each frame, so the camera holds 30 fps; Vibe's grades are blend modes every browser has, never `ctx.filter`); a photo taken or picked from the library is edited with that same row, never a hidden swipe with dots (`editRing` in share-studio.tsx: `LookCarousel` with no `onShoot` is a picker, the ring showing what's on over the picture's foot; a story's photo gets the whole row, else a card's looks; it steps aside while a finger rests on the picture, two fingers frame it or a sticker is dragged toward the bin, `StickerLayer onDragging`), New post's Edit step shows the same row under its photo (no strip of its own), a swipe on the photo walks the filters too with the ring following (two fingers move it), and a clip goes on as it is with the row back on Normal. A filter only ever shows what's already the athlete's to share (first name, the public session title "Block 5 · Week 1" read by `readBlockWeek`, a finished workout's own numbers, the clock), never targets, macros, bodyweight or the coach's day and block names, and is flat colour and scrims, never a glow. A story can tag people (`tagPeople`, @ in its editor → `TagPeopleSheet`): their @ sticker on a photo (`mentionStickerItem`; binning it untags them) and the tag on the story's caption (`withTags`, crew-tags.ts: only the handles not already in it, on a line of their own), which `community_caption_mentions` reads into its mentions and the story viewer shows tappable (`MentionText` in `StoryBar`); posts tag the same way from Tag people, and an answer's story (the reply host) never tags. A story (`community_create_post(_story => true)`, `community_posts.story_only`) is one 9:16 picture or one clip for the crew or Close friends: 24h in the story rail only, never in the feed (`inFeed`), Home's deck, the "N new" count (`community_activity`) or the profile grid / post count; a lock in's story still starts the session (`StudioPost.beforeStory`) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found posting bad next to Instagram (the camera-first studio for every post, and his own + a plain "Write a post" dialog) and wanted Instagram's flow with clean animations through it, and far more fun story filters about training, cheat meals, nutrition, locking in, time of day, motivation, the current workout and block, organised and quick to swipe, with people taggable on stories; a story that also landed in the feed would be a post; then, the same day, that picking a photo from the library dropped the camera's easy row of filters for a swipe with dots, where he wanted the camera's layout (every option laid out, easy to see and pick) after a photo too.
- Crew's create flow moves like Instagram's (Nov 22 2026, swipe-to-create.ts and mode-selector.ts pure and tested, swipe-to-create.tsx, mode-selector.tsx, discard-confirm.tsx, crew-create-flow.test.ts): the camera sits to the left of the feed, where Crew's + is. On the clients' paged feed (never the coach's page or View as client) a finger moving right from anywhere on a post or the stories (`createSwipeIntent`: past `CREATE_SLOP_PX`, `CREATE_SIDEWAYS_RATIO` more across than up or down; never left, which is the carousels' (a workout's recap); never once the page moved) pushes the feed off to the right with the finger, Instagram's (the feed, Crew's bar and the tab bar together, `FEED_LAYERS`, its leading edge rounded, `FEED_EDGE_RADIUS`), and a stand-in of the camera is underneath, a third slower and dimmed until it's in (`cameraUnderFeed`: shifted −30% × what's left and clipped 70% × what's left from its right, so its edge is always the feed's; `CameraStandIn`: the viewfinder, close, the rail's Type, shutter, library, POST · STORY and flip where the real camera draws them); let go past a third (`CREATE_COMMIT_SHARE`) or flick (`createSwipeCommits`) and the story camera opens under it, asked for inside the touchend (`cameraRef` → `beginStory("swipe")`, so an allowed camera doesn't ask again), the stand-in fading off once `[data-share-studio]` is up, else it slides back; a pull is never a tap (a click within `CLICK_AFTER_PULL_MS` is swallowed). Never from a text box, slider, scrub bar or `[data-no-create-swipe]` (`CREATE_NO_START`; a button is fine), a clip's scrub band, a carousel past its first slide or any sideways scroller moved on (`scrolledAcross`), with a second finger, while a sheet, dialog or held clip has the screen (`screenBusy`), or from Safari's back-swipe edge in a Safari tab (`SAFARI_EDGE_PX`). The camera (`ShareStudio entry`) comes in the same way from + and Your story ("slide": `jf-camera-in` with the same shift and clip, and `CAMERA_ENTER_EVENT` pushes the feed off), appears in place from the swipe ("none"), fades when switched to from Post ("fade"), and on a phone closes by the feed sliding back in from the right over it (`leave`, `CAMERA_LEAVE_EVENT`, `LEAVE_MS`; a story reply's camera fades); on a phone its dim overlay is never drawn (it would darken the feed it pushes aside). POST · STORY is one component (`ModeSelector`, `CAMERA_MODES`: only what Crew makes): in the camera the one you're on sits centred under the shutter in bold white (no dot, Instagram's) and the row slides; in the New post screen it's a floating pill whose highlight slides; tap or swipe either (`modeAfterSwipe`), it follows `value` (the caller decides, and may ask first), and each screen opens on the mode the last one was on and slides to its own (`enterFrom`). The camera's library is bottom-left and flip bottom-right (a thumb's reach; a double tap still flips), close top-left and the flash top-centre (Instagram's), its tools a rail down the left (`CameraRail`: each one's name as it opens, then icons only after `RAIL_LABELS_MS`; only the tools that work right now: Type, a story of words on a colour (Instagram's Create, `startTyped`: the story reply's backdrop, the text tool open, the colour wheel, no filter, posted as the story's picture), Timer, and No photo or Write where they apply). The flash is the back camera's light where its track has one (`torchSupported`: a moment before the photo, `TORCH_SETTLE_MS`, and on while a clip films), the screen white for a selfie (`SCREEN_FLASH_MS`), never offered where it can't fire. Nothing is lost by accident (`DiscardConfirm`, Keep editing the easy tap): leaving the camera with a photo, clip, words or caption on it (`requestLeave`, Escape and Android back too), Retake with text or stickers on the photo (`requestRetake`), and the New post screen's STORY with photos picked or words written (`toStory`) all ask first — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for Instagram's creation flow, then sent Instagram's own screens (the feed pushed aside mid-swipe with the camera under it, X · flash · settings across the top, Create · Boomerang · Layout · Effects down the left fading to icons, POST · STORY in bold white) (a finger-following swipe from the feed into a clean full-screen camera, a swipeable POST · STORY selector, gallery, flip and flash in easy reach, nothing lost by accident) without rebuilding what works; the swipe goes right because the camera already lived top-left and a leftward swipe would fight every workout post's swipe to its recap; switching the New post screen to Story silently dropped picked photos, Retake dropped text and stickers with no question, and flip sat top-right, out of a thumb's reach.
- What's posted is what the editor's frame shows, Instagram's way: a post has one shape for every slide (Instagram's carousel rule), 9:16 (fills a phone's screen in the Crew feed), 4:5, 1:1 or 16:9 (`POST_ASPECTS`, the corner button walks them, `nextAspect`), set from its first photo or clip (`startAspect`, Nov 23 2026: anything upright, a phone's 9:16 clip or screenshot, an iPhone photo's 3:4, a 4:5, starts at 9:16 and fills its screen, a square or sideways one keeps the closest of its own, `closestAspect`; unknown 9:16; the camera's shot is framed by its guide, the whole camera at 9:16; editing, the shape the post's first slide shows at, `slideAspect`) until it's picked by hand (`aspectPicked`); a card in a post is 9:16 (the `full` card, the next rule) or, picked with the shape button, the feed's 4:5, a story 9:16. Every slide is framed in that shape (`PhotoFramer`: one finger drags, two pinch, never smaller than the frame; under a card, one finger swipes looks so two fingers frame it): slide 1 (the photo up front) as before, and any photo or clip picked now by tapping it in the strip (`SlideTray onSelect`, `selItem`; a clip plays there, muted, its selection looping, `PhotoFramer video`; its Trim and Cover follow it; one already on the post stays as it is). A photo is baked in at 1080 wide (`framedCanvas`; a strip photo at Post, `useSlideTray.ready(shape)` → `finish`, unless it's already the shape and untouched, its old upload removed), stickers drawn where they sit (no `remapStickers`). Clips can't be re-cut on the phone, so a clip goes up whole with its frame in its `focus` (`ClipFrame` {a, z, x, y}: the shape, the zoom and the middle of what shows, `frameFocus`; `media_focus` for the cover, `focus` on extra slides, cleaned by `community_media_focus`, 20270313090000_clip_frames.sql: `a` 0.5–1.8, `z` 1–5) and a poster baked to that frame (the cover picked in Edit cover, `CoverPicker`, which picks the moment as the frame shows it, else its own first moment); every screen that plays it shows just that part (`ClipFrameBox`, clip-frame-box.tsx: a box at the frame's shape, whole or filling its area, the clip placed in it by `framedMediaStyle`; `InlineVideo frame`, the full-screen clips), lays it out at the frame's shape (`slideAspect`, `screenFit`, `postMediaRatio`) and never crops it again by its focus (`focusPosition` is only the older {x, y} object-position, which posts from before keep).
