A photo, or a clip framed in the studio, fills a post's screen only when it was framed 9:16 (`PHOTO_FILL_MAX_TRIM`); at 9:16 the frame shows where Crew's bar, the caption and the buttons will sit over it while a finger is on it (`SCREEN_ZONES`, `data-screen-zones`, TikTok's safe zone). The camera starts a post's shot framed as its guide showed it (`framingFromGuide`; at 9:16, where a post starts, the guide is the whole camera, nothing dimmed). A rule-of-thirds grid shows only while a finger is on the frame, the centre lines light up yellow when it snaps, and the profile grid's 3:4 tile is outlined — why: the editor used to show a 9:16 frame while the feed cropped to 4:5 and the grid to a square, so nobody saw what their post would look like; and on Nov 13 2026 the coach wanted every post made in the app cropped and optimized for the app, with the placement and size adjustable before publishing like Instagram, where only the first photo could be framed, every other photo went up as it was, a clip could only be dragged (no zoom) inside a frame the feed didn't show it in, and nothing could be made full screen.
- A post fills its screen in the Crew feed (Nov 23 2026, crew-full-screen-posts.test.ts): a new post starts at 9:16 (`startAspect`, the rule above) in the New post screen and the camera (`ShareStudio`, the recap's Create Crew Post and the lock in included), and its card, a workout, lock in or meal look or a story filter, is painted at the `full` size (`SHARE_SIZES.full` 1080×1920, `cardLayout("full")`; story-filters.ts `filterArea("full")`): a story's canvas with its words kept clear of what covers a post's screen, worked out from `SCREEN_ZONES` (`FULL_SAFE`: Crew's bar over its top, the name and caption over its foot, the buttons down the right from halfway), its words laid out between `PAD` and `R` (`CX` their middle; a filter's design across `SW`, while the photo, scrims and full-bleed bands still use the whole width), a title that takes two lines split evenly (`balancedLines`, never a word left alone). The shape button on a card goes 9:16 ↔ 4:5 (`shapeOf`), the only two a card is drawn at. A 9:16 post fills its screen (`screenFit` cover, `PHOTO_FILL_MAX_TRIM`), behind Crew's bar and under its words, TikTok's way. A picture that doesn't fill its screen (every post made before, a 4:5 card with its words baked in, a square or sideways photo) is never cropped to fill it (cropping would cut its card's words): it shows whole between the bars over a soft, dark copy of itself across the whole screen (`AmbientFill`, ambient-fill.ts `paintAmbient`: the photo shrunk to a few pixels on a tiny canvas the screen stretches, darkest under Crew's bar and the words, `AMBIENT_SHADE`; no CSS filter, no extra layer to composite while the feed scrolls; black until the picture is there), never black bands. Instagram exports, stories and the share-out sheet keep their own shapes (`makeShareFile` is feed / story only) — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach sent Fionna's workout post (a photo with the workout card baked in) sitting as a 4:5 picture between black bands on the full-screen feed and asked for posts like it to take the full screen, the picture capturing and all the filters included: the camera framed posts by a 4:5 guide, an iPhone photo started at 4:5, and every card was painted at 4:5 for a post, while a card painted at a story's 9:16 would have put its words under the feed's caption and buttons.
- Photos and clips in Crew posts and stories have filters, one colour engine for preview and export (Nov 22 2026, media-grade.ts pure and tested, media-grade-gl.ts, graded-layer.tsx, filter-panel.tsx; 20270506090000_media_filter_defaults.sql): Original · Auto · Flash · Workout · Bright · Natural+ (`FILTER_KEYS`), a strength, and Adjust (brightness, contrast, saturation, highlights, shadows, warmth, sharpness, each reset on its own, Original puts the picture back). A grade is a white balance, a 256-step tone curve per channel and a saturation stage that keeps skin near its own saturation (`compileGrade`), never a model or the network; Auto and every filter's correction read the picture itself first (`analyzePixels` on a 96 px copy: levels, cast, saturation, skin; `autoCorrection`, every move limited so a good picture is left alone) and keep those numbers on the grade, so the export grades exactly what the preview did. The GPU runs it (`GradeGL`: the same maths as `gradePixels`, the pixel loop a phone without WebGL gets): the studio grades a photo once per change and a clip every frame it shows, on screen size, while the element itself stays underneath playing (`GradedLayer`, `PhotoFramer grade`), so sliders never wait on an encode. What goes up has it baked in: a photo through `framedCanvas(…, grade)` before any card, sticker or words (the composer's cover, `finish` for every slide, the studio's shot, cards and Save); a clip re-encoded once its choice has settled (`GRADE_SETTLE_MS`, `tray.setGrade` → `withGrade`, `planVideo` `graded`: always a transcode with no copy fallback, `renderVideo` `grade` → mediabunny's `process`, the poster graded too), offered only where the phone can encode and has WebGL (`canGrade`). Enhance is a tool of its own (the composer's Edit step and the studio's sliders button), never the Filter row of designs, and a carousel's slides each keep their own filter: "Apply to other slides…" puts this one on the ones picked, Auto read per slide and pulled `1 − CAROUSEL_OWN` toward the set (`consistentStats`). "Save as default" keeps the filter, strength and adjustments (never a picture's measurements, `cleanGrade`) on the account, one for posts and one for stories (`media_filter_prefs`, own row, `useFilterDefaults`, a device copy for the first paint, the newer wins), applied to new media in the composer and the story camera only (never one already on a post, never progress check-ins or chat); changing one picture never changes it, "Your default" taps off. A story is always its whole 9:16: a photo or clip that fills it trimming at most `SCREEN_FILL_MAX_TRIM` starts filling it (framed by hand, a clip posted with its 9:16 `ClipFrame` and a poster baked to it), a wider or taller one starts whole over a soft blur of itself (`storyStartsWhole`, `wholeStoryCanvas` for a photo, the story viewer's blurred poster for a clip, `data-story-clip-fit`), Fill / Fit switches when they'd differ (`storyCanFit`), and the choice and framing hold through every look and filter. A story filter (a "plain" card with its own painter) posts painted, never as the bare photo (`storyPhoto` / `postPhoto` check `card.paint`). Workout cards keep their small mark (`brandRow`: the logo and "JF EFFECT" at the top, `brandFooter`), never on a person's own photo or clip — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for a premium media editor: smart filters for posts and stories (images and videos), a remembered default that never overwrites a one-off change, per-slide or all-slides control, fast on older phones with no network, and exports that match the preview; and every story filter (Leg day, Noir, Polaroid…) posted as the plain photo, because the posted picture skipped the filter's painter whenever the card's look was "plain".
- Sharing a workout outside the app is one sheet (Nov 22 2026, share-out.ts pure and tested, share-out-sheet.tsx): Instagram Story, Instagram post, TikTok, More apps, Save, Copy caption (`SHARE_DESTINATIONS`), from the recap (the workout finished and its PR moment, `RecapPostFooter`, "Share to Instagram, TikTok and more") and the story camera (its bottom-left button, `makeShareFile`), each picture painted when the sheet opens at the shape its destination takes (9:16 for a story or TikTok, 4:5 for an Instagram post) so the tap hands it straight to the phone's share sheet. The caption is written from the workout (`workoutShareCaption`: "Block 1 · Week 1 / lift / set / @jfeffect", a PR "New PR. 107.5 kg × 6." with "Tracked with @jfeffect"; never the loads on a post that hides them), editable, its handle line a switch (`withPromo`, remembered on the phone). The clipboard is written only by a tap that says it copies (Copy caption, and Instagram post / TikTok, whose tiles say "copies caption", since those apps drop text shared with a picture); More apps sends the caption with the picture. Nothing posts for the person or claims to fill another app's caption: the sheet says the phone's share sheet opens and that Instagram and TikTok take a pasted caption — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach wanted athletes to share a workout to Instagram and TikTok in a tap or two with a ready caption, honestly within what those platforms allow a web app.
- The feed shows a post at its own shape between 4:5 and 16:9 (`feedAspect`); grid tiles (`PostTileFace`) show a cover's `media_focus` as its object-position. In the feed a tap on a post's picture never opens it (the post page is only for the workout, the lock in stamp and comments): one tap on a clip pauses it on the clients' paged feed, opens the clips full screen on the coach's Crew page, and turns the sound on or off on the post page (always after the double-tap window), two taps ❤️ at once. A workout post's screen never sends anyone to a page by itself: its workout is the post's last swipe (the next rule), and a tap on its card or recap does nothing (two taps ❤️); a workout post with a picture or a film reads, under the name, their own words first (`PageCaption`, 15px, two lines and … more; our own film captions left out), then the workout's context as one quieter group (`data-page-workout-context`): the set on screen (`data-page-set`: the exercise at 14px over "Top set · 455 lb × 1 @ RPE 6" at 13px, the slide's own from `postSlides`, which the carousel reports as it changes, `onSlide` → `showSlide`, kept per post so another post's slide is never read; a photo or a clip with no set says nothing), and nothing more: since Nov 21 2026 there is no workout chip (the carousel's "4/7" and the swipe are the way to the recap, and a line less gives the clip its room); the recap opens every set in the post's sheet (Workout tab), never the workout page. The workout page (`PostDetailDialog`: profiles, links, notifications, the card feed) opens over the feed, so Back lands on the same post, and opened for the workout it opens scrolled to the workout, past the picture just watched (`at="workout"`, measured at full size while the page zooms in; a profile, a link or a notification opens at its top). It reads as a summary in the app's text face, never the condensed display face, caps or tiles (`WorkoutSummary`): the workout's name at 20px in sentence case, one line of where it sits and which workout of the month (`workoutSubtitle` · `monthWorkoutLine`: "9th workout this month", never "#9"), its numbers in one row at their own widths (Time · Working sets · Volume · PRs, 17px tabular, the PR count amber; closer together under 375px so four still fit), then Exercises with their count and "Best set": each a row of its name (wrapping, never cut), its working sets and its record (`data-exercise-pr`, wrapping whole), and its best set in the unit it was typed in over the other unit, smaller (`exerciseBestLines`: "455 lb × 1" over "206.4 kg", the post-weight rule below; a set with no load has no second line; bodyweight and assisted sets are never a load). A workout with no picture opens on that summary (its heading and numbers are what two taps ❤️), the feed's workout card (`WorkoutHero`) staying the feed's picture — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found View Workout slightly off: the card people look at did nothing on a tap while a second grey bar under it did, the film line's tap was a 20px sliver, and the workout page repeated the card's time, sets and volume as tiles right under it while the card itself cut the volume to "17,902 …"; and on Nov 17 2026 that a film's set sat over the top-left of the clip ("Competition Squat / Top set · 455 lb × 1") and "· View workout" squeezed in beside a workout name cut to "Thursday — Secondary…", where it belongs in the words at the bottom, following the slide, with a proper way into the workout; and then that it was still cluttered (the workout's name, the set, a PR pill and a View workout pill stacked above their caption, so the generated workout info drowned out the person's own words), and that the workout page read like a poster rather than a summary (a 28px condensed caps title, five tall tiles with condensed numbers and an unexplained "#9 This month", and exercise rows whose heavy condensed numbers outweighed the exercise names), and that View workout landed on the photo they'd just watched instead of the workout. A member's own photo / clip post (`isPhotoPost`) shows its picture edge to edge with "Name caption" under it, like a workout photo, and so does every automated post (rule below); a coach's own "+ Post" note keeps the note layout — why: a tap that opened a separate page felt broken next to Instagram, and an inset, rounded member photo wasted a feed's width.
- A Crew workout post's workout is its last swipe, Instagram's carousel way (Nov 20 2026, workout-recap.tsx, workout-recap.ts pure and tested): on a post's screen every post with a finished workout (`recapOn`: not a Pulse, not a coach's note) gets the recap as the slide after its own photos and clips (`PostMedia pageTrail`, `PostCarousel`'s trailing slide), and a workout with no picture (only ever a Coach-only or archived one since Nov 20 2026, the next rule) is two slides, its card (`WorkoutCoverCard`: the workout card with one row, "Workout recap · Every exercise · Swipe" / "2 PRs · Swipe", that slides there) then the recap. The recap (`WorkoutRecap`, full canvas since Nov 21 2026) is a slide of the post like its clips, the whole screen edge to edge (`absolute inset-0 bg-card`, no card, margin or rounded corners: Crew's bar and the "7/7" over its top, the post's words and buttons over its foot), never a card floating in it: its name (26px, up to 3 lines) and one quiet line (`recapMeta`: "2h 13m · 10 sets"; the day is the author line's) at the top, under Crew's bar; its key lifts in the room between that and its last row, centred there (`recapKeyLifts`: at most `RECAP_MAX_LIFTS` (4), as many as the room holds at `ROW_MIN` a row and each given up to `ROW_MAX` of it; a row a long name pushes past the room drops the last lift instead of being cut; names wrap to two lines, never cut with "…"), each with its best set as the big number in the unit it was typed in, the unit and reps a step smaller (`splitBestSet`, `Load`; the other unit is in the sheet), the headline PR amber with the trophy and any other record quiet (`recapPr`, never a PR claimed for a set); one or two exercises are shown big instead of as a short list (`FEATURE_MAX`: 44px, 56px for one); and a quiet last row just above the post's words ("All 6 exercises ›" / "Every set ›", `recapMoreLabel`), all of it inset clear of the buttons down the right (`pr-16`). Every set is the post's sheet's Workout tab (`PostSheet at="workout"`, the next rule), opened by the last row at once and by a tap anywhere on the card after the double-tap window (`onHeroTap`, `[data-recap-card]`), over the post, so closing it lands on the same slide with the post as it was. Its numbers come with the post; its exercises are `community_post` (`usePostDetail`, shared with the sheet, so it opens with them), asked for only once the post is half on screen and the recap is the next swipe (`useOnScreen`, latched), never for every post the feed mounts. Its sheet open, the post's card above it is the same panel at the post's shape with the name and its line at a phone card's width scaled to fit (`FitScale width={300}`), never wrapped word by word. Swipes: the browser locks a swipe to the way it started, so vertical is the feed's and sideways the carousel's (checked with real touch input at 375×667, 390×844 and 430×932: sideways through the clips to the recap, a tap on it, the sheet's tabs, a swipe down to close, 💬 and Escape, every time back on slide 7/7 with the feed where it was). The first time, the carousel's counter becomes "‹‹ Swipe for workout 1/2" (only the chevrons nudge, never the screen) on the first workout post until they reach a recap or open a workout (`community_hints_seen` `workout_recap`, on the account) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found Crew pushed people toward opening the workout instead of a social post (a tap on the workout card or its chip opened a separate page), and wanted the recap to be the post's final swipe, compact and impressive, with the full workout only when someone needs it.
- A workout post's sheet has two tabs, Workout and Comments (Nov 21 2026, post-sheet.tsx `sheetHasWorkout`, workout-details.tsx): the recap opens it on Workout, 💬 and the words on Comments, one tap moves between them (Crew's tab look: the one up at full strength with a short line under it), and both stay mounted while it's open, so a half-typed comment survives a look at the sets. Workout (`WorkoutDetails` → `WorkoutSetList`) never says the workout's name or numbers again: one quiet line (`workoutTotals`: "6 exercises · 11,925 lb lifted (5,409 kg)"), then each exercise by name with its record (`recapPr`), its sets under it in the order they were done, identical sets in a row said once with their numbers (`exerciseSetRows`: "2–4  375 lb × 4  @ 6"), the weight as typed with the other unit quieter at the end (`setLoad` / `setText`: bodyweight "BW + 20 kg × 8" / "BW × 12", assisted "−20 lb", a hold or walk its time, a post that hides weights reps only). The sets are `community_workout_exercises`' `set_list` (20270430090000_crew_workout_sets.sql: each working set's typed number and unit, kg mirror, reps, RPE, seconds and load type, in the order done; `community_hide_exercise_loads` strips `v` / `kg`; derived only). The post page (`PostDetailDialog`, from profiles, links and notifications) shows the same key lifts and opens every set in place (`data-workout-every-set`; opened for the workout, `at="workout"`, they show at once), so its comments are never a long scroll away — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach found the workout carousel and the full workout complicated: a big recap card (stats grid, eyebrow, month chip, an "All 6 exercises" button) sent people to a separate page that repeated all of it with the comments far below, and a workout chip under every clip repeated the swipe; then that the recap still read as a popup inside a post, a card floating in the middle of a black canvas with the space above and below it unused.
- Removing an exercise from a workout (the card's ⋯ "Remove from this workout", or swipe to delete) moves the row and everything on it (logged sets and their reviews, notes, warm-ups, set blocks, % references) to `pl_exercise_row_trash` in one transaction (`pl_trash_exercise_row`, service role only, behind `removeExerciseFromWorkout`'s visible-day check); Undo on the toast and Restore under the list put it back exactly (`pl_restore_exercise_row`). Never delete `pl_row_results` for a removal from the client or a server fn — why: the old swipe-to-delete wiped logged sets and notes irreversibly, and a removal only ever touches that one workout, never the program's other days or its template.
- An exercise note is one row per exercise per workout (`pl_exercise_notes`), edited in place at any time, today's or one from a past workout, block or program (Nov 19 2026, 20270419090000_exercise_note_edits.sql): the notes sheet (`ExerciseNotesSheet`) rewords this workout's note and, under Previous notes, any older note of the exercise right where it is (Edit, its own Save, the sheet's bottom button hidden meanwhile so one save is on screen), always by id (`updateNote`), never a second note. The database keeps every wording an edit replaces (`tg_pl_exercise_note_keep_version` → `pl_exercise_note_revisions`, written only by that trigger, read by the athlete, their coach and admins; tied to the client, not the note, so a note trashed with its exercise and restored keeps its history), stamps `edited_at` (when the words changed: `updated_at` also moves when the coach marks a note seen) and never changes a note's `created_at`, client, day or row once it exists. A note reads by the day it was written (history is ordered by `created_at`, so an edit never moves a note) with a quiet "Edited Nov 19" (`editedLabel`, exercise-note-versions.ts) that opens its earlier versions, oldest first with the original marked (`EditedToggle` / `NoteVersions`, note-edit-history.tsx; the athlete's "Use this" puts old words back in the editor, saved only when they save); edits from before Nov 19 2026 kept no earlier wording and say so. The coach hears about a note once: only its first words post the `workout_note` card (`tg_exercise_note_to_coach`), and every later edit updates that same message as the server (its preview `body` and the attachment's `note`, no edited mark on the bubble, no unread, no push: the sheet pushes only when a new card was made, and an edit never clears `coach_seen_at`); the card and the coach's notes log read the note live, say "Edited …" and open the same versions, and the card reads it again when its message updates (`version` = `message.updated_at`) — why: on Nov 19 2026 the coach asked for notes that can be fixed any time, including old ones, without duplicate notes, duplicate messages or extra notifications: an edit more than 10 minutes after a note posted a second card and a push, an edit overwrote the original for good, and a note's history showed it by its last change (`updated_at`), so editing an old note, or the coach marking it seen, moved it to the top with that day's date.
- "Fastest way to log" (`QuickLogTips`, quick-log-tips.tsx) teaches the logger's quickest moves on a small copy of a card that saves nothing, in this order: reps and RPE are filled in; typing set 1's weight logs the whole exercise (the fill-down puts the weight in every untouched set below and logs them with their reps and RPE, which is how 92% of exercises were logged in the 30 days to Nov 16 2026); or Suggest picks the weight and a tap on set 1's number logs the exercise at it (the same fill-down); a swipe left takes a set out (Delete one they added or a warm-up, Skip one of the program's, Undo either; since Nov 20 2026, replacing the old last-warm-up step); and (a coaching client's only, `lift_review`) the camera on any set or warm-up. It opens by itself once per account on their own unfinished workout (`workout_view_prefs.logging_tips_at` through `workout_view_logging_tips_seen`, 20270329091000_quick_log_tips.sql; `useWorkoutView().loggingTipsSeen`, a per-phone copy for the first paint), never in View as client or over another sheet, and before Focus's tips and invite (they wait for it, and the invite skips that visit); the list view's Log faster (the ? beside List / Focus, `data-testid="quick-log-tips-open"`) and Focus's tips ("Fastest way to log a set") open it any time. Each step loops its beats (`QUICK_LOG_BEATS`); reduced motion holds the last — why: on Nov 16 2026 the coach wanted everyone shown the quickest way to log, once if they haven't seen it and a button to see it again, and Suggest is off by default, so the tap-the-number path alone would have taught most people something they can't use.
- The Focus intro (`focus-intro.tsx`) shows once per app launch (module state, so a reload starts over) in the first workout being logged, until `workout_view_prefs.tried_at` is set by the switch (`workout_view_set`); "Not now" hides it until the next launch, and it waits for the account (`known`) so it never shows to someone who tried on another phone. Focus usage is tallied per person per workout in `workout_view_sessions` (`workout_view_session_add` adds deltas, batched 20s and sent on a switch, a finish, leaving or backgrounding; never a coach's "View as client", never a finished workout opened to look at) and read by admins only via `workout_focus_report` (Workspace Settings' Focus card, `FocusUsageCard`, moved there with Training Intel's removal) — why: the intro must not nag, and the data must answer "is Focus worth keeping" without a request per set.
- A workout has one rest, and it's where the next set is (Nov 21 2026, RestTimerButton.tsx `RestLine`, rest-timer-store.ts, set-keyboard-bar.tsx; logger-rest-line.test.ts): the line right above each card's set table says the prescribed rest before it starts ("Rest 3 min", `restWords`; the program's, else the usual one for the kind of exercise), and a working set logged any way (typed, tapped, filled down, timed: the card's completion watcher, never warm-ups or the day's cardio) starts it by itself, unless "Start rest after each set" is Off in a card's ⋯ (`readRestAutoStart`, this phone, on by default); a tap on the words starts it too. Running, it's one row: pause / resume on the clock (a hairline under it fills as it goes), −15 / +15 (`adjustRestTimer`), start again and stop, the controls that don't fit a narrow card left out (−15 from a 272px row, start again from 320px), neutral; done, "✓ Rest done" in green with how long ago, the timer's buzz and chimes once per rest however many cards show it (`claimRestDoneCue`). It's timestamps in the store (`jf:rest-timer:<day>`), so switching exercises, locking the phone or a reload lands on the right time; one rest runs per workout, and it shows on the card it started on and, once that exercise is done, on the next one with sets to log too (`restHosts` over the workout's lifting in order, `restOrder`), so in Focus the next screen has it; a finished card offers no rest to start; the finish review stops it (`clearRestTimer`). The stopwatch and the other Workout Tools stay separate. Typing a set's number on a phone, a slim bar over the keyboard names the box ("Set 2 · RPE", `boxLabel`) with ‹ › to the box before or after in the card (`boxStep` over `[data-set-box]`: warm-ups, then each set's boxes in the order shown) and Done; moving saves the box like leaving it and opens the next inside the same tap so the number pad stays up (an iPhone's own ‹ › can't: only the box being typed in is a text box), and the row being typed in is kept clear of the keyboard and the bar (`revealDelta`); the tab bar and the shell's bar already step aside while typing, and Focus's top and bottom bars do too, at once, their room going to the card (styles.css) — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach found the rest timer fragmented: logging a set started a page-level timer nothing drew (`ActiveRestTimerProvider`, now gone), while the visible one was "Rest 3 min" on the left and a second timer icon on the right that only a tap started, and in Focus a rest started by an exercise's last set vanished with the swipe to the next one; and logging meant closing the number pad and tapping the next box to open it again.
- Workout Tools (calculator, e1RM, timer, stopwatch, tally) open from the Tools row at the top of every exercise card's ⋯ menu (`CardToolsRow`: one tap per tool, in that card's unit, as a sheet over the list or Focus, a dot on the one running); every tool and the list close back to the workout with Done top right, a swipe down (`swipeToClose`, its grip), a tap above the sheet or Escape, and "‹ All tools" inside a tool only steps over to the list; the ⋯ menu's sheet swipes down too (Nov 18 2026: a Calc or e1RM opened from a card had only "‹ Tools", which led to the list, and no swipe); there is no Tools button at the top of the workout, and a running tool keeps its pill on screen. The ⋯ menu itself is a bottom sheet on a phone (`CardMenu`, card-menu.tsx: the exercise's name, then every action within a thumb's reach above the home bar, scrolling if it's ever taller than 85% of the screen) and a dropdown sized to the room it has elsewhere; its items are written once with `CardMenuItem` / `CardMenuSeparator` for both, and Undo / Redo keep it open (`e.preventDefault()`) — why: one place that's reachable from Focus (where the top of the workout isn't), without another button in the header; and on a phone the dropdown opened upward from a mid-screen ⋯, ran its Tools row under the Dynamic Island out of reach and cut off Move to position and Remove.
- A logged set saves on the set it was typed in: a typed reps / RPE commits like a weight pick (the row flushes now via `commitFlushToken`), the fill-down (`cascadeFieldFromSet`) writes only the sets below, and `useAutosave` drops a pending save only on unmount or when its `key` (the record) changes, never on a re-render (callers pass `equals` inline, so as a dependency it dropped the save on every render). Values an effect puts on a set row (a unit switch, a fill from above, Fill All, undo / redo) are adopted as saved only once they've rendered (`adoptShownAsSaved`, two passes), never before, or they save a second time and a fill-down turns untouched sets into drafts; a change of the person's that hasn't saved yet is never adopted (`keepUnsaved`). A set that can't save (offline, or its first failed try: `permanentFailureAfter: 1`) goes to the durable queue (`enqueueOfflineWrite`) at once, a later successful save of the same set supersedes the queued copy (`supersedeOfflineWrite`), and a value that fails validation is never queued. Everything that lists or walks a workout's sets orders them by the row's place, then `set_index` (`historySetOrder`, `inWorkoutOrder` in logged-set-order.ts); save time only decides which workout came first. Hand repairs to logged sets go in `pl_row_result_repairs` with the old value — why: from Oct 7 every RPE / reps edit that filled down was lost on the edited set (the screen kept it, the database didn't), sets saved together by a fill land in any order, and a queued offline copy could replay over a newer edit.
- What a set or warm-up saves, and so what the suggestion reads, never depends on the order its weight, reps and RPE were typed in (Nov 20 2026, logger-input-order.test.ts): the weight's fill-down (`cascadeFromSet`) saves each set with the reps / RPE it shows (`shownFieldRef`: typed on it, or filled down onto it from a set above, before its weight came), then what it already has, then the source set's; a reps / RPE fill-down onto sets a weight fill-down is still logging waits for those writes and patches the rows they made (`weightCascadeRef`, `savedRowNow`), never only the screen; undo / redo / removing a set starts the shown values over. A warm-up row being typed into (`WarmupLine`, a + Warm-up row) stays mounted (`key={`draft-${draftKey}`}`) while its first save makes it a saved warm-up, and its saves run one at a time, each with what's on screen when it runs, onto the warm-up its first save made (the save returns the new id), so weight, reps and RPE typed in any order, however fast, end up as one warm-up saved as shown; a weight with no reps says so (`data-warmup-needs-reps`). The engine reads every warm-up near the heaviest as one (`readWarmups`, `warmupsForModel`, `finalWarmupRow`: ties by rating, then reps, never the order logged). How much a warm-up counts is a reliability model, not a floor (load-suggestion.ts note 8, `warmupSpread`: ~3.5% per rep in reserve; `warmupReliability`: a read lighter than history pulls down only as far as its rating can be believed, all of it at RPE 7+, none at 5 or easier, a smooth ramp between; a read heavier is a lower bound, its +1.5% kept), and one too easy to read says "RPE too low for a reliable estimate" (`WARMUP_TOO_EASY`) with where the number comes from: history's, or, with no history, nothing until the first set (`warmupTooEasy`, never a made-up weight) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found the suggested weight changing with the order he typed things, and easy warm-ups dragging it down: an RPE typed during the warm-up's first save was lost when its row remounted, a fast second weight could insert a second warm-up, set 2's typed RPE was saved over with set 1's, an RPE typed while the fill-down's rows were out showed but never saved, and every warm-up was read at RPE 6 or harder, so one called easy (RPE 4) read lighter and pulled the number down up to 3%, when 6,149 rated sets showed ratings that easy read ~16% light with a ±25% spread.
- Warm-ups are optional, quiet and in the set table's own columns, and only some of them set the weight (Nov 20 2026, final-warmup-input.tsx `WarmupRows` / `WarmupLine`, final-warmup.ts `warmupStyleOf`): nothing shows until the athlete taps + Warm-up under the sets (every lift, since the later Nov 20 2026 change: there is no open "Last warm-up" row and no suggested warm-up weight anywhere, neither in the set table nor in Suggested's ⓘ, which only says a warm-up logged with + Warm-up counts, `warmupReads`), which mounts a new, blank row (reps, effort and weight empty) and opens its weight box inside the same tap (`addToken` read in the render, never an effect; `flushSync`, `draftEditRef`). A heavy barbell compound (`warmupStyleOf` "last": the squat, bench and deadlift and their barbell variations, `rangeLift`, and any barbell / trap-bar / Smith squat, hinge, hip thrust or press) keeps its logged warm-ups open before set 1 (its first card, `rampsUp`), where they can still tune today's weight (`warmupTunes`); every other lift folds them. Every warm-up is a row typed into like a set (reps, effort, weight, video), saved as it's typed, with a flame in the set column (dashed while new, never a "W" or an orange row); a swipe left shows Delete and the flame's menu has the same Remove (both with Undo, the set-removal rule below); one header line says what they are and did ("Warm-up · 2 sets · ✓ Suggested tuned", "too light to tune today's weight", `WARMUP_TOO_EASY`, Suggest off: "today 280 lb · tap set 1 to log it"), and logged warm-ups fold into it on accessories and once the working sets start (one tap opens them). A warm-up can be logged with 1–30 reps (`WARMUP_LOG_MAX_REPS`, `pl_warmup_sets_reps_check`, 20270425090000_warmup_reps_any_exercise.sql); the engine reads only warm-ups of 8 reps or fewer, only on a "last" lift and only where the weight is RPE-led (`warmupsRead`: never over double progression), and one far under today's working weight reads as nothing (`warmupRelevance`: 0 at 60% of history's working load for today's target, full at 75%, a smooth ramp; the card says "too light", `warmup: "light"`; of the 38 warm-ups logged to Nov 20 the heaviest of each card ran 77–100% of that day's first set, so none changed). Warm-ups never count as work, volume, PRs, rankings or completion (pl_warmup_sets). Accessories and variations on a rep range are coached by double progression (load-suggestion.ts note 12, `repProgression`, `progressionPlan` in the card: not a competition lift, not a barbell squat / bench / deadlift, not a back-off card): last session's working weight (the heaviest it did inside the range) goes up the smallest step they load once every set at it reached the top of the range at no more than the target effort (+0.5), stays while they build reps ("same weight · build to 12"), and comes down a step only when every set fell short of the range rated at or over the target; it compares a range only with itself (`repsText` on the history logs, `repRangeOf`: another range → the RPE model; unknown → never a drop), holds a never-lifted jump over 25% (`PROGRESSION_MAX_JUMP`), needs one session within 28 days (reps, not ratings, are what it reads), and "Next time" on a finished card reads today's sets the same way (`progressionNext`). Weights round to the steps the athlete loads on that exercise (`loadIncrement`: the smallest gap between weights they've typed in this unit, half to one times the default on a bar, up to three times on a stack or dumbbell rack, `equipmentKindOf`; a dumbbell rack defaults to 5 lb / 2.5 kg past the light ones) and land on a weight they've used (`snapLoad`); the competition squat, bench and deadlift keep plate steps. Backtested Nov 20 2026 on 1,754 real rep-range accessory sessions (read-only, about three quarters of 200 days): median error against the weight loaded 6.25% → 5.56%; holds on a weight they actually use 77.7% → 98.9% (error 5.41% → 2.22%); other accessory cards 65.3% → 91.1% (6.25% → 3.77%); after a session at the top of the range the old engine kept 66% on the same weight, and when athletes did go up, 97.5% still reached the range (on 972 such pairs, holding the weight hit the top again 92% of the time). The logger is monochrome: no orange warm-ups, no blue Suggested chip or empty weight box; Suggested is a quiet bordered chip, information beside the sets, never a competing primary action — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach asked for warm-up logging that's consistent, quick and secondary (collapsed on accessories, no W / LWU; later that day the automatic Last warm-up row and its suggested load went too: warm-ups only through + Warm-up), exercise-specific suggestions that don't force a 1RM onto rep-based accessories or let a light warm-up mislead, respect equipment increments, never depend on input order, and a premium monochrome logger without orange and blue accents.
- Which unit an exercise card opens in is one function, `resolveWorkoutRowUnits` (workout-unit-resolution.ts), one unit per exercise per workout: the athlete's saved preference for the exercise (a tap on the card's kg | lb), the unit of its newest loaded logs (`recentUnit`: its last 3 sets with a weight, never a vote over a mixed window; a set with no weight says nothing), the squat / bench / deadlift family's newest logs, the unit nearly all their recent loaded logs are in (`athleteHabitUnit`: at least `ATHLETE_HABIT_MIN_LOGS`, `ATHLETE_HABIT_SHARE` in one unit), then the account's unit (`athleteChosenUnit` = `clients.preferred_weight_unit` once `weight_unit_confirmed_at` is set; only when the logs show no habit, since it's also the unit for maxes, bodyweight and analytics), and only then the coach's row unit, the library default and the competition-lift kg default. The habit is read on every workout (accessory days too), the reads are kept on the phone for a day (`UNIT_CACHE_MS`) and the habit last read is a per-device first-paint hint (`readUnitHabitHint`), so a card never opens in one unit and flips once the logs arrive. The card's switch is a small two-way "kg | lb" with the one in use filled (`UnitSwitch`), never a lone word: both units on show is what says it can be changed — why: Nicolas logs everything in lb, but his first Competition Deadlift (Oct 10 2026, no deadlift-family history: RDLs are a hinge) opened in kg, 135 / 185 / 205 lb saved as kg, and his next deadlift day suggested 440 lb with a ~380 lb last warm-up (fixed by hand, logged in `pl_row_result_repairs`); then on Nov 16 2026 Jared McIntyre's account said kg (stamped "confirmed" by 20261116090000_weight_unit_confirmed.sql, never chosen; all 68 of his weigh-ins are lb) while 148 of his last 150 sets were lb, so every exercise he'd never logged opened in kg (15 lb Cable Hip Adduction and a 25 lb Pull Up saved as kg, which then kept those cards in kg), his Competition Deadlift's 8 October kg sets outvoted November's lb ones, and a lone "LB" in the corner didn't read as something to tap; the same scan found 41 more lb numbers saved as kg on cards that opened in the coach's kg (Shaina, Alyssa, Fionna), relabelled Nov 17 2026 with their old unit in `pl_row_result_repairs`.
- A weight in a post reads the same for everyone, both units (Nov 17 2026, dual-weight.ts, pure and tested in post-dual-weights.test.ts; `WeightOther`, components/dual-weight.tsx): the unit the athlete typed it in leads, as typed (to 2 decimals, read back from the set's 4-decimal kg mirror, so 315 lb and 72.5 lb stay exact), and the other unit follows, smaller and muted, one decimal, worked out from the same stored kg (never from the rounded primary, never relabelled); totals are whole numbers with separators (`dualTotal`). The database names each weight's unit (20270408090000_post_weights_recorded_unit.sql: `unit` on `community_workout_stats`' top lift and each PR, the workout's own `unit` (the unit most of its weights were typed in, which its volume reads in), `best_unit` on `community_workout_exercises`, `prev.unit` on the composer's extras; derived only, no record changes, the stats cache cleared once) and Wednesday Wins' prose names a PR as "185lbs (83.9kg) x 8" in its set's own unit (`community_fmt_set`). A viewer's unit is only the fallback for an older set that never said its own, shown to a plate step as before (`recordedLoad`). Every post surface goes through it: the feed's workout card and photo stats (`WorkoutHero`, the stats under a photo), a Pulse and its story frame, a film slide's set on its screen and over the clip (`SetDetail`, the slide caption's `other`), the workout page's volume and exercises, the share composer's preview, the Home crew deck, Add to story's card (`shareCardWords`), every painted share card (PR, photo, stats, volume, receipt, progress, sticker; `withOther`, `statRow` subs), the after-workout recap's records (each set's own unit, `setUnit`) and its story card, and the Wins / Recap pictures, whose crew-wide totals lead in `CREW_UNIT` (lb: 94% of the crew's sets) for every viewer; a small grid tile keeps the lift as typed only. A post that hides weights shows neither unit — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for every post to show both units with the athlete's own as the hero; until then each viewer saw someone else's lift converted into their own unit (a 315 lb bench read "143 kg" to a kg viewer and nothing said it was typed in lb), Wins rounded a 72.5 lb set to 73, and the painted Wins / Recap pictures differed by viewer.
- A weight is one physical load with one true unit, everywhere (weight-unit audit, Nov 17 2026, weight-unit-audit.test.ts): a logged set is its entered pair (`entered_value` / `entered_unit` = `actual_load` / `actual_load_unit`) with the kg / lb mirrors the `tg_pl_row_results_sync_units` trigger computes, and anything that compares, sums or ranks loads reads a mirror or converts by the stored unit (`displayLoadInUnit`, `physicalKg`; block analytics, Progress comparison's best set and delta, plan vs actual), never a raw `actual_load` beside one in the other unit. A display-unit switch converts what's shown (4 decimals) and saves the stored pair untouched (`persistedLoadForDisplayValue`), however often it's toggled; but the card's kg | lb with weights on it in the unit it's leaving asks first (`UnitSwitchSheet`, unit-relabel.ts: "They were lb · Keep 20 lb" or "Convert to lb · 20 kg = 44.1 lb", in the person's own numbers), and keeping them saves each set and warm-up again with the same number in the new unit through the set's own save (`relabelIntent` in the row's unit effect, never adopted as saved; a set already stored in the new unit shows its own number, `relabeledLoad`), every card of the exercise together, Undo the same way back; a "495 lb" pick on a kg card never asks (`switchUnitNow`) — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach typed his Cable Hip Abduction's 20 lb on a card that had opened in kg, tapped lb to say so, and the box converted it into 44.1 lb (too heavy) instead of keeping his 20. A % of a max is worked out and rounded once, in the unit on screen (`computeRowLoad` with the card's `activeUnit`), with the max's own `rounding_step` only in the max's unit (an lb max's 5 read as 5 kg made 100% of 600 lb show 595 lb); a variation max read from its source converts the source's numbers into its own unit (`effectiveMax`). A fixed prescription lives in one column, its unit's: the builder clears the other column on every load it writes, the unit chip moves the number to the chosen unit, a row without `load_unit` reads its unit from the column holding the number, and copies carry `load_unit`; a % row's load columns are never its prescription unless `manual_override` (`prescribedFixedKg`, which the AI brief reads). AI never guesses a unit silently: Cleo fills a bare load with its row's unit, else the athlete's (`withLoadUnits`, never a blanket lb), Edit with AI warns on a load without a unit and on one about 2.2× off their last top set (`looksLikeUnitMixup`), and a template personalized for an athlete reads in the brief's unit. `weight_unit_confirmed_at` means the person chose it: stamps the 20261116090000 migration made for every kg account were cleared (20270406090000, only sub-millisecond database stamps) so the app asks; and the 63 light lb sets 20260625000001 had multiplied by 2.2046 into kg were restored where their own nearby lb logs agreed (`pl_row_result_repairs`), the ambiguous ones left for review — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for kg / lb to be reliable across the whole app (preferences, analytics, PRs, e1RMs, volume, AI) with no historical record changed on a guess.
- A back-off on its own card (the same exercise as a card before it today: a top set, then its back-offs) gets its suggested load from today's sets of that exercise, read as if they were on its own card (`carryToday` / `carryKg`, workout-day-read.ts, only from cards before it, so a top set's "Next time" never reads the back-offs after it): through the RPE chart, less `BACKOFF_FATIGUE` (6%, the fatigue a top set leaves, measured: see the back-off backtest in load-suggestion.ts), ahead of its own sets once they're in, and history keeps its 25% only to bring the number down, never up (`estimateFor`, `model.carried`, header note 9). The card's ⓘ shows the top set it follows and its e1RM (`carriedTop`, `data-testid="load-backoff-math"`). The capped day factor (±8%) is only for a variation of the family (paused, tempo) — why: on Nov 13 2026 Jared's deadlift top set went 495 lb × 4 @ 10 and the 2 × 4 @ 6 back-offs were still suggested at 485 ("Live +1%"): history (495 × 3 @ 5 the week before) read as "495 for 4 at anything heavy", so the top set rated 7, 8, 9 or 10 all gave the same back-off; and on 214 real first back-off sets (12 athletes) the chart conversion alone was a median 8.3% off and more than 5% too heavy 54% of the time, since athletes load a back-off about 6% under it even at the top set's own RPE (5.5% and 27% with the allowance); now 495 × 4 @ 10 gives 420, @ 8 gives 440, and a harder top set always means a lighter back-off.
- How a lift's history becomes a suggested load depends on the lift (`HISTORY_PROFILES`, `liftKindOf` in load-suggestion.ts, passed as `lift` to `buildLoadModel` / `todayVsUsual` by the logger and the day read): the competition squat, bench and deadlift (`competition_lift_type` / `is_competition_lift`) trust the RPE chart more and match past sets tightly on reps and RPE (alpha 0.85, kernels 0.5), since their programs move between heavy and light days and they're rated with care; every other exercise, variations included, stays near the load actually used and leans on the last session (alpha 0.3, 2-day half-life), since accessories repeat and progress from last time. A card's first suggestion of the day (nothing logged today, not a back-off read from today's top set) reads a past set on a back-off card (a card the program calls one, or a later card of the same exercise that day: `rowId` / `rowOrder` on the history logs) as the top set it implies, ÷ (1 − `BACKOFF_FATIGUE`) (header note 11, `backoffsAsTopSet`, one folded line in the ⓘ), never on a back-off card itself (a later card of the exercise today, `backoffCards`: its own number reads them as logged until today's top set carries); once a set is logged today, a back-off carried from today's top set and the day read across cards (`todayVsUsual`, `backoffs: "as_logged"`) read history as logged. No progression trend is projected forward: RPE-rated sets already carry it (a lift that's moving rates the same load easier) and a falling stretch is usually a light week athletes bounce back from. Change them only with a backtest on real logs (header notes 3 and 11 record the last ones) — why: on Nov 14 2026, 2,807 real first sets of a lift's day predicted from history alone were a median 9.0% off on the competition lifts (where the top set matters most) and 5.3% on the rest with one setting for both; the profiles took them to 7.7% and 4.4%, better in both halves of the time range and for 18 of the 24 athletes with enough sets, worse for 1 (none on the competition lifts), with next-set suggestions and the shown range improving too. On Nov 15 2026 the coach asked whether the engine should follow progression trends; on 674 real squat / bench / deadlift-family first sets a forward trend (damped, capped, gated) was no better in any form and made more predictions worse than better, while back-offs read as logged were the real bias: Jared's 425 lb × 4 @ 6 with 405 back-offs suggested 410–430 for the same 4 @ 6 the next week; read as top sets, competition lifts went 7.57% → 7.02% median off (median bias −2.3% → −0.6%, still slightly light once outliers are trimmed), both halves of the time range better, 4 of 12 athletes better and none worse, accessories unchanged; and on back-off cards with nothing carried (147, 10 athletes) reading them as top sets came out +2.6% heavy and 39.5% over 5% too heavy, against −1.5% and 27.2% read as logged, fewer too-heavy in both halves of the time range and for 6 athletes (none more).
- A percentage prescription in the logger keeps the coach's words on the prescription line ("2 × 4 @ 92.5% | RPE 6", never swapped for the weight in both units) and says what it is today in the card's strip, where an RPE card has Suggested (`PlannedLoadCell`, `plannedLoadView` in planned-load.ts, `data-testid="load-planned"`): "92.5% of top set · ↓ 400 lb" ("After your top set" until it's logged), "62.5% Comp Squat TM · ↓ 147.5 kg", the weight as Suggested's own quiet chip (`FillChip`: one tap logs the first open set at it and the untouched sets below with it, through that set's own number, `logOpenSetsAt` → the set row's handle → `saveCompletionStatus({ weight })`); its ⓘ is its own button and says how it's worked out ("92.5% of your top set (435 lb) is 402.4 lb, rounded to 400 lb"), that the RPE is a cap (a range's cap is its top, `rpeCap`) or, after a set over it, the lighter weight the sets left carry (the chip follows it). The % line under the prescription (`percentLoadLine`, percent-load-line.ts, `data-pct-line`) shows only when the strip can't: "none on file yet, go by RPE" when no max resolves, never a made-up load; a variation read through its competition lift at 100% names that lift (`maxReference`). No weight is ever put in a box for them: an untouched set's weight box starts empty (blue) on every card, whatever the plan, until a tap on Suggested or the planned weight fills it or they type one; the box itself stays blank (never the suggested number in it, `WeightValueInput` gets no `suggested`), and one tap on the set's number logs the set at the weight the strip shows with its reps and RPE and, exactly like typing that weight, fills and logs the untouched sets below (`onCascadeFromSet` after the save; the older way the coach prefers: one tap logs the exercise, a set that went differently is changed) (`quickLoad` → `quickWeight` in `saveCompletionStatus`: the coach's planned weight, else Suggested while it's on; a range, `loadPicks`, logs at its middle, the engine's own estimate (`hint.target`), and a weight picked by feel inside it is typed in set 1; the number's label says "Log set 2 at 375 lb"; with nothing to log at and only the weight missing, the tap opens that set's weight box inside the tap, `weightOnlyMissing` → `WeightValueInput editRef`, never an "Enter reps and weight" toast), so a whole exercise done as planned or suggested is one tap (the number or the chip, the same path). A back-off at a % of today's top set reads only the top set they logged (`topSetBasisForRow`: completed sets), never a weight Suggested filled in that isn't lifted yet, and moves with how that top set went against its prescribed RPE before the first back-off (`topSetAdjustment`, load-reference.ts): over the top of its range, down by what the RPE chart says the miss means, rounded down and never above the plain % (the cap beats the percentage); a clear point under the bottom (`TOP_SET_EASY_BY`), up by half of one RPE's worth and never more, however easy it was rated (the coach's "up toward the top of the range if it moved well"); on plan or unrated, the coach's %. It shows as the chip's weight with one quiet line under the sets ("Top set hit RPE 8.5, over its 7 · back-offs 175 kg", `data-backoff-adjust`, the cap line's slot, which takes over once a back-off goes over the cap) and the reason in the ⓘ; weights already in the boxes stay theirs. RPE-only back-offs already read the top set this way through the engine (`carryToday`); and a set holding only the plan (no weight, reps / RPE as prescribed) is never stored as typed or shown as an amber draft (the set save's `onlyPlan`, `savedBeyondPlan`, `repsOwn` / `rpeOwn`). Jared's hybrid back-off notes lead with the % the card fills in and give the range after it ("Back-offs start at 92.5% of today's top set (the app works it out from the weight you log), up to 95%.", 20270323100000_backoff_notes_lead_with_planned_pct.sql, old text in `program_note_backoff_wording_backup`). A load worked out from a max (`training_max` / `1rm` / `est_1rm`) is the coach's load like a top-set back-off (`pctLoadIsCoachs` → `coachOwnsLoad`): the RPE suggestion engine never suggests over it and waits while the max loads. Maxes resolve by the exercise's library name, block-scoped rows win (`buildMaxIndex`), so a hybrid program's references are block-scoped `pl_client_maxes` (held for the block, reviewed at its end) and a secondary reads its competition lift through `source_lift` at 100% — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach moved Jared McIntyre's off-season program to hybrid % + RPE loading (20270318090000_jared_hybrid_loading.sql: back-offs % of the day's top set, High Bar Squat / 2ct Paused Bench % of a 235 / 150 kg block Training Max, RPE the cap), and the logger showed only the weight once it had one (the % and its reference only in a note) while the Suggest engine could push a heavier weight over a % prescription; and on Nov 15 2026 his Monday squat back-offs read 400 lb (92.5% of the 435 lb Suggested had filled into his top set before he'd lifted it) under "once the top set is logged", kept it after the top set was cleared, clearing a weight saved the planned 4 reps @ 6 as a draft (both back-offs turned amber), the prescription turned into "@ 391 lb / 177.5 kg" once a top set was in, and the note's "Back-offs: 92.5–95%" sat right under "@ 92.5%" reading like a second prescription (on 109 real top set → first back-off pairs at the same reps, 8 athletes, the back-off was a median 93.3% of the top set, 91.9% when rated the same, so 92.5% is the right planned weight and 95% the top of the range); and he liked the top set card's empty weight box with Suggested's chip beside it better than a back-off's boxes pre-filled with the plan; and on Nov 16 2026 that the older way was better ("they would just fill one box and then it would fill the rest below and mark as complete"): a tap on a set's number logged only that set, and the chip only put drafts in the boxes, each still needing a tap; then he asked for the back-offs to adapt before the first one when the top set runs harder or easier than prescribed: on 132 real top set → first back-off pairs (8 athletes, the truth the load at the back-off's own target from what they logged) the plain % ran a median 5.2% heavy after a harder top set (55% more than 5% too heavy; 11 pairs) and 3.8% light after a clear point easier (8 pairs), against −0.3% (27%) and 2.3% light with the adjustment, on-plan pairs unchanged.
- A past day's sets are fixed from the exercise's History sheet, inside the workout (`ExerciseHistorySheet` `editable`, the logger passes `!readonly`): Edit on a day turns its sets into boxes (weight with its own kg / lb chip, reps, RPE; bodyweight / assisted loads stay as they are), and Save writes only what changed (`setEditPatch`, history-set-edit.ts, checked by `setDraftIssue`: a kg number over the cap that's a normal number of pounds says "Tap kg to make it lb") as one update per set with `.select()` so a row RLS refused never reads as saved. The unit chip relabels the number, never converts it (a day logged in the wrong unit is fixed by flipping it), and the database works out the kg / lb mirrors (`tg_pl_row_results_sync_units`) and keeps the workout's version (`workout_version_on_edit`). Saving refreshes everything the set feeds (`HISTORY_EDIT_QUERY_KEYS`: the sheet, rep records, "Last time" and the load suggestions, the open workout's sets, the unit habits) — why: the coach and the athletes found a wrong number in history (Nicolas's 205 kg deadlift that was 205 lb) and had no way to fix it without leaving the workout and asking for a database repair.
- A typed weight is never dropped (`WeightValueInput`, weight-value-input.tsx): leaving the box is how most weights go in (an iPhone number pad has no Done key), so a number over the unit's cap (`WEIGHT_CAP`, 450 kg / 1000 lb) opens "Check the weight" on Done and on leaving the box alike (`askAboveCap`), never a silent cancel. On a card with the KG / LB switch, a kg number that's a normal number of pounds (`overCapAsPounds`) is offered as "495 lb" first: it picks with `unit: "lb"`, the set row switches the card (`onSwitchUnit` = the card's toggle, so the exercise stays in lb like a tap on LB) and saves the number once the switch has rendered (`unitPickRef`, applied in the row's unit effect, never converted as if it were kg); "495 kg" and Edit weight stay. The sheet is portalled to `<body>` with `data-state="open"` (in Focus it sat under the ‹ / Next bar and let swipes through). `pl_row_result_repairs.field` also takes `entered_value`, `entered_unit` and `completed_at` (20270212090000_set_repairs_weight.sql) — why: on Nov 7 2026 Jared McIntyre's 495 lb deadlift on a kg card read as 495 kg, over the cap, and vanished every time he left the box.
- Dropdown menus (`@/components/ui/dropdown-menu`) open on a finger's tap (the click), not as it lands (Radix opens on `pointerdown`); mouse and keyboard work as Radix does, and `DropdownMenu` holds the open state (or follows the caller's `open`) so the trigger can — why: a scroll or a Focus swipe that started on a ⋯ popped its menu open.
- Goals & Setup's "days a week" and "which days" are the program schedule, not a second copy: `clients.committed_training_days` is the one schedule, `client_goals_setup.training_days_per_week` is always its number of days and `available_weekdays` always includes them (plus any other days the person could train). The availability step edits the exact days (`AvailabilityStep`: a number fits them with `pickTrainingDays`, keeping the days they train and spreading out from their available days; tapping a day changes the number), hydrated from the live schedule, never a device draft; `saveGoalsSetupFn` writes new days as the schedule and moves upcoming workouts (`writeCommittedDays` + `realignClientToCommittedDays`, `scheduleForGoalsSave` in schedule-sync.ts, also for older apps that send only the number), and the `clients_sync_committed_days_to_goals` trigger mirrors a schedule changed anywhere else back into Goals & Setup without raising the coach's "Goals & Setup updated" task (`jf.goals_schedule_sync`, 20270114090000_goals_follow_training_schedule.sql). It saves an existing row with an update, never an upsert (an upsert runs the INSERT trigger and logged "(created)" on every save) — why: Marc's Goals said 3 days (Mon/Wed/Fri) while his program ran 4 (Mon/Tue/Wed/Fri), six other clients had drifted the same way, and Generate Next Program and the coach read the wrong number.
- A JF member's plan starts on their committed training days: `StartPlanDialog` fits the days to the plan's workouts a week (`fitTrainingDays`, plan-days-fit.ts: keep their days, add or drop the fewest, spread out) and Start waits until every workout has a day; `startLibraryPlan` refuses a day count that doesn't match `member_plans.days_per_week`, saves the days as the committed schedule (`writeCommittedDays`) and then realigns. `clients.committed_training_days` is the one schedule: the setup wizard writes it too (`updateMyMemberProfile`), and `clients_sync_committed_days_to_member` mirrors it onto `app_members` — why: a 5-day plan on a 4-day week put Day 5 on a day the member never committed to, and the wizard's days never reached the calendar.
- JF members accept the JF Membership Agreement (the `membership-agreement` legal document, published from Admin → Legal), never the Coaching Agreement (`getAgreementSigningContext`, `signCoachingAgreement` and `adminRequestAgreement` refuse `self_serve`). From v3 (`IN_APP_SIGNING_FROM_VERSION`) the app asks each member once per launch (`MemberAgreementGate`: after setup, never over another sheet or in View as client; Account → Agreement keeps it) to tick every `MEMBERSHIP_AGREEMENT_ACKS` and type their name, recorded in `legal_acceptances` (`signature_method = 'typed_name'`, the exact text in `rendered_snapshot`); checkout's box accepts the terms but not the health and risk ticks. Account → Privacy & legal (`listMyLegalStatus`, which the acceptance gate reads too) lists the membership agreement and the Recurring Billing Disclosure only to members, by the same rule (`legalDocApplies`, legal-applies.ts): the agreement from `IN_APP_SIGNING_FROM_VERSION`, the disclosure only with a paid subscription, never to a coaching client; every other document goes to everyone — why: members train alone with no waiver on file, a newly published version has to reach every member, and once that list loaded (it failed for everyone until Nov 6 2026) coaching clients were asked to accept the membership agreement and free members the old $29-a-month terms.
- A client's Nutrition Update Request reaches the coach from the server, never from the phone: Submit fires `form_submitted` (`notifyAppEventFn`), which sends the coach `nutrition_submitted` at once and then writes the AI targets + meal plan there (`startNutritionPlan` in nutrition-ai-plans.server.ts: once per submission, `planBusy`), then `nutrition_plan_ready`. `runPlan` writes its `nutrition_ai_plans` row before anything else, so any failure is on the row as an error. The coach's panel (`NutritionRequestPanel`) shows the request they answered (`currentRequest`, against the latest send, `nf_assignments.updated_at`) and writes a missing or stalled plan itself (`planMissing`). A one-off form someone was asked for stays shown as submitted after Submit until it's asked for again (`sentSinceAsked`), never a blank copy. "Asked" is `nf_assignments.updated_at`, and every request moves it: a coach's send upserts it and the monthly `enqueue_monthly_nutrition_updates` updates it too (never `do nothing`; it also skips anyone who sent one in the last 14 days, coaching clients only, 20270215090000_nutrition_request_asked_at.sql). A request's chat card (`FormRequestChatCard`) shows the answer sent at or after it, whenever its draft was started (`requestCardSubmission`, the same rule as the Forms tracker's `matchFormRequests`), else a draft being filled in — why: Jared's draft from Oct 4, sent Nov 8 for the Oct 26 request, read "Start" to him and "Waiting for client" to the coach with no answers or plan, and the monthly request left "asked" at Oct 4, so the next month's form would have opened as the old one. Also: the plan used to start from the phone after Submit (a lazy file gone after a publish, or a closed app, dropped it silently), and with the coach's only alert being "plan ready", David's request never reached him; a blank form right after Submit made him fill it in twice, and the panel then showed that blank one as "Client filling it out".
- Native forms (`ClientFormRenderer`) are built for a thumb and read like a questionnaire: choices are buttons (`ChoiceButtons`, `choiceLayout`: one "I agree" is a tick, up to four short options share a row, five or more of `CHIP_MAX_CHARS` or less wrap as chips (age classes, body fat ranges), the rest are full-width 46px rows; dropdown and single choice alike, tap again to clear; selected is neutral and firm, `border-foreground`, never the brand red, which is Submit's), text and number boxes are 48px with 16px text, a number opens the number pad (`inputMode="decimal"`, never `type="number"`). Nothing is fixed or sticky over the questions (Nov 17 2026): a slim line at the top says the required questions answered ("3 of 8 required", "All 8 required answered"; a checklist form counts items) and whether it's saved (`ProgressLine`, `data-form-progress`: Saves as you go → Saving… → ✓ Saved, the error in red), and the end of the form is Submit, full width, with "N required questions left · Go to the first" above it (`data-form-missing`, which outlines and scrolls to it) and one line on where it goes ("Your answers go to your coach in your chat"; a form they keep updating has Done instead); the page ends just clear of the tab bar (`.form-page-end` gives back the shell's 140px floating-button room on phones). A question marks the fewer kind, never both (`requiredMark`: mostly required forms say "Optional" beside the rest, else an asterisk on the required ones), the status badge shows only once it's news (sent, reviewed), and an answer that's still what the app knows folds to one line with its source and Change (`data-form-compact`, `COMPACT_TYPES`: short text, numbers and choices whose answer `sameAnswer`s the profile's). A text or number question can carry one-tap answers above the box (`validation.quick`, `quick_exclusive` for "None"-style ones, `toggleQuick` in form-quick.ts) and a number a range and unit (`validation.min` / `max` / `unit`, `numberIssue`), all set per question in the form builder; Submit outlines what's missing or out of range and scrolls to the first. Typed answers save a beat after the last key and on leaving the box, one write at a time per question, and everything waiting is saved before Submit (`flushSaves`); follow-up questions follow what's on screen (`liveAnswers`) — why: dropdowns and small radios were fiddly on a phone, "None" / "That's everything" had to be typed, David's bodyweight went in as 2010 lb, Submit sat behind the tab bar, and a save per keystroke could land out of order; and on Nov 17 2026 the coach found the Competition form bulky on a phone: a floating footer (the count, "Saves as you go", a progress bar and a big red "Send my plan") covered the questions, "2 of 8 answered" sat beside questions numbered to 11, the age classes were seven tall red-outlined rows, and answers the app already knew were asked again in full.
- A member's suggested nutrition targets: one active `member_nutrition_targets` row per member (partial unique index, never `UNIQUE (member_id, active)`). The calculator is one screen of big taps with plain range checks (`bodyInputIssues`, calc-input.ts) and a live result over one Save; a save paints the row into `["m-nutrition-targets"]` at once, refreshes `nutrition-dashboard`, `m-targets-history` and `water-target`, and moves an auto water target with it; on the page the targets card leads with a small Recalculate — why: the old constraint failed every third save, the day's bars kept the old targets, and a full-width Recalculate card with repeated disclaimers took the top half of the screen.
- The phone top bar centres the JF Effect mark (`ShellBrand`: Crew's logo and italic name, the staff shell's name small under it) between search and the light / dark switch on the left (every account, clients included: staff search opens the palette, anyone else More with its search field focused, `focusSearch`) and More / you on the right, 48px plus the safe area (`--shell-topbar-h: 49px`; `TabRefresh` at `3rem + 5px`, the same as Crew's bar) — why: the coach wants the brand centred and clean on every screen, matching Crew.
- How To on an exercise card (`ExerciseHowToButton`, exercise-how-to.tsx) is part of the logger's own code, never a lazy chunk: the tap opens the sheet in the same frame, the button stays in the row while it's up (never swapped for a spinner or taken out), the touch-down prefetches the guide and the live video set (`exerciseGuideQuery`, `exerciseVideoSetGlobalQuery`, the same queries the sheet reads), every demo (`ExerciseDemoVideo`, How To and everywhere else) is a cover with Play until it's tapped: only that tap loads the Vimeo / YouTube player (`demoEmbedUrl(url, { autoplay: true })`, with sound, no second tap in the iPhone app) and takes the audio (`quietAppSounds` first, then `holdPlaybackSession`), never just opening the guide; a reload while a demo plays is reported on the next launch (`markDemoPlaying` / `takeDemoCrumb`, demo-crumb.ts: iOS ending the page vs the app's own reload, which names itself with `noteReloadReason` before every `location.reload()`); the sheet's bars are solid (no backdrop blur over the player), and anything that breaks inside it stays in the sheet (`GuideBoundary`). `lazyWithRetry` renders a chunk that's already fetched straight away (no Suspense fallback) — why: the lazy sheet flashed a spinner in place of the button (React holds a shown fallback ~300ms even for a preloaded chunk), the row shifted, the player started mid-slide, a chunk gone after a publish turned the button into "Update app", an error in the guide replaced the whole workout with "Workout didn't load properly", and loading the player and switching the phone's audio the moment How To opened (cutting the person's music off) is when the iPhone app reloaded on the first tap.
- The finish review ("How'd it go?", `WorkoutReviewEditor` as the quick finish) pops the moment every set is logged (`REVIEW_PROMPT_DELAY_MS` after the last one): "logged" is one reading for the Focus bars, the progress ring and the review (`workoutSetProgress`, workout-set-progress.ts: each exercise's sets on screen, 1…`sets`, stamped done by `isSetLogComplete` and holding a value; skipped ones count, an extra set on one exercise never stands in for a missing one on another). `shouldPromptReview` decides: settled data, online, not finished, once per time it becomes fully logged (Close keeps it shut until a set is cleared or added; marked shown only when it actually opens), the athlete also on opening a fully logged unfinished workout, a coach viewing as a client only after logging a set from that screen (`loggedHere`, set by the page's `refresh`). It never pops while a box on the workout is focused: the last set counts as done at once, but the review waits until the person leaves the box (`reviewPromptDelay`: `REVIEW_AFTER_TYPING_MS` after leaving one, `REVIEW_EFFORT_GRACE_MS` when a timed set finished the workout with no RPE yet, `timedSetAwaitingEffort` on `lastLoggedSet`, re-timed on every focus change) and never blurs a box to open. It never waits on the network (`markInProgress` runs behind it) and stops the rest timer (nothing left to rest for) — why: the cardio walk at the end of every workout counted as done the moment its calories or timer went in, so the review popped over it before RPE could go in; it was off in View as client, a refetch inside its 250ms wait cancelled it for good, it waited on a server call, a summed set count popped it early and then never again, and bodyweight / reps-only / weight-only exercises never counted as logged.
- Pain is reported on an anatomical body map and tracked as dated, structured reports (Nov 20 2026, the map Nov 21 2026; src/lib/pain/, pure and tested in pain-history.test.ts and pain-anatomy.test.ts; 20270428090000_pain_reports.sql, 20270430091000_pain_sites.sql): the review's "Anything hurt?" → Yes opens `PainMap` (pain-map.tsx) full screen by itself the first time (`PainReporter`, pain-reporter.tsx, a Radix dialog over the review, always dark, Escape and ← stepping back one level: details → zoomed → whole body → the review, never out of it by a tap outside). The drawing is an original layered 2D illustration, never a 3D model or a licensed asset (atlas-data.ts, built by atlas.ts: front, back, right side and left side as four tabs, paired parts drawn once and mirrored, the front's screen-left the athlete's right and the back's their left, a deeper layer for the structures under the surface, `Show deeper`: levator scapulae, supraspinatus, pec minor, rhomboids, QL, glute min, piriformis, hip flexor), and a tap is tested against exactly the curve that's drawn (`smoothPolygon`, the same Catmull-Rom as `smoothPath`) on the same fit as the SVG's own (`fitView`, pad 0), so it lands on what's under the finger. A tap on an area zooms there (`frameBox`, at most `FRAME_MAX_SCALE`, keeping `labelRoom` clear each side), where the structures in view get labels in two columns with leader lines (`layoutCallouts`: never overlapping, at most 8, the focused area's first, at least a 40pt tap each and as tall as the name needs, `wrapLines` / `labelHeight`: a long name wraps to up to 3 lines, never cut off, "L" / "R" when both sides of one are in view) that say the plain name over the anatomical one; a tap on a structure or its label picks that spot (`PAIN_SITES`, sites.ts, ~75 spots in the existing regions, keys never renamed: side delt (lateral deltoid), patellar tendon, inside of the elbow…), "Not sure exactly? Pick the whole …" picks the region, Other side switches sides, two fingers pinch (a finger on a label still counts, `onButton`), one pans, and the list (`PlaceSearch`, `searchSites`: plain names, anatomical names and other names, each region's whole area first) finds anything by name. A spot is where they feel it, never what's injured: nothing on the map or for the coach or the AI calls a pick a diagnosis. The map is its own Radix dialog over the review sheet and renders its own `DialogPrimitive.Overlay`, because Radix puts the scroll lock on the overlay (without one the review's lock cancelled every swipe on the map, so a spot's details, the list and the tray couldn't scroll, Nov 21 2026); a pinch anywhere on it is the map's, never the page's. A spot's details are a fixed `DETAILS_SHARE` of the stage, the share the map frames the spot and lays out its labels above, pinned to the visible part of the screen while typing (`useVisualViewportBox`, swipe-to-close off and a pull down drops the keyboard, the Setup sheet's way), and a swipe up or down that starts on the 0–10 row scrolls them (`touch-pan-y`; a tap or a sideways slide picks). Each spot is its own report (`addPick`: a tap never removes one; × with Undo, or its details' bin) with a 0–10 score (`ScoreScale`, a finger slides along it; the one answer it needs, "Done (rate it later)" otherwise and the review's button waits, `unrated`) mapped onto the three severities every reader uses (`severityFromScore`: 0–3 mild, 4–6 moderate, 7–10 severe), then, only as each becomes relevant: what it feels like (sore, tight, dull, sharp, burning, tingling or numb, something else; swollen or a pop as "Also"), when it hurts (during exercise, after training, at rest, certain movements; `timing`, from which `trigger` is still filled for older readers, `cleanReports`), which exercise or movement (this workout's, or something else typed, only once a training timing is picked), whether it changed the session (only for during), a note behind "+ Add a note", and the follow-up below. The review lists each spot as a row with a picture of where it is (`PlaceThumb`), its score and what they added, a tap opening it again. Closing the review with answers not saved asks first (Save it / Discard / Keep editing, `requestClose`), and a pain report an interruption cut short is kept on the phone for a day per workout and comes back (`keepPainDraft` / `readPainDraft`, pain-draft.ts), forgotten on save or discard. A tap on a toast's Undo is never a tap outside an open sheet or dialog (`keepOpenForToasts`, and `[data-sonner-toast]` takes taps over a modal), which otherwise closed the sheet or couldn't be tapped at all. A place reported in the last 6 weeks asks "You flagged this Nov 16 (5/10). Since then? Better / Same / Worse / Something new" (the same spot first, else the region's, named when it's another spot; `followUpFor`, `answerFollowUp`), and only that answer links two reports (`same_issue`, `continues_id`): two reports in one spot are never assumed to be one injury. Each place is a `pain_reports` row written only by `pain_reports_save` (SECURITY DEFINER: the athlete, an admin, their coach in View as client, or the server; rows updated in place by review + region + side + site, so later links hold; `site`, `score`, `timing` added Nov 21 2026, `pain_level` the worst score, at least 1 for its constraint; readers fall back to the older columns while a database hasn't the new ones, `loadPainEntries`), and the review's `pain` / `pain_area` / `pain_level` are filled from them (`legacyColumns`: pain_area in the format it always had, pain_level the worst place), so the coach's chat card, the inbox's Pain filter, Program Intelligence and older apps read it as before; older reviews (pain_area chips, "Other" with the place in the note) and the check-in's typed pain details are read as stored (`entriesFromLegacyFeedback` / `entriesFromCheckinText`, a place read from their words marked `inferred`; the first review's "General" counts only when its note names a body part), never rewritten. One history for every reader (`buildPainHistory`, loaded by `loadPainEntries`, pain/load.ts, with the caller's own session): per region, new / recurring / persistent (3+ days across 3+ weeks or 14+ days in 6 weeks) / quiet (nothing in 21 days), the trend from what they said first then severity, sessions changed or stopped, exercises they named, and `reasons` the coach should look (a warning sign, severe or a stopped session in 14 days, worse, persistent, changing sessions, the same exercise twice); a one-off mild report is `minor` and needs nothing. Exercises it lines up with (`associations`, sessions.ts) count only on 3+ pain days, in 75%+ of them and 20 points more than in their sessions overall, only movements that can load that place (`PLAUSIBLE`), and never beside an exercise they named. Warning signs (safety.ts `redFlags`: numbness or tingling, with back pain urgent; a pop with swelling, severe pain or a stopped session; severe swelling; chest pain not clearly from a lift, urgent) and recovery suggestions (recovery.ts, coach-facing, at most 2 per place, each earned: assess once persistent / worse / stopping or changing sessions / a warning sign, a follow-up when an assessment was suggested and it's still coming up; modify the exercise they named; training stress; keep moving for backs and necks past a mild niggle; one comfort measure (heat for spine and shoulder blades, soft-tissue elsewhere) only for recurring muscular tightness or ache with no warning signs; sleep when short sleep keeps lining up; nothing the coach used for that place in 21 days unless it got worse since) are code, never a model. The athlete sees only a static safety notice (`needsSafetyNotice`: a severe place, numbness, a pop, swelling, chest pain not from a lift: get it checked, and the urgent signs); anything about training or recovery reaches them only in a message the coach writes or approves.
