The coach: the pain card in chat lists each place with what they added and opens `PainReviewSheet` (Pain history), as do Home's "Pain to look at" (`PainWatchCard`, `listPainWatchFn`: coaching clients with a flagged place and a report since he last looked; hidden at zero) and the Health card; the sheet shows each place, its reports and their words, the warning signs, What to consider and, on his tap, a reply drafted in his voice (`draftPainReplyFn`, the coach_write route on the OpenAI key, never Lovable credits: `PAIN_RULES`, `DRAFT_BANNED` checked with one retry, kept as a `draft` in `pain_coach_reviews` until a new report) that "Use in chat" puts in his composer (`queueComposerInsert` when the chat isn't open) and records the suggestions used (`used`); Mark reviewed keeps it off Home until something new. `pain_coach_reviews` is staff only. The weekly check-in recap (`analyzeMessengerCheckin`) and the training AIs (the health profile's pain line, `painAreas`) read the same history with `PAIN_RULES`: never a diagnosis, treatment or a program change, recovery only from its options — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach asked for pain reporting that takes a client 10–20 seconds and gives him something to act on: the 12-chip grid stored one free-text pain_area with one level for every place, half of all 42 reports since June were "Other" with the real place only in the note (neck, mid back, adductor, tennis elbow), nothing could say whether Dwayne's shoulder (10+ reports since June) was better or worse or tied to bench, and the check-in AI never saw any of it; then on Nov 21 2026 he asked for the area picker to become a detailed, accurate, zoomable anatomy (front, back, side, deeper layers, the exact muscle or tendon with its plain and anatomical names, left or right, several spots, 0–10, when it hurts and which exercise, revealed only when relevant) without implying a diagnosis or losing what's typed when the review closes by accident; his references were generated images, used for the direction, not copied.
- Pain is described three ways in one place, and recovery automation is a capability level, never a role (Nov 22 2026, describe.ts, picks.ts, pain-map.tsx, src/lib/recovery-automation/, cleo-capabilities.ts; 20270509100000_cleo_capabilities_recovery_automation.sql, supabase/tests/recovery-automation). The map's whole-body view has "Type or say where it hurts" (`DescribePanel`): the app's own reader first (`describePain`, pure, free, instant: places, sides ("both knees", a plural, "the left is a 5 and the right is a 3"), a score only when they said a number out of 10 (never a weight, sets or days), what it feels like, when, the exercise as their own workout's, "had to stop", "getting worse"), and only when it places nothing the server's (`readPainDescriptionFn`, route `pain_describe`, its answer kept only where it could have been copied from their words, `groundedPlaces`); a spoken description is turned into words (`transcribePainVoiceFn` → `transcribePainAudio`, never stored) by the first of `painTranscribers` that answers: OpenAI's gpt-4o-mini-transcribe, then whisper-1, then the gateway's Gemini (the transcriber Cleo's mic uses) only when both OpenAI ones refuse, each failure logged with the provider's own reason (Nov 23 2026: a 429 on gpt-4o-mini-transcribe gave the coach "Too many recordings" and nothing, his words thrown away); while recording the panel shows a live level meter (`VoiceLevel`) and the seconds, then "Turning what you said into words…", and a take that couldn't be turned into words is kept with Try again (`voiceFail`, the same audio resent, never said twice), every voice message inline under the box, never a toast over it; neither is metered with AI credits (reporting pain never costs a credit) and both are held to 20 a login per 10 minutes and 60 a day. It never guesses a spot: a muscle, tendon or joint only when they name it ("shoulder" stays the whole shoulder), "my back" / "arm" / "leg" come back as a question (which part), and every place is a row to confirm or change (`describedGroups`, Left · Right · Both per row) before "Add these" puts it in the review, then the first place without a score opens for its one question. Zoomed in, the drawing carries small numbered markers and the structures in view are a list under it (`inView`, plain name over the anatomical one, never cut off; "The whole …" first; the area's Left · Right), never floating callout boxes over the anatomy; the map's top sits below the iOS blur band (`SAFE_TOP` = max of the notch and `--app-safe-top`). A sided spot's details have Left · Right · Both (`setSideChoice`): Both is two reports rated on their own, sharing what it feels like, when, the exercise, the impact and the note (`SHARED_FIELDS`, `updatePaired`), and anything taken off (✕, a side, the bin) has Undo in the map itself, never a toast over it. In the coach chat a pain review is a compact "Recovery update" card (`WorkoutNoteCard`: the worst place's picture, `PlaceThumb`, each place with its own score, training impact, Open report). What Cleo may do is three levels decided by the database for every request and job (`cleo_has_feature` for the server, `cleo_feature` for the signed-in person with their two-step sign-in): assist (every active coach and admin, for their own clients: recovery analysis, drafts on request, program suggestions; the coach acts), automation (only through `cleo_entitlement_grants`: plan, account, trial or promo, with dates and revocation; not offered to anyone yet) and owner (`business_owners` only, never grantable: a trigger refuses an owner key in any grant, so no purchase reaches it); the owner's level adds his private voice and examples in automated messages (`owner.voice`: anyone else's drafts use a plain coach voice), follow-ups timed the evening before the next session that loads the area (`owner.training_aware`; a paid plan's are three days later) and oversight (`owner.controls`: the capability overview, the record). Recovery automation: a coaching client's pain in a workout review queues one reply per review for their coach when that coach's level and settings allow it (`tg_pain_reports_queue_reply`, two quiet minutes, never check-in pain, which stays in the check-in's reply); the worker (`coach-automation-tick`, every minute while due; `runAutomationTick` claims each item once, `coach_automation_claim`) checks the level and settings again, classifies the report in code (`classifyRecovery`: urgent = an urgent warning sign; review = any warning sign, 6/10+, stopped, worse, a pop, swelling or numbness, persistent or limiting, an unnamed place; else routine), drafts it (`writePainDraft`, route `recovery_write`, the same writer as the coach's on-demand draft) and routes it by mode (`routeItem`): off; manual (a draft waits in their pain sheet); approval, the default until the coach chooses (queued, the coach pushed); automatic (only routine reports, and only a draft that passes `autoMessageProblems`: never "I saw / I looked at", never a program change, never a diagnosis or medication; review and urgent always wait). Settings are per coach and category (`coach_automation_settings`, Pause) with per-client rules, written only through `coach_automation_settings_set` / `coach_automation_client_rule_set` within the caller's level; a lapsed level steps a mode down (`coach_automation_mode_for`). A message sent by itself is from the coach, `is_automated`, says "Sent automatically" under it (`recovery_auto` marker) and can be taken back for a day; one he approves is his own reply. Sending is idempotent (the chat is checked for the item's message first) and race-free: every worker write is scoped to the run holding the item (`held`: its status and lock), a send takes the item first (`sending`, with the exact text), so a Dismiss or Pause while a reply is being written always wins and the mode is read again right before anything goes by itself; an approved edit is stored with his name and only those words are ever sent, even after a cut-off run; a run is retried three times, then failed; only a report from the last day queues a reply. Items are written only by the server, each coach reads only their own (a second admin reads none of the owner's), and every step is in `coach_automation_events` (append-only). The coach works it in Messages' pinned Coaching queue row (`RecoveryQueueSheet`: To do, Coming up, Sent, Settings), in the client's pain sheet (send the waiting reply, the client's own rule, Adjust their next sessions → Adjust program with the places, applied only by him) and Settings → AI & Cleo (the levels) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found the map's labels covering the anatomy, sides slow, typing pain impossible and the report disconnected from coaching, and wanted automated, personalized recovery communication for his own clients that's a level above anything a coach could ever buy, enforced on the server, with independent coaches still getting real assistance.
- Coaching automation covers more than pain, in one queue (Nov 23 2026, src/lib/recovery-automation/categories.ts pure and tested, run.server.ts, reply-draft.server.ts; 20270510100000_coaching_automation_categories.sql, supabase/tests/recovery-automation; coaching-automation-categories.test.ts, coaching-automation-worker.test.ts): beside pain replies and their follow-ups, `workout_reply` (a workout review with a note, a session rated 1 or 2 out of 5 or a headline PR (the next rule), never one with pain, which is recovery's; `tg_workout_feedback_queue_reply`, three quiet minutes), `checkin_reply` (a weekly check-in once its recap is ready, `tg_messenger_checkin_queue_reply` on `ai_status` turning ready, never one he already replied to or one replaced) and `missed_session` (a planned, visible, required workout yesterday in the client's own time with nothing started or logged and nothing trained since, found by the daily scan, `scanMissedSessions` through pg_cron `coach-automation-missed-scan` with `{ "scan": "missed" }`, at most one a client in six days; outreach the client didn't start, so it needs `automation.follow_ups` like a follow-up). Each kind is read in code before anything is written (`workoutReplyRead`: a one-word note needs no reply; a question, a request to change their training, pain said without being reported, feeling unwell or a low rating is review; words that may need medical help are urgent. `checkinReplyRead`: the recap's urgency and red flags, a request for help, ticked pain. `missedSessionRead`: a second miss in two weeks is review), is cancelled when it no longer makes sense (the coach wrote to them himself since, they trained or logged it since, too old), and goes through the same modes, levels, claim-scoped writes, take-for-sending, idempotent delivery and record as pain (`routeDrafted`); only routine ever goes by itself. A check-in's reply for the owner is its recap's own suggested reply (already in his voice, on his card), rewritten only when it would go by itself and says something an automatic message can't; anyone else's is written in the plain voice (`writeCoachReply`, route `recovery_write`, from the facts the worker read: the workout's all-time PRs (≤ 10 reps, never a block or program best), heaviest set, plan vs actual from `planCheck`, their note and rating; the recap; the missed workout). The coach is pushed only for what isn't already in front of him (`pushesCoach`: pain always, a review or check-in only when urgent, a missed session never) and messages are filed like his own (`messageTypeFor`). Only the client's own words are ever answered: a review counts only when `review_updated_by` is the client's login and a pain report only when `created_by` is (a coach saving either in View as client never queues a reply to the client; 30 of 153 reviews in the 30 days to Nov 23 were coach-written). What a message answers is read again at the last moment, before one goes by itself and before an approval sends it (`stillGoes`: pain added to the review, the check-in replied to or replaced, the missed workout logged or trained past cancel it; the coach having written to them since sends one going by itself to him instead), and replying to a check-in himself cancels its waiting reply (`tg_messenger_checkin_reviewed_cancels_reply`). A safety read that fails fails the item (`must`, retried), never reads as "nothing there". An approved reply closes only what it answers: `tg_staff_reply_closes_reviews` skips an automation message unless it's a check-in reply, so approving a pain, workout or missed-session reply leaves his check-ins and forms waiting. A missed session counts logged sets too (`setsLoggedOn`, `trainedSince`: cardio on its own or a coach logging for them leaves no completion row), and one client's error never stops the scan. A workout reply cancelled for having nothing to answer is marked (`context.not_useful`) and queued again if they add to the note. Every kind defaults to ask-me-first; nothing sends itself until he chooses it. Messages' pinned row is the Coaching queue (`RecoveryInboxRow`, `RecoveryQueueSheet`), ordered by `queueOrder` (urgent, then judgment calls, then routine; pain, check-ins, follow-ups, set videos, workouts, progress check-ins, missed sessions; then oldest), each item showing what it was written from and setting its own client's rule in one tap. The owner gives a coach automation from Settings → AI & Cleo (`CoachGrants`: a trial, a promo or one account, with an end date, taken back in one tap; `grantCapabilityFn` / `revokeCapabilityFn` call `cleo_entitlement_grant` / `_revoke` as the owner, which refuse anyone else and any owner capability) — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach asked for the rest of the automation he'd specified (workout reviews, check-ins, missed sessions, a prioritized inbox and a way to grant the future paid tier) to be finished on the same safeguards as the pain replies.
- Coaching automation also answers what needs the coach's own eyes, and never by itself (Nov 23 2026, 20270511090000_coaching_automation_videos_progress.sql, supabase/tests/recovery-automation/scenario-videos-progress.sql; categories.ts `NEEDS_EYES`, `eyesProblems`): `video_reply` is one reply for the set videos a client sent that day (`tg_pl_set_videos_queue_reply`, fired when a set video's row gets its message, since `set_video_attach` writes the row first and `chat_video_lift` links a chat video later; only a video whose message is the client's own, sent in the last day; keyed `video_reply:<client>:<day in their zone>`; each new video pushes it back to 30 minutes after the last and has a waiting one written again with it), written from each set as its chat card says it and an accepted bar-speed read (`pl_set_video_analyses`: `velocity_accepted`, `set_mcv`, `velocity_loss_pct`), cancelled when the videos are gone or he's written to them since the last one; `progress_reply` answers a progress check-in turning `awaiting_review` (`tg_progress_submissions_queue_reply`, the client's own submission, ten quiet minutes), written from what they sent, their note, when their last check-in was, their bodyweight's 7-day average against four weeks before and their goal (never a body number with an eating-disorder history on their health profile), cancelled when he reviews it (`tg_progress_submissions_reviewed_cancels_reply`), and approving it marks the check-in reviewed (the card says so before he sends). Both are `review` risk with their reason in words, can never be set to send by themselves at any level (`coach_automation_mode_allowed` refuses `automatic` for them, `modeAllowed` too, and `coach_automation_mode_for` steps a stored one down to asking him), never push him (the client's upload already did), and their drafts never say how anything looked (`eyesProblems`: "looked clean", "depth was", "you look", "your physique"…; one redraft, then the card says the draft needs a look); the card says to watch the videos or open the check-in and add what he sees. A workout review is now queued whatever it says (still only the client's own), and the worker decides: a note, a low rating, or a headline PR (`headlinePr`, community.ts: an all-time PR at ≤ 10 reps on the session's main lift or a barbell squat, bench or deadlift), so a PR with no note gets a short reply leading with it; an accessory's record or a block best never does, and the facts call only all-time records PRs (they once listed a block best as one). A changed note has a waiting workout reply written again. A kind the worker doesn't know fails its item instead of being read as pain. My Voice (`coach_voice`) is the owner's: RLS lets only `is_business_owner` read it ("Owner reads coach voice"; every coach could), `getCoachVoiceFn` returns the profile only to the owner, `saveCoachVoiceFn` / `tryCoachVoiceFn` refuse anyone else ("Only the owner…"; any coach could overwrite it), and another coach's My Voice page shows only their own clients' nicknames and who's edgy-OK, with their drafts in the plain coach voice — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach asked for the rest of the automation he'd specified (exercise videos, progress updates, performance changes) to be completed; in the 60 days to Nov 23 he'd answered 5 of 39 set videos within a day and reviewed none of 14 progress check-ins, while a video or a photo is something only he can judge, so these are drafted for him and never sent; nutrition check-ins already ride on the weekly check-in's reply and a Nutrition Update's answer is the plan he publishes, which already lands in their chat.
- How a workout went against its prescription is one deterministic read, plan-vs-actual.ts (src/lib/workouts), never a model and never readiness, and it adds as little to the screen as it can (Nov 14 2026: less clutter, smarter behind the scenes). In the logger a set on target says nothing; one quiet line under a card's sets (`setGuidance`, `data-set-guidance`, amber dot for harder / short) appears only when the latest working set strays (harder = RPE ≥ `HARDER_BY` (1.5) over the top of the prescribed range, short = fewer reps than its bottom, easier = `EASIER_BY` (2) under its bottom with the reps done) AND it changes what comes next: the next set on this card ("RPE 10, planned 6 · next set ~435 lb": the logger's own suggestion when it has one, else the RPE chart from the set just done, rounded down a plate step; easier: "up to ~X lb next set", never more than `MAX_STEP_UP` and never past the RPE target, never on the coach's weight, `coachOwnsLoad`), or a later card today with the same exercise ("· go lighter on the back-offs", `backoffsFollow`). It stays quiet when nothing's left to change (that's the recap's), when the Suggest card on screen already moved the next set's number (`suggestionShown`), on an overshoot the RPE-cap line already says (`data-rpe-cap-next`), and once a later set lands back on target. The recap's "Vs the plan" (`planCheck` in `computeWorkoutSummary` → `summary.plan`, `PlanSection`, `data-plan-check`) replaces the old generic takeaways: "Done as planned" in one line, else at most 3 lines (harder / short / fewer / easier / extra; the working sets' average RPE `AVG_OFF_BY` (1.5) over the top or `EASIER_BY` under; an exercise over two cards said once per kind) and one "Next time" line; the set count stays the Sets tile's (no "14 of 14" twice, no Completed % tile beside it, no second Recovery score); warm-ups never count, a timed row counts only its sets, an exercise with nothing logged is "not logged" (listed under the stats), never missed; and the headline never cheers ("Crushed it!") over a lift that ran hard or came up short. Patterns across sessions stay Program Intelligence's (`progressionSignals`, the RPE-vs-target findings), coach-facing, never a new alert — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach wanted a set like 495 × 4 @ 10 against @ 6 recognized and answered in terms of what's actually left (no "next set" when there isn't one), then made clear the top priority is less clutter: normal performance needs no feedback, and advice belongs on screen only when it could change the next action.
- The Workouts page's calendars, Block view and readiness say what's true (Nov 14 2026): a past workout with nothing logged has its own mark (`workoutMark` kind `missed`, a hollow rose ring) on the week strip and the month, never the grey dot of one still ahead; a month cell is only its date and mark (`h-11`, the workout's name only labels a drag) with the day's workout under the calendar and one `MonthTally` line (done · missed · not finished · ahead, each beside its own mark, so it reads as the key too). The Block view's Priorities are one line per training day (`weekPriorities`, block-priorities.ts: ranked work only, a label starting Primary / Secondary / Tertiary / Quaternary, plain accessory roles like "Lats" and "Assistance" left out, a back-off row folded into its top set as "+ back-offs", nothing twice, a bare rank read with the exercise's name), the block tree loads each exercise's `competition_lift_type`, `is_competition_lift`, `exercise_category` and `movement_family` (`getBlockTree`: without them every family read "Other" and every role "Assistance"), an exercise card is its name, its role only when it isn't Assistance, Comp, and its prescription in one line (`data-row-prescription`: "4 × 5 · RPE 7 · rest 3 min", never a four-cell grid), with no "Tap a block to switch" hint or Priority legend, the at-home backup block is never one of the program's blocks (`isPrimaryProgramBlock`), and the current week is `isCurrentWeek`'s: its start through its last day, never the day after too. Readiness is the last finished workout's score, so from two days on its row and sheet say which workout ("From your Thursday workout", `readinessAge`), one older than `READINESS_STALE_DAYS` (14) isn't shown, the sheet says the score and the factors read different things (`data-readiness-how`) and that it's built from what they log, not a measurement, and a factor with nothing logged never drives the call (`pickRecommendation`: missing sleep is never "Sleep was short"). The month card defines its three numbers under More (`data-month-definitions`: days with a logged set, exercises with a new best estimated max (a first time doesn't count), weeks in a row with a logged set). The cardio card's one tap reads "Done as planned" (it records the planned minutes, so it says so) and a detailed log with the time left blank stores no time, never the plan's — why: the coach found the Block view's Priorities a long list of "Primary Squat Other — Monday" / "Lats Other — Tuesday" lines, the month cells' truncated names noise, missed and upcoming workouts identical, "Today's readiness" read from a workout days ago with "Sleep was short" for someone who never logged sleep, and a cardio shortcut that wrote numbers nobody measured.
- Team tasks can be on several people: `tasks.assignees` (logins, in the order added) is the one list, kept straight by the `tasks_assignees_sync` trigger (each person once, real logins only, nobody on a private task, `assigned_to` / `assignee_name` = the first person; a caller that only sets `assigned_to` means "just them"), and `assigned_to` references `auth.users` (never `coaches`). Sending a private task to someone shares it (`sendToTeamPeople`: the sender stays on it), so it's in My tasks for everyone on it (`splitTasks` / `taskPeople`), each row shows who else has it (`SharedWith`, `othersOn`, `peopleLabel`; everyone on it on the Team board), People in the ⋯ menu and Who's on it in the task tick as many as it's for (`togglePerson`), and whoever's newly on a task gets one push (`notifyTaskSharedFn`: re-read as the caller, content-free, `#task=<id>` opens it) — why: `assigned_to` still pointed at `coaches(id)` while the app wrote logins, so every send / assign failed with `tasks_assigned_to_fkey`, and a task sent to a teammate left the sender's list with no sign it was shared.
- Team notes (`team_notes`, 20270327090000_team_notes.sql, team-notes.ts / team-notes.tsx) are Quick notes shared with a group of teammates, listed right under Quick notes on Tasks, one card per group (`groupTeamNotes`: the same people me aside, whatever order, the group edited last first, a + on each to start a note for those people). Everyone on `members` reads and edits it (RLS: members who work the task board, `works_task_board`; the tidy trigger keeps each person once and teammates only; only a change to the words moves `updated_at`), live (realtime, opened by the section alone). A quick note's ⋯ shares it in one tap with a group shared with before (`shareTargets`: the last group used on this device, `saveRememberedGroup`, then groups with notes, then each teammate alone), or Choose people… (`TeamPeopleDialog`: several people, a team note or a task); the quick note goes to Recently Deleted with Undo, and whoever's on it gets one content-free push (`notifyTeamNoteSharedFn`, `#note=<id>` opens it). Remove from team: a team note goes back into the sharer's own Quick notes (`canTakeBack`; anyone else Leaves it, `team_note_leave`, since an edit must keep the editor on it), and a team task back to the person's private tasks (`makePrivate`), both with Undo; a shared task can also Move to team notes for the same people — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach wanted a note sent to Fionna to stay a note (it only went into the task manager), to share with the same group without picking everyone again, and a way to take a note or task off the team.
- A sale's money reads one way everywhere: `saleMoney` / `salePayments` (sale-money.ts) give its price, what came in, what's left and the next payment in the dollars the client pays (tax included when the rate is known), drawn by `SaleCard` on the client's Billing tab and on the sale page (`/admin/purchases/$id`), and the balance card's `buildBillingSummary` uses the same reading; a sale nothing has been paid on (a payment link out) is "waiting", never counted as owed, and the status badge comes from the same reading (`saleStatus`). Money on a sale only changes through payment rows (`recordPayment` with its tax split, `recordRefund`, `voidLedgerRow`, only on payments recorded by hand), which the database adds up (`recompute_purchase_totals`); no screen or assistant types `amount_paid`, session counts or a payment status, and there is no call that does (`updatePurchasePayment` is gone). The finance login records money in through the same `recordPayment` and dialog as the admin (`payments.record`: written as the server once the permission is checked, never a proof, which an admin attaches; finance's collect list carries each line's rate and next payment from Billing, `owedRows` / `recordMoneyFor`), and so does Cleo (`record_payment`: an amount received with its tax split at the client's own rate, how and when, `cleoPayment`); refunds and voids stay the owner's. Once a sale has payment rows its dollar total follows them whatever else writes it (`purchase_paid_follows_ledger`, 20270328090000_sale_paid_follows_ledger.sql) — why: the old Billing tab listed every sale twice with before-tax and with-tax numbers side by side, a hand-recorded e-transfer counted its GST as price, a cancelled subscription read "Paid in Full", and the sale page let a typed total drift from the payments; and on Nov 17 2026 the finance login's Record a payment and Cleo's "update payment status" still set a status and a typed "paid so far" with no payment row, so that money never reached the books, GST/HST or the client's Billing, while the Stripe checkout handlers' write left four live subscriptions (Alyssa, Nicole, Reece, Jarrett) reading one charge in dollars where their rows held two.
- Every product and payment price is before tax (Stripe prices are `tax_behavior: "exclusive"`, checkout runs Stripe Tax), so while the business charges tax every place one is shown reads "$130.00 + tax": `PlusTax` (billing/plus-tax.tsx) after the price in Add sale's products and its custom "Price before tax", Quick sell, the booking's Charge for it chips, the price cards, the Products page, the sale screen (`AssignOfferDialog`) and the chat's payment card. Whether it's on is one yes / no, `business_charges_tax()` (GST-registered in `business_tax_settings`, which only the owner and finance can read; any signed-in person can ask, 20270221090000_business_charges_tax.sql), read by `useChargesTax`, and unknown says nothing — why: Add sale read "$130.00 · Monthly subscription" while every payment since August carried 5% GST on top, so the coach quoted prices that weren't what the client paid.
- A session package renews in its own size: sessions trained past a client's packages start a renewal of their latest priced package (same sessions, same price: Marc's next 16 for $640 + tax), never a per-session tab. `packageRenewal` (client-owed.ts) is the one reading, used by the balance card, the Sessions tab and Cleo: trained sessions fill whole renewals (owed now), booked ones fill what those have left, and any past that are a heads-up until the first is trained. "Add renewal" on the balance card and ⋯ Renew on a package call `renew_session_package`, which copies the package (dates following on, plan and price kept, `Unpaid`) and holds its sessions at once, so the trained and booked ones move onto it (`sle_apply_unbilled`); a sale with sessions trained on it is owed even before its first payment (`saleMoney`'s `sessionsUsed`), never "waiting". "Add free sessions" is the no-charge grant — why: the coach sells Marc 16 at a time, so 10 sessions past his package meant his next 16 ($672), not $42 × 10, and a renewal he's financing must read as owed, not as an unopened payment link.
- A session package's Stripe auto-pay pays the client's packages in order, the oldest still owed first, and stops only when nothing is owed: `session_plan_payment_target(sale)` (20270303090000_auto_pay_pays_packages_in_order.sql) is the one rule, the subscription's own package and the same client's later pay-later packages (no Stripe subscription, checkout, link or payment of their own, never one sold with a booking) by start date, the first with `amount_outstanding_cents` > 0, else NULL; the webhook's `invoice.paid` records each payment on it (`planPaymentTarget`, stripe-plan-payments.server.ts) and ends the subscription (`cancel_at_period_end`) only when `planStillOwed` says nothing is (other sales keep the count-the-installments rule), and the account sync (`runStripeAccountSync`, stripe-account-sync.server.ts: the admin's Sync with Stripe and pg_cron `stripe-sync-daily` through the `stripe-sync` hook, which also refreshes each live subscription's status and next charge, `refreshSubscriptions`) and Reconcile with Stripe backfill on the same target; the sync never writes a payment from a subscription checkout (its first invoice is that payment, recorded by invoice id) and finds a one-time checkout's payment under its session, payment intent or invoice before writing one (on Nov 12 2026 it recorded six subscription sales' first payments twice, voided the same night). Billing projects it (`autoPayPlan`, session-packages.ts: whole payments, oldest owed first, from `next_billing_date` by `billingPeriod`, read through payment-schedule.ts), and `stripe-sync` `{ clientId, audit: true }` lists a client's Stripe beside their sales, read-only. A payment recorded by hand that leaves nothing owed on the plan sets the subscription to stop at its period end (`endAutoPayIfPaidUp`, `recordPayment`; never a charge, audited `auto_pay_ended_paid_up`), and the same manual payment sent twice within 10 minutes is recorded once (`findRecentDuplicatePayment`, `duplicate: true`) A subscription's next charge (`next_billing_date`) is written by one rule everywhere (`subscriptionNextCharge`, stripe-next-charge.ts: the webhook's subscription.created / .updated, the daily sync's `refreshSubscriptions`, the account sync and Reconcile): the period read from the subscription or, since Stripe API 2025-03-31, its item; a stored date equal to the new period's start kept until invoice.paid rolls it on (Stripe opens the period about an hour before it charges); an event with no period never wipes a known date (on Nov 13 2026 the 22:34 UTC subscription.updated wrote null an hour before Marc's charge, so Billing showed no payment dates and the daily sync kept putting it back). — why: Marc's package 2 subscription ($160 + tax every 2 weeks) was ended by the webhook after its 4th payment, so nothing in Stripe would ever have paid the two renewals (packages 3 and 4, $1,344) the coach added pay later, and any later payment would have piled onto package 2; the coach chose to keep his auto-pay going until he's paid up (Nov 11 2026).
- Auto-pay is changed from the app, never in Stripe by hand and never by changing a subscription the client already agreed to (auto-pay.ts pure and tested, auto-pay.server.ts, auto-pay.functions.ts, `AutoPaySheet` / `PendingAutoPayRow`, auto-pay-sheet.tsx; 20270325090000_payment_proofs_auto_pay_changes.sql). On Billing, the auto-pay plan's Manage (admins) shows what's charging and on which card (`getAutoPayCard`: brand and last 4, read live from Stripe), and a new amount, schedule or card is a new subscription the client sets up themselves: `startAutoPayChange` → `sendAutoPayChange` checks it against the Billing model (`autoPayOptions`: the package it pays first is owed and paid by nothing of its own, never the sale carrying the running one; `changeProblem`), finds or makes a reusable before-tax Stripe price (`findOrCreatePrice`, lookup key `priceLookupKey`), records an `auto_pay_changes` row (one pending per client; an older one is superseded and its checkout expired), makes its checkout at once (`mintAutoPayCheckout`: `metadata.auto_pay_change_id`, the first charge on the date asked when it's `MIN_LEAD_DAYS` out, `stripeFirstCharge`, else at setup, and never before the running one's next charge), and posts the setup card in their chat (`kind: "auto_pay_setup"`, `AutoPaySetupCard`, read live: Set up auto-pay → Set up / No longer needed) with the link `jfeffect.com/autopay/<token>` (autopay.$token.tsx re-mints an expired checkout). The running auto-pay keeps charging until they finish; then the webhook (`checkout.session.completed` → `completeAutoPayChange`) puts the new subscription on the package it pays (payment fields, `Active Subscription`) and cancels the old one at once (no proration, no invoice), so there's never a gap or a double charge, and the checkout lives on the change row, never the sale, until then (a sale with a checkout of its own stops being a pay-later package for the running auto-pay). "Keep pace with their training" suggests the package's price over its own length per period (`paceAmount`: $672 over 4 weeks is $336 every 2 weeks). Stop auto-pay is now or at the period's end (`stopAutoPay`, what's owed stays owed). A balance nothing collects is "no payment method" on Billing (`data-no-payment-method`: Set up auto-pay, Set dates or Record), never overdue. `auto-pay-send` (hook, cron secret, `actor_user_id` an admin, a preview unless `apply: true`) sends the same link from the back office. Nothing here charges anyone — why: on Nov 16 2026 Marc's $168 every 2 weeks couldn't keep up with 16 sessions every 4 weeks ($672), so the coach wanted $336 every 2 weeks and a way to change or cancel a client's auto-pay from the app that stays in step with Stripe and shows when nothing collects a balance.
- The business's money is one workspace, `MoneyWorkspace` (src/components/admin/money/), opened from Sales (Overview · Transactions · Taxes · Reports, the owner's and the finance login's; then Products, Discount codes, Stripe activity, Settings) and the finance login's Books page, over one period in the address (`?p=` / `?cmp=`, period.ts, pure, tested: this / last month, last 30 days, this / last quarter, year to date, last year, a month, a year or a custom range, stepped with ‹ ›; a running period compares with the same number of days before it, a year so far with the same dates last year, `comparisonFor`). Every figure is one reading, `moneyFigures` (money-view.ts, pure, tested, the same pieces as `buildBooksSnapshot`, so a year's figures equal the Taxes card's): cash basis, the day money moved. Sales = payments less refunds before GST/HST; Spent = the business share of what was paid less the GST/HST claimed back (`expenseTaxView`: the half of a meal's GST that can't be claimed is part of its cost), capital purchases in full when paid, personal spending left out; Profit = Sales − Spent; taxable profit = sales less the deductible part and CCA; GST/HST net = collected − ITCs (the regular method, never the Quick Method); income tax + CPP to put aside for a period is what its profit adds to that year's estimate (`periodTaxReserve`: the estimate through its end less through the day before it starts, so a year's periods add up to the Taxes tab's year figure; never a flat rate on all profit), always called an estimate, never a bill. What clients owe is never summed from sales: `getReceivables` (receivables.server.ts, after `assertBooksAccess` finance.read) runs every client's `buildClientPayments` and `receivablesFrom` (receivables.ts, pure, tested on Marc's records) gives outstanding with tax, overdue only where the Billing model says so, the next 30 days, no-dates, and payment links nobody paid apart (never owed); archived and deactivated accounts' unpaid links and bookings are left out. The same read feeds the finance home and Payments pages and Cleo (`buildSummerContext(…, owed)`). Transactions is one ledger of money in, refunds, money out and tax payments (`ledgerEntries`, never a second copy of a payment), each opening to edit, a receipt or the client's Billing; what needs a look (`NEEDS_ATTENTION`) is flagged, never fixed silently. Taxes keeps GST/HST, income tax + CPP and profit apart and lists what the accountant should check instead of guessing. Every report (P&L, transactions, GST lines, what clients owe, year-end package, GST worksheet, receipts book; `reports.ts`, books-export.ts) is a File handed to `saveFiles` inside the tap, never `doc.save()`. Chart colours are `--money-in` / `--money-out` / `--money-profit` (styles.css, validated light and dark, a legend and a table view) — why: on Nov 16 2026 the coach found Sales' books cluttered and hard to read (one year at a time, an 'open sales' list that counted unpaid payment links and archived test accounts as owed, exports that did nothing in the iPhone app) and wanted to see at a glance what the business made, spent, kept, is owed, owes in tax and how that compares, for any period.
- The money screens read right on a phone (Nov 18 2026): the month chart shows only the books' own months (`chartMonths(period, today, booksFirstDay(...))`: never before the first record, a period of 3+ months its own months, a shorter one the months around it, reaching past a past month up to this month while there are under 12 months on the books), every bar at full strength with the period's months on a shaded band and their labels bold (`ReferenceArea`, never a dimmed bar, which read as missing on the dark theme); a comparison is said the way people say it (`comparisonLabel`: "June", "December 2025", "Q2", "2024", a running period's dates) and is dropped when it falls before the books start (the workspace computes `vs` once and hands it to `PeriodBar`, which says "Nothing earlier in the books to compare with" instead of a "None in Jan 1 – Nov 18, 2025" on every figure), and the line under the period shows only on Overview and Reports, where figures are compared. Text wraps instead of being cut ("Where money went", an owed line on two lines with its next payment, Coming up, ledger lines, report descriptions), the owed tiles are whole dollars (`wholeMoney`), "Owed to you" on a past period says it's today's, and a row that scrolls sideways (the Sales tabs, the money tabs, the period chips) fades at the edge only while there's more that way (`useScrollFade`), so a word at the screen's edge never reads as broken — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach found the books not displaying properly on his phone: July's chart was nine empty months before the business existed with thin dimmed bars, "None in Jan 1 – Nov 18, …" cut off under every figure for the year, "Bank & payment processing fe…" and "Package 4 · next $336.00 Nov 27 · au…" cut off, "$1.1k" where the amount was $1,124, and "Products" cut to an "F" at the tab row's edge.
- The books are ready for money that isn't a client's, bills that repeat and a new tax year (Nov 17 2026, 20270331090000_books_other_income_recurring.sql, books-ready-for-data.test.ts). Other income (a sponsorship, a workshop, a referral or affiliate payout, a product sold outside the app) is `business_other_income`, recorded and edited from Sales → Add → Record other income or its Transactions row (`OtherIncomeDialog`, `saveOtherIncome`: owner or finance.record; `deleteOtherIncome` the owner's), with its proof in `business-receipts` and the deposit account like a hand-recorded payment; it reaches every figure through `normalizeRevenue`'s fourth source as `source: "other"` (Sales, GST/HST, the T2125, Cleo, reports, its own "Other income" service, never a client in Top clients), and a payment with no GST/HST says why (`no_tax_reason`: outside Canada, not taxable, not charged) or it's flagged like a client payment with none (`missingTaxReason`). A client's payment is never recorded there: it goes on their sale. A repeating bill is `business_recurring_expenses`, set up on purpose (Add → Set up a repeating bill, or Set it up on a bill the books show paid most months, `recurringExpenses` / `draftFromDetected`, which starts after its last one and never before tomorrow, so months nobody entered aren't assumed paid) and added to `business_expenses` on its day as an expense to check (`books_add_recurring_expenses`: `source = 'recurring'`, `recurring_id`, `needs_review`, one per bill per day through `external_key`, catching up at most 24; pg_cron `books-recurring-expenses` daily, right after a save that's already due, and when the books load with one due), never confirmed by itself and never a payment or charge; its dates are one rule in SQL and on the phone (`books_recurring_next` = `nextRecurringDate`, recurring.ts: the bill's day, the month's last when shorter, never drifting), the form says what a past start adds before Save (`datesDue`), an edit never moves a bill back over days it added (`nextOnProblem`), and Stop leaves what it added. Tax rates go in `TAX_TABLES` only once CRA and Manitoba publish them, never guessed: until then a year uses the latest table with `ratesExact` false and the year's readiness list says so (the `rates` issue, on the Taxes tab's list for the accountant); periods, filing dates and the tax reserve roll over January 1 on their own (`periodTaxReserve` per year). The GST/HST number is saved as CRA writes it (`normalizeGstNumber`) and refused unless it's 9 digits, RT and 4 digits (`gstNumberProblem`; a check digit that doesn't add up is only a "double-check") — why: the coach asked that the books be set up for all of it before the data comes in: income that isn't from a client had nowhere to go, a monthly bill had to be typed every month or was only guessed at from past expenses, 2027's rates aren't published (federal indexation and CPP limits come out in November, Manitoba's in its budget), and the GST number is what the year-end package and receipts print.
- A payment recorded by hand says where it went and carries its proof (payment-proof.ts pure and tested, payment-proof.tsx): `payment_ledger.deposit_account_id` names one of the business's `deposit_accounts` (a label and the last 4 digits only, never a full account number; admins write, finance reads; Simplii Chequing ••6360 the default), asked for on e-transfers, bank transfers and cheques (`needsAccount`), and `proof_path` is a screenshot, photo or PDF (≤ 10 MB) in the private `payment-proofs` bucket under the client's id (`proofPathOk`), admins and finance only, never the client (a bank screenshot shows the business's own balance). Record payment takes both, the payment history row opens `PaymentDetailSheet` (For, How "E-transfer into Simplii Chequing ••6360", Tax, Reference, Recorded, the proof) and an admin can attach or change them later (`attachPaymentProof`, manual rows only, audited); `payment-proof-import` (hook, cron secret) attaches a proof that reached the business outside the app and never replaces one — why: on Nov 16 2026 Marc paid Package 3 ($672) by e-transfer into the Simplii chequing account and the app had nowhere to say which account it landed in or keep the transfer's screenshot.
- A client's Sessions tab is training and Billing is money, never mixed. Sessions (`ClientSessionsPanel` → `SessionsTrackerBody`, client-sessions-panel.tsx) leads with the glance (`PackageSnapshot`, below, without its money line), then past sessions waiting on the coach, a next step only when there's something to do (`sessionsNextStep`: renew once they've trained past the package, book what's available, sell a first package; "all booked" says nothing; sessions past every package as the next one, dashed, "Needs renewing" once one is trained), Upcoming sessions folded, the package list (`PackageList mode="sessions"`, below: a package opens on its sessions, with its payments folded) and "Not on a package" folded; booking tools (Book ahead, Renew, Sell, Add or remove sessions, Google import) are the header's ⋯. Which package a session is on is `sessionPlaces` (session-packages.ts, pure, tested): its latest use nothing gave back (`revert_on_uncomplete` / `reattribute`), else its latest hold nothing released, the database's own reading (`pt_session_sale_id`); counts come from `session_balance()`; the ledger is read whole, page by page, under its own key (`pt-client-ledger`). Renew is `renew_session_package`: the next package added Unpaid, nothing charged, the sessions already past it moved onto it (sessions left on the current one stay there and are booked first), starting the day after the latest package ends, refused while the client already has an unpaid package with nothing trained, booked or paid on it (20270314100000_billing_safeguards.sql), and linked to the package it follows (`renewed_from_id`); then Book ahead opens for the rest — why: the old tab mixed owed / paid / auto-pay lines and a money chip per package into training, so the coach couldn't read either at a glance; a double tap could add two identical renewals that auto-pay would then pay; and a renewal copied from an older package was dated inside one already running.
- A client's money laid out in time is one pure model, `buildClientPayments` (payment-schedule.ts, tested on Marc's real records), read through `useClientPayments` (the `["client-billing", id]` query) by the glance and the package list on Summary, Sessions and Billing (`ClientBillingPanel` → `BillingOverview`) and the sale cards' "next payment" sentence (`SaleCard schedule`), so they never disagree. Each sale with money left gets its own installments from real dates only: auto-pay's covers (oldest owed first, from Stripe's next charge), a Stripe plan's next charge then every period, or, collected by hand, payment k falls k periods after its `first_payment_date` (so a payment recorded moves the rest along; set with `setPaymentDates`, audited `payment_dates_set`, refused on anything Stripe collects); anything else is `how: "none"`, "no payment dates", amber, never overdue and never a guessed date. States: `failed` (Stripe says so), `overdue` (a date passed with nothing in; auto-pay only after `UNCONFIRMED_GRACE_DAYS`, before that `unconfirmed`), `due_today`, `scheduled`; totals are integer cents tax included (`clientTaxRate`, never a hard-coded rate) as outstanding = overdue + due now + scheduled + no dates, beside what's been paid (the ledger, voided rows out); an open-ended subscription's next charge is the next payment but never owed; a payment link nobody paid is `waiting`, not owed, unless it's a pay-later renewal its live auto-pay pays or a hand-collected sale with dates set (`saleMoney` `billed`); training past every package no sale bills yet is `notBilled`, said apart from what's owed with Add the next package. Auto-pay reads on / failing / ending (with what it leaves uncollected) and its last scheduled payment, never "paid up". Billing's layout is the glance rule's; payment history keeps receipts and voided rows struck through, and a balance on schedule is never red — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach found the Sessions tab's money confusing ("Paid up Mar 5" was only the last auto-pay date), balances on installment plans read as debts, packages with no payment dates had a made-up "next payment", and the client's real figures (Marc: $1,512 outstanding, $1,344 paid, $168 due Nov 13 by auto-pay, nothing overdue) had to be worked out by hand.
- Every session package has a permanent identity and its own training and money (20270315090000_package_identity.sql): `purchase_records.package_number` is its number in the client's account, given once in running order and never changed (existing ones numbered by term start, then purchase; a new one takes the next by the `purchase_records_package_number` trigger under a per-client lock; unique per client), and `renewed_from_id` is the package a renewal followed on from (`renew_session_package` sets it to the client's latest package; existing renewals were linked from the date their own record names, "Renewal of the package bought …", only on an exact single match, never guessed). Labels everywhere read it (`packageNumbers` / `saleLabels`, payment-schedule.ts; `buildSessionPackages`' `number`). Billing's Packages section (`PackageHistory`, client-billing-panel.tsx) lists them 1 → 2 → 3 → 4: settled ones (nothing to pay, nothing to train) fold into one line, every other one is a card with its sessions ("16/16 completed" / "7 completed · 9 booked · 0 available"), Total · Paid · Balance, its unpaid delivered training and how what's left gets paid, opening to where it came from (Renewed from Package N on … / Bought …), its payments still to come, its payments received and View sessions. Unpaid training delivered is one method (`PackageAccount`, `buildClientPayments`): a package's price in the dollars the client pays is spread evenly over the sessions it sold, the training delivered is that share for every session completed or counted as a no-show (the ledger's `used`; cancelled and moved ones use nothing; capped at the price, so free sessions added on top add nothing), and what's unpaid of it is that less the payments on the package net of refunds, never below zero or above what's left on it; a subscription or free sale has none; training past every package not billed yet counts at the next package's per-session price (`notBilledDeliveredCents`, not in what's outstanding). It's exposure, never a due date: Billing shows it under the totals beside Overdue · Due now · Future installments · No dates yet, and a renewal never moves a balance between packages or closes the one before — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach couldn't see which packages a client had renewed, how much training they'd already had, or how much of it wasn't paid for yet (Marc: $168 + $672 + $294 = $1,134 delivered and unpaid inside $1,512 outstanding, none of it overdue), and package numbers worked out on the fly could shift when a back-dated sale came in.
- Every session package has two statuses read on their own, never one "Active" (package-status.ts, pure, tested): training (`trainingStatus`: Upcoming, Currently training, In progress, Fully booked, Training completed, Expired, Cancelled) and money (`paymentStatus`: Paid in full, Partially paid (some paid, the rest on its schedule), Payment scheduled (nothing paid yet, on its schedule), Payment due, Overdue, Payment failed, No payment schedule, Payment link sent, Free, Cancelled; worst first). The package being trained now (`pickTrainingNow`: the lowest-numbered package with sessions still to train, booked or available, granted and not cancelled; `ClientPayments.trainingNowId` and `buildSessionPackages`' `currentId` read it the same way) and the package(s) being paid now (`payingNow`: per payment stream, the package its next payment goes to; one auto-pay is one stream that pays one package at a time, the rest queued behind it as `then`; each Stripe plan or plan collected by hand is its own stream, so overlapping plans are all listed, earliest first) are found separately and never assumed to be the newest. Which completed sessions payments cover is a display split only (`sessionCoverage`: the package's price spread over the sessions it sold, payments applied to its completed sessions in order, from the same numbers as unpaid training delivered, so cells × per-session price = the dollar amount; one session part paid says so; paid beyond the training delivered is said as money, a booked session is never delivered; null for a subscription, free or cancelled sale), explained on the package as an accounting split, never written anywhere. The pictures are shared (`package-visuals.tsx`): `SegmentBar` (one continuous bar), `SessionBar` and `CoverageBar` (a cell per session, only inside an opened package: completed / paid for green, trained and not paid for amber, part paid half and half, booked grey, still to book hollow, expired dashed) and the statuses as a dot and words. How the screens show them is the next rule (the glance and the package list) — why: on Nov 13 2026 the coach couldn't tell at a glance that Marc trains on Package 4 while his auto-pay is still paying off Package 2 (one "Active"-style reading made the newest look like the one being paid), and wanted to see how much training was delivered and how much of it is paid for without working it out.
- A client's packages read the same everywhere, from one model (`buildClientPayments`) and one set of words (package-view.ts, pure, tested): the glance (`PackageSnapshot` / `ClientPackageSnapshot`, package-snapshot.tsx) is first on the client's Summary (only with a session package or a coaching plan), the Sessions tab and the Billing tab: Training (`packageGlance`: the package being trained now, "Fully booked" when nothing's left to book, done · booked · left over one continuous `SegmentBar`, the next session, then their final session (`finalSession`, package-view.ts, read from the Sessions tab's own `ptSessionsQuery`: once nothing is left to book on any package they hold, the last one booked, "Final session Mon Jan 25 at 12:00 PM · in 9 weeks"; with sessions left, where the last lands at their usual days by Book ahead's own plan (`planPrebook`: their weekly rhythm after their last booking, holidays and booked days skipped), "about … if the 3 left go on Mon, Wed & Fri", and nothing when there's no rhythm to read; a call that takes no credit never counts; "after Package 1's Dec 6 end date" when the package it lands on is dated to end sooner; nothing booked or left: the last day they trained); the Upcoming sessions fold says its first and last date; else the last package or how many were trained past it) and Paying off (the package the next payment goes to, never assumed the newest: its status, paid of total on a bar, the next payment and how, "Then Package 4" for what the same auto-pay pays after it, how many other plans run at once; else no payment dates, paid up or nothing billed), each row opening its tab; the Summary's adds Outstanding · Unpaid training · Overdue (`showMoney`; off on Sessions and Billing, which show money their own way). The package list (`PackageList`, package-list.tsx) is one component on both tabs: the one being trained first, then the ones with money left, then anything else open, each group by number, the settled ones folded into one "Packages 1, 2 · paid in full" line (`orderPackages`), the chain "1 → 2 → 3 → 4" in its header; a row is its number and period, its training and money in a word each ("Training now" / "Paying now" for the ones that are) and one thin bar each, never a cell per session (on Billing its session bar is green for what's paid for, amber for what's trained and not); it opens to its sessions (the per-session grid lives only here; Billing reads them when opened through the shared `ptSessionsQuery` / `ptClientLedgerQuery`, session-queries.ts), its payments (how it's paid, what's still to come, what came in, and how they cover its training in plain words, `coverageWords`: "$42.00 a session ($672.00 for 16). The $504.00 paid covers 12 of the 16 completed; 4 aren't paid for yet ($168.00).", said to be an even split, never which appointment a payment was for), where it came from (Renewed from Package N on …), Edit dates and the sale page; the tab decides which opens first. Billing (`BillingOverview`) is, after the glance: what's owed (`OwedSection`: Outstanding big, a bar and a line of Overdue · Due now · Scheduled · No dates yet; apart from it Unpaid training delivered, said not to be a payment that's due, with How it's worked out per package), overdue payments, the payment plans (`paymentPlans`: each way money comes in, one auto-pay one plan paying packages one after another, "1. Package 3 · 4 payments · Nov 27 – Jan 8", when it ends, every date one tap away), what needs the coach, the package list, links out and payment history folded, and the Sales cards folded (`ClientSalesTable`, every action kept). Auto-pay says itself only when it's news (failing, ending). The client's tabs (`ClientWorkspaceTabs`) are one row of words with a line under the one you're on, a hairline under the row, no box, icons or blur, scrolling sideways with the one you're on kept in view — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach found Sessions and Billing still bulky and technical: the package being trained and the one being paid weren't the first thing on opening a client, every package card was tall with 16 cells, two statuses, two tags and repeated lines, the money read like an accounting dashboard, and the boxed tab row with icons took a screen's width for four tabs with Billing off its edge; and on Nov 20 2026 that the tracker should say when a client's final session is booked or will be done, so he knows their last day without counting the calendar (Colten's 27 booked ran to Mon Jan 25, seven weeks past his package's Dec 6 end date, while his repeating Google event carried on past it).
- What a client is on that isn't a session package is their coaching plan (`coachingPlans`, coaching-plan.ts, pure, tested; `ClientPayments.coaching` from `buildClientPayments`): a live subscription (its charge with tax, the next charge, auto-pay, ending or failing) or a term paid for up front (`one_time` / `plan` with term dates or a `term_duration_text`: its dates from `service_start_date` / `term_start_date` to `term_end_date` / `package_expiry_date`, time left in words, `timeLeftWords`, and how much of it has gone by), never a session package, a deposit or a one-off with no term, a cancelled subscription or an unpaid link; a term with no dates is `no_dates` (never a guessed start or end) and Billing asks for them (`data-coaching-no-dates`, Set dates opens the sale's `TermDateEditor`); the last `ENDING_SOON_DAYS` (30) are flagged amber with "Sell the next one before it ends", and an ended term stays only `ENDED_SHOW_DAYS` (60) and only while nothing else runs. It's the glance's Coaching row (`CoachingRow`, package-snapshot.tsx) on Summary and Billing, the only row for a client on online coaching alone (Training / Paying off have nothing to say there, and Billing drops its "Paid up" line), and off on the Sessions tab (`showCoaching={false}`, training only) — why: on Nov 15 2026 Alexander Rozbacher's 12 months of legacy online coaching ($3,200 paid in full May 20 on the other Stripe account, Jun 2, 2026 – Jun 2, 2027) was added to his account, and with the glance built for session packages his Summary showed nothing and Billing only "Paid up · $3,200", never what he's on or when it runs out; Ashley Santos's 6 months paid in July has no dates on file at all.
- Billing products' dates (a sale's term, a package's period, payment plans and their steps, Book ahead's basis, the coaching row, the purchase pages the coach and the client see, offer details) go through one formatter, `billingDay` / `billingRange` (date-range.ts, pure, tested): one date drops the year only when it's this year ("Jun 2", "Jan 8, 2027"); a range names its year once when both ends share it ("Aug 10 – Sep 4", "Nov 2 – 29", "Jan 22 – Mar 5, 2027") and both years whenever it crosses one ("Jun 2, 2026 – Jun 2, 2027"), a detail page asks for `year: "always"`, an open end reads "from …" / "until …" (never "?"), and a stored date-only value is that calendar day, never `new Date("2026-06-02")` (UTC midnight, the day before in Manitoba). `payDay`, `period`, `dayRange`, `shortDay` (sale-card), `fmtDate` (sale-row-parts) and `termRange` all call it; a new billing screen does too — why: on Nov 15 2026 Alexander's 12-month term read "Jun 2 – Jun 2, 2027" on his sale card (each date dropped "this year" on its own, so a range across a new year read as one inside it), the payment plans' "Nov 27 – Jan 8" did the same, and the purchase records panel and the client's own Account showed a stored Jun 2 as Jun 1.
- A client's My Purchases (`/portal/purchases` and a purchase's page, Account's Billing row) is the coach's Billing read for the client, never its own sums: `getMyBilling` (my-billing.functions.ts) reads with the caller's own login (RLS decides: the client, their coach, an admin, which is how View as client works; never the service role), sends the sales minus `PRIVATE_SALE_FIELDS` (the coach's `admin_notes` and bookkeeping) and minus archived ones, every payment that isn't voided without its `internal_note` (so what's been paid matches the coach's to the cent), the session ledger and `session_balance`, and only sessions marked for them (`visible_to_client`); `useMyBilling` (use-my-billing.ts, `myBillingView`) runs `buildClientPayments` on it and `myPurchases` (my-purchases.ts, pure, tested) puts it in the client's words. What's owed is outstanding (due later included), never an archived or voided sale, a payment link nobody's paid (that's a "Ready to pay" action, said once) or the training delivered inside a package's balance; "Action needed" is only a payment link to pay, a card Stripe couldn't charge (Update card, Stripe's own page, never in a preview) or a hand-collected payment past its date, never terms unticked or an active subscription. A package says its training and its money apart (`MY_TRAINING_WORD` / `MY_PAYMENT_WORD`), its sessions from the session ledger (`packageSessionLog`: completed, a late cancel / no-show counted, booked, cancelled or moved giving the session back, and a counted session hidden from their calendar by its day, so the list matches the count), and how payments cover its completed sessions as an even split that's part of its balance (`myCoverageWords`). Payments list what each was for, the method (a card number never), tax, "recorded by your coach" for one recorded by hand, and its receipt: Stripe's link when saved, else Get receipt (`getMyReceipt`: asks Stripe once by charge, payment intent or invoice, saves it on the payment row with only empty receipt fields filled, never an amount or status), none for a payment by hand (no receipt is made up). Past purchases (settled packages, ended subscriptions, paid one-offs) fold away; a client with nothing sees one empty state. My Purchases is the coaching layer (`COACHING_ONLY` "purchases"); a member's billing is the Membership page — why: on Nov 15 2026 the page tagged every purchase "Action needed" (terms never ticked), listed Marc's voided "16 Sessions (Final Payment)" with $400 outstanding and Colten's archived single session with $100, showed an active subscription beside "Action needed", read statuses from an older formula than the coach's, had no session tracking or receipts for most Stripe payments, and fetched whole sale rows with the coach's private notes into the client's app.
- A session can be sold with its booking (session-sale.ts): the booking dialog's "Charge for it" (`SessionSalePicker`: one tap on a one-time session product, `saleProducts`, or a typed price per session, `parseDollars`; shown on every new booking that uses a credit, before a client is picked too; on by default only for a client with no package at all) and the session sheet's and Manage Session's "Not on a paid package yet · Charge for it" (`SessionPaymentLine`). The coach books from the calendar or from the client's chat (the + menu's Schedule → Pick the time yourself, `BookSessionFromChat`: the same dialog on that client and type, loaded on first use). The session is booked first and stays booked whatever happens to the sale (`runSessionSale`, Try again on a failure). `sellSessions` (session-sale.functions.ts) prices it on the server (`saleQuote`: a single-session product once per booking, a pack once, a typed price × sessions; never the phone's total), uses the product's own Stripe price when sold once and otherwise finds or makes a one-time exclusive-tax price for that amount (`oneTimePrice`), then `sell_booked_sessions` (20270117090000_sell_sessions_with_booking.sql) makes an Unpaid one-time sale holding its sessions from the start (like a renewal), moves the booked sessions' credits onto it before granting (only ones no package pays for) and returns the same sale on a retry (`offer_snapshot.source = 'booking'`); its link is always its own checkout (`requiresClientSpecificCheckout`, never the product's shared buy.stripe.com link), posted as the payment card in the client's chat. The card (`PaymentRequestCard`) and the session sheet read the sale live (`pt_session_sale_id`, realtime), so Paid shows once Stripe confirms; a single sold on the side never changes what a package client renews (`latestPackage` prefers a package of 2+) — why: the coach books drop-in clients for one session and needs it booked now, paid later, with the payment set up in their chat and tracked without a second system.
- A PT session's credit follows its status, in the database: `pt_session_settle_credit` (run by `tg_pt_session_status_change` on a status or date change) makes Scheduled hold one, Completed and Missed (a late cancel or no-show that counts) use one, and Cancelled / Rescheduled bank it; a no-show that shouldn't count is a cancel (`setPtSessionStatus` maps `deductOnMissed: false` to Cancelled), and "already used" is a `used` row with no `revert_on_uncomplete` pointing at it (`_pt_unreversed_use`), never the latest row by time. Booked sessions turn Completed by themselves once their day is over in their own time zone (`pt_sessions_auto_complete`, pg_cron `pt-sessions-auto-complete` every 15 minutes), except one with an open change request or an archived client (the "Waiting on you" ones); moving a session moves its status (`tg_pt_session_status_follows_date`: a done one moved to today or later is booked again, a booked one moved into a day that's over is done), and a cancel or late cancel comes off Google only before the session has ended (one banked or changed after it stays in Google as history), and a done session added in the last 2 days with no Google event (logged or put back after the fact, ended in the last 30) is created in Google (`gcalAction`, pt-session-gcal.server.ts). Callers never reserve, consume, release or revert a credit for a status change, and nothing offers "undo completion" back to Booked on a day that's over (the job would just redo it): the session sheet's choices are Reschedule (the credit moves with it), Cancel · bank the credit, Late cancel or no-show · use the credit, Didn't happen · bank the credit and Book it again, each with Undo, worded from `session-credit.ts` — why: the coach pre-books every appointment and wants a credit used only once its day has passed, with banking or rescheduling one tap away and nothing to tick off by hand.
- Why a session was cancelled, moved or missed is the coach's own note in `pt_session_coach_notes` (one per session; `client_id` filled from the session by its trigger; staff-only RLS, no client or anon access), written and read only through `session-coach-notes.ts` (`useCoachNotes`, `useCoachNote`, `saveCoachNote`): the session sheet shows and edits it ("Add a note · only you see it"), and Cancel, Late cancel and Reschedule each take an optional "Why", while the client's Sessions tab and the Sessions list show it under the session. The full editor (`PtSessionDialog`) is the same: its Note is this one ("Only you see it", saved on every session a booking made), and `pt_sessions.notes` is only ever "Note to {first}" (on their session and calendar), saved with `client_visible_notes = true`: there is no "Show notes to client" switch, a booking type's hidden notes start as the coach's note, and the Sessions list shows the client's note as "To {first}: …" (20270119090000_session_notes_private.sql moved the hidden ones). Never in `pt_sessions.notes`, and no client screen reads the table — why: clients can read every column of their own pt_sessions rows (`client_visible_notes` only hides notes in the app), reasons like "baby got sick" or "I had NAPF" are the coach's private record, and the full editor's unlabelled Notes box went to the client, so Colten's "booked a foot tattoo friday" sat on his Oct 29 session and calendar feed and never showed in the coach's list.
- "Book ahead" (the client's Sessions tab, admin; `PrebookDialog`) books a client's next sessions on their usual days and times: `recentPattern` (session-prebook.ts, pure, tested) reads their last package's worth of booked / done sessions (`PATTERN_SESSIONS` = 16): how many a week (`perWeek`, the median week with the window's first and last weeks left out), then that many of the weekdays they trained most (a day trained twice beats a one-off; ties go to their last full week, then the latest), each at its most common start (a tie: the latest). The coach can change the days, times and how many before booking (`pattern` / `count`, `withPatternOverride`); a time that clashes with another booking or the coach's Google (`useConflictCheck`, the appointment being linked excepted) starts unticked. It books what's left on their packages by default; with nothing left and a priced package to renew it books the rest of the next one (`defaultCount`: Marc's 5 past package 3 leave 11), up to `PREBOOK_MAX`, those holding nothing until the renewal is added (they move onto it by `sle_apply_unbilled`). It starts after their last booking and never before tomorrow, skips Manitoba's general holidays and Boxing Day (`holidayOn`) and days that already have a session, stops at a package's expiry only for sessions that package pays for, and uses the coach's own Google appointment for a day when there is one. `applyPrebook` re-plans on the server with the same count and days and books only the days the coach kept ticked, one at a time in date order, at their last session's booking type: a Google appointment is linked through `applyGoogleImport` (never a second Google event), any other day is a booked session added to Google by the sync — why: the coach books whole packages ahead on a fixed weekly rhythm, often already as a repeating series in Google; filling the rest by hand doubled events or booked holidays; and Marc, financing 16 at a time, had no credits left, so the old "Book the rest" (credits only, his last 8 weeks, which read 5 weekdays for a 4-a-week client) never even showed.
- Share to Instagram is a coach's (a feed post's ⋯ and ✈️, `InstagramShareSheet`; never in View as client, never on a story): a post's own photos / clips go as posted, anything else as a card painted on canvas (`instagram-cards.ts`: lesson, Wins, Recap, holiday, Saturday scene, workout through `paintShareCard`, words; 1080×1350 for the feed, 1080×1920 for a story), a diagram or scene drawn in as pure SVG (`standaloneSvg`, its colours written in), never a DOM capture. The caption is the post's words with anyone tagged as a plain first name (`plainMentions`) and hashtags he can edit or switch off; the files are made before the tap (`prepKey`: only files made for the shape on screen can go), and the tap copies the caption and calls `navigator.share` before anything is awaited (`sendToInstagram`; a NotAllowedError means one more tap shares, a computer downloads). The Recap's public card leaves out logging and "work on this week", and someone else's post or names in it get a heads-up (`instagramHeadsUp`) — why: Instagram drops shared text from every app (so the caption is a paste), iOS opens the share sheet only from the tap itself, a canvas with HTML drawn into it can't be saved on an iPhone, an in-app @username would tag a stranger on Instagram, and what's only for the crew shouldn't go public by accident.
- Post scores are a coach's view of what keeps the crew interested (`community_post_scores`, staff only; 20261226090000_post_scores.sql): every feed post (never a story or an automatic workout story) is ranked against the other feed posts of the last 120 days on reach (who saw it, of the crew who were in Crew the week it went up), engagement (likes 1, votes 1.5, comments 3, saves and shares 4, per person who saw it, smoothed toward the crew's average) and attention (stopped on it 2s+, kept scrolling after, time on it; once 3 people's is recorded), mixed 30 / 35 / 35 (45 / 55 before attention). Coaches are never counted (`community_staff_people()`: staff logins, their linked athlete accounts, the account coach posts go out as), and a feed chip and the board always agree (one ranking). Attention is a running tally from the feed's own tracker (`post-attention.ts` in `usePostViewTracker`: time ≥ half on screen, or half the screen for a tall post; a stretch counts for at most a minute; leaving Crew or the app on a post is an exit), sent batched to `community_attention_add` (never your own post, a coach, or View as client; the table is closed to clients). A coach sees the score under each feed post and in its detail (`PostScoreRow` → `PostScoreSheet`) and the board on Crew → Insights (`PostScoreBoard`) — why: the coach wanted to see which posts keep people interested from the data already there, and views alone can't tell a post people stopped on from one they scrolled past.
- PT sessions the coach booked straight into Google Calendar come into the app through one import (`gcal-session-import.ts` / `.server.ts`: the Sessions tab's "From Google Calendar", and `/api/public/hooks/gcal-session-import` for back-office runs). An appointment is a client's when it names them (a guest's email, their full name, or their first / preferred name or a 4+ letter nickname that starts it as a word in the title); one naming two clients, cancelled or missed in its title, all-day, or already an app session is left out, and one whose title doesn't read like a session (`looksLikeTraining`: a call, payment, dinner, date) starts unticked. `import_calendar_pt_session` (service role) never duplicates (same Google event, or the same client at the same start, gets linked), makes a past appointment a done session that uses a credit, taking over a day already counted by hand (an unlinked `used`, reversed with `adjusted` / `reattribute`) so it's never counted twice, and an upcoming one a booked session holding a credit (`booked_via = 'google_calendar'`, no confirmation email). An imported session is the default booking type's (the first active in-person type that uses a credit, "1:1 Training" at 800 Vaughan Ave: its name, session type and `booking_card_id`); an upcoming one is at that type's place, never the Google event's own text, and the import writes that place onto its Google event, while a past one keeps where Google says it was (`import_calendar_pt_session`, 20270110090000_session_place_from_booking_type.sql). The Google event otherwise keeps the coach's title, time and notes: the import tags it with `jfPtSessionId` (so the calendar and double-booking checks see one booking) and clears `gcal_dirty`. A booked session's event is pushed as `status: "confirmed"` (`sessionEventBody`), so one deleted in Google comes back on the next sync — why: the coach booked Colten, Nicolas and Marc in Google while their credits were tallied by hand, so the app's calendar was empty and its credits couldn't follow what was actually booked; and Nicolas's sessions came in at the old 511 Robinson Ave gym his repeating Google event still named, with no booking type.
- The coach's Calendar tab (`/admin/calendar`, `AdminCalendarBoardPanel`) is the calendar first: no stat tiles or second title above it (what needs attention lives on the dashboard), Coaching / + Google and Filter (client and type, folded) on one row, and the board under it; a `?tab=board&date=` link opens Day view on that date for that visit (`dayOverride`), "Open calendar" opens the coach's last view (the week's timeline until they pick another, the day's on a phone, `defaultTimelineView`, `useCalendarPrefs`) on today on the Calendar tab — why: tapping a date or Open calendar landed on a stack of zero-count tiles ("Appointments today 0" on a day with two sessions, since it only counted appointments) with the calendar a screen and a half below, and Open calendar reopened whichever tab was used last.
- The coach's Crew page (`AdminCommunityHub`, Nov 14 2026) is the clients' Crew: the same `CommunityScreen` with its own bar (the admin shell steps aside on `/admin/community`, `CREW_OWN_BAR`, and the desktop admin strip hides there), stories and paged full-screen feed, with the coach's tools passed in (`coach`, `CoachCrew`): + writes a post to the crew from the New post screen (`PostComposer` with a coach save, `community_create_note`: photos, filters, a poll; the Crew + rule) and Manage sits beside it (`data-crew-manage`, a chart, a dot only while a report, a comment to answer, a post waiting for his OK or posting paused waits, `hubTabCounts`: the workouts waiting on props are listed on Needs you but never counted, since there's nearly always a week of them and the dot would never go out), opening on Needs you while something there needs him, else Posts, as a page of its own inside Crew with ‹ back to the same spot in the feed and the League as the trophy on its bar (`/admin/league`); the story row leads with his own Your story like everyone's (`canStory`, his stories going out as his main account like his posts), so it's always there instead of only on a day someone else had a story up, the heart and his photo are everyone's, the people-to-follow screen is left out for him, and each post's score sits in the line with its views (`PostScoreChip`).
