Manage is three tabs, the tab in the hash so pushes, old links and Back land on it (`manageTabFromHash`, crew-manage.ts: `#tab=needs`, `#tab=posts` and its old `#tab=daily` / `#tab=birthdays` / `#birthday=`, `#tab=insights`): Needs you (`NeedsYou`: the crew's reports, comments to answer, workouts waiting on props, lessons or birthday posts waiting for an OK, posts paused, each a tap from done; "All caught up" when nothing is); Posts (`CoachPostsTab`, posts-schedule.tsx), one Schedule of every post going out under his name in the next two weeks, day by day in the order they go out (`buildSchedule`, posts-schedule.ts: lessons, Athlete Insights on their planned days while they're on, the series that are on with Wins always, holidays, birthdays written or not yet; the first week shown, the week after behind one tap; a chip only when it's news: Posted, Being made, Needs your OK, Needs a look), with "Next: …" and the one Posting / Paused switch at its top (never repeated on a card), then Recently out (`buildRecent`, the last week), then Manage: Lessons, Athlete Insights, Daily series & holidays (the switched-off ones folded under one "Switched off" line, `data-series-off`) and Birthdays (`UpcomingBirthdaysWidget hideWhenEmpty`), each its own page (`#tab=posts&manage=…`, `manageFromHash`, so Back returns to the schedule; old `#birthday=` links open Birthdays). A tap on a scheduled post opens that post's own editor in place (`LessonSheet`, `DaySheet`, `BirthdayPostSheet`) or, for a series or holiday, its Manage page opened on it (`CoachWeeklyPosts focus`); its times read Manitoba as fixed UTC-5 from Nov 1 2026 whatever the phone's zone rules (`localParts`); Insights is the week's numbers (`WeekCard`) and the post scores board. A tab with something waiting gets a dot (`hubTabCounts`). The League is its own page, `/admin/league` (`AdminLeaguePage`: crew goal, standings, Hall of Strength, tools for non-view-only logins), on the trophy on Manage's bar, in the sidebar, in More and in search; old `#tab=league` links go there (`hubTabFromHash`; `#rankings` is Crew's own Rankings tab, which the coach has too). A view-only login (finance) gets the feed alone under the shell's bar (`CommunityScreen hideTabs`) — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach found his Crew too different from the clients' (the shell's bar over a second row of + · Feed · Posts · Insights · heart · photo, a red "Needs you" box over the stories and a League bubble among them, then cards) and wanted it like theirs, with Posts and Insights still his to manage; before that the page stacked the shell's bar, a Crew title row with four buttons, a four-way toggle and a "Needs you" strip on every tab before any content; League repeated the bar's trophy, and the coach wants to see at a glance only what is waiting on them; and on Nov 22 2026 that the story row disappeared randomly: his Crew page gave it nothing of its own (no Your story), and since automatic stories went on Nov 18 it showed only on a day someone else had posted a story; and on Nov 8 2026 he found Posts "confusing and not optimal for navigating": five long cards (lessons, the series with Athlete Insights inside it, holidays, birthdays), each with its own list, its own "Recent" and the pause switch on the lessons card, so seeing what goes out this week meant reading all of them.
- Every automated post (the daily series, a lesson, a holiday, a birthday: `isAutoPost`) is an Instagram post: its picture edge to edge at 4:5 (`AutoPostHero`), then the coach's name and words (`AutoPostCaption`), in the feed, on its own page, in the grid and on Home alike. The picture is the post's own card unless a photo is on it: a lesson's diagram / truth / reset card, Wins, the Recap, Tuesday's numbers, Thursday's feature, a quote or tip in type (`words`), the holiday's photo, the birthday card. A photo (Saturday's made scene, or one the coach added) replaces a `words`, `birthday`, `holiday` or `scene` card, comes before a numbers card (`infoCard`, `instagramPlan(...).cardLast`, `PostMedia`'s `trail`) and after a lesson's card (`lead`), and Share to Instagram sends them in that order. Cards are painted on clean paper with dark ink and the brand red (`PAPER`, instagram-cards.ts; a deck in its theme's paper, red, slate, blush or bone, deck-cards.ts), never the feed's near-black or a red glow, and made photos are bright and true to life (`PICTURE_STYLE`) — why: the coach found the black / red cards neither clean nor like a real post, a dark picture sinks into the dark feed, and a made gym photo on every post read as too much AI when the diagram, chart or type already made the cover. The painted card is the very 1080×1350 card Share to Instagram sends (`postArtCard` = `instagramPlan(post).card`), painted on the phone that shows it once it's within three screens (`PostArtImage`, post-art.tsx), one paint at a time, the feed's only while the screen is still and the nearest first (the rule below; a tap's ask, Save image or Share to Instagram, goes first), and kept in memory and the browser's Cache Storage under a key of everything on it plus, on a workout card only, the viewer's unit (the fallback for a set that never said its own; `UNIT_CARDS`, `postArtKey`, post-art.ts; bump `POST_ART_VERSION` when a card's look changes). Nothing on a card may be smaller than reads at a third of its size on a phone: a card with too much drops its least important parts (`fitParts`, `keep`) rather than shrinking everything, and a words card leads with one sentence (`splitHeadline`). What's only for the crew never goes on the picture (the Recap's logging and "work on this week" show under the words in the app, `data-recap-improve`). A birthday post carries whose birthday it is in `series_data.birthday` (stamped by `community_birthday_post_marker` when the draft is posted, 20261227100000_birthday_post_marker.sql). A pinch zooms the picture like any photo post's, and a clip's too while it plays (Instagram's: its own video element is moved into the zoom and back, never copied, `liftVideos` in use-media-pinch-zoom.ts, and a second finger is never the hold that pauses a clip) (`data-pinch-zoom` on the painted image too, never on a grid tile; `useMediaPinchZoom` is counted, so a post opened over Crew or from the coach's Insights brings its own without doubling up), Instagram's way: it follows the fingers, lifting one keeps it as big as it is and following the one still down (a second finger back carries the pinch on, `pinchView` / `panView`), and it springs back only when every finger is off, with no tap or like from letting go. A coach gets Save image in the ⋯ (`peekPostArt`: the file on screen, handed to the share sheet inside the tap, downloaded on a computer) beside Share to Instagram, which sends the same file — why: automated posts read as the app's widgets instead of posts (cards under the words, inset and rounded), the coach wants them to look like real uploaded pictures with the caption under them, what's in the feed has to be exactly what he saves and posts to Instagram, a painted card that wouldn't pinch-zoom while photo posts did felt broken, and a zoom that sprang back the moment one finger lifted didn't let anyone look closer; and on Nov 13 2026 pinching a clip did nothing at all (the zoom left every playing video alone, and once clips played by themselves that was every clip).
- Which saved nutrition plan day a date gets is one rule, `pickPlanDayForDate` (client-nutrition-day.ts), used by the client's Nutrition page, the grocery list (`buildGroceryList` `dates`) and the coach's "This week for the client" preview (`PlanDayWeekPreview`, PlanDayRules.tsx) alike: each `nutrition_target_days` row is used on `use_on` (training · rest · high · every_day · manual = only when the client picks it) and only on `weekdays` ('{}' = any day), both NULL = read from its name (`planDayRule`: "Training Day (Friday Only)" = training days, Fridays; "Mon-Fri", "Weekends", "except Sunday"; a plan of one day is every day). A day for this weekday beats one for any day, and one for this kind of day beats an every-day one; a High Day on a plan with no High Day is the training / rest day the schedule says, and nothing ever substitutes another kind of day. The coach sets both in the targets editor (`PlanDayUseEditor`: touching either stores both), and anything that rewrites a plan's days by delete + insert (Cleo, a published nutrition review) keeps them for the days it keeps by name (`carryPlanDayRules`) — why: names were the only signal, so Jared's "Training Day (Friday Only)" lost to the first "Training Day" every Friday, and the fallback Saturday High Day showed "no plan" to clients whose plan has no High Day.
- The Workouts program card (`ProgramRoadmapCard`, program-roadmap.tsx) is the one place that says where you are, in one pass (Nov 19 2026): the program is the heading (`ActiveProgramHeader`: its short name, `programShortName`, "Apex Hypertrophy" without its "— Advanced–Elite • 3-Day" tail, itself the program picker when there's more than one, with Past / Upcoming only while looking at another), Manage beside it (a labelled pill, `data-manage-program`, 32px to see and 44px to tap, only for a member's own program, the next rule), one progress line under them across the card's full width so it never wraps beside the button (`roadmapProgressParts` / `roadmapProgressLine`: "Week 5 of 12 · Ends Jan 10", the meet instead of the end with its flag, "Starts Nov 30 · 12 weeks", "Finished Jan 10"; the program's week is the only week number on the card) and one bar (`ProgramBar`: a segment per block sized by its weeks, filled up to this week in `bg-foreground`; no week pills, no red or green); then this block by its own name (`blockTitle`, never the coach's "Block 1 — …") with "Block N of M" on its right, and this week's label and sentence at 14px; then one Roadmap row (`data-roadmap-toggle`, "N blocks"; "All weeks" for a one-block program) that opens every block, where the current one is a neutral filled number with a "Now" tag and done blocks a green check. No box inside the card, no "% through" or counts under the bar. The Programs page's "On your calendar" is the same header (`OnYourCalendar`: where it came from over the name, `eyebrow`; then one "Go to your workouts" row) — why: the coach found the program experience cluttered (the block's name was the headline while the program's own name was a grey line cut to "Elite Powerlifting — Intermediate • 4…", two week numbers on one card, the program's week hidden on the roadmap row, Manage the card's last row under the roadmap, and the Programs page repeating the card with two more buttons); before that, the old card's big "Week 6 of 28" over a red / green bar, then the block and "Week 1 of 5" again with red week pills.
- A lesson, a Tuesday tip and the older daily posts go out as a deck (Nov 20 2026, post-decks.ts, post-deck-checks.ts, deck-cards.ts, post-decks.server.ts, 20270429090000_crew_post_decks.sql): slides the crew swipes and a caption of its own. A deck is a hook cover (a claim with tension, never a question or a template opener; `*accent*` words in the accent colour; "Swipe" and an arrow under it on a carousel, never in the card's lower right where the post's buttons sit), slides that each do one job (point, stat with its source and two bars when it compares, compare / myth struck out, a 2 to 4 item list, a quote only from the source, the lesson's own diagram, `DeckSlideType`) and a takeaway that lands somewhere the cover didn't; one slide (a cover with its line under it) when the idea doesn't need building, 3 to 7 when it does, never 2. The caption is the coach talking about it (one angle a post: the mistake he sees, who should ignore it, when it matters, what he'd change this week), opening with the thought itself (never a formula like "I care about this because", `CAPTION_PREAMBLE`, never the way a recent deck's caption opened, `captionOpening`), never the slides again (`captionOverlap`), never office talk and never engagement bait. It's painted on the viewer's phone like every card (`IgCard` "deck", one picture per slide keyed `.s<n>` in `postArtKey`, `cardArtElement` gives only the diagram slide its drawing), shown as a carousel through the feed's own `PostCarousel` (`DeckHero`, `PostMedia` `lead` as a list: a vertical swipe stays the feed's, every slide is asked for once the post is within three screens, the cover holds the words on its screen), and Share to Instagram and Save image send every slide. A deck's look is one of `DECK_THEMES` (paper, red, slate, blush, bone: a bold cover and takeaway around calm slides, a heavy sans or the bundled Instrument Serif hook, Anton numerals), never the theme of the two decks posted before it or the next one made for the library (`pickDeckTheme`, `lastItemThemes`); a short accented phrase stays on one line; a block that doesn't fit re-wraps a step smaller before it's ever scaled (`place`), left-aligned. Every deck is written in the background on the OpenAI key (route `post_write`: GPT-5.5 then GPT-5 mini, never Lovable credits) by a writer and an editor, four drafts at most (a dash or markdown fixed in code, `tidyText`, never a redraft): each draft must pass `deckProblems` (the hook, every slide's words against `DECK_LIMITS` and 130 words in all, variety, the takeaway, the diagram rule, the caption's overlap, length and asks, his never-say and AI / slogan / script phrases, dashes, overstated research, and every number over 12 and every two-word name must be in the source: a deck never adds a fact) and then the editor (`parseEditor`: hook, value, voice, caption and flow 4+, accuracy 5, or it goes back with the notes). A deck rides on its library item (`community_series_items.data.deck`) and its post (`series_data.deck`, handed to the app as `deck` by `community_post_json`) with `src`, the sha256 of the words it was made from: the publishers post a deck and its caption only while it still matches the item's words (`community_deck_current`, `community_lesson_post`, `community_publish_series`), a coach's own caption for the day always wins, and an item whose words change is queued again (`community_post_decks_enqueue`). Jobs are `community_post_decks` (server only, staff read; pg_cron `community-post-decks` every 10 minutes, `community_post_decks_kick`, three at once, one wake-up per job it names, the `post-decks-tick` hook): the lessons planned in the next four weeks first, then the Tuesday tips to come, then the rest of the library. A post already out is changed only through a reviewed dry run (`dry`, status `review`, `community_post_deck_apply`: in place, same id, date, likes, comments and views, only while its caption is still the one the deck was made from, never one the coach edited or a lesson he wrote his own caption for that day; `before` kept, `community_post_deck_undo` puts it back). The coach sees a deck in the lesson planner and the series card (`NextDeck`) exactly as it'll go out — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found the automated posts didn't feel like social content: a Tuesday tip card was a generic question and an oversized paragraph on beige paper with empty space, and its caption repeated the card word for word (the words card was painted from the caption's own first sentences), every post looked the same, and he wanted carousels with a hook, slides that build and a takeaway, posts with conviction he'd say himself, captions that add something, quality checks before anything goes out, and the posts already up fixed without losing their engagement.
- Only Saturday's scene gets a made photo by itself (`AUTO_PICTURE_KINDS`, `picturedByItself`): a picture idea with no chart, diagram or words card of its own; every other automated post leads with its own card, and the coach adds a photo to any of them only when he wants one (New picture in the post's ⋯, `makePostPicture`, any `pictureKind`). The photo comes from an image model (`generatePicture`, post-pictures.server.ts: OpenAI's `gpt-image-1` when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set, else Google's image model through the Lovable AI gateway; one bright, true-to-life style for every picture, `PICTURE_STYLE`, each kind's own shot, `KIND_PICTURE`, Saturday's from its scene, `SCENE_PICTURE`, plus what the post says, `postPicturePrompt`, never any words in it). The pg_cron job `post-pictures-tick` (every 30 minutes, one picture per run, 2 hours' wait after a failure, `app_settings.post_pictures_status`) makes it for those kinds only (asked for in the query, `.in("series", AUTO_PICTURE_KINDS)`), most visible first: such a post out in the last 36 hours without one, else Saturday's next library post while it's on (it rides as the item's `media`, so it goes out like a photo the coach added and the coach sees it in Crew → Posts ahead of time, `NextPicture`, shown for Saturday only), else an older one from the last three weeks. A made picture lives under `<author>/post-pictures/` and is the only kind replaced or removed; a photo the coach put on always wins, and the drawing stays as the fallback when no picture could be made. Lesson covers (Illustration) use the same `generatePicture` — why: the coach found the coded drawings weak next to what an image model makes, and wants Saturday's post to look like a real Instagram picture without making it himself.
- At 7 (Winnipeg) on the weekdays the coach picks (`community_series_settings.daily_visuals_days`, ISO weekdays, default Friday, Saturday and Sunday = 3 a week; `community_daily_visuals_set_days`, which drops untouched placeholders and skips made ones on days no longer picked) that no other coach post takes (rule below), Crew gets an Athlete Insight (series `daily_visual`, once "daily pictures"; daily-visuals.ts, 20270124090000_daily_pictures.sql + 20270125090000_daily_pictures_rebuild.sql + 20270126090000_daily_pictures_2d_film.sql + 20270127090000_daily_pictures_vivid.sql + 20270211090000_athlete_insights.sql): a post worth opening the app for, an anime frame in JF Effect's house style and a caption made together, written the way the coach would type it himself (Nov 8 2026; `VOICE_GUIDE`, built on his own posts `VOICE_EXAMPLES` (his casual "i think sometimes people get too caught up…" first, then long sessions and rest days) and what isn't him, `NOT_HIS_VOICE`: the post he called scripted and the motivation-page lines he banned): conversational, direct, confident, occasionally dry, one real training idea, accurate, and never professionally edited: casual grammar, fragments, lowercase starts sometimes, no fixed shape (one paragraph or a few short thoughts, a quick observation or a detailed explanation, varied post to post), about 25 to 100 words (`DAILY_MIN_WORDS` 25 is the floor, every form at most the column's 600 characters), his capitals left as he typed them (`tidyCaption` only fixes what's wrong: "dont" / "im" get their apostrophe, RPE / PR / RIR / e1RM in capitals, no "&" or dashes), his habits occasional and never a format (`habitProblems`: "lol", "gonna", "honestly", "i think" each once a post at most and never all at once, no "lol" in a post right after one, no post opening like one around it, `openingOf`), never a slogan or his banned lines ("every little bit counts", "give yourself grace", "listen to your body", "you got this", "movement is medicine", "worse places to spend your time", in `GENERIC_PHRASES`), never a study, statistic or percentage (`RESEARCH`: the lessons carry research, with sources), his opinions as his and "it depends" where it does, never an experience he didn't have, and no names (his shows on the post; `namedPeople` refuses any crew first name). What a day is about is one of seven categories (`DAILY_PILLARS`, stored as `concept.pillar`, `pickPillars`): Powerlifting IQ 20%, Coach's notes 20%, Training mistakes 15%, Athlete mindset 15%, Recovery and performance 10%, Hypertrophy for strength 10%, Competition prep 10%, kept by how far each is behind its share over the 20 days around it plus the weekday's small lean (`DAILY_THEMES` is only a mood: Mon new week … Sat heavy day, Sun rest & reset, the database keys kept), nudged 0.75–1.3× by how the crew responds (`pillarShares`), never the same category two days running; each has its angles (`DAILY_TOPICS`, stored as `topic`; earlier versions' feel-good topics and forms keep their names through `LEGACY_TOPICS` / `LEGACY_FORMAT_NAMES` and count toward no category), and it turns over newer lifters, strength for life, powerlifters and everyone (`crowdForDay`; the database keys kept). Not every post is technical: an observation about how lifters train (the `observation` form), a challenging perspective or a lesson competitive lifting teaches, always as a principle or a pattern, never a story he didn't live. Blunt about the training, never about the person, and never a body put down. Never aimed at men or women (Nov 7 2026): every post is for every athlete (`audienceForDay` = `community_daily_audience` = 'everyone', every guide the same) and labelled by its training relevance (`RELEVANCE` / `relevanceOf`: All Athletes, Powerlifting, Strength Training, Hypertrophy, Recovery, Athlete Mindset, Competition), and no post carries a Bible verse (Nov 8 2026, 20270217090000_no_bible_verses.sql: a post is its caption alone, `dailyCaption` = `community_daily_visual_caption`; the one post out with a verse lost it in place): Sunday's (rest & reset) may lean on a principle the Bible teaches that good training shares (rest, gratitude, patience, humility, consistency), said in plain lifting terms (`SUNDAY_PRINCIPLE`), and `captionProblems` refuses a verse reference or scripture / sermon language (`BIBLE_REF`, `SCRIPTURE_WORDS`), with no Bible, cross or religious symbol in Sunday's picture. A post is made concept first, in steps (`daily-visuals.server.ts`, the `daily-visuals-tick` hook, one step per run, waking the next run itself): the concept (`CONCEPT_PROMPT`: the category's angle, the mood, a specific training moment, the point it makes (true for most lifters, defensible to another good coach), one of the category's `DAILY_FORMATS` (coach's note, breakdown, mistake, short take, question), the picture planned before any caption (words in it only when they're the point, and never when one of the two posts before had some: `noWords`), then three takes on the point, each labelled with its own form and held to that form's limits, the chosen one's form becoming `format`; the hard checks (`captionProblems`: length both ways, research or statistics, a poem of one-line sentences, a long hook, a body put down, lecturing, slogans, AI phrases and scripted lines (`GENERIC_PHRASES`, `AI_TELLS`, `SCRIPTED_PHRASES`: "the old you", "secretly", "signs you're"…, "it's not X, it's Y" twice: `contrastCount`), the scripted shapes (`scriptedProblems`: a setup line ending in a colon, a "me: / my legs:" script, three sentences narrating to "you", a "that's not X. that's Y." kicker, hype, scare quotes, his example copied back or one of its lines borrowed, `EXAMPLE_LINES`, a moral closer, "it's just tuesday now", a story told as his own, `firstPersonStory`: "I'm checking the clock", "I went", "my legs", "my clients" or the "I" dropped ("planned a quick lift."), while his opinions, what he notices and advice stay fine; a coach explaining to "you" is fine, only a scene walked through step by step, `SCENE_TO_YOU`, is a script), names, negative saturation (`negativeLean`, 5+ heavy words), study-speak (`JARGON` minus the words lifters know, `LIFTER_TERMS`: hypertrophy, RIR, eccentric…; "leverages" the lifting word passes, "leverage" the verb doesn't), a third question ending in a row, the picture's words repeated, close to a recent post) throw drafts out; then the editor (`EDITOR_PROMPT`, `editorVerdict`: one question first, would an elite powerlifting coach post this as his own and would a serious lifter be glad they read it; natural, substance, accuracy, clarity, match, fresh, worth it, all at `EDITOR_BARS` with no scripted / hype / AI phrasing / preachy / invented / shaming / generic flag) picks the draft that sounds most like him and is most useful, not the most dramatic or polished, and only fixes what's actually wrong (never into sentence case; a `polished` flag fails a draft that reads professionally edited), else the writer tries again with its notes (3 tries; a brand-new day may take a clean near miss), all before any picture is drawn; the editor's read is kept in `concept.editor`; the post read first (`concept.read`, `parseRead`: its kind (educational, technical, motivational, conversational, humorous, `DAILY_KINDS`), discipline, who it's for, the best way to show it, and whether people are needed at all), the direction (`ART_STYLES`, `concept.art`: `key_frame` the lift as the episode's key frame, `drama` a character moment around training, `insert` the close-up that teaches it, `explainer` the idea shown simply on a clean background; `artForPillar`: recovery, mindset, competition prep and coach's notes lean `drama`, Powerlifting IQ `explainer`), the treatment (`DAILY_RENDERINGS`, `concept.rendering`: anime key art most days, a soft slice-of-life anime, a flat graphic illustration or manga colour when the read calls for it, another than anime only when none of the last three used it), who's in it (`DAILY_CASTS`, `concept.cast`, `castFor` keeps it to the read: a newer lifter, an everyday member, a powerlifter, a physique athlete, a masters lifter, a coach and athlete, a group, hands, nobody; their build follows who they are, `peopleLine`; the kinds in the last two pictures offered last; a woman in it is a woman who trains, never a man's frame or a bodybuilder's upper body (Nov 20 2026: `WOMEN_LOOK`, a feminine face, a natural female frame, slim to lightly toned arms and shoulders, her strength in her legs and hips, muscle suggested the polished anime way, never mapped; `MEN_LOOK` keeps men athletic and as muscular as who they are calls for), her build from her cast turned over day to day (`womanBuildFor` / `CAST_WOMAN_BUILDS`, stored as `concept.woman_build`: slim, curvy, athletic, soft, strong in the hips and legs, and toned only for a physique athlete; the writer gets today's build per cast in `womenLine`, which never tips a post toward a woman (whoever fits the post, men and women about equally), and never calls a woman sturdy, thick or broad up top; `castFor` holds the writer to the casts offered, so a test's `cast` and `lead` ("woman" / "man") check one kind of person on purpose)), the shot (`DAILY_STYLES`, stored as `style`, 13 with a director's camera: establishing, mid-shot, two-shot, the lift, side-on, low angle, over the shoulder, split panel, a still life of objects; no beach, brunch or park; 20270426090000_insights_bright_look.sql lets the column take them), the light (`DAILY_LIGHTS`, `concept.light`: bright and balanced, different post to post: big windows, clean gym light, fresh morning, an open door, soft daylight, skylights, outdoors, a bright hall; the anime_v1 lights are `LEGACY_LIGHT_NAMES`), the palette (`DAILY_PALETTES`, `concept.palette`, none from the last three pictures) and the place (`DAILY_SETTINGS`, `concept.setting`: a bright powerlifting club, a commercial strength floor, a garage with the door open, a university weight room, a performance gym, a community gym, a home setup, an open-air yard, a bodybuilding gym, a recovery corner, meal prep (only for a post about food), a coach's corner, the meet platform, the warm-up room; the ones not used in the last `SETTING_GAP_DAYS` offered first, `settingLine`), and never the same person, cast, place, palette or composition as a recent post, nor a prop two of the last four pictures leaned on unless the post is about it (`PICTURE_PROPS`, `propsLine` / `propRepeat`: a training log, a phone, a chalk bowl, hanging lamps); no readable words in it, so `typography` is "") with what's worked (`learnDailyVisuals` / `pickChoices`: no shot from the last 4 days, light from the last 3, form from the last 2 or angle from the last 4 weeks, `TOPIC_GAP_DAYS`, and no picture close to a recent one, `visualRepeat`; best first, sometimes an untried one; `visualScore` counts a real comment (a reply or 5+ words) and a share (to a story or a repost) far above a like, from `community_daily_visual_stats`, 20270129090000_daily_posts_feel_good.sql) and the captions the coach rejected, never the best-liked captions themselves (the early ones were in a voice he retired); the writer gets his never-say lists from the voice profile, not his texting rules; then the picture, always JF Effect's illustrated house style, a clean, bright 2D frame in the tradition of modern Japanese sports anime (Nov 20 2026: `picturePrompt`: `FILM_LOOK`, written out word for word at the start of every prompt so every post shares one recognizable look, `FILM_LOOK_VERSION` = `anime_v2` stored as `concept.look` (`anime_v1` was the dark, amber Nov 8 look, `photo_v1` the photo look before it): clean line art, flat cel colour with soft shading, uncluttered backgrounds, bright, even light with a neutral white balance and open shadows, no colour grade, one small JF red accent, one clear subject made for a phone, then the treatment, direction, read, shot, light, palette, place, moment and people (adults with adult proportions from any angle, realistic, natural builds for who they are, no veins mapped, never exaggerated muscle, clothes in colour, never a plain black tee on the main person; a simple load with identical plates on both ends, a machine drawn as the real machine, no gym gear in a kitchen); the angle's own brief, `DAILY_TOPICS[…].picture`, so it shows the lesson: fatigue management = a lifter resting between heavy squat sets, deadlift technique = a side-on setup, progressive overload = the log beside a loaded bar; the equipment and technique right: one straight bar, the same calibrated plates both sides in their real colours (red 25, blue 20, yellow 15, green 10) with collars, the lift's real grip, stance and bar position; `FILM_AVOID`: no orange, amber, golden or sepia cast, no dark or moody light, black areas, spotlight in darkness or hanging lamps over a dim room, no photorealism, 3D render, generic AI-art sheen, chibi / cutesy / moe or giant eyes, nobody who looks under 20, no school uniforms or fanservice, no exaggerated muscles, vein maps or everyone in black t-shirts, no muddy colour, no busy frame, no lifestyle scenes, stock staging, speech bubbles or readable text), made from the words alone (no reference frame: one would hand on its people and gym); a New image that keeps a caption shows what that caption names, and a kept caption from before the seven categories is read for its angle and category (`classify`: every angle offered, the category follows the one picked), never given one by rotation, and a review that looks at it with the caption (`REVIEW_PROMPT` → `reviewVerdict`: it reads as `anime` (or a clean drawn `illustration` when the graphic or manga treatment was asked for), its `maturity` mature, its `colour` bright (dark, tinted orange / amber and flat fail), its `physique` realistic or none (exaggerated fails), its `women` natural, none or unchecked (overbuilt fails: any woman, the background's included, with broad or bulky shoulders, big traps, defined delts, biceps or back or a masculine face; the coach's card's "Natural, believable bodies") and its `clarity` clean (anything else fails), no readable words but a plate's weight or a clock, defects are objective errors only (anatomy, equipment or technique that's wrong, a steroid-looking physique, a brand) and taste is scored, every score at `REVIEW_BARS`, including `uplift`, `on_topic` (strength training, never a lifestyle scene), `teaches` (the picture shows the specific thing the post is about; a good-looking gym scene that could go with any post fails), `technique` (the lift, the gear and the bodies drawn so a coach would show it to an athlete, also in `qualityChecks`' "Technically accurate") and `readable` (the subject reads at a glance on a phone)); a failed picture is made again with the fix, 2 a concept, 3 concepts at most). It goes live only when it passes, in one step (`community_daily_visual_apply`, server only); a day that can't be made keeps what it had and says why (a new day takes its best try only when nothing's wrong with the picture itself), and every post goes out on its own at its time (Nov 8 2026: "i want all posts scheduled and i can edit them anytime without needing approval before post"; `community_daily_visual_held` is always false, 20270213090000_posts_on_their_own.sql; the maker still remakes a failing picture or caption first, 3 concepts at most), and he edits, moves or skips any of them any time before; Publish now puts one out early. `qualityChecks` answers his seven questions (relevant to athletes, the image shows the lesson, real coaching value, sounds like Jared, technically accurate, different from recent posts, worth saving or discussing) and "Bright and clear on a phone" from the editor's and the review's scores, shown on each post. It runs on OpenAI (text, vision, `generatePicture(…, { lovable: false })`); Lovable AI only when the coach switches it on (`community_series_settings.daily_visuals_lovable`, off by default), and with no AI set up the job checks once an hour and the card says what to add. Nothing is lost: every replaced version (remake, rejection, his edit, a restore) is kept by the `community_daily_visuals_keep_version` trigger in `community_daily_visual_versions` (staff read only) and can be put back (`community_daily_visual_restore`). A posted day is remade in place (`community_daily_visual_sync_post`): the same post id, date, likes, comments and author, its `updated_at` put back so it never moves in the feed, no new post and no notification. Test posts (`runDailyVisualTest`, the hook's `{ test: { audience, theme, day?, pillar?, topic?, format?, crowd?, style?, captionOnly?, cast?, lead? } }`; `captionOnly` runs just the writer and the editor and returns the result, nothing saved) run the whole pipeline into `community_daily_visual_tests` (staff read, shown as Test pictures on the card), never a day or the feed; a new look is tried there before it's applied, and existing days move to it only when the coach approves (the card's Approve this look, `community_daily_visuals_restyle`: pictures remade, captions kept, the before in Versions), while new days are made in it. The coach works it in Crew → Posts → Manage → Athlete Insights (`DailyPicturesCard`, titled Athlete Insights, each day also in the Posts schedule opening the same sheet: the weekday picker, Coming up (what needs his OK counted) and Published, each row the image, date, relevance, topic, caption and status; a post's sheet: the post as the feed shows it, its seven checks, Approve / Reject, New image (caption kept) / New caption (image kept) / Whole new post in a shot he picks, Edit caption (his words win), Versions with Restore, Publish now and Move to another free day (`community_daily_visual_move`: never a posted one or a past day, never onto another Insight or a day another coach post takes; the day it left is marked moved so it isn't refilled), Skip, on / off; a published post gets the new look only when he says so, one at a time or with "Give N posts the new look", while upcoming ones got theirs when the look changed), and Pause stops it with the other coach posts — why: on Nov 7 the coach asked for "a serious training community led by a coach who understands the science, execution, and psychology of getting stronger. Not another fitness motivation page", and his own two posts (long sessions, rest days) set the voice; then for Athlete Insights: pictures that teach the lesson in a real strength gym instead of lifestyle illustrations, and on Nov 8, once the photos had the subject right, not photorealism but high-quality, mature anime with serious powerlifting and bodybuilding themes, one consistent, recognizable style with varied characters, gyms, light and compositions ("still frames from a beautifully animated powerlifting anime, not AI-generated photographs"), content organised by training relevance instead of "for him / for her", 3 quality posts a week instead of filling the feed, and weak content held for review rather than published; the feel-good version's pictures drifted to picnics and parks, the first version's one-liners read like an auto-filled motivation account, the second's like a copywriter (Nov 5's "Signs you're not starting over again… The new you just calls it Thursday", "me: / my ego:" bits, scenes narrated to "you" and "that's not boring. that's proof." kickers), and the coach wants every caption to be something he'd naturally post himself ("honestly…", the same photo look every day), the coach wants posts that feel made by someone who gets their life, every picture a frame from a beautiful, colourful hand-drawn 2D film (not 3D or stock, and not the dark, muddy, brown look the first 2D prompt's "painterly", "soft", "sophisticated palette" and dark-room lights produced), different in moment, shot and light each day, with any words in it matching the caption's story, his Lovable balance must never be spent on them, and a remake must never lose a post's likes, comments or the original; and the reviewer's own "empty" for no text read as a stray word (`NO_TEXT`), and on Nov 20 2026 that the anime frames were consistently too dark, too orange, too muscular and repetitive (the seven from Nov 6 to 27 averaged 0.32 lightness with 34% of the frame near black and 52% amber, four of them a thick-armed man in a black t-shirt writing in a notebook under hanging lamps), and he wanted brighter, balanced, varied colour, real and varied bodies, broader places and compositions, and the post interpreted before its picture is planned, people only when they're needed, with the anime look kept and some tasteful variation. Later that day he found the women drawn with excessively muscular upper bodies, like competitive bodybuilders rather than naturally athletic women (Nov 20's athlete, a "sturdy" powerlifter, came out with heavy traps and shoulders and a hard jaw): the casts described a man's powerlifter build for everyone and nothing said how a woman is drawn.
- Cleo has no floating button anywhere in admin: the way in is Cleo's row pinned at the top of Messages (tap to type, the mic to talk), plus ⌘/Ctrl+Shift+S, the `/admin#cleo` push link and `openSummer` from screens that ask Cleo something (Finance home, Taxes & Books). `SummerAssistant` stays mounted in the admin layout only to host the chat sheet and those ways in, and draws nothing of its own; all of them show only for someone who has Cleo (`useCleoAccess`, the AI-branding rule below) — why: the coach found the floating sparkle button cluttered every admin screen when Messages already gives one-tap access.
- Native forms have three more fields (form-fields.ts, pure, tested; `NF_QUESTION_TYPES`): `competitions` (meet levels as chips, each picked one a short card with an optional name, date, place, registered, qualified and federation; `validation.none_option` stands alone; stored as `value_json` `MeetEntry[]`), `ranking` (↑ ↓ rows, the order on screen saved on Submit if never moved; stored as `[{key, label}]`) and `info` (a heading with text, never numbered or answered). A choice or ranking can take its items from a competitions question (`validation.options_from`), a choice can show in groups by how its options start (`validation.group_prefixes`, opening on the athlete's sex), `validation.prefill` also takes `full_name`, `age_class` and `weight_class` (the next rule), and `validation.remember` opens a form sent again on their last submitted answers (never over one already given), all set per question in the builder with help text. Every answer reads as text one way (`answerAsText`, `formAnswerText`: a list, meets and a ranking by their labels), which follow-up questions use too (`shouldShowQuestion` skipped list answers before, so "show when the checkboxes contain …" never matched). The renderer starts from the saved answers only when they change (`appliedAnswers`), since React can re-run that effect with the form's state kept and it wiped a prefilled or just-typed answer — why: the coach wanted forms built for a phone (no giant lists, no retyping meets), and the Competition Calendar + Strategy form needed meets, a ranking of them and a primary meet chosen from them.
- A form question the app can already answer starts from it (form-prefill.ts, pure, tested): `validation.prefill` names the source, the renderer saves it as the answer when the form opens, and the client changes it for this form only (only a changed sex goes back to the profile). The sources: their name, sex and height; a fresh weigh-in; their training schedule (`training_days`: `committed_training_days`, the count or the days themselves); when they actually train (`training_time`: the window most of their last `TRAINING_TIME_RECENT` workouts in `TRAINING_TIME_DAYS` started in, in their own time zone, `workoutStartHours`, read from the coach's own option words by `optionHours`, only when at least `TRAINING_TIME_SHARE` of `TRAINING_TIME_MIN`+ agree); Goals & Setup's goal (`goal`, `goalOption`); every age class their birth year allows that season (`age_class`, `seasonYear`: October on is next year); the weight class (`weight_class`: the coach's next meet prep, then their competition profile (the next rule), else the IPF class their week of weigh-ins puts them in, `ipfClassFor`, a junior's lightest class only while they're one); age classes from the competition profile while their birth year allows them, else every one it allows; the meet a checklist is for (its class falling back to the competition profile's). Each says whether it beats the last answer (`strong`): a fact the app keeps (their schedule, birth year, workouts, meet prep, a weigh-in, their profile) wins over what they said last time, a starting point (Goals & Setup's goal, a weight class from weigh-ins) only fills a question they've never answered, and age classes keep the ones they picked last time that they can still lift. `startingAnswer` is the one rule, used by the form (one seeding effect that waits for both the profile and the last answers, so which lands first never decides, and never over an answer given) and the weekly check-in's Nutrition Update (nutrition-fold.ts). While an answer is still the app's, one quiet line under it says where it came from (`prefillNote`, `data-form-prefilled`: "From your training schedule", "From your weigh-ins (181.2 lb average)"; age classes always say what their birth year allows); it goes once they change it. The builder's "Fill in from" lists every source a question type can take and offers the one its words point to (`suggestPrefill`, `data-prefill-suggestion`; never a handler's name or a meet's goal), and an AI-drafted form starts with it set (`parseFormDraft`). The live Nutrition Update's training days, training time and goal were switched on by 20270404090000_form_questions_sync_profile.sql (only where no fill was set) — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach found forms asking for things the app already knows (the Competition form's age class only hinted at it, and the monthly Nutrition Update asked training days and when they train while their schedule and every workout's start time were on file) and wanted them synced from the profile, still changeable on the form for a one-off; and a remembered answer and a profile value used to race, so last month's "3 days" could win over a schedule that now says 4.
- A lifter's competition profile (`athlete_competition_profiles`, 20270405090000_athlete_competition_profiles.sql; competition-profile.ts pure and tested) is the weight class they compete in and the age classes they enter, stored one way (`canonicalClass`: "83", "120+"; `ageClassKey`: "open", "master 1"), whatever a form's options call them, and read back into any form's own options. It's kept by three hands: the athlete in Account → Competition, their coach (or an admin) in Training → Athlete setup → Competition (the same `CompetitionProfileEditor`, every tap saves; RLS lets each write only their own `source`, 'client' or 'coach'), and every form they send: after `form_submitted` the server writes back what its synced questions answered (`syncCompetitionProfile`, competition-profile.server.ts: `age_class`, `weight_class`, `meet_weight_class`; only what the form asked, source 'form' and the submission). Forms start from it as a fact (form-prefill.ts, `COMPETITION_SOURCE`, "From your competition profile"), so a coach's change reaches the next form and a client's change on a form reaches the coach, with no question asked twice. Both places show it only for someone who competes (`useCompetes`: anything on file, a meet prep, a competition plan, a powerlifting goal or style), and each says who changed it last — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach wanted powerlifting forms to save into the athlete's profile so he has the information, still changeable by the client or the coach, on the profile or on a form, and without the client re-answering it every time.
- Competition Calendar + Strategy (`COMPETITION_FORM_ID`, form_type `competition_strategy`, 20270330091000_competition_strategy_form.sql) is a seeded template on the form engine: About you (name prefilled, age classes with the birth-year hint, weight class grouped Men / Women with Men's and Women's sub-junior & junior-only classes) and Your season (the meets they're actually considering as `competitions`, the original categories kept with World Championships (IPF) and World Games (IWGA) named apart and the old "categories" question folded into it; then, only when competing, their order, the one meet to peak for, the goal there, how many full peaks, non-peak meets, a bodyweight plan; then anything else), every answer remembered for the next send, never auto-archived for going unused. Questions are found by `validation.plan_key`, never their wording. Submitting writes a structured plan (`athlete_competition_plans`, server only through `recordCompetitionPlan` from the `form_submitted` event, with `ensureCompetitionPlanFn` as the coach's backstop; staff-only RLS) with what changed since their last approved plan (`planChanges`). The coach reads it on the form's chat card (`CompetitionPlanSummary`: the plan from their answers, the changes, Approve / Dismiss / Undo, and on Approve optionally the primary meet as the program's dated goal, `program_intel_profiles.long_term_goal` through `planGoal`). Only an approved plan reaches the program AI (`planBriefText` in the brief's athlete section, with weeks out, said to be context and never a reason to rewrite a running program; a newer unapproved one is said to be waiting; the form's raw answers are left out of the brief's check-ins), and nothing here ever writes a program — why: the coach plans an athlete's year around the meets that matter most and wanted the form native, fast and repeatable, with the answers usable by programming only after he's reviewed them.
- A submitted native form shows its answers to the coach right in the chat card (`SubmittedFormAnswers`, messenger-checkin-card.tsx: every answered question in form order via `answeredQuestions`, form-answer-text.ts, first 6 then "Show all"), and the Nutrition Update card also shows its AI plan's state and writes a missing one when opened, like the Nutrition panel (`planMissing`). `nf_submissions.client_id` references `clients` (20270102090000_nf_submissions_client_fk.sql) — why: the coach expects a form's answers in Messenger, not a link to the profile, and without that key every `client:client_id(...)` lookup through a submission failed ("Could not find a relationship"), so no Nutrition Update AI plan was ever written and the coach's panel could not load the request.
- A Nutrition Update AI plan is only ever written in the background, never inside the request that asked for it: Submit (`startNutritionPlan`) and the coach's panel / chat card (`generateNutritionPlanFn`) only queue it (`queueNutritionPlan`: `nutrition_ai_plans.status = 'pending'` with that run's phase / workout-meals choice, or null for the defaults), the `nutrition_ai_plans_queue_run` trigger calls `/api/public/hooks/nutrition-plans-tick` at once through pg_net, and pg_cron `nutrition-plans-tick` (every 5 minutes, only while something from the last 2 days waits) catches the rest; `runQueuedPlan` claims one plan with a single conditional update (pending, or generating for `STALE_RUN_MS`+) and runs `runPlan` — why: the hosting cancels a request when its phone or tab leaves, so a plan written inside it (two AI passes, a minute or more) was cut off mid-plan and stuck "generating" forever, which is how David's plan stalled on Oct 27.
- The Nutrition Update AI adjusts from the plan the client was on (the latest `nutrition_targets` made before the submission): it's snapshotted with its results (bodyweight trend, weekly check-in adherence and flags) into `nutrition_ai_plans.previous_plan` the first time the plan runs and reused on every regenerate, and the form's meal choice / free-meal questions are read by their exact labels (`nutrition-history.ts`) — why: coaches replace or delete old targets, so a regenerate must still see what the client was actually on, and a reworded question would silently stop reaching the AI.
- Cardio is one plan, everywhere: `cardio_targets` is the client's cardio plan (set by the nutrition plan, `source = 'nutrition'`; by the coach, `'coach'`/null; proposed with a generated program when there's none, `'program'`; or read from cardio already in their program, `'program_rows'`, in that order of priority) and `cardio_completions` is what they did. On training days the plan's session is the last exercise of every upcoming workout, linked by `pl_exercise_rows.cardio_target_id` and kept in step by `program_cardio_sync` (DB triggers on `cardio_targets` and a block becoming the client's, plus pg_cron `program-cardio-sync` hourly for programs assigned any other way; a started or logged workout is never changed, and a `'program_rows'` plan only links the program's own rows), and logging that exercise is the day's cardio done (`pl_row_results_cardio_done` → `cardio_completions`, `completion_target = 'workout'`), and a day's cardio shows in one place only: on such a training day it's that workout's own Cardio, after the lifting (`workout-cardio.ts`: the logger orders the rows with the plan's row last, `cardioLast`, whatever its stored place, under a "Cardio" heading in the list (`data-cardio-section`), "Cardio" in Focus's top bar instead of "Exercise 9 of 9" (`focusPlaceLabel`, the card's own "Your cardio plan" line only there), the progress pill counting the lifts with the cardio a pulse line beside them (`liftAndCardioProgress`; never a heart, which read as a favourite), moves and new exercises staying with the lifting; only a row linked to the plan, `cardio_target_id`, never a program's own timed rows; and it's done whenever suits them, before, during or after the lifting or later that day: logging it never starts the workout (`startsWorkout={false}` on its card and set rows, `refreshCardio`: no session clock, no `markInProgress`), the % ring and the finish's logging percentage read the lifts only (`liftLogged`), the finish review pops by itself only once everything's logged, the cardio too (`autoFinishReady`: it never covers the cardio card, the next thing, done right after or later), while the lifts done is enough for Finish to be the review in one tap (`reviewReady`: "Review to Finish" on the cardio card and the list's finish card), and logging the cardio pops it like any last set, the recap and Vs the plan never count it not logged yet as missed (`computeWorkoutSummary` / `planCheck`), the Workouts page's progress and "in progress" leave it out (`getClientWorkouts`: `cardioRowIds`), and the day card has it as its own line under the main button (`item.cardio`, `data-day-cardio`: "Cardio · 10 min · any time today · Log it", or ✓ Done) opening the workout on it (`?focus=<row>`, never Focus mode); done is one rule everywhere, the database's (`cardioSetDone` / `cardioRowDone`: a time over zero or marked complete, as `pl_row_results_cardio_done` writes `cardio_completions`), and the card trusts the day's own small read first (`useWorkoutProgress` → `dayProgress`: the lifts' sets plus `cardioDone`, painted from the list and always asked again), never only the whole-program list; logging the cardio sets the cached list and that read in place (`withCardioDone`) and refreshes Nutrition's line, the cardio card and Home's calendar; the list's step-by-step fallback (when its one nested read fails, e.g. a network blip as an iPhone wakes) asks for ids at most 150 at a time and pages each (`readByIds`), and any read that fails throws, so a failed read is never cached as "nothing logged" (one URL with a long-time client's 838 row ids is refused, HTTP 400, and Jared McIntyre's Workouts card said "Log it" six hours after his Nov 17 walk was logged); its note the plan's incline · speed · zone and the coach's cue, `cardio_row_notes`, never the steps / calories "whichever comes first" wording) and the Workouts tab and the workout page show no card (`ClientCardioSection` returns nothing when `inWorkout`); on a day off it's the Workouts tab's day card, logged there (`useDayCardio`, the next rule); Nutrition shows one status line that opens where it's logged (`variant="line"`, `CardioStatusLine`), never a second card. The card picks the session for the day the label names (`pickCardioTargetForDay`: a training day's session follows a moved workout; a day without a workout never gets the training-day session). Generate Next Program never writes cardio rows (`withoutCardioRows`; the prompts say so): it reads the plan (`cardio.ts`, `ctx.cardio`, `setup.cardio`), gives lifting the stated session length less the training-day cardio (`liftingMinutesWith`) and counts it in the session maximum, and with no plan proposes one from the goal (`proposeCardio`: fat loss, recomposition, general fitness, else none) that Assign writes only when they still have none (`cardioTargetsFromProposal`) before `program_cardio_sync`; the nutrition AI is told the training days and which rows the program set (`currentCardioText`), and Cleo's nutrition saves sync cardio like the editor (20270115090000_cardio_one_plan.sql) — why: Marc's program said a 10-min treadmill walk while his nutrition plan said 15, logging one never checked off the other, and the coach found cardio in three places, out of sync (and then still on three screens: a read-only card on Workouts and Nutrition pointing into the workout, with the workout's own note hidden behind "Read notes"); and on Nov 17 2026 the coach wanted the cardio counted as its own thing but still in the logger (it read as one more numbered exercise, "Exercise 9 of 9", in the lifts' count, and could sit mid-workout once an exercise was added after it); and that day, once the review read the lifts only, it popped over the cardio card right after his last lift (Cable Triceps Pushdown, 5 of 5), before the walk, which "doesn't feel efficient".
- Cardio and daily steps move with the nutrition plan: the AI writes DAILY STEPS TARGETS (the day's total steps → `nutrition_target_days.steps`) and CARDIO SESSIONS per day type, the coach reviews them in the same editor as the day's calories, and saving an Active plan syncs each day type's `cardio_targets` row through `planCardioSync` (nutrition-cardio-sync.ts): matched by day type (Rest = Non-Training), only changed fields written, missing days created, multi-week cardio programs never touched. `cardio_targets.step_target` is the cardio SESSION's steps and stays automatic — why: daily steps (8-12k) written into the session target would make a 20-minute walk "incomplete", and cardio drifting out of step with the calories was the problem being fixed.
- A client's next weekly check-in / nutrition review is never on or before the last one done or skipped (`nextDueAfterLast` in `ensureNextOccurrence`, and the same check in `seed_messenger_checkin_occurrences`), and the seed closes every overdue open one, sent or not (`skip_reason` `superseded_unsubmitted` / `missed_never_sent`, 20270104090000_checkins_never_stuck.sql), since it only adds the next when none is open — why: Nicole's timezone moved from Winnipeg to Edmonton, "a second after the last due time" was still that Sunday, so her next check-in landed in the week she'd just done, was never sent, and blocked every one after it from Oct 11.
- A nutrition plan's text is stored plain: ChatGPT's copy button turns each line break into a trailing backslash ("75 g Oats (dry weight)\") and escapes punctuation ("1\."), so the paste box and the targets dialog clean it (`plainPastedText`, plain-paste.ts) and the database does the same on every save of `nutrition_target_days.notes` and `nutrition_targets.food_weighing_rules` / `client_notes` / `water` (`nutrition_plain_text`, 20270104092000_nutrition_plain_text.sql; the same cases in plain-paste.test.ts must pass on both) — why: Jarrett Simard's foods each showed a slash at the end and his macro lines didn't read as macros, and Cleo or a published review can write the text too.
- AI meal plans are bodybuilding simple and shelf first (`MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT` rule 2d, nutrition-ai-prompts.ts): plain foods only, no recipes, dressings or listed seasonings; chicken / lean beef (sometimes fish) and rice / potatoes / sweet potatoes by default, with other foods only from the client's answers; every food one a normal grocery store always has, picked from the most common up (SHELF FIRST lists the staples by kind), and when a client's diet or allergies rule a staple out the most common food that still fits it (AIP, paleo, grain-free, Whole30 or no-nightshades → sweet potatoes, then fruit, then squash; gluten-free, dairy-free, vegetarian and vegan each named), never a specialty one: a hard-to-find food (`HARD_TO_FIND_FOODS`, plan-foods.ts: cassava / yuca, taro, plantain, jicama, specialty grains and flours, game and organ meat, specialty fats; never kidney beans, goat cheese or artichoke hearts) only when the client named that exact food, a diet's name alone never counting. Code checks it: `hardToFindFoods` reads the menus (never the free meal or the Swap lines) against the client's own answers, a new plan with one goes through the same fix as meal prep (`withPrepFix` with the answers, `mealPrepFixPrompt`'s FIX THE FOODS with the staple by kind, `SHELF_STAPLE`; counted like drift, and a fix that keeps one more is refused), and the coach's preview line says "hard to find in stores: …". Every other path that writes or suggests food holds to it too: a coach's change (Change something, `runRevision`; Change it with AI, `runPlanEdit`) wins, but a hard-to-find food the AI brought in on its own (`introducedHardToFind`: not on the plan before, not named in the coach's words or the client's answers) goes back once for the staple (`withShelfFix`, keys `update_shelf` / `edit_shelf`), and the AI change's review and a pasted plan's preview say "Hard to find in stores: …" (`data-plan-ai-hard-to-find`, `data-paste-hard-to-find`); Cleo and the check-in reply drafts get `SHELF_FIRST_ADVICE` (summer-context.ts, submission-reviews.functions.ts). Cleo's `set_nutrition_targets` sets numbers only: each day kept by name keeps its meals and steps (it used to wipe the client's meal plan), and the card's preview says when the numbers moved under them so the coach can fit the meals with Change it with AI. A free meal keeps to a client's allergies and medical diet (`freeMealRule`'s third argument: "whatever you're craving that still fits your diet", never burger + fries examples), FOOD-WEIGHING RULES names only the kinds of food the plan uses, vegetables are a 75–150 g side never bigger than the meal's carb, fibre is reported, never a target, and FOOD-WEIGHING RULES ends with up to 3 same-macro "Swap:" lines — why: the coach wants plans a client can shop for and prep without clutter, the old 14 g fibre per 1000 kcal target pushed the AI to 400 g of vegetables beside 400 g of rice, and on Nov 12 2026 Fionna's AIP plan put cassava in every meal (AIP rules out rice and white potatoes, and the AI reached past sweet potatoes, which fit AIP and every store has, to a carb most stores don't), with "burger + fries, pasta" as her free-meal ideas and rice, pasta, oats and eggs in her weighing rules.
- AI meal plans count what comes in a unit and weigh the rest (`MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT` rule 3): bread, bagels, eggs, scoops, rice cakes, wraps, cans and whole fruit as "2 slices bread" / "½ bagel" / "1 scoop whey protein" in whole or half units, everything else in grams; the meal plan reader (`splitFoodLine`) and the grocery list (`leadingQty`) read "½", "1½" and "1 1/2" as amounts — why: nobody should need a scale for a slice of toast, and a half that read as part of the food's name broke the shopping list.
- AI meal plans are meal-prep plans, one container (`MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT` rule 2c): every main meal on every menu, the Pre- and Post-Workout meals included, is one protein food and one carb food at ONE set of grams (e.g. 150 g chicken + 200 g rice); the protein may rotate between lean proteins at the same grams and the carb only within rice / pasta or potatoes / sweet potatoes (never rice in one container and potatoes in another), breakfast / snacks are fixed meals, protein and fats never change between menus, and day types differ only by whole things: Non-Training leaves the carb out of 1–2 containers, High adds one container or a counted carb snack (never a doubled or bigger portion), and the last 10–30 g of carbs is closed with counted units (a banana, a rice cake, ½ bagel), never by re-weighing; the container only changes size when the number of meals changes, and then every one changes together. Prep beats precision: a Daily Total within about ±5% of its calories and ±10 g of its protein is on target (`planTotalsIssues`, the prompts' TARGETS line). The targets give it whole steps to move (`TARGETS_PROMPT`: protein and fats flat, Non-Training one 40–80 g carb step under Training, High one or two over), the free-meal High Day keeps the prepped meals (`freeMealRule`), and coach updates land totals the same way (`REVISE_MEAL_RULES`). Code checks it, not the model: `prepDrift` (plan-foods.ts) names every weighed food written at more than one amount (`food`), main meals whose protein or carb grams differ when each food alone is consistent (`container`: a meal is a container when it has a weighed meat / fish / plant protein and a weighed rice / pasta / potato carb; eggs, yogurt, oats and cream of rice are fixed-meal foods) and containers that switch between rice and potatoes (`mixed`); counted foods and the free meal never count. A new plan that drifts, or whose Daily Totals are off their targets or don't add up from its meals (nutrition-intel.ts), gets a fix (`withPrepFix` → `mealPrepFixPrompt`, two rounds at most, keys `fix` / `fix2`, each kept only when it's the same menus with no more drift and fewer problems, drift counting 3× a totals issue), and the coach's plan preview says "Meal prep: every main meal is the same container" or what's weighed differently. Coach updates aren't checked (his instructions win) — why: the coach preps a client's week in one go, and plans that re-weighed every meal per day type (150 g rice on rest days, 200 g on training days, chicken 150 / 165 / 180 g), doubled a portion on the High Day, sized the workout meals bigger by bodyweight or gave each meal its own grams meant measuring each container differently; he'd rather a plan be a few grams off its targets than hard to prep.
- A plan day's meals are read against its targets, never trusted to its "Daily Total" line (plan-coverage.ts, pure, tested), and that check is the coach's alone: the client's meal plan shows the meals as written (names, calories, macros, foods, instructions) and their targets, with no discrepancy line, warning or explanation (a firm product decision, Nov 18 2026). `dayCoverage` adds up each meal's Approximate macros as written (4 / 4 / 9; every way macros are written goes through one reader, `readMacroLine` in macro-line.ts, used by the client's meal cards, the coach's plan view, the PDF and the food log alike: one a line, "36 g protein, 65 g carbohydrates, 12 g fat", "41 g protein / 68 g carbs / 24 g fat", "40P / 60C / 8F", never "30 g protein powder"; and one total heading, `DAILY_TOTAL_HEADING` / `readDailyTotal`: "Daily Total", "Approximate Daily Total", "Totals", its lines one macro a line or on one line, so a total is never read as the last meal's macros), and the meals cover the day within 5% of its calories (`COVER_KCAL_SHARE`) and no more than 10 g under its protein (`COVER_PROTEIN_G`), a coach's "Flexible calories" section (`FLEX_HEADING`: its kcal or macros) counted and shown as its own note after the meals. The coach's plan view (`PlanDaysView`: Current plan and the AI plan's review) puts the issue first in one amber block: meals short of or over the day (`coachCoverageLine`), a meal whose macros aren't what its foods give (`mealsOffTheirFoods`) and an amount that can't be right (`mealMath` `odd`: none of a food, or more than `MAX_SINGLE_FOOD_G`), with Fix the meals with AI on the Current plan (`onFixMeals` → `prefillPlanAiChange(…, BRING_MEALS_TO_TARGETS)`: it lands in Change it with AI to send and review, never applied by itself); a day whose meals don't all say their macros says it can't be checked; a day whose calories aren't what its own macros add up to says so (`targetMathLine`); and Show the math opens each meal as written and by its foods (`MealMathRows`: written = the plan's own numbers, what the client sees; by its foods = USDA values for plain staples, an estimate). The plan editor checks a day's numbers as the coach types (`DayNumbersCheck`: calories against the macros with "Use N kcal", only on a tap, and the meals against them). Check before publishing and Change it with AI's review say the same. An AI plan publishes each day's targets unless its meals cover them (`planDayNumbers`: then the meals' own numbers, so the ring is what they eat; else the targets with calories from their macros at 4 / 4 / 9, so a calorie line its macros don't add up to is never published), so meals that fall short never quietly lower a day's targets, and an edit's answer is read the same way (`readEditedPlan`). Every Daily Total the AI writes is made the sum of its meals in code (`withHonestTotals`: a new plan, Change something, Change it with AI, and the plan as the AI reads it, `planAsMealPlanText`); the generator's check reads the meals against the targets (`planTotalsIssues`), and a meal whose macros aren't what its foods give (`mealsOffTheirFoods`, food-values.ts: USDA values for the plain staples only, raw / dry / cooked as written, counted units at their label size, a meal judged only when every food in it reads, off by more than 12% and 80 kcal or overstating protein by 10 g; never a whole day, where 8–10% is ordinary label error) goes back in the same fix ("FIX THE MACROS"); each draft is judged with its Daily Totals written from its meals, so a model's arithmetic never costs a round. Nothing rewrites a saved plan: no job, migration or screen regenerates, rebalances or republishes one, existing plans change only by the coach's own action (Edit, Change it with AI, Publish), and Devin's stored plan is the regression case, copied word for word in src/test/fixtures/devin-nov-2026.ts and pinned to its production md5 — why: on Nov 18 2026 Devin Buth asked his coach if he could eat more because his three meals came to about 1,900 kcal: his plan (written Nov 9 by the old Gemini pipeline, published Nov 11) put 2,579 kcal on his ring from a Daily Total that copied the targets, over meals adding up to 1,854 (about 2,050 by their foods: Meal 3's 702 kcal of beef and potatoes is about 835); nothing compared the meals with the targets and publishing took the day's numbers from that line; Dwayne's June plan, built by hand in the editor, saved round calories (3,200 / 2,800 / 3,400) beside macros adding up to 3,020 / 2,669 / 3,184, which nothing compared, and its "Approximate Daily Total" written one macro a line was read by the paste reader as protein only and, from #1052, by the meal readers as Meal 5's macros; Marc's and Dwayne's one-line and slash macros showed on their meal cards as a food with no calories and couldn't be logged in one tap; and the coach decided clients never see a discrepancy line: the plan is fixed on the coach's side.
- The Nutrition Update AI uses the training AI's architecture: its targets (calories, macros, steps, cardio) are a coaching decision routed like the program strategy (`src/lib/ai/routing.ts` task `nutrition`: GPT-6 Astra, then GPT-5.5, strong tier only, never a quiet downgrade) and the meal plan, its fix and coach updates are writing (`nutrition_write`: GPT-5.5, then GPT-5-mini), all on the OpenAI key, through `nutritionCall` (nutrition-ai-plans.server.ts → `stagedCall`: background mode, a request polls ≤45 s (`nutritionPollConfig`) and a run still thinking goes back to `pending`, which the queue trigger turns into the next hook call, carrying `nutrition_ai_plans.progress` (the prompts built once, the finished calls, the call being polled; 20270224090000_nutrition_plans_openai_background.sql), so no request waits minutes and nothing is asked twice; `progress` is cleared when a run finishes or fails and when a new plan or update is queued; `app_settings.ai_routes` overrides; the models that answered on `nutrition_ai_plans.model`; admins see the routes at `/admin/settings/ai`); `global_ai_config.default_model` no longer decides nutrition. The targets AI reads, besides the form, the program and the last plan: the athlete as Program Intelligence reads them (`loadNutritionAthlete` → `nutritionAthleteText`, nutrition-athlete.ts: `buildGeneratorIntel`'s `development` via `developmentText(…, { exercises: false })`, so bodyweight phases, each competition lift's strength per bodyweight phase, training age, the weight class and the rate to make it, workout adherence, fatigue and what's missing, plus Goals & Setup's goals, the coach's dated long-term goal and the next meet; never blocks the plan, 30 s at most), and, in the last plan's results (nutrition-intel.ts, pure, tested): the bodyweight trend as a least-squares line with its day-to-day scatter (`weightTrend`: 4+ weigh-ins over 2+ weeks, the plan's first 2 weeks left out when 3+ remain, `clear` only when the slope is 2+ standard errors from flat, so normal fluctuation reads as flat), the app's food log per logged day (`foodLogSummary`, `member_meal_logs` through the client's member row), and the maintenance their results imply (`observedMaintenance`: intake from the food log when it covers most days, else the plan's weekly average, `weeklyAverageKcal`, only at 4/5+ adherence, minus ~7,700 kcal a kg of trend; nothing claimed otherwise). `TARGETS_PROMPT` reasons from those (their results first, rates as starting points adjusted for leanness, meet distance and strength, a change sized from the gap and closed fully only on a clear 4+ week trend, protein 1.6–2.2 g/kg, fats with a 0.5 g/kg floor, carbs for training, the block and the meet read like a coach) instead of fixed deficits, ratios and kcal steps, keeps the coach's format, meal-prep carb steps and previous-plan rules, and ends with COACH NOTES (Why, Watch, Missing, Flags). Safety: `safetyNotes` finds an eating disorder, pregnancy, diabetes, GLP-1 medication, kidney, heart, thyroid, digestive conditions or PCOS in the answers (never from "none", "before bed" or "nursing student") and the AI keeps those plans conservative with "plan this with their doctor or a registered dietitian", never plans dehydration, diuretics or a crash weight cut, and flags a weight class over ~1%/week for the coach to decide. Nothing applies itself: the coach publishes with Update plan as before, and the panel shows "Check before publishing" (`coachChecks`: calories its macros don't add to, under 1,200 / 1,500 kcal, fat under 0.5 g/kg, protein under 1.2 g/kg, health mentions, the AI's Flags). COACH NOTES never reaches a client-readable row (`withoutCoachNotes` on publish and in the editor's prefill: clients can read their own `nutrition_targets.admin_notes`) — why: the coach wants nutrition to reason like the program AI from what the athlete actually did, the targets had run on the general default model with no fallback, never saw a strength trend, a meet's weight class rate, a food log or a trend's noise, and hard-coded a 15–25% deficit and 100–200 kcal steps for everyone, and health flags must never land where a client can read them.
- The coach's Nutrition tab on a client (`/admin/clients/$id`, Nutrition) is five cards in one order (on a computer Food & diet sits left of Current plan), built from the same pieces (`nutrition-ui.tsx`: `CardHeader` 16px sentence-case title with a plain icon, `StatusChip` only when it's news (green done, amber needs you, red failed), `PlanDaysView` (below), `DayRows` a day type per row (the publish dialog, a second running plan), `Section` rows that open in place, `ActionRow`): **Nutrition update** (`NutritionRequestPanel`) says where the request stands in one line and, only when a written plan waits on the coach (`reviewOpen`: ready and not published, or Details on a published one), its review: Check before publishing, the written plan in `PlanDaysView` with each day against the plan they're on now ("Their plan now: … (+150 kcal)", `reviewDays`, never once it's published), the meal-prep line (`PrepLine`), then the one red button (`Update {first}'s plan`; a published plan's is outlined "Publish again") and Review & edit first, and under them Change something, AI targets, Meal plan as text, Client answers and Regenerate as sections; Send a new request, Build a plan by hand and every copy tool (`CopyDialog`) are in its ⋯. **Food & diet** (`FoodNotesCard`, `getFoodHistoryFn`) is what to keep in mind before touching their nutrition, from their whole account (the food history, below): Allergies, Diet and Health as dated items (when and where each was said, their own words; amber for allergies and health; "None on file" / "Not asked yet" when there's none, `isNoneAnswer`), each with ⋯ Doesn't apply anymore; then what they say now (`foodNotes`, food-notes.ts): Eats, Won't eat (Goals & Setup's dislikes), Goal; Day to day folded (meals a day, when they train, eating before it, steps, other activity, how they like to follow a plan, cooking, what they find hard); History folded (every plan and how it went, the food changes asked for, the foods they listed before, what no longer applies with Put back); then Your notes: the coach's nutrition notes (`client_file_notes` with `topic = 'nutrition'`, 20270304090000_nutrition_notes_topic.sql, added here with `addClientFileNote(…, "nutrition")`, `NoteRow` reused), which every nutrition AI path follows (`foodNotesForAi`: Goals & Setup's restrictions and dislikes plus the notes, "follow these unless one would break an allergy or a medical need"; a food they name is allowed past the shelf check). **Current plan** (`NutritionTargetsPanel`) is the plan in one line, an amber due strip with an outlined "Send the update now" only when the update is due, then the plan a day at a time (`PlanDaysView`: day chips each with its calories, the picked one `bg-foreground`; **Targets**, the client's own ring (`MacroTargetsChart voice="coach"`, ≤ `max-w-md`) with steps, water (tap to edit it) and fibre; then **Meal plan** as meal cards, each with its calories from its macros (`mealKcal`), foods name left / amount right, its pre / post-workout tag and macros), then Change it with AI, then Grocery list and Previous plans as rows; Edit, Start a new plan from this one, Download PDF, Build a new plan and Delete are in its ⋯. **How it's going** (`NutritionProgressCard`, nutrition-progress.ts, pure, tested) is whether the plan is working, from what they logged only, under one range (4 weeks · 12 weeks · 6 months): Bodyweight (every weigh-in as a dot, its 7-day average as the line, `withSevenDayAverage`; each plan that started in the range as a dashed line labelled with its calories a day across their week, `planMarkers` = `weeklyAverageKcal`; the trend on the current plan is the AI's own `weightTrend`, "holding steady" when it isn't clear of the day-to-day noise), Nutrition consistency (the weekly check-in's `nutrition_rating` 1–5 as bars, `nutritionConsistency`) and Water (the last 14 days against the target as a dashed line over the bars, `waterDays`, only once they log any); one axis per chart, a hover on each, theme colours as they are (never `hsl(var(--…))`), and an empty state that says what fills it. **Cardio** (`CardioTargetsPanel`) has one outlined Add menu (the default plan first), each session a row with its ⋯ (Edit, Duplicate, Delete), its status only when it isn't running and a warning only when it matches no nutrition day (the orphan rule, `findOrphanedCardio`). No red button anywhere else on the tab, no all-caps titles — why: the coach found the tab cluttered and hard to work on a phone: four red buttons, caps titles on every card, a "Nutrition Plan" and a "Nutrition Plans" card, a table that ran off the screen, a cut-off New request button and workout-meals select, a published plan still showing its whole review, and Build / Add plan and Copy doing the same things from two places; then (Nov 12 2026) that it still didn't read easily: the targets and the meal plan blurred together in one row per day, allergies, the diet and important notes had no place of their own (Fionna's AIP sat only inside a form answer, and `clients.nutrition_notes` on the Notes tab had never been used), and nothing showed whether the plan was working the way the client's own app shows them their numbers.
- A client's food history is one read of their whole account (`buildFoodHistory`, food-history.ts, pure, tested; loaded by `loadFoodContext`, nutrition-ai-plans.server.ts) for the coach's Food & diet card and every nutrition AI path (a new plan's targets and meal plan, Change something, Change it with AI, the copy prompts), so they always agree: every Nutrition Update they sent (all of them, read in one pass), Goals & Setup, the coach's nutrition notes, the coach's past AI changes (`nutrition_ai_plans.revisions`, applied `nutrition_plan_edits`), every plan, their weigh-ins and weekly check-ins. Allergies, diets and health are sticky: said once anywhere (an allergy box answer is read for its allergens, `allergensIn`, else the diet it names, `dietTags`, else kept in their words; "anything I missed" is health only when it names a condition, `safetyNotes` / `BODY`), they stay with first / last said and every mention even when a later form answers "None", until the coach marks one as no longer applying (`client_food_history_dismissals`, 20270305090000_food_history_dismissals.sql, staff only; only a mark after the last mention holds, so saying it again brings it back).
