Preferences change, so the latest answer wins and earlier ones are history, ordered by the full time they were sent (two forms minutes apart keep their order); food changes asked for (the client's "foods to change" answers, the coach's instructions) are kept with their dates; a plan replaced within `MIN_PLAN_DAYS` (3) was a re-publish and isn't history (it stays in Previous plans); each plan carries its calories a day across their week (`weeklyAverageKcal`), the bodyweight trend on it (`weightTrend`), its weekly check-ins' nutrition rating and how often hunger or digestion was flagged (`planHistory`, `planResultLine`). The AI reads it as FOOD HISTORY (`foodHistoryForAi`: allergies "keep every plan clear of these, even when the latest form doesn't repeat them", health planned conservatively, earlier preferences lose to the latest, past changes kept, plans and how they went), the meal plan's "Allergies / dislikes" line carries what's on file (`knownRestrictions`, never "none" while one is) and so does the free meal; and code checks every written plan against it: a food carrying an allergen on file or one a diet on file rules out (`onFileRules` / `liveAllergens`: each allergy, plus `DIET_RULES`: AIP rules out grains, gluten, legumes, peanuts, soy, corn, dairy, eggs, nightshades, tree nuts and seeds; paleo and Whole30 grains, legumes and dairy; vegan, vegetarian, pescatarian, no pork, halal, kosher, no red meat, dairy-free and gluten-free theirs; keto, carnivore and low FODMAP only in the AI's text; `allergenConflicts` / `foodAllergens`: shrimp is shellfish, pasta is gluten and a grain, rice and cream of rice are grains, yogurt is dairy, tomatoes and white potatoes are nightshades, never almond milk as dairy, sweet potatoes, green beans or the free meal; the coach's line names why, `allergenConflictLine`: "cooked white rice (grains · AIP)", "cooked shrimp (shellfish allergy)") sends a new plan back first (`withPrepFix`'s FIX THE ALLERGIES, `mealPrepFixPrompt`, counted 10× and a fix that keeps one more refused), sends a coach's change back once when the AI brought it in on its own (`withShelfFix`, `introducedAllergens`; one the coach or client named stays theirs), and leads the coach's Check before publishing and the Change it with AI review ("Against what's on file: …", `data-plan-ai-allergen`) — why: the coach wants what a client ever said about allergies, diet and health kept and analyzed across their whole account, not just their latest form (a shellfish allergy from July must still shape November's plan after a "None"; David's second form two minutes after his first said "No" where the first named severe shoulder arthritis), and the AI to know how each past plan went when it builds or changes the next one; Fionna's AIP diet was only words to the AI, which is how cassava, rice and eggs could reach her plan.
- A client's health is one profile (`buildHealthProfile`, health-profile.ts, pure, tested; read by `loadHealthRecords` / `healthProfileFromRecords`, health-load.ts, with whatever session it's given), shown and edited in one place, the Health card on the client's Summary (`HealthCard`, `#health`, also Training → Athlete setup → Health & injuries) and read the same way by the nutrition AI (`loadFoodContext` → `FoodContext.health`, its HEALTH PROFILE block, `healthProfileForAi(…, "nutrition")`, which replaces the FOOD HISTORY's own health line: `foodHistoryForAi(…, { health: false })`), the supplement advice and the program AI (Program Intelligence's `loadIntelRecords` → `rec.health` → `ctx.health` and `ctx.injuries.stated`, the brief's Injuries / Health conditions / Medications lines, so Generate Next Program and Edit with AI train around it; the privacy-safe export drops it). It merges what the coach or client added (`client_health_items`: injury with body area, condition, medication, supplement they take, allergy; active or resolved; `coach_only` hides one from the client), what their forms said (the food history's sticky health facts, including the Nutrition Update's "Any health conditions, injuries or medications?" answer split into what it names, `healthAnswerOf` / `splitHealthParts`, each its own fact so "doesn't apply anymore" is per thing), Goals & Setup's injuries and the old intake fields, and pain reported after workouts in the last `PAIN_WEEKS` (6) weeks by area (`pl_workout_feedback`); a form fact an item already says isn't listed twice, and `classifyHealthText` sorts form text into injury / medication / condition (a named condition beats a medication: "hypothyroid on levothyroxine" is a condition). Allergy items feed the food history (`profileItems`, `profileItemsForFood`, so the meal plan's allergen checks see them) and a condition naming a diet becomes that diet (celiac → gluten-free); allergies and diets stay the Food & diet card's and are only listed on the Health card. The coach's settings are `client_health_profiles` (drug-tested and federation, supplement budget; the budget falls back to the form's "How much do you want to spend on supplements?", then "essentials"). The client sees and adds their own under Account → Health & injuries (`MyHealthSection`, coaching clients only, read-only in View as client): RLS lets them read rows that aren't coach-only and write only `source = 'client'` rows, and a change pushes the coach (`health_updated`, once a day per client, opens `#health`) (20270306090000_health_profile.sql, which also added the Nutrition Update's three optional questions after allergies) — why: health lived in five places (Goals & Setup, a form's "anything I missed", check-out pain, coach notes, nothing for medications) and only the nutrition AI saw any of it; the coach wants one place for a client's health that both the nutrition and the training AI plan around.
- Supplements are decided in code, never by a model (`adviseSupplements`, supplements.ts, pure, tested; input from `supplementInput`, supplement-input.ts: the health profile, the food history, sex, age, bodyweight, the next meet from `pl_preps`, the last 8 weekly check-ins' sleep / digestion / energy flags, the plan's foods and calories): food first and only what's worth it. Core: creatine monohydrate for anyone lifting (5 g a day, 3–5 g under 70 kg; started after the meet when a weigh-in is within 6 weeks and they don't already take it), vitamin D3 (1,000–2,000 IU, October to April at least, never over 4,000 IU without a blood test), B12 for vegans. Considered only when the record calls for it (`need`): omega-3 when no fatty fish is in their food (algae oil without fish), magnesium for sleep flagged most weeks, constipation, cramps, an acid reducer or a restrictive diet, calcium when dairy is out (a diet, an allergy, or a meal plan without dairy: a food answer like "I eat anything" never rules dairy out, `planGiven`) or after menopause, psyllium for constipation / IBS / a GLP-1 / digestion flagged often, a basic multivitamin on AIP, vegan, keto or carnivore, low calories or a GLP-1, a probiotic during antibiotics. Check first (blood test or doctor): iron (women with heavy periods, no meat or low energy most weeks; men only with anemia on file), B12 on metformin, a PPI or at 60+. Convenience: protein powder (beef isolate on AIP / paleo, plant on vegan or a milk allergy; skipped when whey is in the plan), caffeine at 2–3 mg/kg (100–300 mg), electrolytes. Safety switches: pregnancy / breastfeeding (prenatal and DHA via their doctor only, caffeine ≤ 300 mg), under 18 (vitamin D only), kidney disease (no creatine or magnesium), a blood thinner (omega-3 is a check), calcium / kidney-stone conditions (no D or calcium), heart, anxiety, ADHD stimulants or lithium (no caffeine), and the medication timing notes (minerals 4 h from thyroid medication, 2–6 h from tetracyclines / quinolones). Drug-tested (`client_health_profiles.drug_tested`): one note under the list (strict liability, NSF Certified for Sport or Informed Sport, batch number and receipt, no pre-workouts / fat burners / testosterone boosters, "nothing guarantees a clean test"), medications matched against `TUE_MEDS` named for a Global DRO / TUE check, and a coach note when they compete and testing isn't set. What they take that isn't worth it (`NOT_WORTH`: BCAAs, fat burners, testosterone boosters, detoxes, greens powders, collagen, ashwagandha, pre-workouts) is named, and stopped while tested. The budget (`essentials` / `some` / `open`) only decides what's on the list and what's an extra the coach can add. The nutrition AI reads the advice (`supplementsForAi`, the targets prompt's GUT HEALTH + SUPPLEMENTS rules: never writes supplements itself, flags what argues against one); `runPlan` decides it again with the written meal plan and stores it on `nutrition_ai_plans.supplements` (picks, extras, skipped, notes), the review shows it (`AiSupplementsReview`: × leaves one out, + adds an extra, `setAiPlanSupplementsFn`), and publishing writes `{ items, notes }` (`planSupplementsFrom`, client lines only) to `nutrition_targets.supplements` (`publishAiPlan`; a plan with none keeps the one before's). The plan editor's Supplements section edits them and suggests from the record (`SupplementsEditor`, `suggestSupplementsFn` → `supplementAdviceFor`), the Current plan card lists them, and the client reads them under their meal plan (Supplements row → sheet, `getCoachAssignedMealPlan` returns them). The Nutrition Help FAQ covers supplements worth it, creatine, vitamin D, fish oil, magnesium, what's a waste of money, medications, drug testing, gut health, budget eating, men and women, and the menstrual cycle (nutrition-help.ts) — why: the coach wants science-based supplement advice that's affordable, personal (sex, age, health, medications, diet, budget) and safe for drug-tested athletes, written into the plan he reviews, with the doses and safety calls never left to a model.
- The nutrition plan editor (`NutritionTargetDialog`, nutrition-target-dialog.tsx: a new plan, Build a plan by hand, Review & edit first, Edit this plan) is one sheet in the Nutrition tab's language (`nutrition-ui.tsx`): a header with the phase and dates, then Phase (with where an auto-filled one came from), Starts and Next update due (2 / 4 / 6 / 8 / 12 weeks or Ongoing as chips), "Paste a meal plan" as a section (open on a blank new plan; `MealPlanBulkPaste` with its copy buttons, the paste box, a preview of the days and an outlined "Use these N days"), Days as rows that open in place (`Section` with `sub`: the name, then "kcal · P · C · F" or "Not filled in yet"; inside: name, `PlanDayUseEditor`, the numbers with the number pad (`inputMode="numeric"`, never `type="number"`), the day's cardio, the meals and `WorkoutMealTagger`), Workout meals beside the Days title and a full-width Add day under them, then This week for the client, For the client (notes, weighing rules, PDF), Private notes and Settings (status, day types, the warning days, visible to the client) as sections, and Cancel / Save pinned at the bottom: Save is its only red button, picked weekdays and workout meals are neutral (`bg-foreground text-background`, an untagged meal's "—" just `bg-muted`), no caps labels, and no Water box (water is the Current plan card's) — why: the coach found the editor a wall of caps labels, tinted boxes, a red import button beside the red Save, every day open at once and a dead Water box, on a phone.
- A coach changes a written AI nutrition plan by telling it what to change (Nutrition Plan → Change something: `reviseNutritionPlanFn` → `queueNutritionRevision`, written by `runRevision` through the same background queue as a whole plan, `nutrition_ai_plans.revise_instructions`): only what's asked changes, targets only when the change is about numbers (`REVISE_TARGETS_PROMPT`), output that doesn't read as a plan is thrown away (`revisionLooksRight`) and a failed update leaves the plan "ready" and untouched; the last 5 updates keep the plan from before them for Undo (`revisions`), a re-queue of a stuck update stays an update and only Regenerate (`fresh`) replaces it — why: the coach tweaks plans after they're written ("salmon on rest days", "4 meals") without rebuilding them or copying into ChatGPT, and a tweak must never cost the plan they already have.
- The coach's Current plan card shows the plan itself and changes it in plain words: each day's meals are under its targets (`PlanDaysView`, `DayMeals`: foods name left / amount right, each meal's calories, macros and pre / post-workout tag), and "Change it with AI" (`PlanAiChange`, under the days) takes what to change (a box with one-tap examples), writes it in the background (`nutrition_plan_edits`, 20270301090000_nutrition_plan_ai_edits.sql: queued by `askPlanEditFn` → `queuePlanEdit`, written by the same nutrition-plans-tick hook with `{ editId }` through `runPlanEdit`, the "Change something" prompts on the `nutrition_write` route, staged OpenAI calls `edit_targets` / `edit_meals` kept in `progress`) from the plan as it is (`planAsTargetsText` / `planAsMealPlanText`, plan-edit.ts: its day numbers and steps as targets, its days' meals in the paste format, the client's latest Nutrition Update answers for allergies and foods), then shows before anything reaches the client what changes day by day (`readEditedPlan` + `planEditChanges`: calories before → after, the macros that moved, which meals, the new meals on a tap; an answer with a different number of days is refused) with one red "Update {first}'s plan" and Discard. Applying (`applyPlanEditFn`) changes the plan in place like Edit this plan (each day keeps its label, order, `use_on` / `weekdays`; numbers from the answer's Daily Totals, steps from its DAILY STEPS TARGETS), only while the plan is still current and reads exactly as the AI read it (else "The plan changed since you asked"), keeps the days from before in `restore` for Undo (the toast and "Last AI change · Undo" for 7 days, refused once the plan was changed again) and tells the client like any plan update (`nutrition_targets_updated`). The table is written only by the server after a staff check through the caller's own login (`canSeePlan` / `canSeeEdit`), staff-read (admins, a coach for `coached_client_ids()`), never the client's; the paste reader is one pure file (`parseMealPlan`, meal-plan-parse.ts) the paste box, the server and the review share — why: on a client's Nutrition tab the coach could see the day totals but not the meals, and the only way to have the AI change a plan was the Nutrition update card's "Change something", hidden under Details once a plan was published and missing for a plan built by hand.
- In the coach's 1:1 chats an admin can always see what a message said before it was deleted or edited: the `messages_keep_history` trigger (`keep_message_history`, 20270106090000_keep_deleted_and_edited_messages.sql) copies the replaced version on every delete for everyone (`message_deletions`, kind `unsent`: the placeholder stays, and "See it" on it shows the words and files) and every edit (`message_edits`: "edited · see original" shows each earlier version). Both tables are admin-only (`(SELECT has_role((SELECT auth.uid()), 'admin'))`; coaches, clients and "View as client" never see them, and the thread asks only when `isAdmin`), read-only from the app, written only by that trigger, `admin_delete_messages` (Remove on a placeholder turns its `unsent` copy into the `removed` one listed under "N deleted") and the server. Member-made group and 1:1 chats are never copied. Clients are told, not surprised: the delete dialog says a copy is kept, the Coaching Agreement says so from 2.3 ("Messages and calls", no re-sign: earlier signers are covered by their privacy section, which defers to the Privacy Policy) and Privacy Policy v3 section 8 spells it out (published from Admin → Legal after review) — why: a client deleted a message a minute after sending it and the coach had no way to know what it said, and nothing overwritten before Oct 28 2026 can be recovered.
- Chats are never deleted with an account (20270107090000_chats_never_deleted.sql): the database refuses to delete a client with coach-chat messages (`clients_guard_chat_delete`), a member with support messages (`app_members_guard_chat_delete`), a coach-run group or 1:1 with messages (`chat_groups_guard_chat_delete`; member crews emptied by `chat_crew_drop_member` still go) and any login that has sent or reacted to a message (`users_guard_chat_delete` on `auth.users`, so the Supabase dashboard too). The app's Delete client / Delete member deactivate instead when there's chat history (portal / access off, login and every message kept), and Delete group archives a group with messages; a deliberate purge uses the same admin-only `SET LOCAL jf.allow_history_delete = 'on'` as logged training — why: `messages.client_id` cascaded, so deleting a client wiped their whole coach chat, and the coach wants every chat kept as a record whatever happens to the account.
- No account is ever deleted on the spot (20270226090000_account_recently_deleted.sql): deleting one, by the person (Account → Privacy & data → Delete my account, `DeleteMySheet` → `requestAccountDeletion` → `account_delete_request`; never a team login or a coach's linked athlete login, `_account_is_staff`) or by an admin (Delete client / Delete member → `account_delete_by_admin`, one step, worded by `adminDeleteCopy`), moves it to Recently deleted (`account_deletions`) for 30 days, iMessage's way. Meanwhile it's held: the client record archived with status 'Deleted' and the portal off (out of the Clients list, every coaching job and the Members list), a member's access off (`manual_access_disabled`), hidden from everyone in Crew (`community_blocked_authors` takes in `account_deletions`), and what the hold changed is kept in `prior` so Restore puts back exactly that. Signing in while it waits shows `AccountLifecycleGate` over the app: Restore for an account they deleted themselves (`withdrawAccountDeletion` → `account_delete_restore()`, which only restores `source = 'self'`), Contact support for one an admin deleted; admins restore only what an admin deleted (Recently deleted, `RecentlyDeletedRow`): someone's own deletion request is theirs alone to take back (20270324090000_account_lifecycle.sql). There is no "Delete now" anywhere: only day 30 deletes, the daily `account-deletions-purge` hook (`purgeDueAccounts`, account-deletion.server.ts): the person's own data always goes (`_account_purge_personal`: everything that cascades from the client record and the login, minus what's kept on purpose: payment, session-credit and sale records, the PT sessions they prove, the coach chat and its check-in threads, signed agreements, legal acceptances and consents, moderation reports; plus their files in the personal buckets, `PERSONAL_BUCKETS`), and the record itself is deleted unless the business must keep it (`hasBusinessRecords`: money or session records, or chat history), in which case it stays as status 'Deleted' with only the name the money is under, and the login is closed (banned) with its email released (`deleted-<id>@deleted.invalid`) so they can join again; the outcome is written on the row (`result`). The hold writes the client row as the server (claims cleared and put back, like `mark_client_signed_in`) — why: the coach wants nobody able to wipe an account outright, with time to undo a mistake, while Apple and Google still require deletion that people can start in the app (the old Account button linked to a public page that pointed back at Account), an admin's delete erased a client's history the moment it was tapped, and day 30 used to keep a chatting client's whole training, nutrition and health history (and failed outright for anyone with a payment, which the ledger's foreign keys refuse to delete), while tax and contract records must be kept and nothing else should be.
- An account is Active, Deactivated (`clients.status = 'Deactivated'`) or Pending deletion (Recently deleted), never more statuses (20270324090000_account_lifecycle.sql, lifecycle.functions.ts / lifecycle.server.ts, account-review.ts, account-lifecycle.tsx). Deactivating is the easy, reversible one: the person (Account → Deactivate account, `DeactivateMySheet`), the client's own coach or an admin (the client's ⋯ → Deactivate, `ClientLifecycleSheet`; a member's page, `MemberLifecycle`); deleting is admin or the person only. Every sheet shows first what it means for this account (`getAccountReview` → `accountReview`, pure, tested on Marc's and a monthly subscriber's records: only lines that apply, every figure the Billing model's own, `buildClientPayments` through `readClientBilling` with the caller's login), and the server works the review out again from the database and acts on that, never on the phone's. Order of every action: who may (`resolveLifecycle`: the person themselves, never a team login; an admin; the client's own coach) → Stripe (`stopSubscriptions`: `cancel_at_period_end=true` only, idempotency key per subscription; if Stripe refuses, nothing in the app changes) → the database (`_account_deactivation_apply` / `_account_reactivation_apply`, idempotent: a second tap is `already`) → tell the coach once (`tellCoach`: an automated staff-only note in the client's coach chat with the verified figures, `lifecycleNote`, and one push to the coach and admins, never whoever did it: `account_deactivated`, `account_deactivation_scheduled`, `account_reactivated`, `account_deletion_requested`, `account_deletion_withdrawn`; no text messages). Deactivating stops an open-ended coaching subscription at the end of its period (nothing more charged, nothing refunded by itself; it can wait until the paid month runs out, `paidThrough`, ≤ 45 days, `deactivate_at`, applied by pg_cron `account-deactivations-due`, SQL only, every 15 minutes while one is due); a session package's payment plan (auto-pay) keeps paying for what they bought unless the coach stops it (`stopAutoPay`; what's owed stays owed), deleting stops every recurring charge. The database part archives the record (so every job, check-in, reminder and roster leaves them out), turns the app off (`AccountLifecycleGate`: Reactivate for one they deactivated themselves; "ask your coach" for one their coach paused; Purchases & payments stays open, `OPEN_WHILE_DEACTIVATED`), a member's access off, hides them from the Crew (`community_blocked_authors`), cancels booked sessions from that moment on with each credit banked by the `pt_sessions` triggers (and off Google), and keeps what it changed in `deactivation_prior`, which reactivating puts back exactly; reactivating restarts no subscription, charges or invoices nothing, moves no refund, rebooks nothing and brings back no package or expired credit. A person can undo their own deactivation, never one their coach made. Every step goes to `account_lifecycle_events` (server-written, staff-read, no foreign keys so it outlives the account; never `client_activity_log`, which clients can write), shown as Account history on the client's banner and sheets (`AccountHistory`, with created and first sign-in from the record). The Clients list shows deactivated clients only under Deactivated, never also under Archived; the Members list's Deactivated reads the member's own record. The member guard `guard_app_members_self_update` lets trusted server processes (no `auth.uid()`) through like the other app_members guards — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach asked for one complete, synchronized lifecycle where no account silently disappears, no subscription silently keeps charging, no debt silently vanishes, no prepaid session is lost and no restored account is charged; before, only an admin could deactivate (archived stayed off, so jobs kept running), "disable portal access" was never enforced, a deactivated member kept their access, nothing told the coach, and the member guard refused every server-side access change, so deleting any JF member's account would have failed.
- Anywhere another athlete sees you (Crew, Rankings / League, Hall of Strength, powerlifting careers, athlete level cards, group chats) your photo is `public.community_avatar(user)`: the one you put on your Crew profile (`community_profiles.avatar_path`), else a coach's team photo for a coach or their linked athlete login, else initials; `community_author` uses it too, and the account photo (`profiles.avatar_url`, `clients.profile_picture_url`) is never a source (20270108090000_community_photos_only.sql). Draw a stored photo with `UserAvatar` or, inside a ui/avatar `<Avatar>`, `AvatarPhoto` (user-avatar.tsx), never `<AvatarImage src={path}>` — why: the account photo is the verification photo taken at setup, for the coach, yet Reece's and Nicole's sat on the League podium while Jennifer, who uploaded one for Crew, showed as initials; and photos are private bucket paths, so a raw `<img>` of one never loads.
- A profile photo opens big the way Instagram's does, through the app's one photo viewer (Nov 19 2026, `PhotoViewer`, photo-viewer.tsx; gestures in photo-zoom.ts, pure, tested): `UserAvatar` (expandable by default) opens it with `useMediaViewer().open(url, { shape: "circle", from, caption, fallback, better })`, and chat photos open in the same viewer (MediaViewer's images; its videos keep `VideoViewer`); there is no second lightbox (chat-image-lightbox.tsx and the avatar's own Dialog are gone). The photo grows out of the avatar tapped and goes back into it (`flipFrom`; a plain fade under reduced motion or when the avatar is off screen), as a large circle with the name under it over a dark, blurred backdrop; pinch follows the fingers (lift one and it keeps its size, `pinchView` / `panView` shared with the feed's pinch), a double-tap zooms 2.5× on that spot and again goes back (`doubleTapView`), one finger moves a zoomed photo with its edges giving a little and settling with no gap (`rubberView` / `clampView`), a swipe up or down on an unzoomed photo, a tap off it, the ✕ or Escape put it away (`dismissIntent` / `dismissCloses`), focus goes back to the avatar, and Ctrl-wheel / a trackpad pinch zooms on a computer. It's a Radix Dialog layer (`touch-none`, its own pointer listeners), so opened from a sheet it closes without closing the sheet. A missing or failed photo opens nothing (`canExpand`: initials stay a plain circle); a photo that fails inside the viewer shows the initials and Try again. `expandable={false}` only where a tap means something else (opens their profile, a story, a list row, a chat header's profile); on someone's Crew profile, your Account header and the client's coach-chat header the photo opens big, and changing your own photo has its own button (the camera badge, or a tap on the circle while there's none; Remove unchanged), never the photo itself. A Crew photo is saved twice (`uploadCommunityAvatar`: `community-<t>.jpg` at 512 px for every face, `community-<t>-full.jpg` up to 1080 px beside it, `fullAvatarPath`), the viewer swapping the full-size copy in once it has decoded (`better`; the 512 one stays when there's none, as for every photo saved before), both removed together (`communityAvatarFiles`), and the copy is readable exactly when its photo is (`avatar_is_shared`, 20270419100000_full_size_crew_photos.sql); account photos are already saved full size — why: on Nov 19 2026 the coach found tapping a profile picture didn't let anyone enlarge it (someone's Crew profile photo did nothing, the Account photo opened its editor, and elsewhere a plain dialog with no zoom or swipe), and a 512 px Crew photo would be soft blown up on a phone.
- A ring around a person's photo only ever means a story (Nov 22 2026, story-ring.ts pure and tested in story-rings.test.ts, story-ring.tsx, story-seen.ts, global-story-host.tsx): no story up, no ring (a plain photo, never a decorative border: `UserAvatar` has no `ring` prop, and someone live is a still dot on the photo's corner, `LiveAvatar`, never a turning ring); a story with a moment you haven't watched is the JF Effect ring (amber into the brand red, `STORY_RING_FROM` / `STORY_RING_TO`), Close Friends green, all watched a quiet grey, and it goes by itself when the story runs out or is deleted. It's read only from the stories actually up (`storyRings` / `groupRing` over `crewStories` / `myStory`: `storyWorthy` and the 24h window, the server's `seen`, plus what this viewer watched this visit; your own watched moments are kept on the phone, `jf-own-story-seen-v1`, since views never count the author), from the crew's last day already loaded: the Crew screen's own host (`StoryHostProvider`) and the app's (`GlobalStoryHost`, the story rail's cached query, never a request of its own, nothing while viewing as a client). One component draws it everywhere a person's photo shows (`StoryAvatar` / `StoryRing`: the story rail, posts, a post's sheet and comments, profiles yours included, Likes, followers / following, Find athletes, Activity, the crew list, suggestions, full-screen clips, chat rows via `ChatListRow storyUserId`): an SVG outside the photo (`ringGeometry`, `max-w-none` since the base styles cap an svg at its parent's width), so the photo and the row never change size, and the only motion is the colour easing to grey (`motion-reduce` off). A tap on a ringed photo plays that person's story right there (`PersonStory`), else does what the photo always did (their profile, the chat, the photo big); a row that was one big button is the photo's button and the name's, never one inside the other; your own profile's camera badge stays its own button — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found every profile photo wore a permanent orange / red ring whether or not a story was up (a hardcoded gradient), and a thin grey border on other photos and the live ring around active people read like watched stories.
- A client's Training tab (admin, `ClientTrainingTab`, client-training-tab.tsx) holds only what builds, assigns and adjusts their training: the program card (what's running, the next workout, done so far, `trainingAlerts` in training-tab.ts: workouts on a live block with no date, a block ending within `ENDS_SOON_DAYS` with nothing planned after, a gap, an overlap, each with its fix; Edit program; one Add: library program, a new block opened straight in the builder, or a built block onto the calendar (Add next block, `AddNextBlockDialog`: it opens on the best start, `bestNextStart` in block-schedule-model.ts = Generate Next Program's `plannedStart`: the Monday after the most recent program ends, else next Monday, said with its reason and a one-tap "Use it"; starting a built block makes it the client's, visible with its workouts on their training days and its cardio synced (`setBlockScheduleFn` `start: true` → `realignClientToCommittedDays`, also for End + start next), and any block of a Generate Next Program draft starts the whole draft back to back through `assignIntelDraftFn`, never one hidden block on its own); the roadmap of blocks, finished ones folded away; tools in its ⋯; Delete this program in the card's ⋯ and Delete program in every roadmap row's ⋯, drafts included (`DeleteProgramDialog` → `deleteProgram`, pl-programs.ts: the sheet says before the tap what goes (`deleteProgramCopy`, program-delete.ts: its weeks and workouts, whether it comes off their calendar) or that a program with logged workouts is archived instead, never deleted; a Generate Next Program draft with none of its programs left is closed like its own Discard)), the Schedule (`ScheduleManagerShell` coach: Week · Month · Program, + Add workout, ⋯ Move several · Change history, no weekly editor; the next rule), Athlete setup (training days, maxes, warm-up as rows that open a sheet) and workout notes. `/admin/clients/$id/schedule` redirects there (`#schedule`); phases and important dates are not on it, and no workout that's done or in a finished block is ever offered as needing a date — why: starting a block suggested "the day after the current block ends" even with nothing running (today) and left a generated draft dated but hidden with no workouts placed (Devin's Block 1, Nov 9 2026: "Active" to the coach, nothing on his calendar); a built draft like Devin's AI-written "Powerbuilding Base" had no way to be deleted from the tab (only from the old All programs page); the coach found the tab cluttered (≈9,000px on an iPhone: the calendar twice, an info-only coach panel, a weekly editor, per-block analytics, phases nobody had used since June, important dates never used) and wanted one dummy-proof page for programming.
- The coach's Schedule on a client's Training tab (`ScheduleManagerShell` coach, Nov 15 2026) opens on this week (Week · Month · Program, the choice remembered on the device, `jf.coach-schedule.view`), and every workout in it reads one way, `coach-schedule.ts` (pure, tested): its status is the athlete's own (`coachStatus`: done = finished, in progress = started, today, missed = a past day with nothing finished, a past optional session is "Skipped (optional)", never missed, upcoming, no date), its mark the athlete's (`coachMark` → `WorkoutStatusMark`: green check, hollow rose ring, a ring, a grey dot), its word only when it's news (`STATUS_WORD` / `STATUS_TONE`: green done, rose missed, amber no date, nothing for one ahead) and its name what the athlete reads (`workoutName`: the subtitle, else the title without a "Day 2 — " the date already says; `getClientSchedule` reads `subtitle` and `is_optional`). Week (`ScheduleCalendar view="week"`) is Monday to Sunday with "This week" / the dates, where it sits in the program (`weekPlace`: "Block 2 — General Strength · Week 4", both blocks across a change) and "2 of 4 done · 1 missed", a row a day (today a filled circle, a day with nothing "Rest"), each workout a full-width row that opens its sheet. Month on a phone is the date and a mark per workout in each cell (names never fit a seventh of a phone), a tap on a day lists its workouts under the grid, and the month's tally (done · missed · in progress · ahead, each beside its mark) is the key; on a wider screen a cell has a line per workout; workouts with no date sit above it ("N workouts have no date", Pick a date). Program (`ScheduleManagerList`) is filter chips with counts (All · To do · Missed · No date · Done, an empty one hidden) over the blocks: the block being trained open ("Now": the one with a workout this week, else the first with anything to do), the rest one line each (dates, "16 of 16 done · 1 missed"), and in a block every week with its dates and done count, a week behind them with everything done folded to its line, one line a workout (mark, "Tue 3", name, the word when it's news). No red anywhere but a miss: no red edges, today is `bg-foreground`, the switch and the chips are neutral. Drag and reorder are unchanged in what they call (`onMoveDay` → `useMoveWorkout`, `onReorder`, completed workouts never drag; a drop on itself or an unchanged order sends nothing); a mouse drags at once and a finger presses and holds (`MouseSensor` + `TouchSensor` delay 250: a `PointerSensor` would claim the touch, and `touch-none` would stop a swipe over a row from scrolling the page). The workout's sheet (`MoveWorkoutSheet coachControls`) has Edit in program (the block editor on that week, `focusBlockEditorWeek`) beside View what they logged. The athlete's own Move workouts page (`mode="client"`) keeps Calendar / Schedule Manager and gets the same month — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found the month cramped and its labels unreadable on a phone ("D…" in every cell), every upcoming workout edged in red like an overdue one, "Overdue" / "Past Due" / "Completed" in the brand red beside the athlete's "Missed" and green check, two levels of tabs, no view of this week, and a Whole program list of ~120px rows from the program's first week with a button on each.
- Equipment & setups (Nov 19 2026, 20270420090000_equipment_setups.sql, src/lib/equipment/: equipment.ts pure and tested, equipment-queries.ts; src/components/equipment/): an athlete's gyms (`athlete_gyms`), the racks, benches, machines and cables in them (`athlete_equipment`: a name, a kind, optional brand / model / note / photo, favorite, `last_used_at`), and how each exercise is set up on each one (`athlete_exercise_setups`: one per athlete, exercise and equipment, null = on nothing in particular; `settings` [{label, value}] cleaned by `athlete_setup_clean_settings` = `cleanSettings`; a note and a photo; `source` athlete or coach). Reads are the athlete's, their coach's (`coached_client_ids()`), an admin's and a linked login's; writes only through the `equipment_*` RPCs (`workout_can_act_for_client`), photos in the private `equipment-photos` bucket under `<client_id>/`. The newest `last_used_at` across an exercise's setups is its current pick (`athlete_current_equipment` = `currentSetup`, never retired equipment), and every logged set is stamped with it (`pl_row_results.equipment_id`, the `pl_row_results_stamp_equipment` trigger: only the first time a set is logged and only today's (18 h), never an edit of an old set, a writer that names equipment wins). The workout card shows it as one line under the exercise's role (`SetupLine`: "Rogue rack · J-hooks 9 · Safeties 4", 14px, whole on up to two lines, broken only between its parts; empty, "Add your setup", only on a live card of a rack, Smith, machine or cable lift before its first set is logged, `offersSetup`, never a dumbbell or kettlebell lift whatever its name (`suggestKind`), so a day's accessories don't each ask: on Nov 21 2026 2 athletes had a setup, all 4 entered by the coach; `cardEquipment`: what the card's sets were logged on, else the current pick, never today's pick on an old workout), opening `SetupSheet` (a bottom sheet with a grip: the equipment with Change and one-tap chips of what the exercise was done on and favorites, the settings the kind usually needs (`setupFields`), a note and a photo folded under "Note or photo" until used, edited as a draft that Done saves (the next rule); settings already typed into a note, "Rack height 33", offered as a start, `settingsFromNote`); the card's ⋯ has Save your setup / Setup & equipment, and saving a note that reads like a setup offers to save it. A pick in a workout being logged (`equipment_choose`) makes it current, moves the exercise's sets logged today with it (a pick is a correction), and when it's in a gym moves the workout's other exercises whose current pick is in another gym to their setup in this one; on a finished workout it fixes that workout's record only (`_this_workout_only`). Weights on two machines are never converted or treated as the same: a machine, cable, Smith or other is load-specific, a rack or bench isn't (`comparesAcrossEquipment`); "Last time" and the weight suggestions read history on the card's machine when there is some (sets that never said count, another machine's never; `preferSameEquipment` in `selectPreviousLifts` / `matchHistoryLogs`), and say "on <machine>" when the only history is another one's; History names each session's machine when it matters, filters by machine when there are two or more load-specific ones (the list, progress line and chart), never compares a progress line across machines (`HistorySession.equipmentKey`), and the records and a set's PR chip say which machine a record was on. Equipment on any logged set (or a set in an exercise's trash) is retired, never deleted (`equipment_remove`: archived, out of every picker and never current; Restore brings it and its gym back; a replacement can take its name); never-used equipment is deleted with its setups; removing a gym does the same to everything in it. Names are one per gym (`findDuplicate`, a unique index; adding one they have returns it). The athlete manages it all in Account → Equipment & setups (`EquipmentManager`), the coach in Training → Athlete setup → Equipment & setups (the same manager; a setup they add is marked theirs and goes on the card). A one-time walkthrough (`EquipmentSetupTips`, `EquipmentIntroGate`) shows on the Workouts page, never in a workout, never over another popup or in View as client, to accounts made before it shipped, until they save a setup, seen server-side (`feature_announcement_views` `equipment_setups_2026_11`), and opens any time from Training help's Guides and Account's How it works — why: on Nov 19 2026 the coach asked for rack heights, J-hooks, safeties, bench, seat, cable and machine settings saved per exercise and per gym, switched in one tap mid-workout, kept on every logged set and PR so machines aren't confused, reviewable and assignable by the coach, without cluttering the card; athletes were already typing "Rack height 33", "Rack 9" and treadmill settings into exercise notes.
- The Setup sheet (`SetupSheet`, setup-sheet.tsx; its rules in setup-draft.ts, pure and tested, setup-sheet-reliability.test.ts) is a draft until Done (Nov 20 2026): nothing saves while typing; Done saves and closes, staying open with "Saving…" until the server has it and "Saved" only after (one save at a time: a second tap or Return waits on the first, `saving`), and a failed save stays open with the draft, "Not saved" and how to try again. Any other way out with changes (a swipe down, a tap above the sheet, Escape, Back, the card's sheet closing) asks Save changes / Discard changes / Keep editing (`requestClose`, the sheet put back where it was), and adding or editing equipment inside it asks the same for that form (`EquipmentForm onDirtyChange` / `saveRef`); with nothing changed it just closes. What counts as a change is the saved form (`draftChanged` over `savedForm`: an offered setting left empty, spaces or a value typed back are none). A draft an interruption cut short (iOS closing the app, a reload) is kept on the phone per login, exercise and equipment (`keepDraft`, `jf-setup-draft:v1:`, 24 h, written on `pagehide` / hidden) and comes back on the next open with "Your unsaved changes from before are back" · Discard, only while the setup it was typed over is still the saved one (`base` = its `updated_at`, so a coach's newer save is never overwritten). While it's open the sheet keeps its own place (a refetch never resets the view or the equipment, `wasOpen`; the load effect never replaces a draft being typed or saved). Switching equipment with changes saves them where they were typed first (`switchPlan`: one machine's settings never land on another), except settings typed on "nothing in particular" carried onto a pick that has no setup yet. The iOS keyboard: the sheet is pinned to the visible part of the screen while it's up (`useVisualViewportBox`: top and height from the visual viewport, never `bottom: var(--keyboard-inset)`, which iOS's own pan of the page doubled, so the sheet jumped up and the overlay showed between it and the keyboard, where a tap closed it), its swipe-to-close is off while typing (`swipeToClose={!kb}`: with the keyboard up a list is always at its top, so any drag down dismissed it) and a pull down takes the keyboard down instead, and the field being typed in is scrolled into view just enough (`revealDelta`). Settings that are numbers get the number pad (`settingInputMode`: J-hooks, safeties, rack height, seat, pad, pins, holes, incline, speed…; words keep the keyboard), Return moves to the next box, and no box is labelled or named so Safari offers Contact AutoFill (`noAutofill`, no-autofill.ts: a `search-` name, autocomplete off; never "name" in a label or placeholder, "What you call it" in the equipment form). Rows keep their own ids (`key={f.id}`), a coach-written setup says "from your coach", and the setting boxes are the logger's neutral well. `PhotoSlot` never deletes a file itself: the sheet and the equipment form remove a replaced photo only once the save that replaced it lands, and drop uploads nobody saved. The card's `SwipeToReveal` ignores touches and clicks that reach it through a portal (`root.contains`), since React bubbles a sheet's events through the card it was opened from — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach found the Setup sheet glitchy on iPhone: the keyboard threw the sheet around, inputs were hard to hit, it closed by itself with changes unsaved, and "Saved" showed before anything was saved; the autosave raced typing (text typed during a save was lost and the server's copy loaded over the box), a drag on the sheet slid the workout card behind it and swallowed the next tap, Safari offered contacts for the "name" boxes, and a photo replaced in a discarded edit had already been deleted.
- Cleo is an AI assistant with no gender (Nov 18 2026): "Cleo", "the AI assistant", or "it" when a pronoun is needed, never she / her / he / him, in UI text, notifications, AI instructions, docs, comments and test names. The system prompt says it once (`IDENTITY:` in `summerSystemPrompt`: no gender, no human backstory, "I" in its own replies and "Cleo" / "it" when something it writes mentions it, no AI disclaimers), every tone keeps its personality without a gendered persona (the Gen Z hype tone's stored key is still `girly_pop`, so saved choices read), and the text-to-speech style never names a gender (each provider voice id sets the sound). cleo-gender-neutral.test.ts checks Cleo's files, the tones and the voice style; "he" / "him" in Cleo's files is the coach — why: the coach wants Cleo to read as a capable AI assistant, not a person; a doc said "Cleo is she/her", the Girly pop tone cast Cleo as the owner's "bookkeeper bestie" with "girl math" jokes, the voice style asked for a "female voice", a screen said "Yours override hers", and comments throughout called Cleo she.
- AI is the engine, never the product's identity (Nov 22 2026, ai-branding.test.ts, cleo-access.test.ts, 20270505090000_cleo_access.sql): no feature anyone uses is named, badged or decorated as AI. Features are named for what they do: Adjust program (was Edit with AI; "Adjust" where a phone's toolbar is short), Adjust plan (was Change it with AI), Fix the meals, Fill in, Generate a program (the Training tab's way into Generate Next Program), Draft from a description (forms), Interpret this report and Interpretation (Program Intelligence), Copy coaching prompt, draft targets / draft meal plan / draft plan (a Nutrition Update's plan before the coach publishes it), Reply drafts (forms' review queue), Auto draft / Reviewed (`AiAssistanceLabel`), roadmap words marked Auto beside the coach's own, Credits (was AI credits, for members and clients) and meal estimates, and an import's Full read (was Read with AI); copy never makes "the AI" its subject ("Their next plan follows it", "Decide for me"). No Sparkles, Sparkle, WandSparkles, Wand, Wand2, Bot, Brain or BrainCircuit icon anywhere in src (a purpose icon at the same size in neutral, or none; a celebration uses the app's own vocabulary, never a sparkle), and no violet tint, gradient orb or glow on anything AI-made. Internal names stay (edit-with-ai.tsx, PlanAiChange, `ai_*` columns, `ai_editor`, `kind: 'ai_credits'`, route keys), and so do Stripe's existing credit products (new ones are named "JF Effect credits · N"). "AI" stays only where it's needed: the admin's Settings → AI & Cleo (`/admin/settings/ai`, admin only on screen and server, listing every place AI runs by the name people see it under and who sees it: `TASK_LABEL`), the admin's model switches (forms' AI settings tab, Athlete Insights' model switch, both admin-only), the books' "AI credits" revenue line, legal and privacy text (the Coaching Agreement, the AI-Assisted Coaching Disclosure, where an upload goes), and Cleo. Uncertainty stays said (estimates are estimates, drafts are drafts) and nothing written by a model is presented as the coach's own words unless the coach reviewed it. Cleo is the one AI identity: "Cleo · AI assistant" with its own mark (`CleoAvatar`, a "C" in a neutral circle, cleo-avatar.tsx; `CLEO_ROLE_LINE`) wherever someone talks to it. Who has Cleo is one rule, `cleo_allowed()`: the admin always; the finance login and coaches only while the admin switches them on (`app_settings` 'cleo_access' through the admin-only `cleo_access_get` / `cleo_access_set`, Settings → AI & Cleo's Who has Cleo; defaults finance on, coaches off); clients and members never, since no client-facing Cleo exists (one would need its own data limits, safety rules and credits); the one exception is Create with Cleo's meal-plan draft for a JF member, which has all three and is never a chat (the meal plan import rule below). `assertCleo` (cleo-access.server.ts) asks it first in every Cleo server function (chat, voice, speech, history, settings, action cards), the policies on a coach's own Cleo chats, settings and attachments use it, and every way in (Messages' pinned row, the chat sheet's mount in the admin layout, Ask Cleo on Finance home and the money workspace, the books' Cleo card, the ⌘⇧S line in Keyboard shortcuts) waits on `useCleoAccess`. A coach's Cleo reads with their own login (RLS: their own clients), never money (no BOOKS or OPEN SALES, `persona.coach`), links only to a coach's pages (`summerLinkCatalog("coach")`), and every change it proposes is a card for the owner's approval — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for JF Effect to feel like a professionally engineered training platform where the intelligence is seamless: red sparkles on Performance Insights and dozens of buttons, "Edit with AI", "AI targets", "AI credits", "Read with AI" and a gradient-orb Cleo told people which features a model runs instead of what they do, and Cleo's access was a hard-coded admin-or-finance check with no way to give it to coaches or hold it back.
- Cleo's "Like me" tone (the default, `like_me` in summer-persona.ts) borrows the owner's style from My Voice, never his identity (Nov 18 2026): Cleo stays Cleo and never talks as if it were him, and in Cleo's prompt My Voice is headed HIS VOICE (`buildVoicePrompt`'s `heading`, summer.server.ts), "how anything written as him must sound", with its "You are Jared" meaning when writing as him; every other caller keeps "HOW IT MUST SOUND". The tone is confident, direct, short and punchy (answer first, the why in a line or two, no headings on a short answer), supportive but honest (praise only what was earned, named; a tied best is a tied best) and reads the room: calm is the default and hype the exception (a PR or real progress, more reps, sets or an easier RPE at a weight counting even when it isn't a record: earned energy, one hype word or emoji at most; setbacks: steady, then what to do; missed workouts: direct, the why and the fix, never shaming; training and nutrition: accuracy first; business: efficient and exact; everyday chat: relaxed, a little humour), emojis rarely and only from his list, extras nobody asked for (a draft client message, a full plan) offered in one line, no generic motivation or compliments for nothing. Change it with the `cleo-bench` hook's `tone` on real situations before and after; the other tones (Gen Z hype, Chill, Professional) are separate and unchanged by it — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach asked for Like me to sound like his communication style, energy matched to the situation, without pretending to be him; the baseline answers were accurate but read like reports (headings on short answers) with the same flat energy everywhere, and his My Voice rules opened "You are Jared" under a heading that said it overrides every tone.
- Every piece of text Cleo sends a model is well-formed (`wellFormed`, well-formed.ts): each lookup's answer (both `safe` wrappers, cleo-tools / cleo-program), the card tool's replies, the system prompt and the chat history drop any half of a character a `.slice()` left — why: on Nov 18 2026 one of Ashley's messages was exactly 600 characters ending in 🫡, the messages lookup's `.slice(0, 600)` kept half the emoji, and OpenAI refused the whole request ("failed to parse JSON value"), so every model failed and Cleo couldn't answer anything about her.
- Cleo matches money by package, never by amount, and calls a PR only once the log agrees (Nov 18 2026): every payment row in Cleo's books section names the package it paid (`RevenueRow.saleId` from the payment's sale, labelled by `Receivables.packages`, the Billing model's own "Package N" for every package, paid off or not), the owed lines name theirs, and the section says to match by package; the PR lookup (`personal_records`) says records count finished workouts only (`client_rep_records` compares only `completed` workouts), so Cleo checks `training_log` for an equal or heavier set before calling an all-time PR — why: asked what needed dealing with before bed, Cleo flagged Marc's $672 e-transfer (it paid Package 3) as maybe not applied to the $672 he owes on Package 4, since the payment row only said the product's name; and Jarrett's 180 kg × 3 on Nov 14 sat in a workout he never tapped Finish on, so the records read Nov 17's 4 × 3 at 180 kg as an all-time PR over 175 kg.
- Cleo's brain is the routing table's `assistant` route (Nov 18 2026; cleo-model.server.ts): GPT-5.5 at low effort, then GPT-5 mini, on the OpenAI key through the Responses API, never Lovable credits by default (an admin's `ai_routes` override can still name a `gateway:` model). Cleo's reasoning carries between look-ups (`store: false` + encrypted reasoning), the prompt is cached per person (`promptCacheKey`), its tools are `strict: false` (a strict tool makes every field required and the model fills optional ones with placeholders), its context is read in parallel with the stable parts first and the time riding with the question, and every answer logs its model, timings, steps and tokens. Its schedule reads both ways a workout is dated (cleo-schedule.ts, the app's rule from scheduled-instances-merge.ts: a workout's schedule entries when it has any, else its own `pl_days.scheduled_date`), and its move card takes a day id for a workout dated on the program (`moveWorkout`, with Undo). Change Cleo's models or effort only with the `cleo-bench` hook's numbers on real questions (docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md) — why: on Lovable credits through chat completions Cleo re-thought every look-up, and said "nothing scheduled" for Nico, who trained twice a week, because most workouts are dated on the workout itself and Cleo read only the schedule table; on the live benchmark (two rounds on real data) GPT-5.5 low answered accurately in 11 s on average on six everyday questions and its "who missed workouts" matched the database exactly, medium was 25-130% slower on most questions for a little more depth, and GPT-5 mini was no faster and less careful (it drafted a client message nobody asked for).
- Cleo reads everything the person asking can see in the app, through their own login (RLS decides, never the service role): the client lookups, `business_documents` (the Coaching Agreement from `coaching-agreement/content.ts`, every current legal document from Admin → Legal, the coach FAQs; split into sections and searched, `cleo-documents.ts`) and `find_data` / `read_data` over every table `cleo_data_catalog()` lists (admins and the finance login only, 20270109090000_cleo_data_catalog.sql). `cleo-data-access.ts` keeps out credentials (`CLEO_HIDDEN_TABLES`, `isCredentialColumn`: tokens, signing links, signature images, hashes), members' own chats (their state tables hidden, `MEMBER_CHAT_SCOPED` reads coach-run groups only) and, for anyone but the owner and finance, the books and ledgers (`CLEO_BOOKS_TABLES`); a new table holding a credential goes in `CLEO_HIDDEN_TABLES` — why: asked what the coaching policy says about ending coaching, Cleo said it didn't have the text, because its lookups only covered one client at a time.
- The coach inbox has a filter per recurring form, Weekly check-ins and Nutrition updates (`FILTERS` "Weekly" / "Nutrition" in messages.tsx, `FORM_FILTER_KIND`), beside Forms to review / Overdue forms for every form: a chat is in one while that form is filled in and not reviewed or sent and overdue (`formKindWaiting`), each item's kind is the Forms tracker's own split (`classifyKind` → `ReviewItem.formKind` / `RequestItem.formKind`), the row's line leads with that form ("Weekly check-in to review" / "… overdue", `formKindTags`), and the strip under the chips counts to review · overdue and opens Forms → Requests on that form at its latest week (`?tab=requests&kind=`, `RequestsTab initialKind`) — why: the coach works the weekly check-ins and the monthly Nutrition Updates as their own batches, and one mixed "Forms to review" list made him open each chat to see which form it was.
- Admin → Forms opens on Submissions (`SubmissionsInbox`, `listFormSubmissionsFn`, form-submissions.ts): everything clients send in, in one list newest first and grouped by the coach's day (`groupByDay`, Winnipeg): weekly check-ins, Nutrition Updates, other native forms, progress photos / videos, old Fillout entries and website applications (tests left out), a chip per kind with its total and, in blue, what's waiting (`toReviewKeys`, review-queue.ts's rule, the next rule; progress has `reviewed: null`, its review lives on the client's Progress tab, so it never counts; `?kind=progress` opens it on progress, where Coaching Hub's old Progress tab lands), a row opens where its answers live and ✓ marks it reviewed (`mark_checkin_reviewed`, the coach's RLS update on `nf_submissions` / `fillout_submissions`). Five tabs (Submissions, Requests, Forms, Applications, Agreements), the rest under More (`TABS` `more`, the old Reviews queue is "AI drafts"), old `?tab=` links mapped (`LEGACY_TAB_ALIAS`, `LEGACY_SUB`). Forms not used in `FORM_UNUSED_DAYS` (60: no submission, send in chat, assignment or edit) archive themselves daily (`archive_unused_forms`, pg_cron `forms-archive-unused`, `archived_reason = 'unused'`, 20270110091000_forms_archive_unused.sql), never the Nutrition Update Request or a form with a send still due; the Forms list shows each form's last use (`nf_form_last_used`) and folds archived ones away — why: weekly check-ins (the most common submission) weren't in Submissions at all, finding a recent submission meant knowing which of ten tabs held it, and six call-script / booking links and an old Fillout nutrition form nobody had used since June cluttered the list.
- The admin Clients list filters: picking several chips shows clients with ANY of them by default (`?match=all` narrows to every one: `MatchToggle`, `admin_clients_directory(p_match)`, 20270110090000_clients_filters_any_or_all.sql, whose default stays 'all' only for older builds); each chip's count is what it finds alone. The quick row (`ClientFilterRail`, `quickFilterKeys`) shows only filters with someone in them (plus any picked), needs-attention first (`FILTER_GROUPS`), every filter stays in the Filters sheet, and the line under it (`ActiveFilterSummary`) is the count, Any / All and Clear. Active / Archived / Deactivated is a select in the toolbar. A row is the photo and name, one plain line of facts (`rowMetaLine`: coaching type, the coach only when there's more than one, last seen; never chips) and at most one status chip, the most important thing waiting on the coach as ranked by `rowStatuses` (row-status.ts: a failing payment, no program / program ended / ending, missed workouts, a check-in to review, payment not set up / awaiting, the agreement, inactive, account setup; red only for a failing payment or an ended program, amber for the rest), with "+N" opening the others and "No program +" assigning one in a tap. A row's way in is Open client (the profile), Client view (eye + label: their app as they see it, `useEnterClientPov`, for a login allowed client view), Workout (only with a program: client view straight onto today's workout, `clientWorkoutHref` = the Workouts page's `?open=<date>`, to log a session or move a workout; an icon on phones) and ⋯ (`ClientMoreMenu`, Nov 23 2026: only what the row's buttons don't do, grouped Training · Coaching · Billing · Account: Program & schedule or Assign Program, Assign Next Block, Schedule a workout, Training PDF; nutrition and cardio in place, Message, Book a session; Sell or send a payment link, Billing & packages, the no-payment switch; Account, access & agreement, Change plan (admins), Archive; program history, the workout archive, agreements and the nutrition PDF live on the profile's tabs; no ⚡ quick actions, no › arrow), and a block ending or ended with nothing queued is said where its dates are (`blockEnd`, calendar days: tomorrow is "1d", never "0d") with "Next block +", plus "Program Ended" in the status column, never "Active" — why: ticking No Payment (4) and No Program (4) showed 1 client because filters only narrowed, a block that ended yesterday read "Active" with "0d left", and the row repeated the end date in a pill under the bar.
- Workouts and Training analytics are one tap apart and say each number for what it is (Nov 22 2026, competition-strength.ts, recovery-trends.ts, analytics-profile.ts pure and tested in workouts-analytics-clarity.test.ts). Workouts: the header's chart button (`data-header-analytics`, from 370pt) and the progress card's first row ("Training analytics", `part="entry"`) open analytics; then readiness as a status word and what it read, never a precise score or a percent ring (an estimate from a check-out: `data-readiness-status`, "a good read is never a reason to go heavier", `pickRecommendation`'s clean read is "Train as planned"), then the 8-week trend; nothing folds ("More about November" is gone). The program card (`ProgramRoadmapCard`) always shows today's program with its phases in view (`data-roadmap-timeline`: done, now with this week's words, next; a week's label never repeats its number, `weekParts`), and every week of the plan, other programs (read-only) and Block View are in one sheet (`ProgramPlanSheet`, "Full plan"), never a "Plan details" fold. Analytics (`ClientAnalyticsDashboard`, the athlete's and the coach's): one row of controls (`AnalyticsPeriodControl`: This block · Last block · 4 weeks · 12 weeks · 6 months · 1 year · All time · Custom · A block…, kept in the address, `searchFromFilter` / `filterFromSearch`; lb | kg beside it), Overview · Strength · Volume · Recovery, and no section repeats the period. Every logged set is read once (`["pl-results", clientId, null]`) and a period only filters it: an all-time PR is judged against the whole history (`recentPRs(allResults…)` then cut to the period; inside a block it used to read the block's second Rear Delt Cable Fly as a PR), a block is its own days (`analytics-block-days`). Overview leads with what fits the athlete (`analyticsProfile`: competition-lift sessions or a meet beat the form; powerlifting → the competition-lift chart, physique → sets a week by muscle, general → working sets by week), then Workouts (of planned), Sets a week, Done as written, All-time PRs, the insights, and for a powerlifter "Where you stand" (`TotalStandingCard`). The competition-lift chart (`CompetitionStrengthChart`) plots each session's estimated max from hard sets only (`comparableE1rm`: RPE 7+, ≤10 reps; light and unrated days counted, never plotted), competition lifts only (variations are their own exercise under "Any exercise"), meets as separate marks (what was lifted), block starts while 6 or fewer, the week's or month's best on long periods (`grainFor`), × bodyweight from that week's weigh-ins (`relativeToBodyweight`, never guessed), one axis from what's drawn, and a block's empty first week shows the last 12 weeks and says so. "Where you stand" keeps four things apart: the last meet (tested, its date a calendar day, `meetDay`), each lift's estimate from the last `CURRENT_DAYS` (42) with the set behind it, an older one with its date, and the coach's training max; a current total only when all three lifts have one (`totalStanding`), and the split is descriptive, never "the best place to look for pounds". Every "a week" number divides by weeks that are over from the first week with training or a written workout, weeks off counting as zero and a week the period barely touches left out, never a week still ahead; before one is over it's "this week so far" (`perWeekBasis`, `perWeekWords`; Volume's tiles and the AI's training-load read alike). Recovery is what was logged at check-out (`RecoveryTrendsCard`: sleep, felt recovered, pain, by week) and a sleep pattern only with two separate groups of 4+ and a 0.5h+ gap, said as a pattern (`sleepPatternInsight`). Colours: the three lifts are the validated split palette everywhere (`LIFT_COLORS` = `--split-*`), chips and pickers neutral, a drop in the text colour, bodyweight change never green or amber. A coach also sees the programming detail (Powerlifting exposure, coach-only insights) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found Workouts and analytics cluttered (two folds and a program list inside the program card, analytics two taps away, a page of period chips, a "Browse Blocks" button and the block's name repeated under every heading) and several numbers misleading: a 1,471 lb "estimated total, +27 vs last meet" from an Aug 5 squat single, an Aug 5 bench single and a 600 lb coach max while every November squat and bench set was RPE 6 (his records: squat 413 from 335×5 @8 Oct 26, deadlift 561 from 495×4 @10 Nov 13, no hard bench since Sep 21), 37 sets read as 9.3 a week (this week over the block's four), "best sessions 7.5h / low-readiness sessions 7.5h" from overlapping groups, a cable fly as the top e1RM gain, a 98% readiness that said to push top sets, and a Sep 18 meet shown as Sep 17.
- Workouts answers four questions in order and Training analytics is five areas, each saying what its numbers are measured against (Nov 22 2026, the second pass; analytics-areas.ts pure and tested in workouts-analytics-areas.test.ts). Workouts: the week strip, the day's card (a finished workout is one quiet "✓ N sets · N min · Recap ›" row, never View and Preview buttons; a rest day one compact row with the next workout, `RestDayCard`), the program as a compact summary (name, "Week 1 of 12 · <current phase> · ⚑ Meet in 11 weeks", one bar, Full plan; upcoming phases and every week's notes are the plan sheet's, never on the card), then one Progress card (`TrainingAnalyticsPreviewCard`: one to three numbers from `progressSnapshot`: this month's planned workouts done (`monthProgress`, today counted only once done, optional and at-home sessions never), the main competition lift's estimated max change over 12 weeks (`snapshotLift`, 4+ hard sessions) or, for anyone else, how many lifts are getting stronger (`liftsTrending`), PRs only when there are some; the whole card is the one way into analytics) and readiness only when it's news for a workout still to do today (`RecoveryPreviewCard onlyWhenNews`). A cardio card says its main target big (`cardioTargetsView`) with what else counts under it, calories "on the machine (an estimate)", and hides only the old "ANY ONE target" boilerplate from a target's note (`cardioNoteForAthlete`: 12 of 32 targets carried it; the stored note is never changed, new targets get none). Analytics is Overview · Strength · Volume · Consistency · Recovery (`ANALYTICS_VIEWS`, `?view=`): Overview is one `AreaCard` per area ordered by `analyticsProfile`, plus at most two insights. Strength shows one competition lift at a time (`CompetitionStrengthChart`: Squat · Bench · Deadlift · Compare, the lift with the most hard sessions first; never three lifts on one pound axis) with its estimate, the set behind it and its change from a stated day (`changeWords`, `strengthTrend`'s `firstDate`); meets (◆, lifted) and tested maxes (▲, `testedNotMeet` drops one that is the meet) are their own marks; the line is straight and breaks where hard sets are too far apart (`liftSeries`, `GAP_DAYS`); an axis never zooms in past a change worth drawing (`niceDomain` with `strengthMinSpan`: 6%, at least 10 lb; the Any exercise chart and bodyweight too: a 1 lb dip on a 308–312 axis read as a crash); Compare is % change from each lift's own first hard set, lined up by week with each lift's stretches as separate lines (`normalizedSeries` `segments`). "Where you stand" is one table of four kinds of number, each labelled (`TotalStandingCard`: Est. now, Last meet, 1RM on file tested / entered, Training max), from `maxesFromRows`: a tested 1RM (`source` tested or a test date), a 1RM a coach typed in, and the `training_max` column, never whichever is newest called "Training max" (Jared's 600 lb deadlift was an entered 1RM; his training max column was empty); no "typical split" read. Then By block (`blockStrengthRows`, phase chips to compare a peak with a peak), Personal records as the sets lifted, competition results, a per-lift trend list for anyone who isn't a powerlifter (`LiftsMovingCard`), and Any exercise; programming detail is the coach's, behind More detail. Volume leads with working sets a week (averaged over full weeks) and hard sets as a share of rated sets; muscle rows say "N sets", "N days a week" and a change only with its weeks ("down 34% (last 2 weeks vs the 2 before)"); By block is `blockVolumeRows`. Consistency (`consistencyOf`, `ConsistencySummary`) is planned workouts done and written sets done for days that have passed, week by week (`WeekLoad.plannedSessions`), and the last 4 weeks against the 4 before, then the recent workouts and, behind More detail, when and how long they train. Recovery keeps "What you logged" (check-outs, `useCheckouts`, shared with the Overview) apart from "Readiness (estimate)"; bodyweight change is between the first and last weeks' averages, never one weigh-in to another. Methodology lives behind the info icons, one "More detail" per tab at most — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found the dashboard and analytics wordy and unclear: the program card listed every phase with a paragraph, the competition chart put three lifts hundreds of pounds apart on one axis, "Up 9%", "Hard sets 66%" and "14.7 sets · 3.4×/wk · −34%" said nothing about what they were measured against, "Training max" showed coach-entered 1RMs, a cardio card told athletes to "Complete the session when ANY ONE target is reached", and a typical-range read drew conclusions the data couldn't support.
- A client's nutrition plans are a dated history, never overwritten: a new current plan (the AI panel's one-tap Update plan, `publishAiPlan`; the editor's save of a new or re-activated Active plan; Use again / New plan from this, `reuseEarlierPlan`) archives the plan before it through `archiveReplacedPlans` (plan-publish.ts: `status = 'Archived'`, `end_date` = the day before the new start via `replacedEndDate`, `last_updated_at` kept), and the plans list shows the current plan then Previous plans with their period (`planPeriod`) and "Updated" (`planUpdatedAt`: `last_updated_at` first, since a migration touched every `updated_at`), the same date the client's Meal Plan card shows. Update plan is the panel's default (Review & edit first is the second choice): it carries what belongs to the client (water, notes to them, the assigned coach, each day's `use_on` / `weekdays` via `carryPlanDayRules`), takes the day's steps from the AI targets and syncs cardio like the editor (`syncPlanCardio`). Use again copies the old plan under new dates (`reusePlanPayload` / `copyPlanDays`) so the old one keeps its own. A plan's end date is only when its next update is due: past it the plan stays the client's (every client read takes the newest plan that isn't Archived, whatever its dates, and no job or date archives one; the nightly `nutrition-tick` only sets `tracking_status`) until a new one replaces it — why: replacing a plan used to leave no trace (every client had exactly one plan and none archived), so a coach couldn't go back to a plan that worked or tell when a plan had changed, and a client must never be left without a plan because an update ran late.
- When a client's nutrition plan goes live or changes, the update lands in their coach chat as a message with the plan's card (`postPlanMessage`, plan-message.server.ts: `kind = 'nutrition_plan'`, its period, phase and each day's calories and macros, a new plan's calorie change from the one before by day name, and Open my plan; the coach's copy opens the client's Nutrition tab), from the coach who published it, `is_automated` so it never counts as a reply that closes check-ins. Every publish path posts it: the `nutrition_targets_updated` app event (Update plan, the editor, Use again) and Cleo; once per plan version (`version` = `last_updated_at`) and a burst of saves posts once (`PLAN_MESSAGE_QUIET_MS`), and the one push (`nutrition_targets_updated`) still goes alongside it — why: the coach wants plan updates in Messages, where the client already looks for what their coach sends, not only as a push that's gone once it's swiped away.
- The client's meal plan cards (`MealPlanReader`, MealPlanReader.tsx) are compact and swipe one at a time: the meal tabs (numbered, a ⚡ / dumbbell for pre / post-workout, `TimingIcon`) sit above the cards so the floating tab bar never covers them; each card keeps its own height (`items-start`, never stretched to the tallest, never clipped or resized while swiping) and one swipe moves exactly one card (`[scroll-snap-stop:always]`); a card reads title + kcal, the timing badge with its hint, the macros as one row (`MacroSummary`), the foods name left / amount right on one line (`whitespace-nowrap`), then the meal's water as its own line under them (`data-meal-water`) — why: every card stretched to the tallest meal with its macros pinned to the bottom, amounts like "1 scoop" wrapped in a fixed column, water sat in the food list, and the dots plus a pre/post legend under the cards sat behind the tab bar.
- Cardio done for time in a workout (a treadmill walk, a bike: `isTimedCardio`, timed-cardio.ts, shared with the program generator) logs Time, an optional Calories box (`actual_calories`, whole, 0–5000, 20270116090000_set_calories.sql; on by default for cardio rows, any row can switch it in the columns menu) and RPE. Calories are never needed for a set to count as done; a timed set is done at its prescribed time once RPE or calories go in (`timedSetAutoDuration`, set-completion.ts), like a reps set at its prescribed reps, and a time logged with the timer or typed in it always wins; an unlogged time shows as a dashed, muted target (`SetTimerInput`), and a card with no weight column has no kg / lb switch — why: the post-workout walk only had Time and RPE, and typing RPE left the set a draft (the "▷ 10:00" is the target, not a logged time), so the walk never showed as done.
- One design system, everywhere (`src/styles.css` tokens + `@/components/ui` primitives): neutral greys with black / white and one accent, `primary` (the brand red), for a screen's one primary action and the bottom bar's active tab; in-page tabs and segmented controls show the active one in neutral (`bg-secondary text-foreground`). Colour means something or isn't used: green = done / positive, amber = needs attention or a PR / record, red (`destructive`) = error, delete, overdue, plus chart series. No gradients (`--gradient-primary` / `--gradient-dark` are flat), glows, coloured shadows, tinted entry cards or coloured icon tiles (a neutral tile is `bg-muted`). Button is `rounded-lg` with no shadow (default = the primary action; outline / secondary / ghost for the rest), Card is `rounded-2xl`, bordered, no shadow, and inputs are `rounded-lg` and borderline only. Titles are sentence case and semibold (page 20–24px, card 16px with an optional icon), never all caps or `font-black`; anything tapped often on a phone is at least 40px — why: screens built at different times had a dozen accent colours, gradient buttons, glowing cards and shouting caps titles, and the coach wants one calm, premium product where colour only ever points at what needs attention.
- The Crew username trigger (`community_username_on_member`, on `clients` and `user_roles`) reads a role only through `to_jsonb(NEW) ->> 'role'` (20270117100000_username_trigger_any_table.sql) — why: `NEW.role` on `clients` (no such column) failed every new client, login link and JF member sign-up from mid-October, since PL/pgSQL resolves every field in a condition before the OR.
- Each thing is asked for or offered once per screen. Client Home: onboarding is one card, the setup checklist (`SetupChecklistBanner`, leading with the agreement), which steps aside for `AgreementDashboardCard` (`whenHidden`) once only the agreement is left; Goals & Setup auto-opens at most once per app launch and only when no other popup is open; calendar sync is a checklist row and the Calendar page's card, not a Home card; installing is the launch popup + `InstallAppCard` (`HomeScreenSetupTracker` only records `home_screen_setup_status`); the calendar card links to Calendar once (its header), `SessionsCard` is credits only, and there is no "Updating…" spinner (only offline / failed-refresh warnings). Bodyweight, Water and Progress share `HomeCardHeader` (plain icon, 16px title, one action); Water's History opens `WaterHistorySheet` in place. Nutrition: Log Food, the Meal plan PDF and Open Cookbook are Nutrition tools tiles (`ToolTile`: a plain icon over the name, no description), How to weigh and the Grocery List are `ToolRow`s in the Meal plan card; the page has no subtitle, the targets card names only the day and phase, and "Your macros add up to…" shows only when they're more than 5% off the target. The coach chat header (client) is Call plus ⋯ (Text, Mark as unread) — why: Home asked for the agreement, Goals, calendar sync and install two or three times each, and every tool on Nutrition had two doors.
- Coach notes an athlete reads are quality over quantity (Nov 18 2026, coach-notes.ts, pure and tested on live notes): a note shows only when it changes how they train today, and nothing stored is ever rewritten to get there. On an exercise card a coach's own words show exactly as written; a note the generator wrote (its " · " parts with "Progress:" / "Swap if needed:", `isGeneratedNote`) shows without its boilerplate (`exerciseNoteForAthlete`, the logger passes the program week): a progression line that's only a method's name or "Add load if RPE < target." (`GENERIC_PROGRESSION`; "Hold a plate if reps are easy." is an instruction and stays), a "Week 1: …" line on any other week, and the model explaining its programming to the coach ("Replacing DB Bench to overcome plateau.", `RATIONALE`); a note with nothing left isn't shown at all. Generate Next Program writes notes through the same rule (`athleteNotes` → `cleanGeneratedNote`), and every AI that writes one (the construct prompt's `ATHLETE_NOTE_RULES`, Edit with AI, Cleo) is told: null unless it changes how they do the exercise, one short sentence in the coach's voice, never restating what the card shows, never programming rationale, no generic cues or motivation. A day's notes (`DayCoachNotes`) are compared with the same workout last week (`pl-day-notes-last-week`, asked only when there's a note and a last week): word for word the same is one line, "Coach notes · Same as last week" with the note a tap away, and a note with a new sentence leads with it ("New this week", `dayNoteView`); no note, no section.
