Validation (Nov 21 2026): 21 rendered and H.264-encoded clips with a known answer (scripts/lift-analysis/gym-scene.ts, scenarios.ts, validate-clips.ts in Node, browser/run.cjs through the app's own WebCodecs pipeline in Chromium: heavy and real-style squats with the walk-out, paused bench, conventional and sumo deadlifts, a 1-rep grinder, a walker in front of the plate, a bottle by it, 400 and 325 mm plates, a phone 25° turned or 12° tilted, a far plate, a handheld phone, a dim gym, 30 and 60 fps); before this, 6 of the 21 were read confidently wrong (the plate sized from an inner ring or the lip, 3–49 % off); through the browser pipeline every one is now found by detection alone, followed in 98–100 % of its frames, its plate sized within 0.3 % (the tilted phone's height foreshortened exactly as it should be), every rep within ±0.017 m/s of the truth, 20 sets logged and the handheld one refused, never guessed; the synthetic unit suites (engine.test.ts, tracking.test.ts); and 12 real bench reps against Qwik VBT and Metric VBT (MAE 0.010 m/s, Nov 20 2026). A rep's end is judged like those apps (the bar under a tenth of its top speed at lockout), so a slow creep to a dead stop isn't counted; squat and deadlift have no real reference device yet — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach asked for velocity tracking that finds the bar by itself, lets the athlete confirm or move it before anything is tracked, never makes them start over, and never invents movement: production's two reads had both needed a tap, never calibrated and jumped to other objects, the plate's size came from inner rings or the lip, two plates on a tree passed for the bar, and the database's velocity columns were never applied.
- Bar speed is read the way athletes really film, read by itself, and judged on physics, not on caution (Nov 22 2026, bar-path/4; verdict.ts, kinematics.ts, auto-check.ts, analyze.ts; real-angles.test.ts): every production clip was filmed 25–35° round from side-on and none had ever been read by itself or logged. A phone turned round (yaw) leaves the plate's tall axis its true 450 mm and the bar's rise square to the lens, so vertical speed is accepted to ~40° (`minTurnedRatio` 0.77; a tilted phone still within ~25°, `minViewRatio` 0.9); a rep's mean concentric velocity is its rise over its time, so a plate hidden for up to `MCV_MAX_HIDDEN_S` (0.6 s) inside the press with both ends seen, `MCV_MIN_COVERAGE` of the rep followed and the bar seen rising across every gap stays measured (the peak and sticking point don't); a rep is complete when the highest the bar got reaches the set's tops (`s.y[l.to]`, never the concentric's timed end, which stops short on a slow lockout); the plate lost inside the set for longer than a rep (`maxLostInsideS` 1 s) or followed in under `minTracked` (0.75) of the clip is never logged. Read by itself: a high proposal is read and kept when its read is a lift (`autoReadCheck`: a scale from the plate's edge, followed in 80 %+, a rep range for the lift, `ROM_BANDS`, a press 0.2–12 s, no faster than a loaded bar), a medium one is read too and kept only when the whole set passes every logging rule (`velocity.accepted`), else it waits for one look as before. Validated on 27 rendered clips (the 21 above plus 6 at production's angles, plate sizes and thickness, `stackMm`), old engine and new on the same files: through the Node decoder 22 sets logged by themselves against 17 (5 of the 6 real-angle clips against 0), every logged set within ±0.022 m/s, the original 21 read identically; through the app's own browser pipeline (WebCodecs, Chromium) 24 of 27 logged by themselves, every one within ±0.017 m/s, the handheld clip and a clip whose first reps were hidden at the unrack refused, and squat-real-30 (followed in 71 %, 1 of 3 reps, by old and new engines alike) now asking for a look instead of showing a partial read as done; and the one wrong auto-read a looser medium rule made (a ring 32 % too big, scale off by a quarter) back to "check the bar". A per-press camera-drift check (the background's vertical travel against the rep's rise) was tried and dropped: on propped clips the lifter crossing the corner tiles and small registration bias over a press flagged 6 still cameras, so slow pans and zooms are caught only when the frame-to-frame shake is large (median over 1.5 px); a broadcast clip that zooms over a plain wall reads as a still camera. No reference device has measured squat or deadlift footage — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found automatic tracking kept failing and athletes had to find the bar by hand, defeating the point; production's reads were refused for angles that don't affect vertical speed, a brief hand over the plate, and a finished rep called incomplete.
- The bar is never lost while it's in plain view, and a rise that isn't a rep is never counted (Nov 22 2026, bar-path/5; recover.ts, follow.ts, detect.ts, find.ts, kinematics.ts, raw-track.ts, reps-edit.ts; recovery-reps.test.ts): the coach's 185 kg × 4 competition deadlift (phone ~38° round from side-on, 30 fps) was found, then lost for 3.6 s across rep 4's lockout with the plate in view, counted five reps, and said 1.4 % velocity loss ±11.3 %. Following (`PlateFollower.push`): the plate's own picture where its own motion put it is always searched; the backbone (the finder's four-a-second circles, which can link another round thing for a stretch) is searched too only once the plate has been gone `BACKBONE_AFTER_S` (1 s), a match there counts only when it's sure (`SURE`, its rim at its size, 40 %+ of it showing), the plate's own region wins a near tie, and the backbone vetoes a match only when taking the plate back after a loss and the match isn't sure of itself (`SELF_SURE` 0.88, or sure with its rim): while it's followed frame to frame the backbone never overrules it. Size is the rim's longer axis against the key's (`sizedRim`): a turned or tilted plate narrows one way and keeps its full size the other, where the mean of the two shrank a plate turned 38° by 11 % and every sure match was refused. A round ring the athlete confirms on a turned plate learns the plate's shape at that spot (`shapeAtMarker`: an ellipse fitted to the rim, accepted only with 45 %+ of it, its centre within a quarter of a radius and its long axis within 15 %); ellipses read down to `MIN_ELLIPSE_RATIO` (0.4, a phone about 66° round). The plate's size is its own rim, never the ring's: a ring found round the plate's face and the stack's edge behind it together (one rounder, bigger outline on a turned phone) measured only rims of its own wrong size, so the still frames' rims now outvote it when most of them agree on another size (`plateSizedRims`, rim-outer.ts: 3+ clear rims, 70 % within 4 % of each other, more than `RIM_SIZE_TOL` smaller than the track's size, never bigger, since with no plate in view the lifter's outline would win; else nothing changes). A bar moves sideways no faster than 1.1 m/s (`MAX_BAR_SPEED_MM_S.x`, a walk-out is 0.3–0.5), and the finder links circles by what a bar can travel, up to 4.5 radii up or down but 2.5 across (`reachOf` / `reachDistance`). Recovery (`gapsToRecover` / `recoverGap` / `applyRecovered`; run.ts's "recovering" stage, engine.ts in memory): every stretch lost inside the clip with the plate seen on both sides (≤ `RECOVER_MAX_GAP_S` 8 s, ≤ `RECOVER_MAX_TOTAL_S` 20 s of a clip, shortest first) is read again on its own, forward from the frame before and backward from the frame after with fresh followers cut from those very frames, merged by `mergePasses`' rules (agree, or one unbroken chain; never a frame they disagree on), filling only frames that were lost; only those frames are decoded again, and nothing is drawn across what stays lost. Reps (`analyzeReps`): a last rise that never reached the top and was never seen coming back down (the bar picked up to move it as the clip ends) isn't a rep (`ignored` `after_set`, the note "Movement after the set wasn't counted"); a missed rep is seen falling back. The athlete or coach can say a rep isn't one: every read keeps its followed track packed (`track.raw`, `packTrack`: 30 bytes a frame, left off past `RAW_MAX_FRAMES` (6000) so a read stays inside the server's 600 KB) with what reading it needs (`track.reread`: size, shake, direction), and "Not a rep" in a rep's detail re-reads the set from it without the video (`readWithout` / `toggleRep`: `exclude` by the rep's concentric start within `EXCLUDE_MATCH_S`, the reps renumbered, the note "Reps edited", Put back undoes it; the same numbers to 4 decimals with nothing taken out), saved like any read, so the server's own rule puts its speed on the set once its reps match what was logged; a read from before bar-path/5 has no track and offers Read it again (its keys, its exclusions kept). When the logged reps and the full lifts the video shows differ the results say so ("You logged 4 reps; the video shows 5 full lifts", `data-reps-mismatch`), never forcing either. Velocity loss is said only when every complete rep's speed was read (`partial_set` otherwise) and only as a measurement within ±`VL_MEASURED_PTS` (4) points or an estimate within ±`VL_ESTIMATE_PTS` (7), else "too uncertain"; never ±11. The stages say what's happening (Preparing video · Identifying the barbell · Tracking repetitions · Recovering frames where the bar was lost · Adapting to the camera angle · Calculating velocity), and Technique has a Horizontal line layer (a dashed level through the bar where the rep's drive starts: the depth, the touch, the floor, to compare rep to rep). Validated on 30 rendered clips (the 27 above plus three of the coach's set: 185 × 4 at 38° with someone passing the plate at rep 4's lockout, the same with the bar moved after the set, and a phone 58° round), old engine and new on the same files: through the Node decoder 27 sets logged by themselves against 22 (both 185 × 4 clips at 4 reps, never 5; deadlift-conv, bench-yaw30 (86 %), squat-far and both 185 × 4 clips (56 % at 38°) now followed 97–100 %, squat-real 82 → 97 %), every logged set within ±0.025 m/s; through the app's own browser pipeline (WebCodecs, Chromium) 26 of the 27 earlier clips logged by themselves against 24 (squat-real-30 and bench-yaw30-30, which lost the plate for 16 % of the clip, now read whole), still within ±0.017 m/s, the handheld clip still refused; the 185 × 4 replica at 38° logged at +0.003 to +0.035 m/s a rep (its small plate's dark rim line blurs into the edge, so its size reads ~3.7 % small: the measurement's limit at that size, said here rather than hidden), the same set with the bar moved after it waiting for one look and, confirmed, 4 reps 100 % followed within +0.025 m/s (shown, not logged: one rep only an estimate), and the 58° clip 3 of 3 reps followed (the old engine: 0) and shown as estimates. Before the plate-size rule the browser logged the 38° replica 8 % slow (−0.054 m/s), which is why it's there. Pose is never used to place the bar (it supports Technique only), sleeve or shaft references aren't followed, a phone ~58° round can still get a wrong round object proposed (the athlete confirms or moves the ring), and no reference device has measured squat or deadlift footage — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach found his 185 kg × 4 deadlift read lose the plate during the set and show five reps where he did four; the stored reads showed the plate lost at rep 4's lockout and never read back, a backbone that had linked another round object vetoing a plate in plain view (matches 0.96), circular keys on a 0.79-wide plate refused as the wrong size, and a rise after the set counted as a sixth rep in the other clip; both stored reads also show five full-height lifts at a steady ~3 s cadence, which nothing but the footage can settle, so the athlete gets the count said beside what they logged and one tap to take a rise out.
- A lift video sent in the coach chat is read like a set video (Nov 22 2026, 20270504090000_lift_videos_in_chat.sql, chat-lift.ts pure and tested on a real evening's batch, chat-lift-run.ts, chat-lift-line.tsx, bar-path-overlay.tsx): clients sent 55 lifting videos straight into the chat in 60 days ("Last set comp bench", "1st set close grip bench") against 39 set videos, and none was ever analysed. After a client's video lands (`doSend`, the file itself when it went as it was, else its link), or when the athlete or the coach opens the chat within `CHAT_LIFT_AUTO_HOURS` (48) and that device hasn't looked (`wasTried`), the sets logged that day are read (`setsAround`: 14 h before to 20 min after), the bar is read on the device, and `chat_video_lift` makes it a `pl_set_videos` row with `origin` 'chat' pointing at the message it's in: never a new message, never the client's words rewritten (the attachment gains `set_video`, written as the server by `_chat_lift_set_attachment`), never in the workout's film or given a feed copy (`set_video_needs_server_copy`, the film's queries, `workout-film-client`), never on a warm-up. Which set is `matchChatLift`: a lift is one lift across rows (`liftKey`, a top single then back-offs), its sets in the order done (`liftOrder`: row by row, then set number; a fill-down logs a row in one moment); a set is linked only when the words or the logging time name one lift (the time only when it's the only barbell lift done within `ONE_LIFT_MIN`) and the words or the time name one logged set, and two signals agree or the video's rep count matches the set's; a set the reps don't match, or an unlogged one, is offered (`suggest`: "Is this Competition Bench · Backoff 4?" Yes / Another set / Not a lift); a warm-up, no lift named in a batch of several, or the same evening is the workout only (Which set is this?); and nothing logged, no lift named and no plate found leaves the video alone (`captionNamesLift`). Variation words keep "comp bench" off the close grip bench (`VARIATION_WORDS`). Linked, its speed goes on the set by the same server rule as a set video, and "Change" / the logger's sheet move it (`set_video_move`, the chat's words kept); taking it off a set leaves the message (`set_video_remove` for origin chat); "Not a lift" removes the read only (`chat_video_unlift`). In the chat the video's still carries only the bar path (`BarPathOverlay`: the read's own smoothed path, white over a soft halo, broken where the plate wasn't followed, laid out like the tile's object-cover), with the read's one line and the set under it. Validated on production in rolled-back transactions: created with a suggestion, idempotent, analysis saved as the athlete, linked with the words and message type kept and no edit history, moved again, warm-ups refused, unlinked keeping the message, unlifted keeping the video, a stranger refused, the 41 existing set videos unchanged; rollback in docs/rollback/2026-11-22-lift-videos-in-chat-rollback.sql — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for every barbell video a client sends in their coach chat to be analysed automatically, linked to the right set or confirmed in one tap, with only a clean bar path on the video and the numbers beneath it.
- What bar speed to expect is the athlete's own, and only when they've earned one (Nov 22 2026, velocity-model.ts pure and tested, `useVelocityModel` in queries.ts): one definition throughout, the set-average MCV of accepted video reads (`set_mcv`, `velocity_accepted`; device-typed speeds are left out, a device's multi-rep number is its own choice of rep), fitted per exercise as a weighted least-squares line on load (+ reps when the athlete filmed 2+ rep counts twice each; recent sets count more, half-life 60 days, 180-day window), a slope only when the loads span 10 % and 10 kg and it falls clearly (t ≤ −2), else a flat estimate offered within 4 % of the loads measured; never outside the loads (±5 %) or rep counts seen, never with a 95 % prediction interval wider than ±0.10 m/s, at least 5 sets from 2 workouts; today's measured sets move it by their average difference shrunk n/(n+1). The logger's empty velocity box shows it as "≈0.42" (placeholder, never a value; its title gives the range and how many sets), a measured one keeps its video mark, and the analysis view's "Expected and measured" compares this set against the line drawn without it, calling it faster or slower only outside the interval (`compareToExpected`), else "No expected speed yet" with why (`whyNoPrediction`). No universal velocity table, threshold or 1RM-from-velocity anywhere; no production athlete has enough accepted reads yet, so it says so until they do — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for predicted velocity in the logger that improves with each athlete's own data, stays clearly apart from measured, and knows when there isn't enough; the analysis view's old comparison used the fastest rep with a fixed 6 % day-to-day swing.
- A set video's technique is read from the athlete's body, on the device, and never replaces the bar (Nov 22 2026, src/lib/lift-analysis/pose/, pure and tested in pose-technique.test.ts; technique-panel.tsx, overlay-draw.ts; 20270506090000_lift_video_notes.sql): the analysis view is Velocity · Technique (`data-analysis-modes`), Velocity exactly as before and first, so bar speed never waits on the body. Technique starts the read when it's opened (`useTechnique` → `runPose`, pose-run.ts): MediaPipe's BlazePose (Apache-2.0, `@mediapipe/tasks-vision` pinned 1.2.2, its WebAssembly through Vite `?url` and `public/models/pose_landmarker_full.task`, all served by the app, never a CDN, so nothing leaves the device), loaded only then (about 24 MB the first time, its progress the bytes downloaded, then frames read, never a timer), the GPU where it's quick and the CPU where the GPU is software (`SLOW_GPU_MS`), `POSE_FPS` (15) frames a second over the set's window, each frame read on its own (`runningMode: "IMAGE"`: VIDEO mode kept following whoever it found first, a coach beside the lifter kept and the lifter dropped), the athlete the body at the bar, else where the athlete just was, never someone else (`pickAthlete`: -1 rather than a stranger, `MAX_BODY_JUMP`, `BAR_REACH`), smoothed without bridging a gap over `MAX_GAP_S` (`smoothTrack`), the skeleton on the video as frames come in (`onPartial`). What it can say depends on where the camera was (`viewOf`, from the body itself): side-on gives sagittal angles (knee, hip, elbow flexion, torso and shin from vertical, the thigh against horizontal: `anglesOf`, each only from joints over `VIS_MIN`, on the near side, `nearSide`), front or behind gives side-to-side shift only, an angled camera gives neither, and the view says why (`limits`); one camera never claims a 3D asymmetry. Per rep, at the moments the bar's read marks (`readTechnique`: squat depth by joint centres said to be a guide, torso and shin at the bottom, the torso's change in the first third of the drive; bench elbow and forearm at the touch, the touch point from the shoulder; deadlift hip, torso and shin off the floor, hips against bar in the early pull, when the knees finished straightening, bar to shin), distances on the body from the plate's scale always "≈" whole cm (the plate is nearer the camera), and observations only from patterns past `ANGLE_NOISE_DEG` with the rep and moment to jump to (`observe`), never "wrong", "dangerous" or a diagnosis. Every overlay is its own switch, remembered on the phone (`Layers`: bar path, skeleton thin with the far side quieter, joint angles and torso & shin only from a side view, a vertical line through the bar's start, hip & knee paths), and a ghost of another rep or the start position is lined up by phase (`samePhaseTime`: bottom with bottom). Film notes (`lift_video_notes`, read by whoever sees the set video, written only through `lift_video_note_save` / `_sent` / `_remove`, each note its author's to edit, hide or remove): pause on a frame, draw lines, arrows, circles or pick a joint (it follows the joint as the clip plays), add a comment, save it on the video or send it in the coach chat as the marked frame (`sendFilmNote`: a picture made from the frame decoded at the clip's own size with the overlays drawn by the player's own code, `grabFrame` + `drawOverlays`, a `film_note` on the attachment, `message_type` 'Film Note'), whose card opens the clip on that frame in Technique with the note picked (`FilmNoteOpen`); nothing is sent while a coach views as the client. A frame or the rep as a clip with its overlays is saved from the panel (`grabFrame`, `renderAnnotatedClip`: H.264 encoded on the device, the original never changed). Validated only on the repo's own photos (a squat and a two-person scene, through the real decoder and model in Chromium: landmarks on the joints, the lifter picked over the person beside her); no real set footage has been read for pose, and no angle has been compared with a reference system, so its numbers are a guide, said as such. The bar's own read got quicker the same day without changing a number (the 27 validation clips read identically): each sampled frame is searched for circles as it's decoded (`frameSightings`), and the last clip's frames and circles are kept (`lastDetected`, run.ts), so Confirm → Analyze, Fix tracking and Start over never decode or search them twice; the progress line is the run's stages weighted by their measured times (progress.ts) shown as a percent, and a rep's mean propulsive velocity shows when it differs from its mean — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for intelligent film review: automatic body landmarks with a thin skeleton, toggleable joint-angle and reference overlays, powerlifting-specific reads for the squat, bench and deadlift, rep and set comparisons, and coach annotations with timestamped comments that reach the athlete through the existing coach chat and open on the exact frame, all without slowing bar speed, inventing numbers, labelling a movement dangerous, or uploading footage.
- A cardio exercise's history and analytics read time, calories and effort, never load / reps / velocity: the exercise's History sheet (`ExerciseHistorySheet`) switches to cardio when `isCardioHistory` says so (cardio-history.ts: `isTimedCardio` by name, or everything logged is time / calories with no load or reps) and shows Time · Cal · RPE per set (`cardioClock`), a summary of the range (`cardioHistorySummary`: a day is one session, its sets' time and calories added up and its hardest RPE, the same reading as `tg_pl_row_results_cardio_done`) and its Edit fixes time, calories and RPE (`cardioEditDraft` / `cardioDraftIssue` / `cardioEditPatch` in history-set-edit.ts; a bare number typed for time is minutes, `parseCardioTime`; the plan's `cardio_completions` follows by its trigger and `HISTORY_EDIT_QUERY_KEYS` refreshes the cardio log and analytics). Training Analytics → Cardio (`CardioAnalyticsSection`, the client's and the coach's alike) adds, from `summarizeCardio` (cardio-adherence.ts, the one place the math lives): average effort (RPE), calories (total and per session, read as a trend: they're the machine's or the watch's) and heart rate, only when logged; Effort and Calories trend views beside Minutes and Adherence (each week's `avgRpe` / `calories`); the effort trend (`effortTrend`: the first few rated sessions against the last few, one modality at a time, a half point of RPE the smallest change it calls, never "easier" when sessions got shorter or "harder" when they got longer); and every session with what was logged and whether it was in a workout (`sessions`, `completion_target`). The card's "Last time" chip is the same for a timed exercise (`PreviousTimedChip`, opening the same History): `selectPreviousLifts` reads a card marked `timed` (`isTimedCardio` → "cardio", else a time-tracked row → "hold") from its last session's `durationSeconds` / `calories` / RPE (`summarizeTimedSession`: cardio added up with its hardest RPE, a hold's longest set; `formatPreviousTimed` with `formatClockSeconds`, duration.ts), never load × reps. Chart marks use the theme tokens as they are (`var(--primary)`, `color-mix(in oklch, …)`), never `hsl(var(--…))`: the tokens are oklch, so that's no colour at all — why: a treadmill walk's card had no "Last time" at all and its History showed Load / Reps / Velocity as dashes beside its RPE, so the time and calories logged every session were never seen, the coach's cardio analytics had no effort or calories to judge a client's conditioning by, and every cardio, block-progress, e1RM and recovery chart drew its bars and lines in an invalid colour.
- AI a member or client triggers is metered by AI credits (20270313090000_ai_credits.sql, `src/lib/ai-credits/`): one wallet per login (`ai_credit_wallets`), a free allowance each Winnipeg calendar month by who they are (`ai_credit_tier`: staff = `community_staff_people()`, never charged but still logged at 0; a coaching client; else a member; the numbers are `app_settings` 'ai_credits', default member 30 and coaching 300, set by an admin on Settings → AI credits & models, `ai_credits_set_allowances`) used first, then `extra` (bought or given by an admin, `ai_credits_admin_grant`, never expiring). A feature's cost is the database's (`ai_credit_cost`, mirrored by `FEATURE_COSTS` in packs.ts, the test keeps them equal; today only `meal_estimate`, the AI food log, 1 credit). Spending is server only (`ai_credits_use` / `ai_credits_settle`, service role; nobody can refund their own use) through `withAiCredits` (meter.server.ts): taken before the model is called, given back exactly once if the call throws or its answer can't be read (`MeteredCallError`), with the model, tokens and `estimateCost` kept on the use; out of credits is a reply (`{ ok: false, outOfCredits, credits }`), never an error. A new member- or client-triggered AI feature adds its cost to `ai_credit_cost` + `FEATURE_COSTS` and goes through `withAiCredits`; coach-only AI (Cleo, Generate Next Program, Edit with AI, Nutrition Update plans, Athlete Insights) and a coaching chat's voice-memo transcripts aren't metered (the coach's own spend / service). The AI food log runs on the OpenAI key (route `meal_estimate`: GPT-5 mini then GPT-5.5, `callModelNow`, answered inside the request), never Lovable credits. More credits are packs sold through Stripe (`CREDIT_PACKS`: 100 / $2.99, 300 / $6.99, 1,000 / $14.99 CAD before tax; each a Stripe product + price found by `packLookupKey`, Stripe Tax adding GST, `PlusTax` on screen) on the web only: the App Store / Play builds (`isNative()`) never offer a purchase, they say when the free credits come back. A pack's Checkout Session and PaymentIntent carry `metadata.kind = 'ai_credits'`: the webhook adds its credits once per session (`ai_credits_record_purchase`, also called by `AiCreditsReturn` when they land back on the app with `?credits=done&cs=…`, and by the Stripe account sync for one the webhook missed), skips its PaymentIntent events, and a `charge.refunded` takes back the credits it paid for that are still unused (`ai_credits_record_refund`); the account sync never matches a credit pack to a coaching sale. Packs are their own sales (`ai_credit_purchases`, business-owner readable), never a `payment_ledger` row, and Taxes & Books counts them with their GST (`normalizeRevenue`'s third source). Credits show at the point of use (`AiCreditsChip` beside the AI action, amber at 0, opening `AiCreditsSheet`; a refused action opens it as "You've used this month's AI credits") and in Account → AI credits (`#ai-credits`, hidden for staff); a coach viewing as a client sees the client's credits (`ai_credits_for_user`), never buys for them, and their own AI use there never spends the client's — why: JF Effect is free to join, so an unmetered AI feature is open to anyone who signs up, and the coach wanted a fair, profitable way to let heavy users keep going (Nov 13 2026: the only member AI then, the AI food log, had never been used and cost about $0.002 a use, so the packs are priced for Stripe's C$0.30 fee and perceived value, 75–80% margin even at the most a call costs).
- The JF Effect library programs (Nov 13 2026: the 12 of the JF Effect 12-Program Master Build Specification, three each under Athletes / Men / Women / At Home) are defined in code, `src/lib/jf-programs/` (programs.ts: every day, slot and week's prescription, each program's coach guide and `changes`, the reasons it departs from the spec; progression.ts: the week-by-week rules, `hyp` RIR by level with deloads in weeks 4 and 8 and a rep-PR week 12, `pl` RPE top sets with each back-off its own row at a % of the top set just lifted (`percentage_basis: "top_set"`), `plAccessory` keeping accessories 2+ RIR in the powerlifting peak; catalog.ts: every exercise pinned to the live library's own id, the one clients already have history on when the library holds two, `DUPLICATE_PAIRS` for a later merge, and the six it lacked as `NEW_EXERCISES`), built into `pl_templates` rows by build.ts under fixed ids (`JF_TEMPLATE_IDS`) and written as supabase/migrations/20270314090000_jf_effect_program_library.sql by scripts/jf-programs/write-migration.ts (generated, never hand-edited: jf-programs.test.ts fails when they disagree). Production got them through the `jf-library-install` hook (`installJfLibrary`: a preview unless `{ apply: true }`, then insert-if-absent by id, never an update, a new exercise only when no live one has its name, every program read back and counted), so once a coach edits one in the builder his edit is the program and re-running either adds nothing. They're the members' programs, in the membership app only (Nov 14 2026, the coach: "they should be just in the membership one, it doesn't need to be anywhere else"; `isMembersProgram`, library-view.ts: a "JF Effect" tag + a category): the coach's library shows them only under Membership app, grouped by category (`inScope`, `libraryCategorySections` on that scope; still there when the listing is taken down, "Not in the app", so it can go back up), never in All or On clients, with no Assign in its ⋯ or Preview, and the coach-side pickers (`coachingTemplates`: the client's Add program, the template picker sheet, quick assign, the assign page) and Cleo's template list leave them out, and Cleo's assign refuses one. A member gets their own copy by starting it (`startLibraryPlan` → `pl_assign_template_to_client`), which now also copies each row's role (`purpose_label`, only where the copy has none) and an optional day (`pl_days.is_optional`) through `pl_apply_template_meta` (20270314080000_template_roles_and_optional_days.sql); an optional day never counts as missed (`optional_skipped` in workout-status.ts, coach-intel's due days, block analytics, Program Intelligence, the Clients list's missed workouts) and counts when it's done. Under Membership app they're grouped in their categories (`libraryCategoryOf`: the "JF Effect" tag plus a category tag, beginner first) above any other program listed for members, each row reading the program itself (`summarizeTemplatePayload` counts a v2 multi-block template's live blocks, weeks and workouts, where it used to read 0 weeks and 0 days, and `programLine` says an optional day apart: "3 days a week + 1 optional"), a back-off row reads "90% of Top Set" and adds no warm-up or setup to the day's estimate (`estimateRowSeconds`: a `top_set` / `prev_set` row carries on the lift above it, so the Friday SBD day reads 95–115 min, not 130–160), and Edit with AI reads the program's own coaching guide (`programGuideText` from the template's notes, also for a copy through `pl_blocks.source_template_id`) so it adapts one without rebuilding it. The validator walks every block of a multi-block template (it used to check none of them) — why: the coach wanted programs with every week written out (never Week 1 twelve times), reusing the library's exercises with no duplicates and never touching a client's program or history; assigning used to drop every row's role and would have counted a skipped recovery day as a missed workout.
- Every text the app sends goes through one sender, `sendSms` (sms-gateway.server.ts), never its own Twilio call: it asks `public.sms_claim` first (20270316090000_sms_cost_guard.sql: one advisory lock for every worker; the master switch, the separate `security_texts_enabled` for account access, opt-out, `allowed_prefixes` (+1 only), the idempotency key (`sms_log.idempotency_key`, unique; a failed send frees it, `_max_attempts` tries), the per-client daily cap for automatic texts, the monthly budget (`monthly_budget_cad`: nonessential categories stop at 100%, everything but `security` at 2x, 50 / 80 / 100% each once a month, 80 and 100 pushed to admins) and writes the `sms_log` row before Twilio is called), sends with `SmartEncoded`, and reports back through `sms_finish` (a Twilio refusal is not billed; 21610 marks the person opted out). The category (`SmsCategory`, sms-policy.ts) decides how the budget treats a text: `security` (recovery links, setup / sign-in links, invites; links redacted from the log), `session` / `manual` / `test` essential, `reminder` / `payment` / `automation` / `staff_alert` / `bulk` nonessential. Push and the in-app chat come first: the unread-message nudge pushes and texts only someone the push didn't reach, for the coach's own words (`runReminderSweep`, sms-unread-reminders.server.ts), a session change is texted only when the chat's push may not reach them in time (`changeTextDecision`), staff alerts push the coach before any text, the payment-request dialog opens on the chat card, and session texts always fit one segment (`fitOneSegment`). Delivery, price and the month's real Twilio bill are read back by the 10-minute SMS tick (sms-status.server.ts; no status webhook, since the Lovable connector can't verify Twilio's signature), and Settings → SMS → Cost this month (`SmsCostCard`, `sms_cost_summary`, admin only) shows them — why: on Nov 13 2026 ten copies of the Twilio sender each had their own (or no) limits, staff alerts logged under a kind the table refused (so they went out unlogged and their dedupe never held), the workout-load alert texted the coach on every report, nothing capped monthly spend or counted segments, every evening-before reminder was 165-178 characters (2 billed segments), and the unread-message texts (the biggest line: 64% to people who had push) didn't get messages read (read on average 42-95 hours after the text).
- A prescription's load is one of: RPE only, RIR only, a % of a max with its RPE as a cap, or an RPE top set with % back-offs (`pl_exercise_rows.percentage_basis`: `training_max` / `1rm` / `est_1rm` / `top_set`, no new column), read by one helper, `load-reference.ts` (src/lib/workouts), for the logger, the builder and the generator. A % of a max reads the exercise's own max by name, else its competition lift's (`referenceFamily`: the row's movement family, then the exercise's `competition_lift_type` / `movement_family`; `competitionMax` matches "Competition Bench" to a "Competition Bench Press" max), so a High Bar Squat at 65% TM with no max of its own is 65% of the Competition Squat TM (the builder's row says "65% Comp Squat TM", `plannedLoadLabel`); a % of e1RM reads a stored one, else one from their logged competition sets (`loggedE1rm`: RPE-rated sets of 1–8 reps at RPE 6.5+, 2+ sessions in 6 weeks, the median of the last three), and a TM is never stood in for or made up (`computeRowLoad` says `no-max`). In the logger a planned % load is the weight (the coach's load, `coachOwnsLoad`), one tap from the open sets' empty boxes, with its source and cap said once, by the % rule above (the strip's `PlannedLoadCell`, else `percentLoadLine`; a logged e1RM is named by its competition lift, `computeRowLoad`'s `refLift`); a set logged over the cap (`hasRpeCap`, `rpeCapValue`) brings the open sets down to what sits at the cap for those reps, rounded down a plate step, never above the plan (`capAdjustedLoad`, `capHeld`, said once under the sets, `data-rpe-cap-next`); nothing forces the %. Generate Next Program picks the method per exercise (`LOADING_RULES`: % only on competition lifts and their barbell variations with that max on file, RPE / RIR on accessories, an RPE top set + 88–95% back-offs with a cap, RPE when in doubt), told what's on file per lift (`loadingRefsFrom` / `loadingRefsText`, the context's `loading_refs`), and every built block goes through `withLoadingGuard` (pipeline.server.ts `guardLoading`, after construct and repair, listed in `result.loadingAdjusted`): a % on an accessory becomes RPE / RIR, a % of a max that isn't on file becomes their logged e1RM for a 1RM or the RPE that load means (`rpeForPercent`) for anything else, and a top-set back-off with no top set before it becomes RPE. Existing programs and logged sets are never rewritten. A generated note or progression rule never carries a weight to lift: `withoutFixedLoads` (loading.ts) takes "Start ~430lb", "Start with 145lb (last block's top set)" and "Use 50lb DBs" out, in `withLoadingGuard` (listed in `loadingAdjusted`) and again in `athleteNotes` (so an older draft assigned later is clean too), while steps ("add 5 lb") and relative cues ("a little under last block's top set") stay; the prompts say so (construct, and Edit with AI: a planned weight goes in `load`), and the logger's Last and Suggested lines are the live number — why: the coach wants percentages where they make a lift better and the athlete to just see the weight, while 126 "Competition Bench" rows at % of 1RM had never shown a load (the max is named "Competition Bench Press"), variations at a % had no max to read, and a model left alone would happily write 70% of a training max nobody had; and on Nov 15 2026 Nicolas Galli's deadlift card said "Start ~430lb" beside his 185 lb sets: the Nov 2 run read his Oct 10 205 lb set while it was still stored as 205 kg (repaired Nov 8), and a number frozen into a note can't follow a corrected log; the same run had put a fixed start weight on every week of Colten's, Fionna's and Marc's programs, so by week 3 they read like a reset to week 1 (20270323090000_program_notes_no_fixed_weights.sql cleaned the 51 untrained rows and Nicolas's deadlift, 20270323091000 the 24 finished ones, so no program row in the app carries one; old text in `program_note_weight_backup`; the draft preview reads them through `withoutFixedLoads` too).
- The coach's Messages badge (`adminBadgeMap` → `messagesBadge`, use-admin-nav-badges.ts), the Inbox chip's number and the blue dot on a chat are one thing: a chat with a new message from the client the signed-in staff member hasn't read, or one they marked unread. `staff_inbox_state`'s `unread` / `unread_count` read only the client's messages after the read marker that `message_is_new_for_staff` allows (sent, not unsent, not an internal note, never the check-in form card), 20270319090000_inbox_unread_is_new_messages.sql; a form sent in, a check-in, a lift upload or a chat waiting on a reply never adds to it (those are the inbox's filters and the line under a name), and archived chats don't count because a new message reopens one (`trg_messages_reopen_archived_chat`). Every filter says what it shows in one line under the chips and in How Messages works (the ? beside the search), both from `FILTER_HELP` / `filterHelp` (inbox-guide.ts); a new filter adds its sentence there — why: the badge counted every chat marked "needs reply" plus forms sent in (6 on Nov 14 2026 when 1 chat had a new message), so it never matched anything the coach could see, and nothing on the screen said what each filter was for.
- A JF member's Support chat (`member_support_threads` / `_messages`; members have no coach chat) is answered in the coach's Messages: it's a row in 1:1 Chats among the coach chats, newest first (`staff_support_threads()`, 20270320090000_member_support_in_messages.sql: threads with a message, an admin's all, a coach's only their assigned members; `supportChat` / `mergeByRecency`, member-support.ts; `SupportChatRow` with "JF member · Support" under it), opening in place (`MemberSupportPane`, `?support=<thread>`; a self-serve client's `?client=` and their profile's Message open it too, starting the chat when there's none), and a self-serve client never gets a coach-chat row (`allRows` leaves them out). Its new messages are new messages: the Messages badge, the Inbox chip and the blue dot count it (`messagesBadge`'s `support` rows: `unread_for_team` > 0, not Done); swipe Read / Unread / Done, Done sits under Archived and a new message reopens it. Each side gets a push when the other writes (`support_message` to the team, opening the chat; `support_reply` to the member, "Coach Jared replied…", opening Support), sent by the server after the message saves and never failing it; the member's Support tab shows a count of replies they haven't read. Opening Support never makes a thread (`getMySupportThread` only reads; the first send makes it). The member's screen reads like Messages (`/portal/support`: the JF logo, "Usually replies within a day", one box, no categories); a coach previewing a member reads it and can't send. Communication's old Support tab is Alerts: app problems only (`support-inbox.ts`), old `sub=t:<id>` links open the member's chat — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach asked for member support to connect to the admin messenger so he can respond there; it sat on its own tab with no push to him or the member, its badge counted members who had only opened the page (two empty threads), and a member's row in 1:1 Chats opened a coach chat they can't see.
- Running is the library's fourth kind of program and a run is logged like any timed set, never a separate system: the three JF Effect running programs (Run Foundation 0 → 5K, 12 weeks, 3 days; Distance Builder 5K → half, 12 weeks, 3 days + 2 optional; Marathon Performance half → marathon, 18 weeks, 4 days + 2 optional) are defined in code (`src/lib/jf-programs/running.ts`: every week and day, its purpose in `pl_weeks.notes`, the coach guide Edit with AI reads, the changes from the master spec and why), built by `running-build.ts` (v2 template, one block per phase, fixed ids `RUN_TEMPLATE_IDS`), written as `20270321090000_jf_effect_running_library.sql` by the same generator (never hand-edited) and installed by the `jf-library-install` hook alongside the strength twelve (insert-if-absent), in the library's Running category (`LIBRARY_CATEGORIES`). Sessions are rows on the library's own Running and Walking exercises (warm-up, main set, cool-down; `purpose_label` is the kind of run), prescribed by time and RPE, never distance or a pace chart; an interval session is sets × time with the recovery as the rest; a race or the 5 km has no time target and names itself in `reps_text` (the program check accepts that); core days come first and optional days (easy / recovery only) after, because workouts land on training days in order; and the tests (`jf-running-programs.test.ts`) hold the coaching rules: a lower-stress week at least every 4th week, weekly running never more than 20% over the most already done, the long run at most 15 minutes past the longest so far, one quality session a week, no speed work in Run Foundation, a falling taper. A run's set logs distance (`pl_row_results.actual_distance_m` in metres + `actual_distance_unit` km / mi, 20270321091000_run_distance.sql; km by default, the card's Km / Mi header switches it per phone, `readDistanceUnit`), on by default for running exercises (`isRunningExercise`) in place of calories, and its card reads like a run (`runPrescription`: "3 × 8 min · 2 min easy jog between | RPE 6–7") with distance · time · pace under the sets (`runSummaryLine`, run-log.ts; pace is never stored). Long runs take hours: the set timer and time boxes read and show h:mm:ss (`parseMMSS`, `parseDurationInput`), and a timed row's session estimate is its own time (`estimateRowSeconds`; an all-timed day adds no general warm-up). Last time for a timed card is the same kind of session when there is one (a threshold run's last threshold run) with its distance; the History sheet reads a run by distance and pace and fixes distance in the unit it was logged in. Training Analytics → Running (`RunningAnalyticsSection`, shown only when there's running) is `running-analytics.ts`: a session is one workout's running filed under its main set's kind, pace only ever compared within one kind and from the main set alone, and readiness from the last 14 days (planned runs done, easy runs still easy), never because time passed. Edit with AI can change a run's time (`duration_seconds` in its edit fields, timed rows only) and follows the program's guide — why: the coach wanted professional running programs for beginners to marathoners inside the existing app, and a template written in distance, a pace chart from gym strength, or a plan that progresses on the calendar would hurt the athletes it's meant for; slower runners get the same stimulus from the same time, and the logger had no distance at all (a 90-minute long run's day also estimated at about 20 minutes).
- An exercise card in the logger says each thing once, quietly (Nov 14 2026): The card's top reads in one pass (Nov 15 2026, the coach's mockup): the exercise's name at 20px with the card's unit as one quiet word top right ("LB", `UnitSwitch`: a tap switches the card like KG / LB did), its role in plain words under it (`data-exercise-role`, no chip, no order-number tile: Focus says "Exercise N of M"), the prescription as the card's one big line (24px, `data-rx`; a run's longer line 18px), then one quiet strip of what it's read against (`data-rx-refs`, `REF_BAR`: `bg-muted/50`, rounded, no border, two halves split by a hairline, each at least 52px): "Last time" on the left is the History button (`PreviousLiftChip`: the date short ("6d", "Sep 20", never the part that's cut), a chevron, a press state; the top set with its unit, "55 lb × 12 @ 8", from `lastTimeLoad` in exercise-history.ts: the number as logged in the card's unit, else converted exactly to one decimal and marked "≈" ("≈330.7 lb", the History rule below); "None yet" still opens history), and "Suggested ⓘ" on the right (`LoadSuggestionCard`: the weight or a range, "410–430 lb", plus "× 4 @ 7" only when the prescription doesn't already say it, `rxSaysPlan`; a green dot when today's sets moved it; a tap opens what's behind it: the range, the audibles, how it's worked out, Turn off suggestions). One tap on Suggested's weight (a quiet neutral chip with a fill-down arrow, information first: Nov 20 2026, no blue) logs the first open set at it and the untouched sets below with it, exactly like a tap on that set's number (`onApply` → `logOpenSetsAt`; a range logs at its middle, `hint.target`, said on the chip's label and in the toast; "3 sets logged at 375 lb" with Undo, `runCardHistory("undo")`, whenever one tap logs more than one set), and its ⓘ beside the label is its own button for the explanation; there's no row of weight buttons to pick from, and with nothing to fill (read-only, every set logged) the whole half opens the explanation. With suggestions off the Suggest switch takes Suggested's half; a finished card's half says "Next time: 280 lb" (`LoadSuggestionsDone`) with "✓ All sets in · ran …" under the strip (`LoadSuggestionsDoneNote`); a card that can't suggest (timed, bodyweight, a coach's %) is Last time across the whole strip. Right above the set table is the card's rest line (`RestLine`, the rule on one rest per workout) with How To · Note as borderless 40px icons on its right (`ExerciseQuickActions`), and the card's ⋯ sits top right beside the name and its kg | lb (Nov 21 2026); the Suggest chip shows only while suggestions are off, and the ⋯ has the switch both ways. Every open set starts with the prescription's reps and RPE filled in (a range's top, `prescribedRepsStr` / `prescribedRpeStr`), shown at full strength like a value they typed (`TypedValueInput` has no faded "planned" state), so logging a set is typing its weight and a tap types over a rep or RPE; the weight box is empty until they act, and a set holding only the plan is never a draft (`repsOwn` / `rpeOwn` in `hasAnyEntry`). A back-off's % follows its top set only while both are the same exercise (`withLivePercent`, backoff-basis.ts: a swapped top set leaves the back-off on plain RPE, never another lift's weight). History & progress is the History sheet with the Analytics lift card for that one exercise above the days (`LiftProgressCard single`, `liftHistoryFromRows`, exercise-progress.ts: completed external-load sets, Epley e1RM, the same reading as Analytics). Swap ends in one tap, never a radio and Apply: Swap for today, or "Today + N upcoming workouts"; once a set is logged on the exercise today, today's choice is "Add it to today's workout" (`onAddAfter`, after the card) and the server refuses to move a row with sets (`applySwap`, `SWAP_LOGGED_MESSAGE`; upcoming workouts already started keep theirs, `rowsWithLoggedSets`); a result picks from its whole row (`data-swap-pick`). Add exercise opens on Recent (the client's last logged, `recentExerciseIds`) with the library's muscle groups as chips. The rest timer, Fill All, the swap / add sheets' chips and Use / Add are neutral; red stays the screen's one main action. Every set box is the same neutral well whatever its state (`typed-value-input`, `weight-value-input`, `quick-value-select`, `set-timer-input`): empty is a faint "—", planned is faded, typed is full strength; an empty working-set weight box is an open field (`border-muted-foreground/40 bg-background`: where the weight goes, what Suggested's chip fills; never a colour since Nov 20 2026), and a warm-up's cells are lighter than a set's (`quiet`, `plain`); a timed set's target is a dashed neutral box and a logged time the theme's `success` green. The card has no movement-family stripe down its left edge (the builder keeps its own), and the set table's headers are sentence case ("Reps · RPE · Weight (lb)", `loadColumnLabel`), never caps — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach asked for the auto fill and the blue weight boxes back after both were taken out that day ("I was using for a while"); and that day he found "Last" didn't look tappable or say its unit ("55 × 12 @ 8" beside "70 lb × 11 @ 8"), Suggested opened a row of big weight buttons that repeated the set boxes, and the two sat cramped on one line; and that day he sent a mockup of the card he wanted (a big name and prescription, the unit as "LB", "Load guide ⓘ", Last and Suggested on one line, then "Rest 5–7 min" with quiet icons over the sets), where the card had a number tile, a role chip, a KG / LB pair, two lines with a label column and a row of outlined buttons and a red countdown; and that day the coach's squat card in Focus showed an empty weight box tinted blue beside the plain reps and RPE boxes, which read as selected, and an amber stripe that reads as "needs attention" in this design system; on Nov 15 2026 he asked for Last time and Suggested to sit with the RPE target they're read against as quiet supporting lines instead of a separate section of two bordered boxes (which also repeated the prescription's "× 5 @ 6"), and anything that doesn't fit a 375pt phone has to wrap, never be cut to "×." beside the weight; and on Nov 16 2026 the coach didn't like the suggested weight sitting in the weight boxes ("I just don't like having the numbers in there"), and before that found logging too much tapping (the Suggested chip filled every open set as a draft that then needed a tap on each set's number, a fill and then a tap per set), and asked for the old way back for reps and RPE (pre-filled so athletes only fill in the weight and can easily adjust the rest): faded "planned" reps read as empty boxes to be filled in; and on Nov 14 2026 the coach asked for a cleaner logger without rebuilding it: the card stacked a red suggestion card with its range, audibles and warm-up all open, a second chip for Last time, amber "draft" rows for numbers nobody had typed, a swap that asked for a radio then Apply and could move logged sets onto another exercise, and a swapped top set left its back-off at 92.5% of a different lift.
- An exercise's History (`ExerciseHistorySheet`, opened from the card's Last time or ⋯ History & progress) is the athlete's past sessions first, Strong / Hevy's way (Nov 19 2026): one sticky bar (‹, the name, "N sessions · Jun 18 – Sep 20"), one progress line when there's an honest one (`progressSince`, exercise-history.ts: the latest session's top set against the last one in the same program, block or `prep_id`, with the same reps and an RPE within a point, "Up 20 lb for 12 reps since Nov 8 (55 → 75 lb)"; never across programs, whose machines and setups differ, never across rep counts), then, on a competition lift, its family since its last session (`relatedSince`: "Bench work since Sep 20", each barbell variation (`rangeLift`) by the program's name for it with its last session's top set, one tap opens its history in place, ‹ comes back), then the sessions newest first under their block's name once per run (`blockShortName`; the open workout left out: it's on screen), each its date with "6 weeks ago" and a Meet chip on a meet (`isMeetSession`: a day named for one, 🏆 / Championships / Meet Day, or a card whose role is Competition), its workout by name (`workoutName`) and week, and its sets as a few lines (`sessionLines`: "Top set 255 lb × 4 @ 6" / "Back-off 245 lb × 4 @ 6 · 2 sets", roles by `historyRoles`, the server's pl_day_set_names rule read from each card's label and notes, `rowMeta`), a tap replacing them with every set in its place (warm-ups first, quieter, `warmupsFor`; a meet's singles Opener / 2nd / 3rd attempt) and Edit sets (values as logged, the unit a relabel); ten at a time, read whole page by page; finished sets only unless "Show sets that weren't finished" is ticked. Every weight reads in the unit you're working in (the card's kg | lb, `weightView`): as typed when logged in it, else converted exactly to one decimal, never rounded to a plate, with the number as logged in brackets right after it ("330.7 lb (150 kg) × 1 @ 8"). A session's day is `trainedOn`, one rule for History and the card's Last time: the workout's day on the calendar (its scheduled instance's date, `instanceDates`, which is where a moved workout went, else the program day's), never later than the day its sets were logged, else the day it was logged. Sessions of the open workout's split are marked "Same split" on a tinted row, with an All / <workout> filter when there are both, decided from stable ids (`sameSplit`: the same day, a copy of it in its slot (`source_day_id`, even renamed), the same slot of the same block unless named apart, or the same named workout in another block of the same program; never the same weekday or the same slot of another program, and a generic "Day 1" never matches across blocks). "Progress and records" is folded under the list: the lift card (its chart reads exactly the list's sessions) and the rep records (`ExerciseRecordsPanel`: "Best weight for each rep count", All-time, then This program / This block only when they differ). The lift card's Strength tile reads hard sets only (RPE 7+, ≤ 10 reps) and says "Too early" with fewer than three such sessions, never a % from every set read as all-out, and "latest session lighter" when the newest hard session is 5%+ under the recent best; an accessory's tile is its last top set ("75 × 12", "Heaviest: 85 × 12") and its header no estimated max — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found History led with charts, estimated maxes and records before the sessions people open it for; Nicolas's Belt Squat said "Strength Up 51.9%" from a 40 lb × 8 primary set at home against 55 lb × 12 accessory sets in the gym while his latest session was lighter; and "Same day" tagged every session that was day 2 of its week in any program (Full Body B of two older programs beside Strength & Hypertrophy B). No set in 13,908 belongs to another athlete's program (checked that day); history is read by the athlete's own id. On Nov 19 2026 the coach thought his Competition Bench history had lost October: nothing was lost (his last Competition Bench was the Commonwealth meet, Sep 20; October's main bench was 1ct Paused Bench, plus 2ct paused, Spoto and Larsen, each its own exercise by design, since merging them would corrupt records and suggestions), but nothing on screen said so; the card read "Last time · Sep 21" (when the meet's attempts were typed, the next evening) while History read Sep 20, History read only the program day's own date, so 71 moved workouts across the app showed their old day (his "Taper / 4 Days Out" read Sep 14, trained Sep 16), and sessions alternated between kg and lb lines, with conversions rounded to the nearest half.
- An optional session (`pl_days.is_optional`: Elite Powerlifting's recovery day, the running programs' extra easy runs) never takes a training day from a core workout: the realign (`planCommittedRealign`, committed-schedule-realign.ts) maps core workouts to the committed days by their order among core workouts, wherever the optional one sits in the week, gives an optional one a committed day only when the person trains more days than the core workouts, else puts it on a free day of that week (spread out, kept where it is on a re-run, never in the past), and the Workouts day card says "Optional". A membership listing's days a week are the core workouts (`listingDaysPerWeek`, core-days.ts; the member plan page adds "+ N optional"), so a member picks 3 days for Distance Builder, not 5; its workout count and PDF read every block (`countPayloadWorkouts`, `payloadWeeks`). The members' library shelves running programs on their own Running shelf and category (`running` goal, facets.ts; first in `GROUP_SECTIONS`), home programs under Home Training before Muscle Building, a bare "build" is never a muscle goal for a powerlifting program, and a running program is never recommended to someone whose goal isn't running. The 15 JF Effect programs were listed for members by 20270322090000_jf_library_in_members_app.sql (insert-if-absent, applied by hand) — why: on Nov 14 2026 the coach asked for all 15 in the members' app, and a member would have had to pick 5 days for "3 days/week + 2 optional runs", Elite Powerlifting's optional Day 3 would have taken a 3-day lifter's third training day and pushed the main squat-bench-deadlift day off the calendar, every multi-block program listed "0 workouts" with an empty PDF, and the running programs would have been shelved among the lifting plans by level.
- A suggested weight is a range only where a lifter picks the weight by feel (Nov 14 2026): `suggestionPicks` (load-suggestion.ts) gives the day's first heavy set of a barbell squat, bench or deadlift (the competition lift or a barbell variation, `rangeLift` in pick-range.ts: never a dumbbell, machine, band or belt-squat lift of those families, which progress like accessories) prescribed by RPE / RIR, read from enough history (`source` "history", not `early`), on the lift's first card today with no set of it logged on another card (`firstHeavyCards`, program order; a back-off card or a variation after the lift's first card is one number whether or not the top set was rated) and nothing logged on this card, rated or not (`loggedHere`), no final warm-up and not `carried`, the loads one audible either side of the estimate (`audibleStep`: ~2.5% rounded to plates, 3.3% on average, a little under one RPE on the chart; plate steps from the target out, at most `PICK_RANGE_MAX_LOADS`, never past the model's own calibrated range): "Suggested 185–195 kg". The width is the swing a lifter feels in their warm-ups, not the model's error: cold, the middle half of real first sets runs −4% to +10% of the estimate and the model's spread sits at its 5% floor two times in three, so it can't size a range; one audible held as many real first sets as an exact ±1 RPE band (37% vs 36%). Everything else is one number: today's sets have read the day, a % of a max or a coach's fixed load never reaches the engine (`coachOwnsLoad`), accessories progress from last time, and an early estimate would claim a precision it doesn't have. A tap on Suggested logs the open sets at it like a tap on set 1's number, a range at its middle (`logOpenSetsAt` with `hint.target`, Undo on the toast; never a row of weight buttons, never drafts in the boxes; a weight picked by feel inside the range is typed in set 1, which fills the sets below), and the last warm-up ramps to the weight they mean to use (`intendedLoad`: the first open set's weight, else the target). Last time is the latest session that had the card's role (`selectPreviousLifts`: a back-off card reads its own last back-offs even when the lift's latest day had none), working sets only (warm-ups live in `pl_warmup_sets`). A coach's note on an exercise is one line that opens in place (`ExerciseNotesBlock`) (one too long for the line shows its first sentence without its stop, `noteLead`, never the line cut with "…" right after a period), a warm-up is only ever one they add with + Warm-up under the sets (the warm-up rule above; effort blank reads as RPE 7, the engine's own default; nothing about it is suggested); with Suggest on it tunes Suggested ("✓ Suggested tuned" in the warm-up header), with Suggest off (the default) a logged warm-up's weight is what set 1's tap logs (`warmupTodayLoad` into `quickLoad`), said in the warm-up header ("today 280 lb · tap set 1 to log it"); no "Use for N sets", the "film your sets" tip waits for a set that's done (`SetVideoCell done`), never a weight filled in ahead, and Focus names a repeated exercise by its role (`focusNames`: "Competition Squat · Backoff", "· Top set", the label, else its sets × reps) — why: on Nov 21 2026 the coach, logging Nicolas's Competition Squat in View as client, tapped Suggested 150–160 lb and got an empty weight box with the keyboard instead of his sets: a range that only opened the box read as broken, so it logs at its middle like a single number; on Nov 15 2026 the coach found ranges not always appearing and asked for the rule to be deliberate: a range showed on a back-off card before its top set (and after an unrated one, which never carried), on incline dumbbell presses and belt squats (bench / squat family in the library), and stayed on a card after a set was logged without an RPE; on Nov 14 2026 the coach wanted the card smarter and simpler: a single number read as false precision for a top set chosen from warm-ups (and the engine's honest ±6% range was too wide to choose from), every suggested weight had to be typed by hand, the italic notes paragraph took two or three lines, the orange warm-up row competed with the sets, and "Next: Competition Squat" showed while the athlete was already on Competition Squat.
- Strength is read one way on every athlete screen (Nov 15 2026, strength-read.ts): an estimated max is compared session to session only from sets that can show one (`comparableE1rm`: RPE 7+ / RIR ≤ 3, ≤ 10 reps, `estimate1RMWithRpe`, Program Intelligence's formula and athlete-development's `INFORMATIVE_RPE`), so a light day, a technique single or an unrated back-off is never read as strength lost. Each lift session carries `strength` beside the old every-set `e1rm` (`groupLiftSessions`); the lift card (`LiftProgressCard`, the Analytics page and History & progress) plots "Est. max" from it once 3+ sessions have one (else every set, said to be a floor), says how many lighter sessions aren't plotted (`data-est-note`), shows whole numbers for an estimate and the heaviest weight actually lifted beside it (`data-lift-bests`), opens on Top set for anything that isn't a squat / bench / deadlift (or a variation), and its Strength tile is `strengthTrend`: the best of the first few comparable sessions against the best of the last few, steady under `STEADY_PCT` (2%), "Too early" under `TREND_MIN_SESSIONS` / `TREND_MIN_DAYS`, never a line fitted through every set. Performance insights read the same max and trend ("Squat getting stronger", never the window's first week against its last). Athletes read "all-time PR", never "ATPR" (set chips, record badges, the recap; the coach's screens and the League keep their words), a day in an exercise's History is one line of coach shorthand that opens to its sets (`data-history-day-summary`) with its date first, and the suggestion's ⓘ is two lines with "How it's worked out" folded (`data-testid="load-why"`). The bodyweight adjustment stays the backtested allometric one (`BODYWEIGHT_EXPONENT` 0.67 on the 7-day average, ignored past ±12%), said in the ⓘ as about two thirds of the change; Coach Intel's PR alerts and the block summaries still read every set (left alone: changing them would stop clients who don't log RPE from ever getting one) — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found the strength charts jumping between sessions; on 180 days of real competition-lift logs the every-set estimate moved a median 10% session to session (1 in 10 moves over 39%), comparable sets a median 6%, and the worst of what's left is light sets rated hard and kg / lb slips, which no formula fixes, so the trend reads the best sessions rather than the average one.
- Missing program data is one reading (program-gaps.ts, pure, tested): `program_gaps()` (20270325091000_program_gaps.sql, SECURITY DEFINER: an admin's every coaching client, a coach's `coached_client_ids()`, the service role as an admin) returns the rows that might be missing something on live programs (visible, dated, not Completed / Archived, never the at-home backup blocks) and only on workouts nothing has touched (`pl_day_touched` on the client's own day, `pt_local_today`), plus workouts with no date while their week isn't over, days with a workout from two live programs (`doubled`: a replaced program never ended), training days and active maxes; `buildProgramGaps` decides with the builder's own `rowProblems` (a timed row needs only its time; otherwise an RPE, RIR, a weight or a % the app can read, and a "manual" % with no weight and nothing else is "what its % is of") and the logger's own `computeRowLoad` (a % of a training max or 1RM whose lift has no max on file for that block: the athlete sees no weight; a % of e1RM falls back to their logged e1RM and isn't flagged). One query (`useProgramGaps`, key `program-gaps`, refreshed by `invalidateScheduleQueries`) feeds Messages' Program data filter (amber, `FILTER_HELP.Program`), a chat row's line (`signalTags` key `program`), the Brief ("Missing program data", opening the page on that client), the Training tab's alerts (prescriptions and maxes only: dates, overlaps and training days are its own) and /admin/program-gaps (every client, the soonest first: Open = the block editor on that week through `focusBlockEditorWeek`; Fill in with AI = Edit with AI with `fillInstruction` in the box (under its 4,000 characters), reviewed before anything applies; Add maxes / Set days = Athlete setup; Use estimated max = `program_gaps_use_estimated_max` (the coach's own client, untouched workouts, each row backed up: the sets read `est_1rm`, keep their %, and get `rpeCapFor`'s cap, the chart's RPE + 1.5 within 5–9, when they have no RPE or RIR); Place them / Review = the Training tab). Nothing waits on the coach to look: pg_cron `program-gaps-tick` (8:07 Winnipeg, 20270325093000_program_gaps_alerts.sql, program-gaps.server.ts) pushes `program_gaps_due` to the coach and admins for each client with a gap on a workout in the next `ALERT_DAYS` (3), once a client a day (`gapsToAlert`), and Generate Next Program's audit fails (repairable) any set with no effort target that isn't timed. Effective volume reads the library's own fields when an exercise has no variation type (`variationTypeOf`) — why: Vicky Tshibasu's peak (from Nov 16 2026) had back-offs, accessories and taper singles with no RPE, RIR or weight ("12% below" and "55–60%" only in the notes, "manual" % rows with no weight typed), Alyssa Burg's meet prep was a % of a 1RM nobody had entered and a program replaced in September still put a second workout on her days, every program read "0 effective" sets, the builder flagged every treadmill walk, the generator only marked a set with no effort target "needs review", and the coach only found out from screenshots.
- Coach calendars say who and when at a glance (Nov 15 2026): a month cell's chip is the start time and the person ("10a Nicolas G.", `chipParts`, calendar-labels.ts, pure, tested; the item's title only when it isn't with someone, and never the "Google Calendar" source label as a name), with the full name, time and length, what, where and status on hover (`itemDetailLines`, a neutral popover, not the red tooltip); day and list rows lead with the person ("Colten Murray" / "12–1 PM · 1:1 Training · 800 Vaughan Ave", `rowLines`). Month cells are top-aligned columns. The Calendar page's view, the Home card's view (Day · Week · Month, each stepping with ‹ › and Today, `home-calendar.ts`) and Coaching / + Google are one remembered choice per account (`useCalendarPrefs`, table `calendar_view_prefs`, own row only; a device copy for the first paint, the newer copy wins), never a `localStorage` key of their own — why: a month of identical "1:1 Training" chips said nothing about who or when, the coach re-picked Week and Google on every visit and device, and the dashboard card was stuck on one view.
- The Food Log (`DailyNutritionPanel`: the client's Log Food / Today's intake sheet and inline on a member's Nutrition page) reads the person's own calendar day, never a UTC one: the phone sends its local midnight-to-midnight (`localDayBounds`, `weekStartFor`, food-log.ts) as `dayStart` / `dayEnd` / `weekStart`, `getNutritionDashboard` / `undoSupplementLog` use them when they're a plausible day (`plausibleDay`), else the old UTC day for older builds, and the week is bucketed by local date (`weekDays`). Every food-log surface reads one query (`useFoodLogDay`, food-log-context.tsx) and logs through `useLogFood` (toast with Undo). A coaching client's targets are the coach's plan day for that date (`FoodLogPlanProvider` on the client Nutrition page: `targetFor`, the same `resolvePlanDaySelection` as the page, today's the day on screen), their plan's meals log in one tap (`mealsFor` → `planMealsFromNotes`, food-log-plan.ts, only meals whose P / C / F the plan states) and the coach's supplements are one-tap adds to the daily checklist; a member's targets are still their own (`targetFromDashboard`). One red action (Log food), a logged meal opens to edit (`updateMeal`), a delete has Undo, supplements tick off for today only, Quick add works calories out from the macros when left blank (`quickAddCalories`). The Meal Plan PDF tile hands the file to the share sheet (`mealPlanPdfFile` + `saveFiles`, a "ready · Save" toast when the phone needs a second tap; the coach's `downloadMealPlanPdf` is unchanged), and Macro Calculator shows only for a client with no coach plan (`showCalculator`) — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found the Food Log incomplete: every evening meal (after 7 PM in Winnipeg) was filed under tomorrow and "today" flipped to tomorrow, coaching clients saw "Set up your nutrition targets" and an empty week chart beside a coach plan, the 7-day chart ignored the date picked, supplements had a red + per row, nobody had ever logged a meal (0 rows), `doc.save()` does nothing in the iPhone app, and a self-calculated estimate beside a coach's plan only invites second-guessing it.
- The Cookbook is a short curated list defined in code (`src/lib/cookbook`: `foods.ts` per-100 g values, `library.ts` 32 recipes with ingredients, steps, a cutting and a bulking line; every number worked out from the ingredients, `recipeMacros`, never typed) and written to `recipes` by a generated migration (`sql.ts` → 20270326091000_cookbook_bodybuilding_library.sql, `npx tsx scripts/cookbook/write-migration.ts`; cookbook-library.test.ts fails when they disagree): 31 audited recipes rewritten in place (same id, views kept), 1 new, the other 128 archived, every audited row backed up first in `recipes_cookbook_backup_20261115`, each step only on a recipe still exactly as audited (`audit-2026-11.ts` md5s), nothing deleted. The bar (tested): ≤ 10 min hands-on (meal prep ≤ 15, 4+ servings), ≤ 5 counted ingredients and ≤ 4 steps, 25 g+ protein a meal (15 g+ a snack or sweet), calories within 10% of 4 / 4 / 9, Health Canada temperatures. The Cookbook loads every published recipe once (`listCookbookRecipes`) and filters on the phone (`filterCookbook`, view.ts: meal type, Cutting / Bulking from the numbers, `goalFit`, search over titles and ingredients); a recipe opens inside the sheet (`CookbookRecipeView`: numbers per serving, Log a serving, ingredients, steps, Make it fit your goal, tips) and Back returns to the list where it was; `/portal/recipes` redirects to Nutrition with `?cookbook=1`, `?recipe=<id>` opens one, and a shared link (`/portal/recipes/$recipeId`) shows the same view. The recipe disclaimer (`RECIPE_DISCLAIMER`, nutrition-disclaimer.ts: estimates and labels, allergens and diets, food safety, not medical advice) is one line that opens in place under the list and every recipe; the Nutrition disclaimer is unchanged — why: on Nov 15 2026 the coach found the cookbook had too many recipes and too many complicated ones: 159 published (21 zero-calorie mocktails, a dozen 1,000+ kcal "cheat meals" with 7–10 ingredients, placeholder prep times, a 200 g-protein taco bowl, the same idea three or four times), behind three rows of filters, opened as a page that left Nutrition and came back to an old recipe list; 10 people had ever opened one.
- The coach's forms are one library (form-library.ts, form-library.queries.ts; 20270401090000_form_library.sql): a form belongs to the login that made it (`nf_forms.owner_id`, set on insert by `tg_nf_forms_owner`; null = the organization's, everything made before Nov 17 2026), a template (`is_template`) is the organization's starting point and never archives itself for going unused, and a coach shares one with colleagues with `shared`. What a coach sees and edits is `nf_staff_form_ids()` (their own, the organization's, colleagues' shared ones; an admin all) and `nf_editable_form_ids()` (their own; an admin all), read once per query; a coach makes and edits only their own forms and can never broadcast one to every client (`visibility = 'selected'`, `auto_assign_new_clients = false` in the policy), versions follow `nf_can_edit_form`, and clients see a form only through an assignment or a broadcast exactly as before. `category` is the coach's own words (`CATEGORY_SUGGESTIONS` only offered), favorites are `nf_form_favorites` (own rows; a starred form never auto-archives), Recently sent is `nf_assignments.last_sent_by` / `last_sent_at`, and the library's counts are one call (`nf_form_library_stats`: questions, sent, answered, waiting), never two queries per row. Send Form in a chat (`FormPickerSheet`, the + menu) opens on Suggested (`formSuggestions`: the meet checklist inside 4 weeks of a meet it wasn't sent for, the competition plan for someone who competes and has none, onboarding for a lead; never sent by itself), then favorites, then recent; search reads title, category and description; a form is made (blank, `draftFormWithAiFn` on route `form_draft` read by `parseFormDraft`, or a copy) or copied for that client in place over the chat, and a card sent from the + menu goes `standalone` (never an internal note or a reply, `message_type` Form) so the coach's typed message stays in the box. Sending is `sendNativeFormFn` (the form read as the sender, so only one in their library; a draft is switched on; the assignment keeps what it was sent with in `settings`), and saving which forms a client can open changes only the rows that changed (a kept form keeps its "asked" time and settings). A copied form's rules point at its own questions (`remapQuestionRefs`). The client profile's Forms card (`ClientFormsOverview`) and Forms → a form's Responses read the same assignments and submissions as the chat card — why: forms were one admin's list with no owner, search, favorites or categories, the picker was a plain list that wiped whatever the coach was typing and could send a form as an internal note, saving a client's form list deleted and re-added every assignment (resetting when each was asked for), and a copied form's follow-ups still pointed at the original's questions.
- A checklist question (`question_type = "checklist"`, form-checklist.ts) is a list of items the athlete marks Ready, Still need or N/A, each item `{ key, label, help, priority: critical | important | optional, show_if }` in `validation.items` (edited in the builder, `ChecklistItemsEditor`), its answer `[{ key, label, state }]`; an unmarked item is "not confirmed", never missing, a required checklist needs its critical and important items marked, and only N/A carries to a new copy (`carriedChecklist`: packed last meet isn't packed this one). Readiness is worked out from the answers every time, never stored (`readiness`: an item marked Still need or a choice in `validation.flag_options` is an issue by its area, `validation.area`, named in the form's `settings.readiness.areas`; critical first; the verdict is never "ready" while a critical or important one is open, and no percentage stands in for it). A form with `allow_updates` stays open after it's sent: the athlete keeps changing it (a nudge re-send is the same copy until it's sent for a different meet or the last is 45+ days old, `settings.new_copy_since`), every change after sending is kept by `tg_nf_answers_history` in `nf_answer_history` (staff read) with `nf_submissions.answers_updated_at`, and the chat card, which refreshes live (`nf_submissions` is in the realtime publication), says where it stands. The Meet Day — Final Preparation Checklist (`meet_checklist`, meet-checklist-template.ts, written into 20270401091000_meet_day_checklist.sql by scripts/forms/write-meet-checklist-migration.ts, compared in meet-checklist.test.ts; fixed ids, insert-if-absent, so the coach's builder edits stay his) keeps every item of the old Google Form, reads its meet, weight and handler by `validation.meet_key`, prefills the meet from what the athlete can see (`pickMeet`: the one the coach sent it with, their next visible meet prep, their own Competition Calendar answers; `athlete_competition_plans` is staff-only) and the morning weigh-in only when it's today's or yesterday's (`bodyweightSuggestion`, lb or kg relabelled, never converted), flags a bodyweight over the class limit within two weeks of the meet for the coach (`weightCheck`: critical over 2% inside a week) and never works out a cut, and reminds the athlete by push only (`meet-checklist-reminders`, at most three a meet, off per send or per form). The coach sees it on the chat card, the client's Forms card, the form's Responses and the dashboard's Upcoming meets card (hidden when nobody has a meet coming) — why: the coach's Google Form was one long checkbox list where an unchecked item read the same as a missing one, a missing photo ID looked like a missing pair of headphones, an athlete couldn't change it after sending, and the coach had no way to see who wasn't ready.
