- Security boundaries (Nov 17 2026 audit, docs/SECURITY_AUDIT_2026-11.md, 20270423090000_security_hardening.sql, security-hardening.test.ts): anyone can sign up free, so "signed in" is never a trust boundary, and a server function that uses `supabaseAdmin` checks the caller itself (a role helper, or a read through `context.supabase` that RLS answers) before any service-role read, write or paid call; a write through the caller's own client that may match nothing (RLS) is `.select()`ed and checked before its side effects run. Password recovery finds the login first, exactly (`auth_user_id_for_email`, `lookupRecoveryTarget` in account-recovery-lookup.server.ts), texts only a phone saved on that login's own records, never one from a public application record no coach has taken on (`recoveryPhoneTrusted`: `applied_at` and no `assigned_coach_id`, since that record is linked to whoever later joins with its email), and never texts a team login (any role but client; `STAFF_ROLES`); a sign-in or reset link texted by staff (`sendAuthLinkBySms`) is only for the record's own login, by that login's own email, never a team login; a public form never writes contact details onto an existing record (`upsertApplicantClient`); setup / reset links refuse every team role. Public, signed-out functions that cost money or reach people (the coaching application and its drafts, online booking) are limited per connection through `rateOk` / `allRatesOk` (public-rate-limit.server.ts, `_challenge_rate_ok`, keyed on `cf-connecting-ip`, failing open). Worker and job SQL functions are the service role's only (REVOKE from PUBLIC, anon, authenticated); a SECURITY DEFINER function a signed-in user may call checks `auth.uid()` itself. Legal acceptance records are written only by the server, stamped by `trg_legal_acceptance_stamp` (the published text, the time) and never changed or deleted except with their login (`trg_legal_acceptance_keep`); a version once published keeps its words. AI prompts and the coach's methods live in `*.server.ts` modules (the nutrition prompts: nutrition-ai-prompts.server.ts; screens use nutrition-request.ts and ask `getManualNutritionPromptFn` for a finished prompt, copied in the tap with `copyWhenReady`), because every client chunk, admin ones included, is downloadable by anyone. A security migration that changes policies on busy tables sets a short `lock_timeout`, and its section-by-section rollback lives outside supabase/migrations (docs/rollback/). Pages carry `frame-ancestors` and a Permissions-Policy (`withPageSecurityHeaders`, security-headers.ts, from src/server.ts). The retired paid checkout stays off (`PAID_SIGNUP_ENABLED`) — why: an application with the admin's email and someone else's phone was enough to have the admin's reset link texted to them; the retired checkout handed out a sign-in link for any existing email; any free account could claim and read due scheduled coach messages, read every client's account photo and the athlete records' coach notes, and forge its own legal acceptances; and the nutrition AI's full prompts shipped to every coaching client's phone.
- Two-step sign-in (Nov 20 2026, mfa-rules.ts pure and tested, 20270424091500_staff_mfa.sql; texts, passkeys and trusted devices Nov 23 2026, two-step.ts, webauthn.ts, two-step.functions.ts, 20270512090000_two_step_sms_passkeys.sql): a team login (everyone but a client or member, `isTeamRole`) that has any way to confirm (an authenticator app in `auth.mfa_factors`, a phone number confirmed by a code or a passkey in `two_step_methods`) holds its staff rights only in a confirmed session: aal2 after an authenticator code, or a session the server marked in `two_step_sessions` after a texted code, a passkey or a trusted device, counted only while its `auth.sessions` row exists (`two_step_session_live`), so signing out ends it at once. Checked in three places that must stay in step: the app (`StaffMfaGate`: a client or member passes with no request; a team login asks `getTwoStepStatus`, except a session this device confirmed already, `wasConfirmedHere`, which opens at once while the server is asked in the background; the admin, finance and coach logins, `MFA_REQUIRED_ROLES`, set one up on first open), every server function (`staffMfaGuard` in src/start.ts → `checkStaffMfa`, mfa-guard.server.ts: aal2, else `mfa_enforced_logins()` read once a minute and `two_step_session_live` for the token's `session_id`, a confirmed answer remembered a minute, an unknown one refused) and the database (`caller_mfa_ok` inside `has_role` for every role but client, `has_permission`, `is_admin_viewer` and `is_business_owner`: aal2, or no way set up, or the token's session marked and live, only for the caller's own session and only after the role is found). A new function every staff rule goes through gets the same `caller_mfa_ok` check, and a new route that takes a user's token itself checks `checkStaffMfa`; the server functions that confirm a session (`MFA_EXEMPT_FNS`: the status, `sendTwoStepCode`, `verifyTwoStepCode`, `startTwoStepPasskey`, `finishTwoStepPasskey`, `confirmWithTrustedDevice`) are callable before it's confirmed, so each acts only for the signed-in team login, adds a way to an unconfirmed session only when the login has none yet (its first setup), and has its name once in the app (the guard goes by name). Supabase's own phone and WebAuthn factors are switched off in this project and can't be configured from the app, which is why texts and passkeys are ours. The ways, asked in the order they chose (`two_step_settings.preferred`, else Face ID, a text, the app; `orderMethods`): a passkey (Face ID / Touch ID / Windows Hello / a screen lock, verified on the server with no library: CBOR, authenticator data, ES256 or RS256 only, the rp id's hash, user verification required, a one-time challenge for that session spent before it's checked, the counter never going back; jfeffect.com and www share one rp id, `rpIdForOrigin`; the challenge is fetched before the tap so the tap opens Face ID at once; never in the iPhone or Android app, whose web views aren't tied to jfeffect.com, `passkeysPossibleHere`), a 6-digit code texted through the one SMS sender as an account-security text (`category: "security"`, the code never in the log, one segment, ending `@jfeffect.com #123456` so phones autofill it only on our site, Android reading it in by WebOTP; +1 numbers only, up to 3; hashed per login and session, 10 minutes, a resend after 30 s, 5 an hour and 10 a day per login on top of the sender's 5 security texts a day per phone, 5 wrong tries kill a code and 10 wrong in an hour lock sending and checking, `two_step_code_issue` / `two_step_code_check` under a per-login lock) and the authenticator app (Supabase's, as before; a verified code also marks the session, `confirmTwoStepTotp`, so removing the app later never unconfirms it). "Don't ask again on this device" (ticked to start on a phone or the installed app, off on a computer) trusts the device for a fixed 30 days (`two_step_trusted_devices`: only a token's hash, 10 per login, the oldest go); its token lives on the device (`jf-2step-device:<login>`, kept across sign-out, which is the point) and only ever skips the second step of a sign-in whose password was right. Two-step answers are never written to the phone's saved query cache (`two-step-` in `DO_NOT_PERSIST_PREFIXES`). A login that can only confirm here with the authenticator app (no phone, no passkey this device can use, `needsFasterWay`) is offered Face ID or a text right after its code (`FasterWayScreen`, "Skip the code next time": the one added becomes the way asked first; "Not now" keeps it away on that device for `FASTER_OFFER_SNOOZE_DAYS`), and the code screen says so while it waits; removing a way that would leave only passkeys warns that the apps can't use them (`leavesOnlyPasskeys`). Account → Two-step sign-in (`TwoStepCard`) lists the ways (which is asked first, add one, remove one: never the last while the role needs one, and removing an app first marks the session), and the trusted devices (Forget, Forget all, Trust this device); adding or removing a way pushes the person; a login that needs two-step goes straight in once its first way is set up. An emailed code is the backup, never a way on its own (20270515090000_two_step_email_backup.sql, `two_step_email_backup_state` / `_set`, `emailCode` in two-step.functions.ts): a 6-digit code to the login's own email (the `two-step-code` template, through the transactional queue, never logged), offered last under "Other ways to confirm" and first only when nothing else works on that device (Face ID only, in the iPhone app), sign-in only, on the same table, limits and lock as texted codes (one budget, wrong tries counted together); it never counts toward enforcement (`two_step_login_has` unchanged), goes only to the address saved when it was turned on (`two_step_settings.email_backup`) and only while that's still the login's email, and waits 3 days after a password reset or sign-in link went to that inbox (`auth.users.recovery_sent_at`) or a change of email was asked for (`email_change_sent_at`, a pending `email_change`), so someone holding the inbox can't reset the password and confirm with it too; the database picks the address (`two_step_code_issue` with `_channel` 'email' answers with it), never the request. It's on by default, set from a confirmed session the first time a login has a way (`emailBackupOf(…, { ensure })`, `_only_if_unset`; the migration turned it on for logins that had one), then the person's choice (Account → Two-step sign-in's "Email me a code" switch, `setTwoStepEmailBackup`, a push when turned on); a sign-in confirmed by email pushes "You signed in with an emailed code". There are no recovery codes (20270513100000_no_recovery_codes.sql deleted the saved ones and the functions that made and took them): a lost phone is an emailed code, another way on another device (a passkey syncs to their other Apple devices, a trusted computer, the authenticator on a second device), the same number on a new phone, or the owner's Reset two-step on Team for a teammate (`resetTeammateTwoStep`, mfa-recovery.functions.ts: admin with a confirmed session, never their own; removes the authenticator apps, phone numbers, passkeys, trusted devices and confirmed sessions, `staff_mfa_reset_record`); a reset pushes the person and the owner, `staff_mfa_events` keeps the record, and the owner's last resort is deleting their rows from `auth.mfa_factors` and `two_step_methods`. Setup that can't start never locks anyone out (Continue for now: nothing is enforced until a way exists); every table is the server's only; rollback in docs/rollback/2026-11-23-two-step-sms-passkeys-rollback.sql (back to authenticator apps only) and docs/rollback/2026-11-20-staff-mfa-rollback.sql — why: the admin and finance logins can read every client's records and the business's money, so a password alone (phished, reused, or reset through a hole like Nov 17's) must not be enough; the finance login had a code step until Oct 9 2026, removed at the owner's request for asking after every sign-in, and on Nov 17 2026 the owner asked for it back on both logins; a check in the app alone is a screen, not a lock, since ~70 server functions check admin themselves and then act as the server; and on Nov 23 2026 the owner found typing an authenticator code at every sign-in unnecessary friction and asked for a texted code, Face ID / Touch ID and trusted devices, with the authenticator kept optional, stronger protection kept for coaches and admins, and nobody locked out (the finance login, required since Nov 20, still had no authenticator); that day, once it was live, he signed in and got the authenticator screen again with nothing saying a faster way was one step away; and he found the recovery codes (ten codes to save for a lost phone) one more thing to keep track of when a password, texts, Face ID, the authenticator and trusted devices are enough, and asked for an emailed code as the backup instead).
- A program editor's ← goes back to where the coach came from, never to a fixed page (Nov 17 2026, editor-back.ts): the block editor (`/admin/blocks/$blockId`) and the template editor save anything unsaved first (`useEditorBack` `beforeLeave`: a failed save keeps the coach there with the error), then go back through the app's own history (`router.history.canGoBack()`), and only an editor opened cold falls back (a client's block to their Training tab, `clientTrainingHref`; a template to the library). The client profile overlay keeps its tab in the address (`clientTab`, replaced, `useSetOverlayClientTab`) and its scroll (`data-scroll-restoration-id`), so going back lands on the tab the coach was on, and the block switcher replaces the editor in history (`replace: true`) so ← still leaves the editor — why: the ← was a link to the client profile, which opened on Summary whatever tab the coach had come from, and the overlay never put its tab in the address, so even the browser's back lost it.
- The program editor has a Sheet view on a computer (Week · Whole block · Sheet; `ProgramSheet`, program-editor/program-sheet.tsx; what a cell means is program-sheet.ts, pure, tested): a week's exercises as rows of cells (sets, reps or a timed row's time, RPE, RIR, load, tempo, rest, notes) or one field across every week (Across weeks), edited like Google Sheets: a click selects (a drag or Shift+arrows select a range), typing replaces, Enter / Tab move, Esc puts a cell back, Cmd/Ctrl+Enter fills the selection, Cmd/Ctrl+D fills down, Delete clears, Cmd/Ctrl+C / V copy and paste blocks to and from Sheets or Excel, Fill right / a Progression (a start and a step a week, effort capped at 10) across weeks. It writes the same row fields the cards do through the editor's own `setWeeksData` (one undo step, autosaved, the cards show it at once), reads a load as the card editor's basis (`parseLoad`: "75% TM", "80% 1RM", "92.5% top", "100 kg", blank = none; a client's block shows "= 150 kg" from their max, `computeRowLoad`), refuses a value it can't read with a toast and the old value back (never a guess), and Cmd/Ctrl+Z in a cell nobody is typing in is the program's undo. A phone never gets it (one week of cards). The block editor saves `purpose_label`, `movement_family` and `rest_seconds_override` (they were missing from its `ROW_FIELDS`, so a label or a rest set by hand was dropped on save) — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach found editing a whole program a fight of opening and closing cards, and wanted it to feel like the spreadsheet a powerlifting coach programs in.
- Training load is one deterministic read (training-load.ts, pure, tested; `buildTrainingLoad`) for the analytics page, the Workouts card and the AI: working sets (warm-ups never; conditioning and stretches out), hard sets (RPE 7+, unrated counted apart), sets credited per muscle (primary 1, secondary ½, once per muscle per set, `MUSCLE_CREDIT`, finer muscles than the athlete's 13: delts by head, upper back apart from erectors), movement patterns (the library classifier, `patternOf`), the competition lifts (sessions, sets, heavy sessions, competition vs variation, relative intensity as % of the best estimated max in the range, rep and RPE spread, the strength trend from strength-read.ts and volume change), the latest week day by day, prescribed vs completed vs missed vs extra from the workouts dated in the range (`loadPlanRows`, the viewer's own login; a plan that can't be read leaves those numbers out, never the rest), and at most 5 insights that say what's happening, the evidence, confidence, a possible reason (a hypothesis), what to do and whether anything needs to change (often nothing), coach-only ones apart (`audience`). The analytics page is one dashboard for both (`ClientAnalyticsDashboard`; the coach's `/admin/client-programs/$clientId/analytics` and the client profile pass `coach`): Overview · Strength · Volume · Recovery (`?view=`, a `#recovery` link opens Recovery), the insights first on Overview ("Coaching insights" / "What stands out"), squat · bench · deadlift sections only for someone who trains them; the Workouts card's header is the way in, with 8 weeks of working sets and the one insight that stands out. Generate Next Program, Edit with AI and the interpret job read the same read over 12 weeks (`trainingLoadFromRecords`, the brief's "Training load" section) with `TRAINING_LOAD_RULES`: a flag is context, never an instruction; never more work because they progress or less for one hard session; redistribute before adding — why: on Nov 17 2026 the coach asked for analytics that lead to programming decisions (how much stress, where it goes, whether it's working) instead of more numbers, the same for coach and client, with the AI planning from it; the coach's analytics page was an older, separate set of charts, and the "Sets in range" tile claimed to include warm-ups and leave bodyweight sets out while it counted every logged set.
- Coach-side writing runs on the routing table too (Nov 18 2026, `coach_write`: GPT-5 mini, then GPT-5.5, on the OpenAI key, through `askRoute`, src/lib/ai/route-call.server.ts, the admin's `ai_routes` override honoured): the weekly check-in recap and suggested reply, AI drafts of a form reply, a lead's first text, the inbox Brief and My Voice's Try it, each recording the model that answered; nothing here calls the Lovable gateway by default any more, and the picture jobs never fall back to it by themselves (`{ lovable: false }`). The AIs that touch one client read one set of rules and one computed context: the calorie step is `MAX_STEP_KCAL` (the nutrition targets prompt and Cleo, which reads the weight-management read in its nutrition look-up and shows `coachChecks` on its nutrition cards), the training-day walk is the treadmill caps (`TRAIN_MINUTES_MAX` / `_PL`, a quarter of a booked PT session, which the nutrition AI's athlete read now names), an e1RM comes only from RPE 7+ sets of 10 reps or fewer (`COMPARABLE_*`, the check-in training readout included), one hard session is one session's read (check-in replies, like DEVELOPMENT_RULES), Cleo's loads, notes and session flow follow the generator's rules, the training brief carries the nutrition plan they're on (`nutritionNowLine`: a cut holds volume, a gain can carry more) and the interpret job the health profile. Cleo's own model chain and Edit with AI's GPT-5-mini fallback are unchanged on purpose (docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md) — why: on Nov 18 2026 the coach asked for the most capable, reliable and cost-efficient model per task with consistent context and no conflicting recommendations; an audit found five coach-side writers on Lovable credits with one Gemini Flash call and no fallback, the nutrition AI allowing a 300 kcal step and 25-min walks while the weight-management engine capped 250 kcal and 20 min, check-in replies reading one grindy top set as a reason to lower next week, two e1RM rules (8 vs 10 reps), Cleo free to set calories that didn't add up from their macros, and the training AI blind to a planned cut.
- Every exercise search is one engine (Nov 20 2026, src/lib/exercise-search.ts, pure; exercise-search-real-library.test.ts runs it on a snapshot of the real library, src/test/fixtures/exercise-library-2026-11.json): the logger's Add and Swap sheets, both program builders, the inline editor, the library pages, the challenge picker, Cleo's library lookup and Edit with AI's name resolution (`searchableLibrary`, exercise-search-library.server.ts), never a screen's own `ilike` or word match. The query and every name are read as the same canonical words (plurals singular, "pull-ups" = "pullups" = "pull ups", "DB" = dumbbell, "laying" = lying, `WORD_CANON` / `COMPOUNDS`), equivalent names are one term (`EQUIVALENT_NAMES`: "RDL" = Romanian deadlift, "side raise" = lateral raise; an abbreviation means only its phrase, never everything sharing a word), a muscle, equipment or movement word can be met by the exercise's own tags (`muscle_groups`, `equipment`, `primary_movement_pattern`, `exercise_family`, and its name), a half-typed word or a slip is read only when the library has no word spelled that way (`buildLookups`: "spoto" is never "spot"), and grip / attachment words are required only when the library uses them. Ranking: the exact name, then the same words in any order, then everything in the name ranked by how much of the name the query explains (each name word it doesn't say costs, so "lateral raise cable" puts Cable Lateral Raises before "Lateral Raise To Shoulder Press Resistance Band Cable"), then matches through tags; whenever anything matches every word, partial matches are dropped; camera-angle copies ("Left", "Side View") and stretches come after the exercise itself unless asked for; an exercise the library doesn't have offers `RELATED_NAMES` alternatives, said to be alternatives (`alternative`, "Similar to …"), never as the same exercise; a picker's `boost` (the athlete's recent exercises, the coach's recent and favourites) only breaks ties inside a tier. A description ("that back exercise where you pull a cable toward your waist", `isDescriptiveQuery`) drops its filler, turns its cues into movements (`DESCRIPTION_RULES`: "toward your waist" = a row), and makes its postures and muscles refine rather than be required. Pickers use `useExerciseSearch` (or `useDescribedExercises` beside their own search) and show the AI's read with `DescribedMatches` in their own row; the placeholder says "Search or describe an exercise". The AI (exercise-identify: route `exercise_identify`, GPT-5 mini then GPT-5.5 on the OpenAI key, never Lovable credits; prompt in exercise-identify.server.ts) is asked only for a description or a few words nothing matches by name (`shouldAskAi`), once typing pauses (`DESCRIBE_PAUSE_MS`), once per description in a session (and per server instance while it runs), at GPT-5's minimal effort (`effortFor`: "low" on a model without it; at "low" it took 6-10 s in production), and only names exercises: the app finds those names in the picker's own pool (`matchGuesses`), so it can only suggest an existing exercise the person may pick, never creates one, never picks for them, and says "Likely match" (the AI sure and the library has that exact exercise) at most, else "Possible match". It isn't metered with AI credits (searching shouldn't cost a credit; a fraction of a cent a call) and is held to 30 per login per 10 minutes and 150 a day (`_challenge_rate_ok`) — why: on Nov 20 2026 the coach asked for exercise search that finds the right exercise whatever the word order, terminology, shorthand or spelling, or a description, quickly and without clutter; the old matcher ranked "Lateral Raise To Shoulder Press Resistance Band Cable" first for "lateral raise cable", made every deadlift a full match for "RDL", read "side delt cable", "latteral rase" and descriptions poorly, and kept loosely related partial matches under every answer, and three pickers read the library in one request the server cuts at 1,000 rows.
- A nutrition goal becomes a periodized roadmap (Nov 22 2026, src/lib/nutrition-roadmap/, pure and tested in nutrition-roadmap.test.ts on the 12 scenarios it has to get right; 20270501090000_nutrition_roadmaps.sql): one deterministic engine for coached and self-coached athletes, built on the weight-management read (`buildAssessment`: the trajectory's least-squares trend and its scatter, `assessTarget`'s rates by sport and goal, `decide`'s reasons a scale number lies), never a second trend or rate table. The goal is read from what's on file (`roadmapGoal`: a meet's class or a show first (a meet prep with no class takes the competition profile's, `athlete_competition_profiles.weight_class`), then `clients.bodyweight_goal_*`, else the nutrition plan's phase with no target, said under `missing`); a meet with no class at all is still planned around (`phaseMeet`: the phase until a 3-week fueled peak, meet week ending on the meet, recovery, transition, "confirm the weight class"). Energy (energy.ts): Mifflin–St Jeor × an activity factor from training days and steps, replaced by `observedMaintenance` (logged food, or the plan at 4/5+ adherence, minus 7,700 kcal a kg of trend) as soon as there is any, the band and confidence said, never a body-fat, lean-mass or metabolic number; the floor is resting energy or 25% under maintenance, whichever is higher (no fixed calorie floor); protein 1.8–2.4 g/kg on an adjusted weight past a BMI of 30, fat ≥ 20–25% and ≥ 0.6 g/kg, carbs the rest with a 2 g/kg floor for training, a training/rest carb split only for 3–5 training days that keeps the week's average and never takes a rest day under the floor. Feasibility (feasibility.ts) is realistic / aggressive / unlikely / unsafe (or needs weigh-ins when the last one is 10+ days old: drawn from it, judged on nothing, never "reached") from the rate it needs against the comfortable and limit rates, whether the deficit can be eaten above the floor, and their own trend's projection ("Their current trajectory projects 70.2–71.6 kg by …"), with confidence in words (never a probability), the trade-offs and options that work (a later date, a reachable target, the next IPF class up, the total first); a class is made by the start of a fueled peak (`fueledDaysBeforeEvent`). Blocks (blocks.ts `planSegments` → `planBlocks`) are decided, not templated: a baseline only with no trend and room for it; a far-off meet with a small gap fuels training first and cuts late at a gentle pace (`maxKgPerWeek`, never past the limit or the floor), then a fueled strength peak, meet week ending on the meet (the federation's 2 h / 24 h weigh-in said, `WEIGH_IN_RULE`: never dehydration, saunas, diuretics, water or sodium manipulation), post-competition recovery and a transition (to the next meet when there is one); a contest prep uses all its time unless that's under 0.4%/week, slows in its last 8 weeks with protein up, holds show week, then steps back to maintenance over 4 weeks; long diets get a 1-week diet break every 10 weeks when the rate stays comfortable; months of surplus stabilize; ≤ 12 weeks a block; every block has dates, objective, weights, energy (the plan they're on for the current block when it's a plan for that phase, the engine's own as `engine`), rate, priorities, 2-weekly checkpoints, move-on criteria, keep / change / extend / end rules and why. Only the current block is final; the rest is a forecast. Progress (progress.ts) is ahead / on track / behind / insufficient data / needs attention against the planned path (`expectedAt`), a weigh-in off the line named as water, never a reason. Decisions (adjust.ts `decideRoadmap`) in order: health (escalate, food back toward maintenance, never more restriction), data, the weight-management reasons (digestion, adherence, recovery, workload, target), a rate that costs them, a new kind of block starting or a plan for another phase whose trend isn't already doing what the block asks (the plan they're on for this very block is never moved to an estimate: the trend judges it, and a coached athlete with no plan numbers gets a hold showing the block's targets, never a start from the formula, which only a self-coached member is offered), then against the block (on track or ahead → hold; behind with < 4/5 adherence → find out why; behind with consistent eating and a reliable trend → 100–250 kcal, never under the floor, never within 14 days (21 on a gain) of the last change); holding is the usual answer. Safety (safety.ts `screenSafety`, its terms from `safetyNotes`): under 18, pregnancy or breastfeeding, an eating-disorder history and a BMI under 18.5 get no weight-loss plan; a missed or irregular period in a deficit, low energy with fast loss or intake under resting energy (IOC REDs) stop cutting; a medical condition keeps loss at the comfortable rate and refers. Check-ins (checkins.ts `scheduleCheckin`) are decided by what the plan needs to know (about 7 days on a new baseline, 10–14 when stable and reliable, 3–7 near a milestone, a competition or off plan, 2 with a health flag; a coach's cadence wins except over health), the date and reason kept, with follow-ups only to tell causes apart (`asks`: tracking, barriers, confounders, the cycle every 28 days for women in a deficit, training): a self-coached athlete answers a few taps on Nutrition (questions.ts, the weekly check-in's answer keys), a coached athlete inside their weekly check-in (`planCheckin` extra `roadmap` → `roadmapSteps`), never a second form, and the weekly check-in itself is unchanged. Who decides: a coached athlete's roadmap is the coach's to approve (nutrition_roadmap_revisions `decider = 'coach'`, invisible to the athlete until approved: RLS lets an athlete read only revisions that are their own decision or were approved (`decided_at`, `athleteSees`, never a draft a newer draft replaced) and a coached athlete no roadmap row at all, since it's the coach's working state; their screens read it on the server after the identity check (`myRoadmapView`)), and approving a target change only puts it in Change it with AI (`planChangeInstruction`, `prefillPlanAiChange`), never writes the plan; a self-coached athlete's first roadmap is in effect at once and every target change waits on their Accept (`writeMemberTargets`: a `member_nutrition_targets` row with `input_snapshot.source = 'roadmap'` and its revision), except small routine ones (≤ 150 kcal, no health flag, not a competition phase, not their first targets) when they switched on consent. Revisions are append-only (`nutrition_roadmap_revisions_keep_record`: only the decision and the words change), deduped by hash; the daily `nutrition-roadmap-tick` reforecasts only for a changed goal, 14+ days off plan or 2 missed check-ins, and `compareRoadmaps` says what changed in words; locked competition phases stay as approved while the date holds (`applyLocks`). Words: the engine's own text everywhere, and two lines per revision from the `nutrition_roadmap` route (GPT-5 mini then GPT-5.5 on the OpenAI key, never Lovable credits) only after `readExplanation` checked them (no number the engine didn't give, nothing the app never plans). The Nutrition Update AI and the check-in recap read the approved roadmap (`roadmapText`) beside the weight-management read. Screens: the athlete's Nutrition page leads with one compact card (`NutritionRoadmapCard`: block, goal and date, status, week of the journey, next check-in, next milestone) opening the journey (chart of actual against planned with its band, the timeline, feasibility, history); the coach's Nutrition tab has `RoadmapCoachCard` (`#roadmap`: the draft or change first with Approve / Decline, then status, chart, journey, settings: review cadence and locked phases); Home's `RoadmapBoardCard` lists who needs a look (hidden at zero) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for goal-based nutrition periodization that plans the whole journey to a date, is honest when a goal isn't realistic, checks in when the plan needs to know something rather than every 7 days, and refuses to change anything without a reason, a safe guardrail and an approval; nothing planned toward a goal or a meet existed, the weight-management card was reactive and coach-only, and self-coached members had a calculator with no direction; and the first run on every coaching client (Nov 22 2026) drafted 150–275 kcal moves to the formula's estimate for three clients the trend said were on track, a calorie start for a client whose plan had no numbers and no weigh-ins since October, and journeys that ignored Alyssa's Dec 5 meet (its class was only on her competition profile) and Jared's Feb 8 Nationals (class not decided); `ROADMAP_VERSION` is in every plan's hash, so bumping it supersedes waiting drafts from an older engine.
- The app is one business until the coaching-platform isolation gate passes (Nov 22 2026, docs/COACHING_PLATFORM.md): `organizations` / `organization_members` / `organization_audit_log` (20270507090000_organizations_foundation.sql) exist, JF Effect is the only organization (`jf_org_id()`), and its members are read from staff access, never written by hand (`_jf_member_sync`, triggers on `user_roles` and `business_owners`: owner, admin, coach, finance; no staff role = revoked, the row and its history kept). The access record is append-only and database-written (who joined, lost or regained access, every `clients.assigned_coach_id` change), read by the organization's owner and admins (`my_admin_org_ids()`, a definer set: a policy on `organization_members` that queried itself recursed) and shown on the coach's page (`CoachAccessRecord`). Nothing here grants access: no independent organization can be created, an outside coach never gets the global `coach` role, and per-person money permissions aren't grantable to a coach until the money functions (`recordPayment`, payment links, discounts) take a client and check it against the coach's assignments, since today they act for any client once the permission passes (built for the finance login). A coach's access follows their coach record in the database (`coaches_access_follows_status`, 20270507091000_team_access_hardening.sql): anything but Active, or archived, takes the `coach` role away at once and Active gives it back, and `acceptCoachInvite` completes only a pending invite sent to that login (`coachInviteAcceptable`) — why: on Nov 22 2026 the audit for the coaching-platform expansion found the schema single-tenant throughout (~447 admin policies, 59 tables and 75 functions trusting the bare `coach` role), suspending or archiving a coach changed only the record so the role kept every door open, and a revoked coach could replay their old invite's `coach_id` (it stays in the login's metadata) to set themselves Active again.
- Purchase term dates change only for the admin, the client's own coach or the server (`_purchase_dates_allowed`, both `update_purchase_term_dates` and `auto_calculate_purchase_term_dates`, which answer "not there" and "not yours" the same way); sign-up asks for the Terms and Privacy Policy with an unticked box the server requires and records the published, reviewed versions (`recordSignupAcceptances`, context `signup`, a document with no such version named in `missing`, never invented); Cleo's table list is cached per login (`loadCatalog(db, caller)`); and the Stripe webhook gives an event's dedupe claim back when handling throws — why: on Nov 22 2026 any coach could change any client's purchase dates and any signed-in account could date an undated purchase and read the whole row back, `/activate` said "By joining you agree…" with no box and no record, Cleo handed a coach the table list an admin had loaded, and a handler that failed returned 500 while Stripe's retry was then dropped as a duplicate, losing the payment event.
- The Nutrition Studio builds a plan's meals food by food on the plan's own text, never a second copy (Nov 22 2026, src/lib/nutrition-studio/ pure and tested in nutrition-studio.test.ts, src/components/nutrition-studio/; 20270508090000_nutrition_studio.sql): a day's meals are read from its paste-format `notes` (`readDay`, the same `MEAL_HEADER` and `structureMealPlanDay` every reader uses) and written back (`writeDay`): a meal nobody touched comes back byte for byte (`raw`), an edited meal's Approximate macros are what its foods give plus what any line it couldn't read already gave (`unreadBase`, from its written macros), and when anything changed the Daily Total is the meals' sum (`withHonestTotals`); a line it can't place on a food is kept exactly as written ("not in the food list"), never guessed. Foods are the app's own tables (`BUILTIN_FOODS`: every value points at food-values' staples or the cookbook's USDA ingredients through `BUILTIN_SOURCES`, a test holds each to its source; nothing new is made up), the person's own foods from a label (`nutrition_custom_foods`, per 100 g or per serving, 100 g can't hold over 100 g of macros, a calorie line that doesn't match its macros flagged, never changed) and the cookbook's recipes as servings; search reads plurals, word order and half-typed words (`searchFoods`), swaps keep what a food is there for (`swapOptions`: protein for a protein, carbs for a carb or fruit, fat for a fat, counted foods in whole or half units), saved meals are a meal's lines (`nutrition_meal_templates`), both private to their maker (RLS, `org_id` empty until organization libraries exist). The coach's plan editor builds a day's meals (Build, the default) or edits its text (Text) on the same `notes`; a JF member (self-serve only) writes their own plan in the same tables (`my_self_serve_client_ids()`, `my_own_nutrition_plan_ids()`; `tg_nutrition_targets_member_plan` keeps a member's own plan visible to them and never naming a coach; a coaching client still can't write theirs) at `/portal/nutrition-studio` (`MemberPlanStudio`: days with their targets, "Use my targets" from their calculated ones, a new plan archiving the one before, `archiveReplacedPlans`), their Nutrition page offering Build it yourself · Import a plan · Create with Cleo when they have none and Edit plan when they do, and their food log reading the plan day for each date (`memberFoodLogPlan`: their committed training days decide training or rest) with its meals one tap away and the grocery list beside it — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for a complete Nutrition Studio for clients and members now (build manually, import a plan or create with Cleo, food search, custom foods and recipes, templates, duplication and substitutions, day types, grocery and PDF) that reuses the existing plan records and readers instead of a second nutrition system; members had only a calculator, and a coach typed every meal and its macros by hand.
- A meal plan comes into the Studio as a draft, and nothing is saved until the person saves it (Nov 22 2026, src/lib/nutrition-import/ pure and tested in nutrition-import.test.ts; src/components/nutrition-studio/ studio-start.tsx, plan-source-picker.tsx, plan-preview.tsx, cleo-questions.tsx, plan-file-import.tsx; 20270508091000_nutrition_imports.sql, supabase/tests/nutrition-imports). Import (`/portal/nutrition-studio?start=import` for a JF member, Import a file in the coach's plan editor's paste section): pasted text or a file (.txt, .csv, .xlsx, .docx read on the phone by the program importer's `extract.ts`, the original kept privately; a PDF or 1-8 photos uploaded to the private `nutrition-imports` bucket under their own folder), after the rights tick (`MEAL_IMPORT_RIGHTS_TEXT`, its exact words and time on the row). The app's own reader goes first, free (`readPlanText`: `parseMealPlan`, or meals with no day headers read as one "Every day" menu; only days whose meals have foods with amounts count); otherwise AI copies it into the paste format in the background (route `nutrition_import`: GPT-5.5 then GPT-6 Astra, OpenAI key only; the `nutrition-imports-tick` hook, woken by its queue trigger and pg_cron; a member's costs 5 AI credits, `nutrition_import`, given back on failure or cancel; a coach's is never charged) and code checks the answer against the source (`planGroundingFlags`: any number or food word not in the file is flagged, "cooked" added to "100 g rice" included; a PDF or photos against the transcript the read returns), never trusts it. The preview shows the days, what's worth a look and the source a tap away; "Edit in your plan" hands `seedFromPlan`'s days to `MemberPlanStudio seed` (a coach's "Use these N days" fills the editor's days like a paste), and once the plan is saved (`onSaved` → `markMealPlanImportSaved`) the original is filed in the owner's documents (`recordDocument`, category `nutrition_plan`, private, linked `nutrition_import`, each photo its own document by `pageLinkId`) before the import's files are deleted. Files go when it's saved or discarded and within 7 days either way; an unsaved row within 30 days, a saved one keeping only its rights record (`nutrition_imports`: server-written, own rows read, nobody else's). Create with Cleo is the one place a member meets Cleo, and it's a meal-plan draft, never a chat: a few tapped answers (`cleanAnswers`: meals, training days and time, likes, what they won't eat, diet, cooking, budget, health), their own saved targets (`member_nutrition_targets`, else their own plan's numbers; none means set targets first; a model never decides a number), a safety screen in code before any call (`createSafety` over `screenSafety` and the Nutrition Update's `safetyNotes` terms: under 18, pregnancy or breastfeeding, an eating disorder, or a medical condition named in their answers, health profile or words means no draft and a kind referral, an eating disorder's never pointing at counting food), written under `MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT` with their answers as the client details (`createUserPrompt`, one EVERY-DAY menu at their targets) on route `nutrition_write` (10 AI credits, `nutrition_create`), one fix round (`withPrepFix`), every Daily Total the meals' sum (`withHonestTotals`), then `draftFlags` (a food they said they avoid, hard to find, meal prep, meals off their targets) and the same preview, labelled a draft to check and never advice from a coach — why: on Nov 22 2026 the coach asked for members to start a plan from one they already have (any file, photos included) or from Cleo, with the import copying and never inventing, the rights confirmed and kept, the cost metered like every member AI feature, and the coach able to bring a client's plan file in the same way; and that a saved import's original be filed in their documents so it outlives the 7-day clean-up.
- A person's files are one library, `documents` (Nov 22 2026, 20270507095000_documents_library.sql, src/lib/documents/: documents.ts pure and tested, documents.server.ts, documents.functions.ts; supabase/tests/documents): what they upload (Account → Documents, `/portal/documents`; a coach on the client's Documents tab, More → `files`; staff's own at `/admin/documents`) and originals other features file for them through `recordDocument` (service role, copied into the owner's folder, linked by `linked_kind` / `linked_id`, idempotent, private unless told otherwise), in the private `documents` bucket at `<owner_user_id>/<uuid>-<safe name>` (25 MB; PDF, images, DOCX, XLSX, CSV, TXT). RLS decides every read: the owner; a client sees what a coach saved for them (`visibility = 'client'`); their assigned coach (`coached_client_ids()`) and admins see `coach` / `client` ones, never a `private` one; a staff member's own (no client) is always private; storage reads follow the rows. Writes are the owner's (a coach for an assigned client): `documents_guard` reads size and type from storage, keeps the path in the owner's folder, needs the rights confirmation (`RIGHTS_TEXT`, unticked to start, stamped by the server) on an upload, and lets only the name, category, tags, visibility and the 30-day Recently deleted change; signed links come only from `getDocumentFile` after the caller's own read of the row; the `documents-purge` hook clears day 30 and an account's final purge takes its documents too. Uploading never makes anything public, and the upload sheet says so once — why: members, clients and staff needed one private place for the programs, plans and files they keep, and imported originals needed a home that never reaches anyone the person didn't choose; consent to upload a file is not permission to share it.
- A Cleo card runs as the person who asked, never as whoever approved it (Nov 22 2026, `CLEO_ACTION_RULES` in cleo-actions.ts, 20270507093000_cleo_actions_run_as_requester.sql): each kind needs tasks, client work, a money permission or the owner, and Cleo offers only what the asker's role can do (a coach role never carries money; the finance login is never offered client work). A coach's card for their own client waits for the owner, whose Approve only authorizes it: it then runs through the service role after `cleo_action_target_allowed` checks the coach's role and the client's assignment again (`requesterActor`), every row naming the coach, and `decided_by` / `decided_at` / `executed_as` record the approval (a check constraint holds `executed_as` to the requester). A kind that runs through request-bound server functions (`fn({ data })`) is never approvable. A coach reads their own clients' money and writes none of it (20270507094000: `purchase_records`, `member_payment_ledger`, `coaching_package_enrollments`, `payment_share_links`; "Charge for it" writes through `sell_booked_sessions`). Any client-keyed table a coach reads is scoped with `client_id IN (SELECT coached_client_ids())`, rows that belong to no client left to the admin (20270507094500), and a server function that checks a staff role and then uses the service role for a client's data asks `staffCanReachClient` (staff-client-access.server.ts) first; supabase/tests/coach-authorization holds all of it as each person, the service role and a suspended coach — why: approving a coach's card ran it with the owner's session and identity; any coach could write sales marked paid through the API, or read every client's AI meal plans, alerts and unmatched submissions; and nutrition-plan server functions let any coach read and message any client.
- AI failures are the provider's or the job's, never a stand-in (Nov 23 2026, src/lib/ai/: ai-errors.ts, ai-usage.ts, ai-health.server.ts, ai-monitor.server.ts, ai-usage.functions.ts; 20270512100000_ai_reliability.sql; ai-reliability.test.ts, supabase/tests/ai-reliability). `classifyAiError` names every failure (credits, auth, rate, outage, timeout, paused, answer, other; `ai_error_is_provider` is its SQL twin, the same cases tested on both): credits, a refused key, an outage and the breaker's own refusal are the provider's (`isProviderFailure`), and a job that hits one never uses an attempt, is never given up on and never gets a templated stand-in: it parks ("Waiting for AI: …", the coaching worker's `AI_WAIT_MS`, Athlete Insights' `fail`, the training roadmap's queue) and is redone once AI answers. Every model call is one row in `ai_call_log` (server only, no policies: task, feature, model, ok / failure, tokens incl. cached, seconds, estimated US$ from `AI_PRICES`, published rates only, never a guess), written by `recordAiCall` from model-client (`callModel`, `callModelNow`), `stagedCall`, `askRoute` (its `tag`, e.g. `recovery_write:workout`, `checkin_recap`), Cleo, pictures and Athlete Insights; a new call path records itself the same way and passes a `tag`. `ai_provider_health` is the circuit breaker: credits or a refused key mark OpenAI down at once, an outage after `OUTAGE_AFTER` failures in a row, and while it's down `assertProviderUp` refuses calls before any request (free) until `next_probe_at`; a dead key's other models are never asked (`askRoute`, `stagedCall`: one call per outage, not two), and a provider failure is never remembered as a model's own (`stagedCall`'s `tried`), so the redo asks it again. The `ai-health-tick` hook (pg_cron every 5 minutes) probes a down provider with one tiny GPT-5 mini call, opens and resolves `support_alerts` (`ai_unavailable`, `ai_failures`, `ai_stuck_jobs`, `ai_unusual_usage` = `unusualUsage`, `ai_budget` from `app_settings.ai_budget`) with one push to the owner per incident, and after a recovery redoes failed check-in recaps from the client's own answers (`recapCheckin`, three a tick, only never-ran, provider-failed or legacy stand-ins, so nothing is retried forever) and calls `ai_recovery_requeue`: background work only (coaching replies, Athlete Insights still to come, Crew decks, a fresh lead's text, a Nutrition Update plan never written), every safeguard running again in its own worker (approval, dedupe, stillGoes, the coach's mode); what a coach asked for himself and a member's paid import are only counted. A check-in recap the model didn't write is `ai_status = 'failed'` with `ai_error` in plain words and a stand-in with no reply (`suggested_response: ""`); an answer missing its summary or reply is a failure, never filled from the stand-in (`readRecapAnswer`); a failed Redo keeps the real recap; the card says the recap is missing with Try again and never offers Use reply on it. Abandoned background responses are cancelled (`cancelBackground`), a dropped or overloaded poll is "still running" (never a second model submitted), and nutrition plans and plan edits stop after `MAX_CUTOFFS` cut-off runs. Crew post decks never run while OpenAI is down and the library backlog goes at most four a day (`community_post_decks_kick`). Settings → AI & Cleo shows it (`AiHealthCard`: status, waiting work, last recovery, Check now; `AiUsageCard`: spend and calls per feature over 7 or 30 days, the monthly trend with a table view, recent failures, the budget) and the Coaching queue says when AI drafts are paused (`useAiPaused`), shows parked items as Waiting for AI, and bands To do (Needs you now, Needs your judgment, Routine, Couldn't be written) with the next item up at once after Send or Dismiss — why: on Nov 21–23 2026 OpenAI's credits ran out (the third time in two weeks; the deck backlog drained them in 25 minutes) and nothing said so: three Athlete Insights never posted, 55 Crew decks and two coaching replies failed after three quick tries, two weekly check-ins got a templated "good update…" saved as their AI recap (with Use reply, and a part-template answer could go out by itself), and only two features kept any record of their calls or cost.
- What AI and texts cost is one page, Insights → Usage & spend (`/admin/usage`, Nov 23 2026; usage-model.ts pure and tested, usage.functions.ts, usage-page.tsx; 20270517090000_admin_intelligence.sql), admins only (the server checks again), read from the ledgers that already exist, never a second log: `ai_call_log` (`ops_ai_daily`, `ops_ai_by_model`, `ai_usage_by_task`, `ai_usage_by_month`) and `sms_log` (`ops_sms_daily`, `ops_sms_by_category`, `sms_cost_summary`), plus `readHealthView` (exported from ai-usage.functions.ts) and `ai_credits_overview`. Both vendors bill in US dollars, so spend is US$; the text budget is the coach's CAD figure and compared in CAD. Every AI task and text purpose belongs to one business area (`aiArea` / `smsArea`: Coaching, Clients & members, Membership, Crew, Cleo, Sales, Automations & alerts, Sign-in & security, else Other; a new AI tag adds its area there), and the page is: what needs a look (`usageAlerts`: AI paused with what's waiting, over or near a budget, no AI budget while AI costs something, unusual use, a feature failing 10%+ of 10+ calls, texts not delivered, any spend through Lovable credits; each with its next step, nothing on a quiet month), the month for each (spent, budget meter, pace to month end, `projectMonth`), spend by day (AI and texts stacked, one axis, table view), where it goes (by area, a tap filters the features), the most expensive features and the models (cost per answered call, success, seconds, "Lovable credits" on gateway models), texts (asked → delivered / not confirmed / not delivered / not sent, by purpose), health and waiting work (Check now), and Budgets & limits. A cost a model has no price for is "unpriced", counted and never costed; a text Twilio hasn't confirmed is "not confirmed", never delivered. The one new limit is the background-AI hold: with a monthly AI budget set and "Hold background AI past the budget" on (`app_settings.ai_budget` `hold_background`, on unless the owner turns it off; `setAiBudgetFn`), `refreshSpendHold` (ai-monitor.server.ts, hourly in the ai-health tick and at once on a budget save, `spendHoldFor` pure) writes `app_settings.ai_spend_hold`, and `community_post_decks_kick` starts no background deck (priority 40+, the library backlog) while `ai_background_held()` says so; lessons and tips due soon, coaching, client features, Cleo and anything the coach starts never wait, and the first hold of a month pushes the owner once. The budget and usage pushes open `/admin/usage`; Settings → AI & Cleo keeps who has Cleo, the models and credit allowances — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach asked for one place that shows AI and SMS cost, usage, reliability and spending controls by feature and business area; OpenAI credits had run out three times in two weeks (the Crew deck backlog drained them in 25 minutes) with no AI budget ever set, and the cost of texts and AI sat on two settings pages that never compared them.
- How people use the app and the site is one page, Insights → App & website (`/admin/insights`, Nov 23 2026; app-intel-model.ts pure and tested, app-intel.functions.ts, app-intel-page.tsx, place-map.tsx; `app_intel_report`). Visits are first-party and privacy-safe (src/lib/app-pulse: pulse-rules.ts pure and tested, pulse-client.ts, pulse.server.ts, `/api/public/app-pulse`; `app_sessions` / `app_page_views`, server only, RLS with no policies, kept 400 days by pg_cron `app-visits-retention`): `AppPulse` (root, loaded once the app has painted) beats once a minute while the app is on screen and sends the pages opened as a path with ids and tokens taken out (`normalizePath`) and the feature it belongs to (`featureOf`; a new page area adds its rule there), the surface (site / app / admin), the platform (iPhone app, Android app, home-screen app, browser), a device class and an OS family, never a model; a visit ends on 30 minutes away (`SESSION_IDLE_MS`); a signed-in person is identified by their own bearer token (`getClaims`), anyone else by the join funnel's anonymous browser id (`visitorId`). A place comes only from the host's IP geolocation on a visit's first beat, kept to a city and a point rounded to 0.1° (`coarsePlace`); the IP is never stored, src/server.ts copies Cloudflare's `request.cf` onto `x-jf-geo-*` headers for that one path after dropping any a browser sent, and the map shows only places (city bubbles), never a person. Not recorded: a coach in View as client, a test login, Lovable's preview hosts and local development, bots, and an anonymous visitor whose browser sends Global Privacy Control or Do Not Track (a signed-in one is counted with no place); 120 beats a minute per address, 600 pages and 5 minutes of active time a beat per visit (`app_pulse_record`). Definitions on the page: active = a signed-in client or member who opened the app that day (staff and test logins never count; before Nov 23 2026 only coaching clients' `signed_in` records exist, so earlier days undercount members), live = a heartbeat in the last 5 minutes (`getLiveNowFn`, every 30 s), website visitor = an anonymous browser on the landing page (`membership_funnel_events`). The page answers one question per section: what's worth acting on (`takeaways`, from its own numbers and only with enough data), who's active (week, month, sign-ups, website visitors, each against the period before), active by day, how people find you (landing → saw prices → applied → joined, sources, devices), getting started (account → came back → trained in week 1 → active in week 4), what people do (from each feature's own records, 30 days) and the pages they open, where they are, coming back (monthly cohorts), when they open the app (weekday × hour, Winnipeg), devices, revenue (cash in before tax, voided left out) with subscriptions running / ending / failing and coaching starts and ends, and staff pages used (never counted above). The report reads each source once and groups by day (never a scan per day) and looks back at most 200 days — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach asked for live and active users, sessions, registrations, funnels, retention, churn, feature adoption, devices and an approximate map; the app recorded only coaching clients' sign-ins and the site's funnel steps, with no sessions, pages, devices or places, and the privacy policy already discloses login records, app activity, device details and approximate location from IP.
- The Membership page (`/admin/membership`, Nov 23 2026; membership-home.tsx, membership.functions.ts, membership-model.ts pure and tested; `membership_overview`) leads with what decides whether a free membership works: members (and new against the period before), active this week, trained in 30 days, members who became coaching clients (and what they've paid), growth by month (members and how many opened the app), and joining (applied → approved → joined → trained in week 1, `membership_funnel`); then Needs you (applications waiting with the oldest's age, approved not joined, members a week+ in who haven't trained with Client view and Plan on the row, setup unfinished, and only while there's any, legacy paid payments failing and access that ran out with Grant 7 days), then Plans (the free membership with its library and credit sales; the listed coaching packages with prices as the step up; legacy paid subscriptions only when any exist), then the members (`MembersDirectory`). A member is the one athlete record, `clients.service_tier = 'self_serve'` with a login, never staff, a test login or a preview sandbox. Paid JF Membership is retired, so its pages (Action Needed, Subscriptions, Stripe events, checkout settings, setup and reset links, onboarding email, signup stats) are out of the menu and in the page's ⋯ with the member-app preview, never deleted; the menu row is Overview and Members. Changing plan is one sheet (`ChangePlanSheet`, from the Membership home's rows and the Clients list ⋯, admins): free member ↔ coaching client on the same record, so every workout, record, post and message stays; it shows what changes first (`planChangeLines`) and what stops it (`planChangeProblems`: no login, a deactivated or pending-deletion account, and moving to the free membership while a coaching subscription would keep charging: stop it from their Billing first), `changePlanFn` reads all of that again on the server, writes only the tier, the status word and the coach (a free member record is made for someone who never had one), never charges, refunds or calls Stripe, leaves what they owe owed and booked sessions booked (said first), and records `moved_to_coaching` / `moved_to_membership` in `account_lifecycle_events` (the member page's Move to coaching records it too) — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach found the Membership page cluttered and operationally weak (four rows of navigation cards and nine status tiles for a paid membership that no longer exists, while applications, members who never trained and the path into coaching weren't on it), and there was no way to move a coaching client back to the free app without deactivating their account.
- The way out of client view says so: the View as client banner's button is "Back to admin" (filled, `exit`) and its label "Client view: what they see", never the bare "Coach" — why: on Nov 23 2026 the coach asked for coach and client views clearly told apart with an obvious return to admin.
